  that set `OnToolCallStart:` together with `OnToolExecutionStart:`, or
  `OnToolCallFinish:` together with `OnToolExecutionEnd:`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you set both. Move the deprecated
  callback's work into the new one.

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A PrepareStep that sets only System changes the step instructions {#preparestep-system-override}

- **What changed:** in `GenerateText` and `StreamText`, a `PrepareStep`
  callback that sets `System` and returns the options it got now sends that
  system prompt for the step. In v0.5 the `Instructions` pointer it got back
  still held the old value and won, so the new `System` was ignored. TS reads
  `prepareStepResult.instructions ?? prepareStepResult.system`.
  `Instructions` still wins when the callback sets it (a new pointer, or a
  write through the given one).
- **Am I affected?** `PrepareStep:` callbacks that assign `System` (for
  example `step.System = ...`) and return the options they got.
- **What to do:** no action unless such a callback relied on its `System`
  being ignored; remove that assignment to keep the v0.5 prompt:

  ```go
  opts := ai.GenerateTextOptions{
  	PrepareStep: func(ctx context.Context, step ai.PrepareStepOptions) ai.PrepareStepOptions {
  		step.System = "Answer briefly." // v0.6: used for this step; v0.5: ignored
  		return step
  	},
  }
  _ = opts
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ExperimentalStreamTranscribe reports the cancel reason after Close {#stream-transcribe-cancel-reason}

- **What changed:** after `FullStream().Close()`, `Text()` and the other
  `StreamTranscriptionResult` accessors return `Transcription stream was
  cancelled.`, the reason TS `Transformer.cancel` aborts the pipe with. v0.5
  returned that text only when the pipe was blocked sending a part; while it
  waited on the provider it returned the provider's `context canceled`. When
  your own `ctx` is cancelled instead, the accessors return the ctx's cause
  (`context.Canceled` unless you set one), also while a send was blocked
  (v0.5 returned the cancel text there).
- **Am I affected?** `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` or
  `"Transcription stream was cancelled."` checks on `StreamTranscriptionResult`
  accessor errors.
- **What to do:** match the text after `Close`, or cancel your own context
  and check `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)`:

  ```go
  func closed(res *ai.StreamTranscriptionResult) bool {
  	stream, err := res.FullStream()
  	if err != nil {
  		return false
  	}
  	_ = stream.Close()
  	_, err = res.Text()
  	return err != nil && err.Error() == "Transcription stream was cancelled."
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Streaming transcription responses carry no body {#stream-transcribe-response-body}

- **What changed:** In v0.5, `StreamTranscriptionResult.Responses()` copied the `Body` of a provider `response-metadata` part. In v0.6 it keeps only the timestamp, model ID and headers, as TS stream-transcribe.ts does. `TranscriptionStreamOptions.ProviderOptions` is now `{}` (not nil) when the caller sets none, as TS's `providerOptions = {}` default (review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** You are affected if you read `Responses()[i].Body` from `ExperimentalStreamTranscribe`, or if your custom `provider.TranscriptionStreamer` checks `opts.ProviderOptions == nil`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the streamed body. Read the raw provider messages with `IncludeRawChunks` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Streaming audio results wait for the FullStream reader {#stream-audio-fullstream-demand}

- **What changed:** In v0.5, after `FullStream()` was claimed, `ExperimentalStreamTranscribe` and `ExperimentalStreamTranslate` processed provider parts that are not forwarded to the stream (stream-start, response-metadata, finish) without waiting for the reader, so an accessor could resolve before the stream was read. In v0.6 the pipe processes provider parts only while the stream's reader has a `Next` pending, as TS does (TransformStream high-water mark 0). An accessor called without `FullStream()` still drains the stream itself (review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** You are affected if you call `FullStream()` and then wait on `Text()`, `TranslationText()` or another accessor before you read the stream to its end.
- **What to do:** Read `FullStream()` to `io.EOF` (in another goroutine if needed) before you wait on an accessor, or use the accessors without `FullStream()`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ExperimentalStreamTranslate reports the TS cancel reasons {#stream-translate-cancel-reason}

- **What changed:** after `FullStream().Close()`, the
  `StreamTranslationResult` accessors return `Translation stream was
  cancelled.` also while the provider is still reading (v0.5 returned the
  provider's `context canceled` there). When your own `ctx` is cancelled they
  return the ctx's cause, also while a send was blocked (v0.5 returned the
  Close text there), as `ExperimentalStreamTranscribe` does. After your ctx is
  cancelled, a part the provider still returns goes to a `FullStream` reader
  that is waiting for it (TS passes `abortSignal` only to the model); after
  `Close` no further part is written. The same holds for transcription.
  v0.5 picked either at random (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** `"Translation stream was cancelled."` or
  `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` checks on `StreamTranslationResult`
  accessor errors; FullStream readers that stop at the first part after
  cancelling their ctx.
- **What to do:** match the text after `Close`, or cancel your own context
  and check `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)`. Stop reading after you
  cancel if you do not want the provider's last parts.
- **Kind:** error text only

### ExperimentalStreamTranslate reports an unknown part type with the TS text {#stream-translate-unsupported-part-text}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ExperimentalStreamTranslate` failed on a provider
  part of an unknown type with `unsupported part type: <type>`. v0.6 uses the
  TS `stream-translate.ts` exhaustive-check text, which formats the part
  object: `Unsupported part type: [object Object]` (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches the text of errors from
  `ExperimentalStreamTranslate` result accessors or `FullStream().Next()`:
  search for `unsupported part type`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare this error text; compare it
  to `"Unsupported part type: [object Object]"`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Stop conditions run only when the loop would continue {#stop-conditions-only-when-continue}

- **What changed:** `StopWhen` conditions are evaluated only when the loop
  would otherwise run another step (its tool calls were resolved), as TS. A
  step that ends the loop on its own does not call them.
- **Am I affected?** Stop conditions with side effects, such as counting or
  logging every step.
- **What to do:** move side effects to `OnStepFinish`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Generated IDs use the TS formats {#generated-id-formats}

- **What changed:** response, approval and UI message IDs use the TS
  defaults: `aitxt-` plus 24 characters for text calls and the agent loop,
  `aiobj-` plus 24 for object calls, and 16 characters for
  `CreateUIMessageStream` message IDs. v0.5 used `call-` plus 24 characters.
  Approval IDs come from the call's ID generator
  (`InternalOptions.GenerateID`). Telemetry call IDs keep the `call-` prefix.
- **Am I affected?** Code or tests that check the `call-` prefix or the
  length of these IDs.
- **What to do:** treat IDs as opaque strings.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### JSON is not HTML-escaped {#json-not-html-escaped}

- **What changed:** JSON written to requests, telemetry and UI streams keeps
  `<`, `>` and `&` literal, as `JSON.stringify`. v0.5 wrote `<`,
  `>` and `&`. Query strings use the `URLSearchParams` byte set
  (`~` is encoded, `*` is not).
  Round 96 adds the error text of a provider-executed tool result flagged
  as an error (`StreamText` and the AI Gateway V4 wire), MCP JSON-RPC
  messages and transport bodies, the Cerebras body rewritten for per-call
  extras, and the `WorkflowChatTransport` request body.
- **Am I affected?** Golden files or request matchers that expect the escaped
  form.
- **What to do:** update the expected JSON.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Numbers beyond the double range read as Infinity and are written as null {#out-of-range-numbers}

- **What changed:** A provider value holding a number beyond the double range (for example `1e400`) failed the decode of the whole body, frame or chunk in v0.5 (`cannot unmarshal number 1e400`), or dropped the provider metadata it was in. v0.6 reads it as `math.Inf(1)` / `math.Inf(-1)` wherever Go decodes into `interface{}` or a map (provider metadata, raw chunks, `Usage.Raw`, tool inputs, error data, generated objects), as TS `JSON.parse` reads Infinity, and writes NaN and ±Inf as `null` wherever the SDK serializes such a value (request bodies, provider metadata JSON, the UI message stream, telemetry attributes, MCP messages, speech stream envelopes), as `JSON.stringify` does. `jsonstr.Marshal` writes NaN and ±Inf as `null` instead of returning an error. Where TS validates the value with zod (`z.number()`, `z.json()`, `.catchall(z.json())`), a non-finite number fails the response or chunk as in TS, and the zod-style issue carries `"received": "Infinity"`. A typed numeric field (`float64`, `int`) still fails the decode (review round 96, main ruling 4).
- **Am I affected?** Code that calls `jsonstr.Marshal` and expects an error for NaN or ±Inf, and code that runs `encoding/json.Marshal` on provider metadata, raw chunks or tool inputs (a value read from `1e400` makes that call return `*json.UnsupportedValueError`).
- **What to do:** No action unless you marshal provider values yourself: use `jsonstr.Marshal` from `pkg/providerutils/jsonstr`, which writes `null` like `JSON.stringify` (known difference "Infinity in provider values").
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The final SSE event needs its blank line {#sse-final-event}

- **What changed:** SSE streams follow TS `eventsource-parser` 3.0.8: a final
  event with no closing blank line, or a last line with no line terminator,
  is dropped. v0.5 dispatched it. This applies to every SSE provider and the
  AI Gateway video reader.
- **Am I affected?** Custom servers, proxies or test fixtures that end an SSE
  stream without a blank line after the last event.
- **What to do:** end every event with a blank line (`\n\n`).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Rerank `OnEnd` gets its own ranking {#rerank-onend-ranking-copy}

- **What changed:** `RerankOnFinishEvent.Ranking` passed to `OnEnd`,
  `ExperimentalOnEnd` and `ExperimentalOnFinish` is now a copy of
  `RerankResult.Ranking`. In v0.5 it was the same slice, so editing
  `event.Ranking[i]` in the callback changed the returned result. TS builds
  the event ranking with its own `ranking.map(...)` (rerank.test.ts "should
  isolate the result from ranking mutations in onEnd", review round 71).
- **Am I affected?** `OnEnd`, `ExperimentalOnEnd` or `ExperimentalOnFinish`
  callbacks of `ai.Rerank` that write to `event.Ranking` and expect
  `RerankResult.Ranking` to change.
- **What to do:** no action unless you edit the ranking in the callback.
  Edit the returned result instead:

  ```go
  func rerankTop(ctx context.Context, model provider.RerankingModel, docs []string, query string) (*ai.RerankResult, error) {
  	result, err := ai.Rerank(ctx, ai.RerankOptions{Model: model, Documents: docs, Query: query})
  	if err != nil {
  		return nil, err
  	}
  	if len(result.Ranking) > 0 {
  		result.Ranking[0].Score = 1 // edit the result, not event.Ranking
  	}
  	return result, nil
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### EmbedMany returns the first batch failure in time {#embedmany-first-failure}

- **What changed:** when several parallel `EmbedMany` batch calls fail, the
  returned error is the one that failed first, not the lowest batch's. TS
  `embedMany` awaits `Promise.all`, which rejects with the first rejection
  (review round 72). EmbedMany returns as soon as the first call fails; the
  group's other calls finish in the background (bounded by the ctx) and
  their results are dropped (review round 87e). v0.5 waited for every
  sibling.
- **Am I affected?** Code that inspects the error of an `ai.EmbedMany` call
  with several failing parallel batches (`MaxParallelCalls`, a model with
  `SupportsParallelCalls`) and expects the first batch's error.
- **What to do:** no action unless you depend on which batch's error is
  returned.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Embed rejects only a missing embedding, with InvalidResponseDataError {#embed-no-embeddings-invalid-response}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `Embed` rejected an empty vector, and the built-in
  `DoEmbed` methods returned plain `fmt.Errorf` errors for a response
  without embeddings: `no embedding data in response` (OpenAI, Cohere,
  Fireworks, Mistral, Ollama, Together, Voyage, Google),
  `no embedding returned from Alibaba` and
  `no embedding returned from Azure OpenAI`. Google `DoEmbed` also failed on
  `values: []`, and Google `DoEmbedMany` failed on an empty `values` or with
  `google embedding response count N does not match input count M`. v0.6
  follows TS `embed()`, which rejects only `embeddings[0] == null`: those
  `DoEmbed` methods return `*providererrors.InvalidResponseDataError` with
  the message `No embedding generated.` (`Data` is `[][]float64{}`), an
  empty vector is returned as the result, and Google returns the embeddings
  as sent (`EmbedMany` still rejects a count mismatch with
  `*providererrors.InvalidResponseDataError`
  `Expected N embeddings, but received M.`) (review round 80). Amazon
  Bedrock and Perplexity now follow the same rule (review round 81): a
  response without embeddings makes `DoEmbed` return
  `*providererrors.InvalidResponseDataError` "No embedding generated."
  (Bedrock returned `no embeddings in response`; Perplexity panicked), and
  Bedrock `DoEmbedMany` returns the embeddings as sent, so `EmbedMany`
  reports "Expected N embeddings, but received M." instead of Bedrock's own
  `bedrock cohere embedding batch returned ...` error.
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches those error strings, calls
  `DoEmbed`/`DoEmbedMany` directly and relies on them, or relies on `Embed`
  failing for an empty vector; `bedrock` and `perplexity` embedding models;
  error strings `no embeddings in response`, `bedrock cohere embedding batch
  returned`.
- **What to do:**

  ```go
  var invalid *providererrors.InvalidResponseDataError
  if errors.As(err, &invalid) {
  	// the response had no embedding
  }
  if len(res.Embedding) == 0 {
  	// an empty vector is now a result
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Response headers fall back to GenerateResult.ResponseHeaders {#response-headers-fallback}

- **What changed:** when a provider's `ResponseMetadata` has no `Headers` but
  its `GenerateResult.ResponseHeaders` is set (gmicloud, zai), the step and
  result `Response.Headers` of `GenerateText`, the step-end and end events,
  and `GenerateObjectResult.Response.Headers` now carry those headers instead
  of nil. TS `response.headers` is the HTTP response headers (review round
  72).
- **Am I affected?** Code that expects nil `Response.Headers` from those
  providers or from a custom model built the same way.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamObject logs warnings only after a finish part {#streamobject-warning-logging}

- **What changed:** `StreamObject` logs the model's warnings when the stream
  sends a finish part (also after an error part), or when it ends cleanly
  without one. A stream that fails without a finish part logs nothing. TS logs
  the warnings in its finish part handler (review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Code that counts `SetLogWarnings` calls for failing
  `StreamObject` streams.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamObject provider metadata comes from the finish part {#streamobject-finish-provider-metadata}

- **What changed:** `StreamObject`'s result `ProviderMetadata` (and the
  telemetry end events) is the provider metadata of the stream's finish part,
  or nil when the finish part has none. v0.5 used the last metadata of any
  chunk, so text-part metadata such as an OpenAI Responses `itemId` could
  become the call's metadata. TS stream-object.ts assigns `providerMetadata`
  in its finish handler (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `StreamObjectResult.ProviderMetadata`
  with providers that put metadata on text chunks and none on the finish part.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText steps carry their warnings {#stream-step-warnings}

- **What changed:** each `StreamText` step result now carries the warnings of
  its model call (`types.StepResult.Warnings`), as TS `step.warnings` does,
  so `Steps()[i].Warnings`, `FinalStep().Warnings`, the step-end callbacks'
  `StepResult` and the end event's `Steps` show them. v0.5 left
  `StepResult.Warnings` empty for `StreamText` (`Warnings()` and the end
  event's `Warnings` were already filled) (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `Warnings` on `StreamText` step results
  (`Steps()`, `FinalStep()`, `OnStepEnd`, `OnEndEvent`'s `Steps`/`FinalStep`)
  and expects it empty.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the empty field.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A cancellation during tool execution aborts the StreamText step {#stream-abort-during-tools}

- **What changed:** when the caller's context ends while `StreamText` runs a
  step's tools, the step is aborted even if every started tool returns: the
  full and UI streams end with the `abort` part right after the tool call,
  without the late tool result or a `finish-step`, the step is not recorded,
  and `OnAbortEvent` gets only the steps completed before it. This is TS's
  behavior (its stream stops when the abort signal fires). v0.5 emitted the
  tool result and `finish-step`, recorded the step, and then sent `abort`
  (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** `StreamText` callers that cancel their context from a
  tool (or while tools run) and read `ToolResults()`, `Steps()`,
  `OnAbortEvent` `Steps`, or `tool-result`/`finish-step` parts after the
  cancellation.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the late result.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText ExperimentalContext follows a PrepareStep runtime context {#stream-experimental-context-prepared}

- **What changed:** the deprecated `ExperimentalContext` field of the
  `StreamText` step-start, tool execution, step-end and end events now carries
  the same value as `RuntimeContext`, also after a `PrepareStep` replaced the
  runtime context, as `GenerateText` already did. v0.5 kept the call's initial
  context in `ExperimentalContext` on every later event. TS has one
  `runtimeContext` (review round 74).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `ExperimentalContext` on `OnStepStartEvent`,
  `OnToolCallStartEvent`, `OnToolCallFinishEvent`, `OnStepFinishEvent` or
  `OnFinishEvent` from `StreamText` and sets `PrepareStepOptions.RuntimeContext`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the initial value; read
  `RuntimeContext`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A step's response headers are its own {#stream-step-headers-per-step}

- **What changed:** `StepResult.Response.Headers` of a StreamText step (and
  `StreamTextResult.ResponseHeaders()` / `ResponseHeadersMap()`, the last
  step's) are the headers that step's model stream reported. v0.5 kept the
  last headers seen in the run, so a later step, or a recovered
  `StreamRetries` attempt, whose stream reported none showed an earlier
  step's or the failed attempt's headers. TS takes each finish-step's
  `response.headers` from that step's `doStream` result (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading `Response.Headers` of steps after the first,
  or `ResponseHeaders()` / `ResponseHeadersMap()`, with providers that report
  headers only on some calls.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on earlier headers carrying
  over; read `Steps()[i].Response.Headers` of the step you need.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Timeout.PerChunk starts with the first output chunk {#stream-per-chunk-after-first-output}

- **What changed:** `TimeoutConfig.PerChunk` (TS `chunkMs`) now bounds only
  the gaps between semantic output chunks. v0.5 also applied it to the wait
  for the first chunk of every step and stream retry (and to every read), so
  a slow start failed with `TimeoutReasonChunk`. The wait for the first
  output is bounded by `FirstChunk` (TS `firstChunkMs`) only, as TS starts
  its chunk timer after output begins. An error part also stops both timers
  until the next output chunk (TS `clearModelOutputTimeouts`), so a pause
  after a soft error part is no longer a timeout (review round 82). The
  first-chunk and chunk timers also stop at the model call's finish part
  (TS `clearModelOutputTimeouts()` on `model-call-end`), so a pause between
  the provider's finish part and the end of its stream is no longer a
  `TimeoutError` with `TimeoutReasonFirstChunk` or `TimeoutReasonChunk`
  (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** StreamText calls that set `Timeout.PerChunk` without
  `FirstChunk` and rely on it to fail a model that never starts answering;
  checks for `TimeoutReasonChunk` or `TimeoutReasonFirstChunk` on a slow
  first chunk, after an error part or after the finish part.
- **What to do:** Set `FirstChunk` to bound the first chunk:
  `Timeout: &ai.TimeoutConfig{FirstChunk: &first, PerChunk: &perChunk}`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Timeout errors use the TS abort reason text {#timeout-error-text}

- **What changed:** `(*ai.TimeoutError).Error()` reads like the TS abort reason when the
  configured duration is known: a step, first-chunk or chunk timeout reads
  `Step timeout of 100ms exceeded` / `First chunk timeout of 50ms exceeded` /
  `Chunk timeout of 50ms exceeded` (TS `setAbortTimeout`), in StreamText and
  (step) GenerateText; a total timeout and a tool timeout read `The operation
  was aborted due to timeout` (TS `AbortSignal.timeout`, `mergeAbortSignals`),
  in GenerateText, StreamText and `ToolLoopAgent`. v0.5 read `<reason> timeout
  exceeded: context deadline exceeded`, which a `TimeoutError` without a
  duration still reads. New additive field `TimeoutError.Timeout` and method
  `TimeoutError.ErrorName()` (`"TimeoutError"`), so a tool that fails with its
  timed-out context reaches the model as `TimeoutError: The operation was
  aborted due to timeout` (TS `getErrorMessage(error)`; was `Error: tool
  timeout exceeded: context deadline exceeded`). The StreamText total
  deadline's `context.Cause` is the `TimeoutError`, so `OnAbortEvent.Reason`
  is a `*ai.TimeoutError` (`errors.Is(reason, context.DeadlineExceeded)` still
  holds) (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares `err.Error()` of an `*ai.TimeoutError` or
  matches `timeout exceeded`; tool-error text the model sees for timed-out
  tools; `OnAbortEvent.Reason == context.DeadlineExceeded`.
- **What to do:** Match with `errors.As` and the reason, or `errors.Is(err,
  context.DeadlineExceeded)`:
  ```go
  var timeoutErr *ai.TimeoutError
  if errors.As(err, &timeoutErr) && timeoutErr.Reason == ai.TimeoutReasonChunk {
  	// handle a chunk timeout
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** error text only

### The abort part reason is the TS reason text {#abort-part-reason}

- **What changed:** the `abort` part (`StreamChunk.AbortReason`) and the UI message
  stream `abort` chunk's `reason` are TS `getErrorMessage(abortSignal.reason)`:
  `AbortError: This operation was aborted` for a plain context cancel (v0.5:
  `context canceled`), `TimeoutError: <timeout message>` for SDK timeouts and
  `TimeoutError: The operation was aborted due to timeout` for the caller's own
  context deadline (v0.5: `context deadline exceeded`). A
  `context.WithCancelCause` cause keeps its `Error()` text (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** Code or clients that read `AbortReason` or the UI `abort` chunk's
  `reason` and compare it with `context canceled`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the reason text; compare with the new
  strings or use `OnAbortEvent.Reason` with `errors.Is`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Step and total timeouts are a TimeoutError everywhere in StreamText {#stream-timeout-classification}

- **What changed:** a step or total deadline that ends a stream retry's reopened model
  call, a generated-file or reasoning-file download, tool-call parsing or
  repair, the approval pre-check or a tool input callback (`OnInputStart` /
  `OnInputDelta` / `OnInputAvailable`) is now a `*ai.TimeoutError` (step or
  total) and an abort with the timeout reason, as TS (the step deadline is
  cumulative across retries, `stream-text-timeout.test.ts` 772). v0.5 returned
  the raw context error there (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `StreamTextResult.Err()` checks that expect
  `context.DeadlineExceeded` itself (`err == context.DeadlineExceeded`) with
  `Timeout.PerStep` or `Timeout.Total`.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)` or `errors.As`
  with `*ai.TimeoutError`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A tool that returns after its timeout keeps its result {#tool-timeout-result}

- **What changed:** v0.5 turned the result of a tool that returned after its
  `TimeoutConfig.ToolMs` / `Tools[name]` deadline into a `TimeoutError`, even
  when the tool returned without error. v0.6 does what TS
  `execute-tool-call.ts` does: the deadline only cancels the tool's context. A
  tool that still returns a result keeps it; only an error the tool returns is
  reported as the tool timeout (`GenerateText`, `StreamText`,
  `ToolLoopAgent`), with the text of [Timeout error text](#timeout-error-text)
  (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** Tools that ignore `ctx.Done()` with `TimeoutConfig.ToolMs` or
  `Tools` set; code expecting `TimeoutReasonTool` for them.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the late result being discarded;
  return `ctx.Err()` from the tool to report the timeout.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText merges an Output's text deltas and repeats a block's provider metadata {#stream-output-text-deltas}

- **What changed:** v0.5 forwarded every provider text delta as it arrived. v0.6 runs TS `createOutputTransformStream` (stream-text.ts 1061-1224): with an `Output` (object, array, choice, json), the deltas of the step's first text block that do not change the parsed partial are held and published together with the next one that does (or before the block's `text-end` / the step's `finish-step`). In every default StreamText run (and with an Output in `StreamTextLowMemory`), a delta of that block without provider metadata carries the block's latest metadata. An empty delta without metadata publishes no partial (`PartialOutput()` stays nil).
- **Am I affected?** Code that counts or keys on `ChunkTypeText` parts from `FullStream()`, `OnChunk`, `Chunks()` or UI `text-delta` chunks with `Output` set; code that reads `ProviderMetadata` on text deltas; code that expects `PartialOutput()` to be `""` after a stream whose only text was an empty delta.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on one delta per provider chunk. Concatenate the deltas (the text is unchanged).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### SmoothStream prefix error text {#smooth-stream-prefix-error}

- **What changed:** The error for a chunking function whose match is not a prefix of the buffer now reads as TS `a71172de0d`: `Chunking function must return a match that is a prefix of the buffer. Received: "<match>" which is not a prefix of buffer "<buffer>"`.
- **Am I affected?** Code matching the old `smoothStream` / `SmoothStream` prefix error message.
- **What to do:** Match the error type, not the text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### FixJSON at an array start {#fix-json-array-start}

- **What changed:** `jsonparser.FixJSON` no longer treats the position right after `[` as a valid end: `[-` repairs to `[]` (v0.5: `[-]`, invalid JSON) and whitespace after `[` is dropped (`{"a": [ ` repairs to `{"a": []}`) (TS `8ba3a5c254`). Partial objects from streaming output therefore parse one chunk earlier for arrays that start with a negative number.
- **Am I affected?** Callers comparing `jsonparser.FixJSON` / `ParsePartialJSON` text output.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare the repaired text.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText streams each tool result when it settles {#streamtext-live-tool-results}

- **What changed:** v0.5 ran a step's tool calls and then emitted every `ChunkTypeToolResult` in call order once the last call returned; a cancel during the tools dropped them all. v0.6 streams each executed call's result as soon as it settles (TS `executeToolsFromStream`), so with the default unlimited `ToolCallConcurrency` parallel results arrive in completion order, the step's `Content` and `ToolResults` follow that order, and a result that settled before a cancel is still streamed before the `abort` part. The tool message in `ResponseMessages` keeps call order. `ToolCallConcurrency: 1` keeps call order. WorkflowAgent keeps writing a step's results in call order after all its calls settled (TS `writeToolResults`) (review round 86). Each result is streamed after the user and telemetry tool-end notifications, as TS (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `ChunkTypeToolResult` from `Stream()` / `OnChunk` / the UI stream, or `step.Content` / `step.ToolResults`, and assumes call order; tests that pin it.
- **What to do:** Match results by `ToolCallID`, or set `ToolCallConcurrency: &one` (`one := 1`) for sequential execution in call order.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText toolResults follow the step content {#streamtext-tool-results-content-order}

- **What changed:** A step's `ToolResults` (also `OnStepEndEvent`, `OnEndEvent`, `StreamTextResult.ToolResults()`) listed locally executed results first and streamed provider-executed results after them. v0.6 lists them in content order, as TS derives `toolResults` from the step content: a provider result streamed during the model call comes before a local result (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** Steps that mix provider-executed tools (web search, code execution) with local tools, and code that indexes `ToolResults` by position.
- **What to do:** Match results by `ToolCallID`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText resumed approvals stream in completion order {#stream-resume-completion-order}

- **What changed:** v0.5 StreamText emitted the results of approved tools resumed from history, and built the tool message before the first step, in call order. v0.6 uses completion order, as TS streamText does (each result is enqueued when its execution settles). GenerateText keeps call order (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** `StreamText` with `ToolApproval` continuations of several approved tools; code reading the leading `ChunkTypeToolResult` chunks or `ResponseMessages()[0]` by position.
- **What to do:** No action unless you index results by position; match by `ToolCallID`, or set `ToolCallConcurrency: &one` for call order.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText forwards the parts TS forwards before a failure {#stream-failure-part-order}

- **What changed:** v0.5 StreamText failed before forwarding the tool-call part when the call's approval could not be resolved (for example a tool context that fails its schema), stopped at a provider-executed tool result whose `ToModelOutput` failed, and failed a local tool's `ToModelOutput` before the step's finish-step. v0.6 forwards those parts first, as TS does: the tool-call part, then the error; and every part of the step through `finish-step`, then the `ToModelOutput` error (TS converts tool outputs when it builds the step's response messages). `OnStepEnd` is still not called for that step. WorkflowAgent keeps the TS WorkflowAgent order (no tool result or finish-step before the error) (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** Code that inspects the chunks before `Err()` on a failing `ToModelOutput` or an approval with `ContextSchema` / `ToolsContext`: `ToModelOutput`, `ToolApproval`, `ContextSchema`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on the stream ending before those parts.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A SmoothStream detector failure ends the StreamText run {#smooth-stream-detector-error-ends-run}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `SmoothStream` inside `StreamText` turned a chunk detector failure (a `ChunkDetector` returning `""` or a non-prefix match, a `*regexp.Regexp` matching the empty string) into an error part, called `OnError`, and passed the rest of the stream through. v0.6 ends the run there as TS (the transform throws): no error part or later part is emitted, no `OnError` / `OnStepEnd` / `OnEnd`, and `ReadAll` / `Err()` return the error. Calling the transform directly still returns the error part (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** `ai.SmoothStream` with a custom `Chunking` detector or regexp passed to `ExperimentalTransform`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the stream continuing after a detector error; fix the detector so it never returns an empty or non-prefix match.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamObject reports the request body {#streamobject-request-body}

- **What changed:** `StreamObject` results and its `OnStepEnd` / `OnEnd` events had an empty `Request`. v0.6 sets `Request.Body` from the provider stream (`provider.StreamRequestBody`), as TS `streamObject` resolves `request` from `doStream` (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading `GenerateObjectResult.Request.Body`, `ObjectOnStepFinishEvent.Request.Body` or `ObjectOnFinishEvent.Request.Body` after `StreamObject`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on it being nil.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A failing ToModelOutput does not skip the remaining tool calls {#tomodeloutput-error-concurrency}

- **What changed:** With `ToolCallConcurrency` below the number of calls, a `ToModelOutput` error stopped the calls not yet started (GenerateText and the agent loop). v0.6 runs every call, as TS, and then returns the first conversion error (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** Tools with `ToModelOutput` that can fail, combined with `ToolCallConcurrency`.
- **What to do:** No action unless a later tool must not run after a conversion failure.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Middleware streams keep the request body and URL {#middleware-request-metadata}

- **What changed:** StreamText and StreamObject behind `ExtractReasoningMiddleware`, `ExtractJSONMiddleware` or `SimulateStreamingMiddleware` reported no request body (with `Include.RequestBody`): the wrapper streams hid `provider.StreamRequestBody`. v0.6 forwards the body and the new request URL, as TS middlewares return `{...rest, stream}` / `request: result.request` (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** `middleware.ExtractReasoningMiddleware`, `ExtractJSONMiddleware`, `SimulateStreamingMiddleware` with `Request.Body` / `Request.URL` readers.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on an empty request.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Timeout contexts end with the TS TimeoutError cause {#timeout-abort-reason}

- **What changed:** In v0.5 the contexts GenerateText, StreamText, batch calls and the ToolLoopAgent derive for `Timeout.Total`, `Timeout.PerStep`, `Timeout.PerChunk`, `Timeout.FirstChunk` and tool timeouts (`Timeout.ToolMs` / `Timeout.Tools`) ended with cause `context.DeadlineExceeded`, and a StreamText chunk or first-chunk timeout cancelled the model call's context with a plain cancel. In v0.6 `context.Cause(ctx)` is a `*ai.TimeoutError` (`ErrorName()` `"TimeoutError"`): "Step timeout of <ms>ms exceeded", "Chunk timeout of <ms>ms exceeded" and "First chunk timeout of <ms>ms exceeded" for a step, chunk and first chunk, "The operation was aborted due to timeout" for total and tool timeouts. That is the TS abort reason (`setAbortTimeout`, `AbortSignal.timeout`). `ctx.Err()` is still `context.DeadlineExceeded`, and `errors.Is(cause, context.DeadlineExceeded)` still holds. A ToolLoopAgent run whose total timeout fires between steps now fails with `step N failed: The operation was aborted due to timeout` (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** `context.Cause(` in a tool `Execute`, a custom `provider.LanguageModel` or middleware, under `GenerateTextOptions.Timeout`, `StreamTextOptions.Timeout`, `AgentConfig.Timeout`, `PrepareCallConfig.Timeout` or a batch `Timeout`; string matches on `context deadline exceeded` in ToolLoopAgent errors.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare `context.Cause(ctx)` with `context.DeadlineExceeded` using `==`:
  ```go
  cause := context.Cause(ctx)
  if errors.Is(cause, context.DeadlineExceeded) { // was: cause == context.DeadlineExceeded
  	var timeoutErr *ai.TimeoutError
  	_ = errors.As(cause, &timeoutErr) // timeoutErr.Reason is the boundary that fired
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText OutputErr reports a failed run {#stream-output-err-no-output}

- **What changed:** In v0.5 `OutputErr()` stayed nil when the run failed before recording a step (a model call error, an abort) or the model stream closed without a finish chunk. In v0.6 it returns the error TS rejects `result.output` with (stream-text.ts flush): the abort error, the no-finish `*NoOutputGeneratedError`, else a `*NoOutputGeneratedError` "No output generated. Check the stream for errors." WorkflowAgent results are unchanged (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `OutputErr()` (or `Output()` with a nil check) after a failed `StreamText` and treats a nil error as success.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on `OutputErr() == nil` after `Err() != nil`. `errors.As(r.OutputErr(), &noOutput)` finds the new case.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamTextResult.PipeTextStreamToResponse writes the response head {#stream-pipe-text-head}

- **What changed:** In v0.5 the method wrote only the text. In v0.6, when the writer is an `http.ResponseWriter`, it first sets `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8` and writes status 200, like TS `result.pipeTextStreamToResponse` and the package function `ai.PipeTextStreamToResponse` (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** Calls of the method `result.PipeTextStreamToResponse(ctx, w)` followed by `w.WriteHeader` or header changes (now too late).
- **What to do:** Set headers before the call, or use `ai.PipeTextStreamToResponseWithInit` for another status or headers.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Reasoning-file parts marshal in the TS message shape {#reasoning-file-message-json}

- **What changed:** `json.Marshal` of a `types.ReasoningFileContent` (in `Response.Messages`, `ResponseMessages()` and step content) wrote `{"type":"reasoning-file","fileData":{"type":"data","data":...},"mediaType":...,"data":<base64 or null>}`. v0.6 writes the TS ModelMessage reasoning-file part (`to-response-messages.ts`): `{"type":"reasoning-file","data":"<base64>","mediaType":...,"providerOptions":...}`, a URL file as `"data":{"type":"url","url":...}`, and no `fileData`. `UnmarshalJSON` reads both shapes (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** Code that persists or forwards response messages or step content as JSON and reads `fileData` of reasoning-file parts.
- **What to do:** No action unless you parse the JSON yourself; read `data`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### StreamObject stream failure calls only OnError {#streamobject-stream-failure-callbacks}

- **What changed:** When the provider stream itself fails (`Next()` returns an error), v0.5 called `OnStepEnd` / `OnStepFinish` and `OnEnd` / `OnFinishEvent` with the error. v0.6 calls only `OnError`, as TS (its flush handler never runs). The error is still returned, and an error part still ends through `OnStepEnd` / `OnEnd` (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** `StreamObjectOptions.OnEnd`, `OnFinishEvent`, `OnStepEnd`, `OnStepFinish` used to observe stream failures.
- **What to do:** Handle the failure in `OnError` or from the returned error.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Transcribe ignores a generic downloaded content type {#transcribe-audio-media-type}

- **What changed:** A downloaded audio `Content-Type` was used verbatim. v0.6 lower-cases it, drops parameters and keeps only a full `audio/<subtype>`; anything else (`application/octet-stream`, `audio/*`) is detected from the bytes (TS 5d18351e1e, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `Transcribe` with `AudioURL`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Transcribe sends URLs to providers that take them {#transcribe-native-url}

- **What changed:** With a model that implements `provider.TranscriptionURLModel` (Groq) and no `Download` / `DownloadWithMetadata`, an http(s) `AudioURL` is sent to the provider instead of downloaded; if the provider declines, the validated download runs. Telemetry has no audio size or media type then (TS 36802d48af, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `Transcribe` with `AudioURL` and a Groq model.
- **What to do:** Set `Download` to force the local download.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Transcribe reports undecodable base64 audio with the TS text {#transcribe-base64-error-text}

- **What changed:** `Transcribe` with an `AudioBase64` that does not decode returned "Invalid argument for parameter audio: invalid base64 audio data". v0.6 returns the TS InvalidDataContentError text "Invalid data content. Content string is not a base64-encoded media." The error is still a `*providererrors.InvalidArgumentError` with `Field` "audio" (TS data-content.ts; review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches the text of a `Transcribe` error for an invalid `TranscribeOptions.AudioBase64`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match that text. Use `errors.As(err, &invalidArgErr)` with a `*providererrors.InvalidArgumentError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Uploaded videos are counted, not converted {#video-uploaded-outcome}

- **What changed:** An `uploaded` video data part failed as an unknown type. v0.6 counts it in `GenerateVideoResult.UploadedVideos`; an upload-only result succeeds with `Video` nil and `Videos` empty (TS 2e44144812; TS throws from a `video` getter, see the known difference; review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** Code that dereferences `result.Video` after an upload delivery.
- **What to do:** Check `UploadedVideos` before reading `Video`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Video image data strings with an http(s) URL are sent as URLs {#video-datastring-url}

- **What changed:** A `VideoPromptImage.DataString` that starts with `http://` or `https://` was base64-decoded, and the call failed. v0.6 sends it as a URL file, as TS normalizeImageData does (review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** `VideoPromptImage{DataString: "https://..."}` in the `GenerateVideo` / `StartVideo` prompt image, frame images or input references.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GenerateText lists invalid tool call errors before executed results {#generate-text-invalid-tool-errors-first}

- **What changed:** In v0.5, `StepResult.Content` / `GenerateTextResult.Content` listed the `types.ToolErrorContent` of an invalid tool call (unknown tool or input that fails the schema) among the executed tool results, in call order. v0.6 lists invalid-call tool errors first, as TS generateText answers invalid calls before it executes the valid ones (`clientToolOutputs`). The tool message in `ResponseMessages` is unchanged (sorted by call order) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads tool parts by position in `StepResult.Content`, `GenerateTextResult.Content` or `OnStepFinishEvent.Content` after a step with an invalid tool call.
- **What to do:** No action unless you index tool parts by position; match them by `ToolCallID`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamObject publishes a null partial {#stream-object-null-partial}

- **What changed:** A model that streams the JSON text `null` now gets one `OnChunk(nil)` partial, as TS streamObject publishes a null partial object (`parsePartialJson` returns a parsed `null`). v0.5 published none (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `StreamObjectOptions.OnChunk` handlers that assume a non-nil partial.
- **What to do:** Handle a nil partial.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tool choice is checked only when the model call finishes {#stream-tool-choice-no-finish}

- **What changed:** v0.5: when a model stream ended without a finish part after some output, StreamText still enforced `ToolChoice` (required, a named tool, or none), failed with `ToolChoiceViolationError` and finish reason `error`. v0.6: as in TS (stream-language-model-call.ts checks the tool choice in its finish handler), such a step ends with finish reason `other` and no error (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `ToolChoice` set to `required`, a tool, or `none` on `StreamText` (or agents and workflows that stream) with a provider stream that ends without a finish chunk; code that expects `IsToolChoiceViolationError(err)` then.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on that error for incomplete streams; check `FinishReason() == types.FinishReasonOther` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Empty text block IDs are unique across steps {#stream-empty-part-id}

- **What changed:** v0.5 left a text or reasoning block whose provider ID is `""` as `""` in every step. v0.6 reserves `""` like any other ID (TS createPartIdReserver), so a later step's `""` block gets a generated ID (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** A provider (or custom model) that streams `ChunkTypeTextStart` / `ChunkTypeReasoningStart` with an empty `ID` in more than one step, and code that matches chunk IDs.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match block IDs against `""`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### CosineSimilarity length error text {#cosine-similarity-error-text}

- **What changed:** The length-mismatch error of `ai.CosineSimilarity` reads `Invalid argument for parameter vector1,vector2: Vectors must have the same length`; v0.5 added `(vector1Length=…, vector2Length=…)` to the message. As TS, the lengths are the error's value: `InvalidArgumentError.Value` holds `{vector1Length, vector2Length}` (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching that text.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the old text; read `Value` for the lengths.
- **Kind:** error text only

### GenerateVideo drives start/status-only video models itself {#video-start-status-only-models}

- **What changed:** `ai.GenerateVideo` without `Poll` / `Webhook` used to call `DoGenerate` on video models whose TS twin has no `doGenerate`. v0.6 drives `DoStart` / `DoStatus` itself for them, as TS `useStartStatus` does: KlingAI, fal, Replicate, ByteDance, xAI, Alibaba, Google, Google Vertex, HeyGen and Topaz. The start request carries `idempotency-key: aisdk_vid_<random>` (a caller-supplied `Idempotency-Key` wins); a failing status poll is retried on its own instead of restarting (and re-billing) the job; a failed job returns the provider's status text as a plain error (before: `*klingai.Error`, `*providererrors.VideoGenerationError`, `*bytedance.Error`); a timeout returns `Video generation timed out after <ms>ms.`. The v0.5.2 `pollIntervalMs` / `pollTimeoutMs` provider options (and HeyGen / Topaz `Config` poll values) are still honored; `GenerateVideoOptions.Poll` overrides them; Topaz polls every 5000 ms by default (its shim polled every 2000 ms) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `ai.GenerateVideo` with those providers' video models, and code that matches the failure or timeout error with `errors.As` on `*klingai.Error` / `*bytedance.Error` / `*providererrors.VideoGenerationError` or the old timeout texts.
- **What to do:** Match on the error text, or call the model's `DoGenerate` directly to keep the provider error types. No action for the header.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Downloads have no client timeout; set a context deadline {#download-no-timeout}

- **What changed:** Generated-video downloads in `ai.GenerateVideo`, `ai.Transcribe` audio URLs and prompt file URLs (`ai.CreateURLDownload` / `CreateURLDownloadWithMetadata`), provider status polls and result downloads (`TrustedOriginDownloadOptions` users: BFL, Replicate, fal, Topaz, Fireworks, MiniMax, xAI, Rev.ai, OpenAI, Anthropic batch results), and the OpenAI / Azure MAI / DeepInfra / Prodia image and Gemini tool-result downloads no longer stop after 60 s (30 s for some image downloads and polls). Only the context ends them, as TS download / getFromApi set no timeout; large videos on slow links were failing. Redirect, size and SSRF limits are unchanged (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Calls without a context deadline that relied on the internal timeout to stop a stalled download.
- **What to do:** Pass a context with a deadline (`context.WithTimeout`) to calls that download large files.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OutputErr reports a stream failure or abort {#stream-output-err-failure}

- **What changed:** When a `StreamText` run failed with a thrown error (a model stream read error, a failed file download, a failing tool input or approval callback, an output parse error) or was aborted after at least one step, v0.5 left `OutputErr()` nil, and a thrown failure in the first step gave `NoOutputGeneratedError`. v0.6 sets `OutputErr()` to that error or the abort reason, recorded steps or not, as TS rejects `result.output` with the stream's error or the abort reason. `NewStreamTextResultFromParts` results do the same for an error or abort part (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `OutputErr()` after `Err()` is non-nil, or checks `IsNoOutputGeneratedError(r.OutputErr())` for a stream that failed mid-step.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on `OutputErr()` being nil after a failed or aborted run. Check `Err()` first.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Cancelling during a step's end callbacks aborts the run {#stream-abort-at-step-end}

- **What changed:** A ctx cancelled while `OnStepEnd` / `OnStepEndEvent` (or `OnChunk` for the step's `finish-step`) ran was not noticed: the run wrote `finish`, called `OnEnd` and `Err()` was nil, or it started the next step. v0.6 ends the run with an `abort` part, runs `OnAbortEvent` / `OnAbort` but not `OnEnd`, and `Err()` is the cancellation, as the TS outer stream checks the abort signal before every part (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that cancels the `StreamText` ctx from a step callback, or a cancellation that lands while those callbacks run.
- **What to do:** No action unless you cancel from a step callback and expect `OnEnd`. To stop after a step, use `StopWhen`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider-executed MCP calls no longer get a client tool-not-found result {#provider-executed-mcp-no-client-error}

- **What changed:** A provider-executed call of a tool that is not in `Tools` (OpenAI `mcp.*` calls, the Anthropic MCP connector) produced a `tool not found` tool result and a role:tool response message, which the next OpenAI turn rejected. v0.6 produces neither: the call is the provider's, as TS generate-text.ts (clientToolCalls) and execute-tool-call.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `tool not found` results, `ResponseMessages` with `RoleTool` after OpenAI MCP or Anthropic MCP connector calls.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on that error result.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GenerateText has no 1000-step ceiling {#generate-text-no-step-ceiling}

- **What changed:** `GenerateText` stopped a tool loop after 1000 steps even when `StopWhen` allowed more. v0.6 ends the loop only through `StopWhen` (or a step without client tool calls), as TS generate-text.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `StopWhen` conditions that allow more than 1000 steps.
- **What to do:** Add `ai.StepCountIs(n)` to `StopWhen` if you relied on the ceiling.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GenerateObject and StreamObject call IDs start with aiobj- {#object-call-id-aiobj}

- **What changed:** `GenerateObject` / `StreamObject` call IDs in callbacks and telemetry started with `call-`. v0.6 uses `aiobj-` and 24 characters, as TS generate-object.ts / stream-object.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `CallID` checks for a `call-` prefix in object callbacks or telemetry.
- **What to do:** No action unless you parse the call ID.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamObject calls OnError for preparation failures {#stream-object-onerror-prep}

- **What changed:** A `StreamObject` prompt, download or response-format failure returned the error without calling `OnError`. v0.6 calls `OnError` with the (gateway-wrapped) error after the telemetry error event, as TS stream-object.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `StreamObjectOptions.OnError` with invalid prompts or failing downloads.
- **What to do:** No action unless `OnError` must not see these errors.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Generated file URLs use ExperimentalDownload {#generated-file-experimental-download}

- **What changed:** URL-backed generated and reasoning files a model returns were always downloaded with the default downloader. v0.6 uses `ExperimentalDownload` when it is set, in `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and the agent loop, called with `IsURLSupportedByModel: false`; a nil result is a `*DownloadError` `Generated file URL <href> must be downloaded, but the download function returned null`, and the function's own error is returned as is (TS 2ebc78b654). Without it, the validated, DNS-pinned default downloader stays. The download function gets the URL serialized (`https://x/a%20b.png`), as TS `url.toString()` (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** `ExperimentalDownload` functions that return nil when `IsURLSupportedByModel` is false, or that assume only prompt URLs.
- **What to do:** Download (or reject) generated file URLs in your function too.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Sandbox description no longer added to the system prompt {#sandbox-description-not-in-prompt}

- **What changed:** `GenerateText` and `StreamText` appended `ExperimentalSandbox`'s description to the system prompt of every step. TS never does this (the description "is not added to the model prompt automatically"); v0.6 leaves the prompt alone. Tool description functions and `PrepareStep` still read it (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `ExperimentalSandbox` sessions built with `WithShellSandboxDescription` (or any sandbox description) that the model was meant to see.
- **What to do:** Put the description in the prompt yourself:

```go
res, err := ai.StreamText(ctx, ai.StreamTextOptions{
	Model:               model,
	System:              "You are helpful.\n\nSandbox:\n" + sandboxDescription,
	Prompt:              "Run the tests.",
	ExperimentalSandbox: sandbox,
})
```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### DefaultSettingsMiddleware and PrepareStep merge typed provider options {#default-settings-typed-options-merge}

- **What changed:** A Go-typed provider-options value (typed option struct, `map[string]string`, pointer) in a `DefaultSettingsMiddleware` default, or in options merged for `PrepareStep` and approval resumes, replaced the other side instead of merging with it. v0.6 merges every object through its JSON form, as TS mergeObjects, and skips `__proto__` / `constructor` / `prototype` keys (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `DefaultSettingsMiddleware` or `PrepareStep` provider options given as typed values.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the call's value replacing the default.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### tool-input-start carries the tool title and metadata {#tool-input-start-title}

- **What changed:** A forwarded `tool-input-start` chunk had no tool title or metadata. v0.6 sets `Title` and `ToolMetadata` from the tool (UI chunk `title` / `toolMetadata`), as TS stream-language-model-call.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Consumers of `ChunkTypeToolInputStart` or UI `tool-input-start` chunks that compare them exactly.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Deltas after a block ends are flagged, not recorded {#orphan-block-delta-error}

- **What changed:** A text or reasoning delta (or end) for a block that had already ended was recorded. v0.6 forwards it followed by an error part `text part <id> not found` (full stream only) and leaves it out of the text, as TS stream-text.ts; deltas of blocks that never started are still recorded (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Providers or transforms that reuse a block ID after its end.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### StreamText output errors carry the final step's usage and response {#stream-output-final-step-context}

- **What changed:** A `NoObjectGeneratedError` from `StreamText` output carried the total usage and no response. v0.6 carries the final step's usage, response and finish reason, as TS stream-text.ts parseCompleteOutput (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Reading `Usage` / `Response` of that error.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Zero timeouts use the TS error text {#zero-timeout-text}

- **What changed:** A configured zero or negative timeout produced the Go fallback text. v0.6 reads `Step timeout of 0ms exceeded`, and for a tool `The operation was aborted due to timeout` (TS set-abort-timeout.ts, merge-abort-signals.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching timeout error texts with zero or negative durations.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Decision question and option keys follow JS key order {#decision-js-key-order}

- **What changed:** Decision question IDs and choice options were ordered as sorted strings (`1, 10, 2`). v0.6 puts array-index keys first in numeric order (`1, 2, 10`), as JS objects enumerate them; the telemetry file state part writes type, mediaType, filename, data; and unpadded or base64url file data strings decode (TS convertBase64ToUint8Array) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Decision prompts or telemetry with numeric question or option IDs.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### testutil.MockTextStream.Read follows io.Reader {#mock-text-stream-read}

- **What changed:** `testutil.MockTextStream.Read` reported the whole chunk length even when the buffer was shorter, so `io.ReadAll` panicked, and returned `0, nil` for non-text chunks. v0.6 copies at most `len(p)` bytes, keeps the rest for the next call and skips non-text chunks (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Tests that read a `MockTextStream` with `Read`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Element streams follow the TS output stage {#element-stream-first-block}

- **What changed:** `ElementStream` and `ElementStreamWithOutput` reparsed the whole step text on every chunk, across every text block of the step, and kept a failed attempt's text after a stream retry. v0.6 emits the elements of the step's first text block only; for the call's own `ArrayOutput` they read the partial outputs `StreamText` publishes, so each element is validated once and a `StreamRetries` retry restarts them, as TS createElementStreamTransform (TS 91a67c2f56, review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** `ElementStream` / `ElementStreamWithOutput` on steps that stream several text blocks, or with `StreamRetries`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on elements parsed from a later text block of the same step.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Element streams read their own stream cursor {#element-stream-own-cursor}

- **What changed:** In v0.5, `ElementStream` and `ElementStreamWithOutput` read the result's shared `Stream()` cursor. In v0.6 each element stream reads its own cursor, which replays the stream from the start, as each TS `elementStream` access is its own tee of the stream. Element streams over a harness result now follow the turn's first text block, as TS `HarnessStreamTextResult.elementStream` does, and are not reset at step boundaries (review round 93).
- **Am I affected?** You are affected if you call `ElementStream` or `ElementStreamWithOutput` and also read `result.Stream()`, `FullStream()` or `Chunks()` on the same result, or if you build element streams from `harness.Agent` stream results.
- **What to do:** No action is needed unless you relied on the element stream consuming chunks from the shared `Stream()` cursor. Each reader now sees every chunk.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Prompts and validation {#prompts-and-validation}

### Prompt validation matches TS {#prompt-validation}

- **What changed:** `GenerateText`, `StreamText`, `GenerateObject`,
  `StreamObject`, batch text requests and the agent calls return
  `*errors.InvalidPromptError` when neither or both of `Prompt` and
  `Messages` are set, or when `Messages` is an empty non-nil slice, as TS
  `standardizePrompt`. v0.5 sent an empty prompt, or ignored `Prompt` when
  `Messages` was set. `WorkflowAgent` `Generate` / `Stream` (and the
  `WithOptions` forms) return the same `*InvalidPromptError`; v0.5 returned
  plain errors ("workflow: either prompt or messages is required",
  "workflow: use either prompt or messages, not both"). As TS, a
  `WorkflowAgent` call without a prompt or messages is checked after
  `PrepareCall`, so a `PrepareCall` hook may supply the messages.
  System-role messages in `Messages` without `AllowSystemInMessages` (agent
  or call) are rejected there too, with `*InvalidPromptError` ("System
  messages are not allowed in the prompt or messages fields. Use the
  instructions option instead."). `StreamWithOptions` / `Stream` return the
  error directly, before any callback or telemetry. In v0.5, `Generate`
  returned it as `step 1 failed: ...` after `OnStart`, and `Stream` returned
  it from `ReadAll`/`Err`. The checks run in TS order: missing prompt,
  invalid instructions, empty messages, system messages (review round 70).
- **Am I affected?** Calls that set both `Prompt:` and `Messages:`, neither,
  or `Messages: []types.Message{}`; matches on the `workflow: either prompt or
  messages` error text; `types.RoleSystem` messages in
  `WorkflowGenerateOptions.Messages` / `WorkflowStreamOptions.Messages`.
- **What to do:** pass exactly one of them.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GenerateObject and StreamObject reject system messages in Messages {#object-system-messages}

- **What changed:** as TS `allowSystemInMessages` (default false),
  `GenerateObject` and `StreamObject` reject system-role messages in
  `Messages` unless `AllowSystemMessages` (alias `AllowSystemInMessages`) is
  set. `GenerateText` already did. The error is
  `*prompt.UnsupportedSystemMessageError` with the TS text "System messages
  are not allowed in the prompt or messages fields. Use the instructions
  option instead."
- **Am I affected?** `types.RoleSystem` messages in
  `GenerateObjectOptions.Messages` or `StreamObjectOptions.Messages`.
- **What to do:** put the system prompt in `Instructions` (or `System`), or
  set `AllowSystemMessages: true` for trusted messages.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Invalid instructions are an InvalidPromptError {#invalid-instructions}

- **What changed:** as in TS, `InstructionMessages` that are not all system
  messages fail `GenerateText`, `StreamText`, the legacy `ToolLoopAgent` loop
  and batch text requests with `*providererrors.InvalidPromptError`
  ("Invalid prompt: instructions must be a string, SystemModelMessage, or
  array of SystemModelMessage"). In v0.5 the error was an
  `*InvalidArgumentError` ("Invalid argument for parameter instructions:
  ..."). It is now the `Cause`, so `errors.As` on either type matches. The
  checks run in TS order: a missing prompt first, then invalid instructions,
  then empty messages. A `WorkflowAgent` `Instructions` value of an
  unsupported type (not a string, `types.Message`, `[]types.Message` or
  `[]string`) is the same `*InvalidPromptError`, from `NewWorkflowAgent` and
  from the calls; only the effective value is checked (the call's
  `Instructions`, else its `System`, else the agent's), and not one that a
  `PrepareCall` hook replaces, as TS validates `options.instructions ??
  options.system ?? this.instructions` after prepareCall (review round 70);
  v0.5 returned "workflow: unsupported instructions type T".
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares this error's text, including
  `workflow: unsupported instructions type`.
- **What to do:** compare error types, not text:

  ```go
  var promptErr *providererrors.InvalidPromptError
  if errors.As(err, &promptErr) {
  	// invalid prompt or instructions
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tool calls without results fail the prompt {#missing-tool-results}

- **What changed:** a prompt with assistant tool calls that have no tool
  result fails with `*types.MissingToolResultsError`, as TS
  (provider-executed calls are exempt). A tool result that answers no tool
  call fails with "Tool call `<id>` not found."
- **Am I affected?** Conversation histories you trim or rebuild that can drop
  tool results.
- **What to do:** keep every tool call's result in `Messages`, or remove the
  call too.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The JSON Schema validator enforces more keywords {#json-schema-validator}

- **What changed:** the JSON Schema validator enforces `if` / `then` /
  `else`, `contains`, `dependencies` and `dependentSchemas`. Input that
  passed because they were ignored now fails.
- **Am I affected?** Tool or output schemas that use these keywords.
- **What to do:** fix the schema or the producer of the input.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Struct-tag schemas validate the decoded value {#struct-schema-validates-decoded-value}

- **What changed:** in v0.5 (v0.5.2 included) a struct-tag schema
  (`schema.NewSimpleStructSchema`, `schema.NewStructSchema`) ran its
  validation on the decoded JSON map, which always failed with
  `validator: (nil map[string]interface {})`. So `ObjectOutput`,
  `ElementOutput` and `ArrayOutput` with a struct schema (including the
  getting-started `EmailSummary` example) and `GenerateObject` with one
  always returned `NoObjectGeneratedError`, and a code-mode tool with a
  struct `Parameters` always failed input validation. v0.6 decodes the value
  into the struct type and validates that, as TS validates the parsed value
  with the schema: valid output parses, and the struct's `validate:` tags
  are enforced (a value that does not decode into the type fails) (review
  round 80).
- **Am I affected?** Code that uses a struct-tag schema with those helpers
  and worked around the failure.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the failure; check that
  the struct's `validate:` tags accept the output you expect.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Struct-tag schemas send their fields to the model {#struct-schema-json-schema}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `StructValidator.JSONSchema()` (behind
  `schema.NewStructSchema` and `schema.NewSimpleStructSchema`) returned
  `{"type":"object"}`, so a struct-tag tool or object output told the model
  nothing about its fields. v0.6 generates the struct's JSON Schema by
  reflection, in the shape TS sends for the matching zod object
  (`zodSchema(z.object(...))`, `asSchema(...).jsonSchema`): `$schema`
  draft-07, `properties` in field order under their `json` names, `required`
  for the fields with `validate:"required"` (or `jsonschema:"required"`),
  `additionalProperties: false`, pointers as `anyOf` with `{"type":"null"}`,
  the `validate` `min`/`max`/`len`/`gt`/`gte`/`lt`/`lte`/`oneof` rules as
  keywords and `jsonschema:"description=..."` as descriptions (see
  [StructValidator](../07-reference/schema/json-schema.mdx#structvalidator)).
  Integers carry the range zod `.int()` gives them (-9007199254740991 to
  9007199254740991 for `int`, `int64`, `uint`, `uint64` and `uintptr`, from 0
  when unsigned; the size range for 8 to 32 bit kinds); of `gte` and `gt`
  (and of `lte` and `lt`) only the tighter bound is written, as zod does; a
  description is written where zod `.describe()` on the whole field puts it
  (first on an optional field) (review round 83). **`Validate` rejects an
  integer outside that range:** a struct-schema tool input, object output or
  `Validate` call with, for example, `{"id": 9007199254740993}` for an
  `int64` field was accepted in v0.5.2 and now fails validation (zod
  `.int()` rejects it; JSON numbers past 2^53 are not exact in TS).
  Struct schemas reach tool `Parameters` and `ResponseFormat.Schema` (object
  output, and the array wrapper of `ArrayOutput` and `GenerateObject` /
  `StreamObject` array mode) as an `*schema.OrderedSchema`, so the
  properties keep the field order on the wire. Workflow `SerializeToolSet`
  keeps a struct schema's field order in the serialized `inputSchema` and in
  the tools `ResolveSerializableTools` rebuilds, and a bare `schema.Validator`
  as `Parameters` is serialized with `hasOwnValidator: true` (unless it is a
  `*schema.JSONSchemaValidator`) (review round 83). `Validate` also checks a decoded value against the schema, so the
  two agree: `null` for a non-pointer field and a missing
  `jsonschema:"required"` field fail; unknown keys are still ignored, as
  zod strips them (coordinator ruling, review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** `schema.NewSimpleStructSchema` / `schema.NewStructSchema`
  as a tool `Parameters`, an `ObjectOutput` or a `GenerateObject` schema;
  code that reads `StructValidator.JSONSchema()`; custom providers or
  middleware that type-assert `ResponseFormat.Schema` or resolved tool
  `Parameters` to `map[string]interface{}`; OpenAI `strict` structured
  outputs, which need every property required; struct fields of type `int`,
  `int64`, `uint`, `uint64` or `uintptr` that receive values beyond
  ±9007199254740991.
- **What to do:** No action unless you read the schema as a map or use strict
  outputs: read a schema value with `schema.ParametersMap(v)` instead of a
  type assertion, and mark each field the model must send
  `validate:"required"` (`jsonschema:"required"` on a pointer that may be
  `null`). For integer values past 2^53, use a `string` field (or
  `json.Number` with `,string`) instead of an integer kind.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### URL file parts are WHATWG-normalized {#url-file-part-normalized}

- **What changed:** v0.5 sent a file part's URL string to the provider as given and
  rejected `file:`, `javascript:`, `vbscript:` and hostless URLs with Go-only
  errors. v0.6 does what TS does: core prompt conversion parses every URL file
  part as `new URL(url)` does and sends its serialization (`url.toString()`).
  The scheme and host are lowercased, a default port is dropped, dot segments
  are resolved, spaces and non-ASCII characters in the path, query and
  fragment are percent-encoded, IDNA hosts become punycode, and `\` becomes
  `/` for http(s)/ws(s)/ftp/file. Download planning, `SupportedURLs` matching
  and `DownloadRequest.URL` use that form. A URL that does not parse fails
  with `*prompt.InvalidURLError` (`Invalid URL`, the TS `TypeError` text); UI
  file and reasoning-file parts are checked with the same parser in
  `ConvertToModelMessages` (`Invalid URL: <url>` becomes `Invalid URL`, and
  net/url-only acceptances such as `http://x:65536/` now fail). Any other
  scheme is accepted, and is forwarded only when the model's `SupportedURLs`
  match it: the default downloader still fetches only http, https and data
  URLs and still refuses private, loopback and link-local hosts, so a
  `file:///etc/passwd` part is never read or fetched. New, additive:
  `types.FileData.OriginalURL` (JSON `originalUrl`) holds your string when
  serialization changed it, for `FileContent.URL`, `ImageContent.URL`,
  `FileContentBlock.URL` and legacy `file-url`/`image-url` tool-result blocks,
  as TS keeps `originalUrl` for a string URL; Google and Vertex send it for
  `gs:` URLs (TS `originalUrl ?? url.toString()`). A legacy `file-url` block
  without a media type infers it from the URL path, not the host (review
  round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `FileContent{URL:`, `ImageContent{URL:`, `FileContentBlock{URL:`,
  `FileData{Type: types.FileDataTypeURL`; middleware, mocks or
  `ExperimentalDownload` functions that compare `FileData.URL` /
  `DownloadRequest.URL` with the string you passed; code matching
  `remote file URL must` or `Invalid URL: `; prompts relying on `file:` /
  `javascript:` URLs being rejected before the model call.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare the URL with your input. To read your
  original string:
  ```go
  url := part.FileData.URL
  if part.FileData.OriginalURL != "" {
  	url = part.FileData.OriginalURL
  }
  ```
  To match a bad URL, use `errors.As` with `*prompt.InvalidURLError`
  (`pkg/providerutils/prompt`).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Reasoning-file data URLs take their media type {#reasoning-file-data-url-media-type}

- **What changed:** v0.5 kept a `ReasoningFileContent`'s declared `MediaType` when its
  `FileData` was a `data:` URL. v0.6 uses the data URL's media type, as TS
  `mediaType ?? part.mediaType` does (file parts already did). An unsupported
  reasoning-file variant now fails with the TS text `Unsupported
  reasoning-file data type: <type>` (was `reasoning-file data type "<type>" is
  not supported; ...`); the error type `*prompt.ReasoningFileConstraintError`
  is unchanged (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `types.ReasoningFileContent{FileData: types.FileData{Type:
  types.FileDataTypeURL, URL: "data:...`; code matching `allowed types are`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the old text; use `errors.As` with
  `*prompt.ReasoningFileConstraintError`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Image parts log the TS deprecation warning {#image-part-deprecation-warning}

- **What changed:** v0.6 logs TS's `deprecated` warning for every `types.ImageContent`
  part of a user message (default logger line: `[AISDK_DEP_IMAGE_CONTENT_PART]
  AI SDK Warning: Deprecated: ""image" content part". The "image" content part
  type is deprecated. Use a "file" part with mediaType: 'image' (or a more
  specific image/* subtype) instead.`), through the warning logger
  (`SetLogWarnings`, `DisableLogWarnings`, `AI_SDK_LOG_WARNINGS=false`), once
  per user message on every step's prompt conversion, without provider or
  model, as TS `convertToLanguageModelPrompt` does: GenerateText and
  StreamText (every step), GenerateObject and StreamObject, the batch API and
  the agent legacy loop. The default logger writes each code once per process
  to stderr (review round 84). Since round 86 GenerateText and StreamText log
  the warnings of each step's own messages: an `ImageContent` part in a
  message added by `PrepareStep` or a steering message is logged on the steps
  that send it, and a prompt message `PrepareStep` removes is not.
- **Am I affected?** `types.ImageContent{` in prompts; custom `SetLogWarnings`
  functions; tests that capture stderr.
- **What to do:** No action, or switch to `types.FileContent{MediaType: "image/png",
  Data: ...}`; `ai.DisableLogWarnings()` silences it.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Decide rejects non-finite numbers and cycles first {#decide-serializable-json}

- **What changed:** `ExperimentalDecide` reports a NaN/±Inf or a cyclic map/slice in the state's JSON content as `InvalidArgumentError` `state`: "JSON content must not contain non-finite numbers, cycles, or array holes", and in a question's instructions or criteria as `questions.<id>`: "instructions and criteria must not contain non-finite numbers, cycles, or array holes" (TS `b6b9dcaec7`). v0.5 used the older texts and crashed (stack overflow) on a cyclic value. Other input checks keep their v0.5 texts (see the known difference "Input checks TS removed in ai@7.0.155").
- **Am I affected?** Code matching the strings "must be a string, JSON object, or array of text, file, or json parts", "instructions must be a JSON-compatible string, object, or array" or "criteria descriptions must be JSON-compatible" for NaN/Inf input.
- **What to do:** Match `*providererrors.InvalidArgumentError` with `errors.As` instead of the message.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Base64 string file data gets its detected image media type {#file-data-string-media-type}

- **What changed:** v0.5 detected the image media type of inline file data only from bytes (`FileData.Data`). v0.6 also sniffs a base64 string (`FileData{Type: FileDataTypeData, DataString: ...}`) in user file parts and tool-result file content, as TS `convertPartToLanguageModelPart` does with `detectMediaType`: a PNG/JPEG/... payload declared as `"image"` (or with another subtype) is sent with its detected type, and a `DataString` whose prefix is not base64 fails the call (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** `types.FileData{` with `DataString:` in `types.FileContent` or `types.FileContentBlock` parts, especially with `MediaType: "image"`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the declared media type being sent unchanged or pass a `DataString` that is not base64 (decode or fix it first).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Legacy tool-result content types are logged and shaped like TS {#legacy-tool-result-content-deprecation}

- **What changed:** v0.5 decoded the legacy TS tool-result content types (`file-data`, `file-url`, `file-id`, `file-reference`, `image-data`, `image-url`, `image-file-id`, `image-file-reference`) into `types.FileContentBlock` silently, keeping every `filename` and item `mediaType`. v0.6 logs the TS `deprecated` warning for each one on every prompt conversion that sends it (codes `AISDK_DEP_TOOL_RESULT_*`, once per code with the default logger) and shapes them as TS `mapToolResultOutput`: only `file-data` keeps `filename`; `file-id` / `file-reference` are `application` and `image-url` / `image-file-id` / `image-file-reference` are `image` whatever `mediaType` the item has; a `file-url` with `"mediaType": ""` keeps `""` (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** JSON tool-result outputs with `"type": "file-data"`, `"file-url"`, `"file-id"`, `"file-reference"`, `"image-data"`, `"image-url"`, `"image-file-id"` or `"image-file-reference"` decoded into `types.ToolResultOutput`; `SetLogWarnings` consumers.
- **What to do:** Use `"type": "file"` content with `data: {type: "data" | "url" | "reference", ...}` and a `mediaType`, or no action if the log line is acceptable.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### GetTextFromDataURL follows TS getTextFromDataUrl {#data-url-text-ts}

- **What changed:** `ai.GetTextFromDataURL` returned the decoded bytes as a Go string when the data URL declares no charset; TS returns `atob`'s binary string, so each byte is one character (UTF-8 text reads as Latin-1, `w6k=` gives `"Ã©"`). A declared charset is decoded with TS `new TextDecoder(charset)` semantics (WHATWG labels; an unknown or replacement label is `Error decoding data URL`), and the charset is matched with JS `\s` and a JS `/i` that does not fold `s` to U+017F (review round 86b).
