- **Am I affected?** Callers of `GetTextFromDataURL` with non-ASCII text in a data URL without `charset=`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on UTF-8 decoding: add `;charset=utf-8` to the data URL.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Empty text parts with metadata stay in response messages {#empty-text-provider-metadata}

- **What changed:** `Response.Messages` / `ResponseMessages` dropped every empty text part. v0.6 keeps an empty text part whose `ProviderMetadata` is set (anything but absent or JSON `null`), with the metadata as `ProviderOptions`, as TS `toResponseMessages` (fd7e8a75d7), so a Google `thoughtSignature` on an empty text part is sent back on the next turn. Anthropic and Bedrock drop such foreign empty text parts again, Bedrock only when their provider options carry no `amazonBedrock` / `bedrock` entry.
- **Am I affected?** Code that inspects or persists response messages and assumes no `types.TextContent{Text: ""}`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you filter text parts by content.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Empty text parts are removed from the prompt {#prompt-empty-text-parts}

- **What changed:** v0.5 sent empty text parts to the provider. v0.6 removes every `types.TextContent` with `Text == ""` from user messages, and those without `ProviderOptions` from assistant messages, before each model call, as TS `convertToLanguageModelPrompt` (review round 87). A message can end up with no parts, as in TS.
- **Am I affected?** Prompts with `TextContent{Text: ""}` parts.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Validated downloads reject HTTPS to HTTP redirects {#download-https-downgrade}

- **What changed:** Validated downloads (prompt files, image / video results, `PollJSON`) followed a redirect from https to http. v0.6 fails with `DownloadError` "Redirect from HTTPS to HTTP was blocked" before the http target is requested, trusted-origin hops included (TS f608c056a2, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** Download URLs whose redirect chain goes from https to http.
- **What to do:** Keep the chain on HTTPS, or start on HTTP.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Data URLs without a ;base64 marker are accepted {#data-url-without-base64-marker}

- **What changed:** v0.5 rejected a file or image data URL without `;base64` (`data:text/plain,aGk=`), and one whose media type `mime.ParseMediaType` refused, with `InvalidInlineDataURLError`. v0.6 parses it like TS `splitDataUrl`: the text after the first comma is base64 data, and the media type is the header segment as given (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `FileContent.URL` / `ImageContent.URL` / `FileData{Type: "url"}` / `promptutils.ParseDataURL` values with a data URL lacking `;base64`, or code that expects `InvalidInlineDataURLError` for them.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on that error.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ExperimentalDownload is called on every prompt conversion {#download-called-with-empty-plan}

- **What changed:** v0.5 called a custom `ExperimentalDownload` only when the prompt had URLs to plan. v0.6 calls it on every prompt conversion (each step, GenerateObject / StreamObject, batch requests), with an empty `requests` slice when there is nothing to download, as TS `downloadAssets` does (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Custom `ExperimentalDownload` functions that index `requests[0]` or count their calls.
- **What to do:** Handle an empty slice by returning an empty (or nil) result.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### File media types are passed on as given {#file-media-type-unvalidated}

- **What changed:** v0.5 rejected a file part whose media type `mime.ParseMediaType` refused (for example `text/` or `a/b;;c=d`). v0.6 passes the media type to the provider as given, as TS convertToLanguageModelPrompt does (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that relies on an error for a malformed `FileContent.MediaType` / `FileData.MediaType`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on that error.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A data URL in the data file variant reports the TS text {#data-variant-data-url-text}

- **What changed:** `FileData{Type: FileDataTypeData, DataString: "data:..."}` failed with a base64 decode error. It now fails with `*promptutils.InvalidInlineDataURLError` whose cause is the TS text "Data URLs are not valid inline data. Pass them as { type: "url", url } instead." (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches that error's text.
- **What to do:** Use `FileDataTypeURL` for data URLs.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Legacy image-data and file-data tool results keep their media type {#legacy-tool-result-media-type}

- **What changed:** A legacy `image-data` / `file-data` tool-result block had its media type sniffed from the bytes. v0.6 keeps the declared type (new `FileContentBlock.LegacyContentType()`), and a URL media type uses the whole pathname when it has no dot, as TS convert-to-language-model-prompt.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Tool results decoded from `image-data` / `file-data` JSON with a wrong `mediaType`.
- **What to do:** Send the correct `mediaType`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### A nil provider option override replaces the base value {#provider-options-null-override}

- **What changed:** Merging `PrepareStep` provider options onto the call's options, and a tool message's options onto its last part, skipped a nil (JSON null) override and kept the base value. v0.6 sets the key to nil, as TS merge-objects.ts skips only undefined (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** `PrepareStep` results or tool messages whose `ProviderOptions` maps hold nil values.
- **What to do:** Leave the key out to keep the base value.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Tools {#tools}

### Tools in a step run concurrently {#tools-concurrent}

- **What changed:** the tool calls of one step run concurrently by default,
  as TS `Promise.all`, in `GenerateText`, `StreamText`, `ToolLoopAgent`
  (including the legacy `Execute` / `GenerateAgent` loop), approval resumes
  and `WorkflowAgent`. v0.5 ran them one after another. Results stay in call
  order.
- **Am I affected?** Tool `Execute` functions or tool callbacks that share
  state without locking, or that depend on running in order.
- **What to do:** guard shared state with a mutex, or run tools one at a
  time:

  ```go
  one := 1
  opts := ai.GenerateTextOptions{ToolCallConcurrency: &one} // v0.5 behavior
  _ = opts
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An unset ToolChoice reaches the model as auto {#tool-choice-auto}

- **What changed:** as TS `prepareToolChoice`, `GenerateText`, `StreamText`
  and `ExperimentalStartBatch` pass the model `ToolChoice{Type: "auto"}` when
  neither the call nor `PrepareStep` sets one. Requests with tools now carry
  the provider's explicit auto choice (`"tool_choice": "auto"` for OpenAI and
  OpenAI-compatible servers, `{"type": "auto"}` for Anthropic,
  `toolChoice.auto` for Bedrock, mode `AUTO` for Google). The Gateway always
  sends `toolChoice`, and the OpenAI completion and Prodia models warn that
  `toolChoice` is unsupported. Events still report the choice as given. Z.AI
  no longer sends `tool_choice` without tools.
- **Am I affected?** An OpenAI-compatible server that rejects an explicit
  auto choice (for example vLLM started without
  `--enable-auto-tool-choice`), or request matchers that expect no
  `tool_choice`.
- **What to do:** enable auto tool choice on the server, or wrap the model
  with a middleware that restores the v0.5 request:

  ```go
  clearToolChoice := &middleware.LanguageModelMiddleware{
  	TransformParams: func(_ context.Context, _ string, params *provider.GenerateOptions, _ provider.LanguageModel) (*provider.GenerateOptions, error) {
  		p := *params
  		p.ToolChoice = types.ToolChoice{} // send no tool_choice, as v0.5
  		return &p, nil
  	},
  }
  model = middleware.WrapLanguageModel(model, []*middleware.LanguageModelMiddleware{clearToolChoice}, nil, nil)
  ```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ToolChoice none is enforced {#tool-choice-none}

- **What changed:** a tool call under tool choice `none` now fails
  `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and the agents with
  `*ai.ToolChoiceViolationError` ("Model response contained a tool call even
  though tool choice was none."), as TS. v0.5 ran the tool. Any tool-choice
  violation (none, required or a specific tool) ends the step with finish
  reason `error`.
- **Am I affected?** `ToolChoice: types.ToolChoice{Type: "none"}` with models
  that call tools anyway.
- **What to do:** handle the error:

  ```go
  if ai.IsToolChoiceViolationError(err) {
  	// the model called a tool under tool choice none
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A StreamText tool choice violation ends with finish {#tool-choice-violation-finish}

- **What changed:** the `ToolChoiceViolationError` is an `error` chunk after
  the model output (`OnError` once, as before). The run then ends with a
  `finish` chunk (finish reason `error`), `OnFinish` and `OnEnd`, as TS.
  `Err()` still returns the violation. v0.5 ended the run without `finish`,
  `OnFinish` or `OnEnd`.
- **Am I affected?** Code that treats a missing `OnEnd` as a failure.
- **What to do:** check `Err()` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A provider-executed tool call is never run locally {#provider-executed-not-local}

- **What changed:** in `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and the legacy
  `ToolLoopAgent` loop, a call the provider marks `ProviderExecuted` no
  longer runs a local tool of the same name, as TS runs only calls without
  `providerExecuted`. v0.5 ran the local tool too and added a second result.
- **Am I affected?** A local tool with the same name as a provider tool (web
  search, code execution).
- **What to do:** give the local tool a name the provider's tools do not
  use.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider tool results stay in the assistant message {#provider-tool-results-assistant}

- **What changed:** as in TS, every tool result or tool error a provider
  returns now has `ProviderExecuted` set, in `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and
  the legacy `ToolLoopAgent` loop. The step's `Response.Messages` keep it in
  the assistant message next to its call. v0.5 put a result whose provider
  left the flag unset (OpenAI Responses `web_search_call`,
  `image_generation_call` and `program_output`, xAI Responses hosted tools in
  `GenerateText`, a deferred result with no call in the step) in a separate
  `tool` message, which the next turn sent back as a function output. Results
  of your own tools are unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** Code that looks for provider tool results in `RoleTool`
  messages, or reads `ProviderExecuted == false` on them.
- **What to do:** read them from the assistant message:

  ```go
  for _, part := range result.FinalStep.Response.Messages[0].Content {
  	if r, ok := part.(types.ToolResultContent); ok && r.ProviderExecuted {
  		_ = r.Result
  	}
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ToolResults holds no bookkeeping entries {#tool-results-bookkeeping}

- **What changed:** `ToolResults` no longer holds entries for calls awaiting
  approval, auto-denied calls or provider-executed calls without a result.
- **Am I affected?** Code that finds pending approvals or denials in
  `ToolResults`.
- **What to do:** read the approval parts (`ToolApprovalRequestContent`,
  `ToolApprovalResponseContent`) in `Content`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider tool calls are validated against the tool's Parameters {#provider-tool-input-validation}

- **What changed:** `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and the agents validate the
  input of a provider tool call (a tool with `ProviderExecuted: true` or
  `Type: "provider"`) against the tool's `Parameters`, as TS `parseToolCall`
  checks every tool's `inputSchema`. An invalid provider-executed call is
  marked `Invalid` and `Dynamic`; it gets no client tool error, and the
  provider's own result is kept. v0.5 never validated provider tools. Provider
  tools without `Parameters` are not validated. The Parameters of four
  constructors now are the TS input schemas: `xai.MCPServer` and
  `xai.FileSearch` (`{}`; their config, including the MCP authorization
  token, was in `Parameters` as defaults and stays in the tool args),
  `groq.BrowserSearchTool` and the mistral web search tools (no
  `additionalProperties: false`), and the Anthropic regex tool search (no
  `maxLength` on `pattern`) (review round 71). The AI Gateway Browserbase
  search tool enforces TS's `query` length (1-200) and integer `num_results`
  (1-25), Browserbase fetch requires a URI `url`, and the Exa/Perplexity
  union inputs use `anyOf`. The Anthropic code execution tools (2025-08-25,
  2026-01-20) accept `file_text: null` and the Anthropic memory tool accepts
  non-integer `view_range` / `insert_line` numbers, as TS does. The Google
  code execution and OpenAI code interpreter tools now carry their TS input
  schemas (review round 72). The google tools are now `ProviderExecuted` and
  carry TS's input schemas (`{}` objects; code execution `{language, code}`),
  and the OpenAI local shell, shell, apply patch, computer and programmatic
  tool calling tools, file search and image generation (`{}`), and the xai
  web search, X search, code execution, view image, view X video and image
  generation tools now carry their TS input schemas, so their calls are
  validated like other provider tool calls (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Provider tools you define yourself with
  `ProviderExecuted: true` or `Type: "provider"` whose `Parameters` do not
  describe the call input; code that reads `Parameters` of `xai.MCPServer(`
  or `xai.FileSearch(` (read `ProviderOptions` for the config);
  `tools.NewBrowserbaseSearch(` and `tools.NewBrowserbaseFetch(` calls with
  out-of-range input; provider-executed `google.CodeExecutionTool()` or
  `openaiTool.CodeInterpreter(` calls whose input lacks `code` / `language`
  or `containerId`; `responses.NewLocalShellTool()`, `NewShellTool(`,
  `NewApplyPatchTool()`, `NewComputerTool()` calls whose input does not match
  TS's shape.
- **What to do:** no action unless your provider tool's `Parameters` are not
  its input schema. Give it the input schema, or none:

  ```go
  var webSearch = types.Tool{
  	Name:             "web_search",
  	Type:             types.ToolTypeProviderDefined,
  	ProviderExecuted: true,
  	// The input the model sends, not the provider's configuration.
  	Parameters: map[string]interface{}{
  		"type":       "object",
  		"properties": map[string]interface{}{"query": map[string]interface{}{"type": "string"}},
  	},
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tools with an OrderedSchema or a bare JSONSchemaValidator are validated {#tool-input-ordered-schema-validation}

- **What changed:** a tool call is now validated (and its schema defaults
  applied) when the tool's `Parameters` is an `*schema.OrderedSchema` or a
  bare `*schema.JSONSchemaValidator`, as it already was for a map or a
  `*schema.SimpleJSONSchema`. A call that breaks the schema is `Invalid` and
  `Dynamic` with an `InvalidToolInputError`, does not run, and the model gets
  the error (or `RepairToolCall` runs). `ToolCallRepairOptions.InputSchema`
  returns the schema of a bare `JSONSchemaValidator` instead of
  `{"type":"object"}`. TS `parseToolCall` validates every tool's input schema
  (review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Tools built with `schema.NewOrderedSchema(...)` or
  `schema.NewJSONSchema(...)` as `Parameters` whose model calls do not always
  match that schema.
- **What to do:** no action unless such a tool relied on receiving input its
  schema rejects; loosen the schema or repair the call in `RepairToolCall`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tool input is validated with every input schema {#tool-input-every-schema-validated}

- **What changed:** v0.5 (v0.5.2 included) validated a tool call's input
  only when the tool's `Parameters` was a JSON-schema map or a
  `*schema.JSONSchemaValidator`. A struct-tag schema
  (`schema.NewSimpleStructSchema`, `schema.NewStructSchema`) or your own
  `schema.Schema` / `schema.Validator` was skipped when the call was
  parsed, so input that broke it reached `Execute`. v0.6 validates with
  every input schema, as TS `parseToolCall` validates with the tool's
  `inputSchema`. A struct-tag schema decodes the input into the struct and
  now enforces its `validate:` tags on tool input; a value that does not
  decode, such as a string for an `int` field, fails. A custom validator's
  `Validate` runs. A failing call becomes an invalid tool call
  (`*ai.InvalidToolInputError`): dynamic, answered with a tool error and
  passed to `RepairToolCall`. Approval revalidation
  (`ValidateApprovedToolApprovals`) and `ValidateUIMessages` use the same
  validator, so they reject the same input. A nil schema or a typed nil
  pointer (`(*schema.SimpleStructSchema)(nil)`) is no schema; v0.5 panicked
  in `Validator()` there (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Tools with `Parameters: schema.NewSimpleStructSchema(...)`,
  `schema.NewStructSchema(...)`, or a value of your own `schema.Schema` /
  `schema.Validator` type, used with `GenerateText`, `StreamText`,
  `ParseToolCall`, `ToolLoopAgent`, `ValidateApprovedToolApprovals` or
  `ValidateUIMessages`; struct fields with `validate:` tags.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on `Execute` receiving input
  that the struct's `validate:` tags or your validator reject. In that
  case, relax the tags or validator, or set `Tool.ValidateInput`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider tool output schemas match TS {#provider-tool-output-schemas}

- **What changed:** the `OutputSchema` of the AI Gateway tools
  (`pkg/providers/gateway/tools`) is now TS's zod JSON schema: nullable fields
  are `anyOf [T, {"type":"null"}]` (v0.5 used `"nullable": true`, which the
  validator ignores, so null fields failed), unions are `anyOf` (was
  `oneOf`), and TS's `required` lists are present. `groq.BrowserSearchTool`
  no longer sets `additionalProperties: false`. `xai.MCPServer`,
  `xai.FileSearch`, the mistral web search tools and the Anthropic regex tool
  search now have TS's output schemas (v0.5 had none). `ai.ValidateUIMessages`
  checks tool outputs against these, as TS `validateUIMessages` does (review
  round 72). Review round 73 adds them for the Anthropic web search, web
  fetch, code execution, BM25 tool search and advisor tools, every google
  tool (`GoogleSearchTool`, `EnterpriseWebSearchTool`, `GoogleMapsTool`,
  `UrlContextTool`, `FileSearchTool`, `CodeExecutionTool`,
  `VertexRagStoreTool`), every OpenAI tool constructor (the custom tool has
  none in TS) and every xai tool, so every built-in provider tool now carries
  TS's output schema.
- **Am I affected?** `ValidateUIMessages` with these tools in `Tools`; code
  that reads `OutputSchema` of these tools (`["oneOf"]`, `"nullable"`,
  `[]string` required lists, which are now `[]interface{}`).
- **What to do:** no action unless you read `OutputSchema`. Read `anyOf` and
  decoded JSON types:

  ```go
  func outputVariants(tool types.Tool) []interface{} {
  	// v0.5: tool.OutputSchema.(map[string]interface{})["oneOf"]
  	variants, _ := tool.OutputSchema.(map[string]interface{})["anyOf"].([]interface{})
  	return variants
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider error results are tool errors {#provider-tool-error-results}

- **What changed:** a provider tool result that reports an error (`IsError`
  true, for example an Anthropic `web_search_tool_result_error`) is now a
  `types.ToolErrorContent` part in `Content` (`Error` holds the provider's
  error value) and is replayed in the response messages as an `error-json`
  output, as TS `convertLanguageModelContent` maps `isError` results to
  `tool-error` parts. v0.5 kept a `ToolResultContent` with `IsError` and a
  `json` output, which the Anthropic converter could not send back as an
  error block. `GenerateText` and the legacy `ToolLoopAgent` changed.
  `StreamText` did this only for results that also set `Error`; a streamed
  provider result with only `IsError` set is now a tool error too (UI
  `tool-output-error`, not in `ToolResults`) (review rounds 71 and 72).
- **Am I affected?** Code that looks for `types.ToolResultContent` with
  `IsError` in `Content` or in the response messages, or for
  `ToolResult.IsError` on streamed `ChunkTypeToolResult` parts.
- **What to do:** read the tool-error part:

  ```go
  func providerErrors(content []types.ContentPart) []interface{} {
  	var out []interface{}
  	for _, part := range content {
  		// v0.5: types.ToolResultContent with IsError set
  		if te, ok := part.(types.ToolErrorContent); ok && te.ProviderExecuted {
  			out = append(out, te.Error)
  		}
  	}
  	return out
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Streamed provider tool results carry the tool's metadata {#stream-provider-result-tool-metadata}

- **What changed:** a provider-executed tool result streamed by `StreamText`
  now carries its call's `ToolMetadata` (from `types.Tool.Metadata`): in
  `ToolResults()`, the step's `types.ToolResultContent` and the UI
  `tool-output-available` chunk's `toolMetadata`, as TS does and as
  `GenerateText` already did. v0.5 left it empty (review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `ToolMetadata` on streamed provider tool
  results, or UI clients that expect no `toolMetadata` on them.
- **What to do:** no action unless you depend on the empty field.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Response messages replay tool errors and outputs like TS {#response-tool-error-replay}

- **What changed:** in `ResponseMessages`, a local tool error's `error-text`
  value is always a string, as TS `getErrorMessage`: `ErrorText` when set,
  else the string itself, `"unknown error"` for nil, `"Error: msg"` for an
  `error`, JSON text for anything else (v0.5 passed the raw `Error` value
  through). A provider-executed tool error's `error-json` value is the JSON
  round trip of `Error` (v0.6 betas used `ErrorText` when set). An approval
  request's `InputSchemaInput` is omitted when it equals the call input (TS
  `isDeepEqualData`). A tool output containing NaN or ±Inf replays with
  `null` in those positions (v0.5 replayed `null` for the whole output)
  (review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Code that builds `types.ToolErrorContent` with a nil or
  non-string `Error` and reads the replayed `Output.Value`; code that reads
  `InputSchemaInput` from response messages; tool outputs with NaN/Inf
  floats.
- **What to do:** no action unless you read `Output.Value` of a replayed
  error as a non-string.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ToolResults holds only successful calls {#tool-results-exclude-failures}

- **What changed:** `ToolResults`, `StaticToolResults` and
  `DynamicToolResults` of `GenerateTextResult`, `StreamTextResult`,
  `types.StepResult`, the step-end and end events, `AgentResult` and
  `NewStreamTextResultFromParts` results no longer hold failed calls (a tool
  execution error, an invalid or unknown tool call, a provider error result).
  A failed call is only a `types.ToolErrorContent` part in the step's
  `Content`; the model still gets it as an `error-text` / `error-json` tool
  result. TS `toolResults` holds only `tool-result` content parts (owner
  ruling, review round 71). Telemetry step-end events
  (`TelemetryStepEndEvent.ToolResults`) still include the failures, since
  that event has no content.
- **Am I affected?** Code that loops over `ToolResults` and checks
  `r.Error != nil`, or that expects one entry per tool call.
- **What to do:** read the failures from the content. The typed error is in
  `OnToolExecutionEnd`'s `ToolOutput.Error`:

  ```go
  func toolFailures(result *ai.GenerateTextResult) []string {
  	var failures []string
  	// v0.5:
  	// for _, r := range result.ToolResults {
  	// 	if r.Error != nil {
  	// 		failures = append(failures, r.ToolName+": "+r.Error.Error())
  	// 	}
  	// }
  	for _, part := range result.Content {
  		if te, ok := part.(types.ToolErrorContent); ok {
  			failures = append(failures, fmt.Sprintf("%s: %v", te.ToolName, te.Error))
  		}
  	}
  	return failures
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tool approval and tool-context errors fail the call {#tool-errors-fail-call}

- **What changed:** a tool context that fails the tool's `ContextSchema`, an
  approval function whose context fails it, an unknown approval status and an
  unsupported `ToolApproval` value now end `GenerateText` with an error (and
  error `StreamText`'s stream and the agents' runs), as in TS. v0.5 turned
  them into tool-error results the model saw. `GenerateText` also resolves
  every call's approval before running any tool. A tool's own `Execute` error
  is still a tool error.
- **Am I affected?** Tools with `ContextSchema:`, calls with `ToolApproval:`.
- **What to do:** fix the tool context or approval configuration, or handle
  the error:

  ```go
  // v0.5: the call succeeded; result.ToolResults[0].Error held the error
  // v0.6: the call fails with it
  var validationErr *providererrors.ValidationError
  _ = errors.As(err, &validationErr) // validationErr.Context.Field == "tool context"
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### PrepareStep ActiveTools and ProviderOptions are applied {#preparestep-active-tools}

- **What changed:** a `PrepareStep` `ActiveTools` is honored, and
  `ToolLoopAgent` passes the full tool set so `PrepareStep` can widen it.
  `PrepareStep` `ProviderOptions` are deep-merged onto the call's options.
- **Am I affected?** `PrepareStep` functions that return `ActiveTools` or
  `ProviderOptions` that had no effect in v0.5.
- **What to do:** return only the tools and options the step needs.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Some provider tools are client-executed {#client-executed-provider-tools}

- **What changed:** OpenAI Responses computer, local shell, shell and
  apply-patch tools, and Anthropic computer, text editor and memory tools, are
  client-executed, as TS. Your tool's `Execute` runs them.
- **Am I affected?** Code that uses these tools and expects the provider to
  run them.
- **What to do:** give each one an `Execute` that performs the action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tool output NaN and Infinity become null in typed containers {#tool-output-non-finite-typed}

- **What changed:** a tool output whose typed map, slice or array
  (`map[string]float64`, `[]float32`, `[2]float64`, a pointer to one, a named
  float slice) held NaN or ±Inf became a `null` model output. v0.6 keeps the
  value with those numbers as `null`, as TS
  `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(output))` does (review round 73). A struct field
  holding NaN still makes the whole output `null` (see the known difference
  "Tool output structs holding NaN").
- **Am I affected?** Tools returning float containers that can hold NaN or
  Inf.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A tool that returns an error value is a tool result {#tool-error-value-result}

- **What changed:** v0.5 treated a tool whose `Execute` returns a Go `error`
  as its result value (`return err, nil`) as a failed call in step content:
  the step got a `types.ToolErrorContent` part and the model got
  `error-text` ("Error: boom"), yet the call still counted in `ToolResults`.
  v0.6 matches TS, where an execute that returns an Error object is a tool
  result: the step content is a `types.ToolResultContent` whose `Result` is
  the error and whose `Output` is its JSON value (`{}` for `errors.New`),
  and that JSON value is what the model gets (TS `createToolModelOutput`).
  `providerutils.ConvertToResponseMessages` also replays an error-valued
  `ToolResult.Result` or `ToolResultContent.Result` as JSON and keeps a set
  `ModelOutput`. Only the second return value (`ToolResult.Error`) makes a
  tool error (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** Tools whose `Execute` returns `(someErr, nil)`, and
  callers that put an `error` in `types.ToolResult.Result` /
  `types.ToolResultContent.Result` and pass it to
  `ConvertToResponseMessages`.
- **What to do:** return the failure as the error:

  ```go
  // Before (v0.5 showed the model "Error: boom")
  func lookupBefore(ctx context.Context, input map[string]interface{}, opts types.ToolExecutionOptions) (interface{}, error) {
  	return errors.New("boom"), nil
  }

  // After
  func lookupAfter(ctx context.Context, input map[string]interface{}, opts types.ToolExecutionOptions) (interface{}, error) {
  	return nil, errors.New("boom")
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Invalid tool call errors carry no provider metadata {#invalid-tool-call-no-provider-metadata}

- **What changed:** v0.5 copied an invalid tool call's `ProviderMetadata`
  (unknown tool, invalid input, failed repair) onto the tool error it
  synthesized: the step's `ToolErrorContent.ProviderMetadata` had it, and
  the tool-result part of the next request carried it as `providerOptions`.
  v0.6 leaves it off, as TS generateText and streamText do. The tool call
  part itself keeps its metadata (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `ToolErrorContent.ProviderMetadata` or
  `ToolResult.ProviderMetadata` of an invalid call (`ToolCall.Invalid`), or
  that depends on provider options on the tool message that answers it.
- **What to do:** no action unless you read that metadata; read it from the
  tool call part (`ToolCallContent.ProviderMetadata`).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### GenerateText invalid tool call errors carry no tool metadata {#generate-invalid-tool-error-no-tool-metadata}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `GenerateText`'s tool-error for an invalid tool call
  carried the tool's metadata (`ToolErrorContent.ToolMetadata`, UI
  `tool-output-error` `toolMetadata`). v0.6 leaves it off, as TS
  `generateText` builds that error with only the call ID, tool name, input
  and error; `StreamText` keeps it, as TS `streamText`. The invalid
  tool-call part keeps it in both (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** `types.Tool.Metadata` read from
  `ToolErrorContent.ToolMetadata` of `GenerateText` (or
  `ToolLoopAgent.Generate`) step content, or from the UI chunk built from it.
- **What to do:** read the metadata from the invalid `ToolCallContent` part
  instead.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### StreamText invalid tool call errors carry the tool's title {#stream-invalid-tool-error-title}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `StreamText`'s tool-error content part for an
  invalid tool call (unknown tool, input that fails the schema, a failed
  repair) had an empty `Title`. v0.6 sets it to the tool's title, as TS
  `streamLanguageModelCall` (`title: toolCall.title`). `GenerateText` and
  executed tool failures still have no title. The SDK marks these results
  with the new internal field `types.ToolResult.InvalidToolCall`
  (`json:"-"`) (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading `types.ToolErrorContent.Title` (or the
  `title` key of a `tool-error` part) from `StreamText` /
  `ToolLoopAgent.Stream` step content.
- **What to do:** no action unless you treat a non-empty `Title` on a
  tool-error as an executed tool's error; check the matching
  `ToolCallContent.Invalid` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An invalid tool call's error follows the call in the stream {#stream-invalid-tool-error-position}

- **What changed:** `StreamText` emits the tool error of an invalid client
  tool call (unknown tool, input that fails validation) right after its
  tool-call part, before the model's later parts, as TS
  `streamLanguageModelCall` does. v0.5 emitted it after the model stream
  ended, together with the executed tool results, so a later tool call or
  text came first. The step content follows the same order. With an
  enforced `ToolChoice` (a specific tool or required) that the step
  violates, the invalid call now gets its tool error before the violation
  error part; v0.5 emitted none. Tool choice none still withholds both. A
  `WorkflowAgent` step that is blocked or ends with a stream error streams
  the tool error but adds no tool message for it (TS model-call-iterator)
  (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Code that depends on the position of
  `ChunkTypeToolResult` parts with an `Error` for `Invalid` tool calls in
  `FullStream`/`NewStream`, `OnChunk` or the UI message stream
  (`tool-output-error`), or on the order of `ToolErrorContent` in
  `StepResult.Content`; code that expects no tool error after a
  `ToolChoiceViolationError`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on that order; match parts by
  `ToolCallID`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ToolCallRepairError messages show the cause as TS does {#tool-call-repair-error-message}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ToolCallRepairError.Error()` was
  `Error repairing tool call: <message>`. v0.6 is
  `Error repairing tool call: Error: <message>`: the cause's TS `toString()`
  (an SDK error cause shows its `AI_` name), as TS
  `getErrorMessage(cause)`. The tool-error text the model sees for a call
  whose repair failed changes the same way (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Code comparing `ToolCallRepairError` messages or the
  `ErrorText` of an invalid call whose `RepairToolCall` returned an error.
- **What to do:** match with `errors.As` and read `Cause` instead of the
  text:

  ```go
  // Before
  if strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), "Error repairing tool call: boom") { /* ... */ }

  // After
  var repairErr *ai.ToolCallRepairError
  if errors.As(err, &repairErr) && repairErr.Cause != nil && repairErr.Cause.Error() == "boom" { /* ... */ }
  ```
- **Kind:** error text only

### InvalidToolInputError shows its cause as TS does {#invalid-tool-input-error-cause}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `InvalidToolInputError.Error()` was
  `Invalid input for tool <name>: <cause.Error()>`, with the raw validator or
  JSON parser message for a tool's `Parameters` schema. v0.6 shows the cause
  as TS `getErrorMessage` (its `toString()`): a `Parameters` schema failure
  is `AI_TypeValidationError: Type validation failed: Value: <input
  JSON>.\nError message: Error: <validator message>`, input that is not JSON
  is `AI_JSONParseError: JSON parsing failed: Text: <input>.\nError message:
  <parser message>`, and other causes (the approved-input mismatch, refiner
  errors) get an `Error: ` prefix (`Invalid input for tool deleteFile:
  Error: Approved tool input does not match the validated schema output.`).
  A `nil` cause is `unknown error`, as before. The cause is now a
  `*providererrors.TypeValidationError` or `*providererrors.JSONParseError`
  (the validator error or `*json.SyntaxError` stays reachable with
  `errors.As`). The tool-error text the model sees changes the same way.
  Tools with `ValidateInput` already had the TS text (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** Code comparing `InvalidToolInputError` messages, the
  `Error` / `ErrorText` of an invalid call's `ToolCallContent` or
  `ToolErrorContent`, or `InvalidToolApproval.Error.Error()`; code that
  type-asserts `InvalidToolInputError.Cause` to a validator error or
  `*json.SyntaxError`.
- **What to do:** match with `errors.As` instead of the text:

  ```go
  // Before
  if strings.Contains(err.Error(), "Invalid input for tool weather: $.city") { /* ... */ }

  // After
  var invalid *ai.InvalidToolInputError
  var typeErr *providererrors.TypeValidationError
  if errors.As(err, &invalid) && invalid.ToolName == "weather" && errors.As(invalid.Cause, &typeErr) { /* ... */ }
  ```
- **Kind:** error text only

### GenerateText resolves a deferred provider call from any tool-result or tool-error part {#deferred-result-any-result-part}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `GenerateText` kept a deferred provider call
  (`SupportsDeferredResults`) pending, and called the model again, unless the
  same response held a `types.ToolResultContent` value for it; a
  `types.ToolErrorContent` part or a pointer part did not count. v0.6 counts
  every tool-result and tool-error part (value or pointer) of the model
  response, as TS `pendingDeferredToolCalls` deletes the ID of every model
  tool-result, and as `ToolLoopAgent.GenerateAgent` already did (review
  round 80).
- **Am I affected?** Custom `provider.LanguageModel` implementations that
  return `ToolErrorContent`, `*ToolErrorContent` or `*ToolResultContent` in
  `GenerateResult.Content` for a provider-executed tool with
  `SupportsDeferredResults: true`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the extra model call.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### RepairToolCall InputSchema of a tool without Parameters is the TS empty object schema {#repair-input-schema-empty-object}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ToolCallRepairOptions.InputSchema(name)` returned
  `{"type":"object"}` for a tool without `Parameters`. v0.6 returns TS
  `asSchema(undefined).jsonSchema`:
  `{"type":"object","properties":{},"additionalProperties":false}`, which an
  unknown tool already got (review round 80).
- **Am I affected?** `RepairToolCall` functions that call `InputSchema` for
  tools without `Parameters` and inspect or forward the schema.
- **What to do:** no action unless you compare the schema.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A tool without Parameters is sent the TS empty object schema {#tool-without-parameters-schema}

- **What changed:** v0.5 handed providers a nil `Parameters` for a function tool
  without a schema (most converters then sent `{"type":"object",
  "properties":{}}`, some `null`). v0.6 sends, through `GenerateText`,
  `StreamText`, the batch API and `ToolLoopAgent`, TS's `asSchema(undefined)`
  schema, `{"type":"object","properties":{},"additionalProperties":false}`,
  for a function or dynamic tool whose `Parameters` is nil, a typed nil or a
  zero schema value; the OpenAI Responses, Moonshot and Open Responses models
  send it for such a tool on direct `DoGenerate` / `DoStream` calls too.
  Provider tools are unchanged (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `types.Tool{` without `Parameters:`; tests asserting the request
  body of a no-argument tool.
- **What to do:** No action unless you assert the old schema on the wire.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Schema-valued tool Parameters reach every converter as JSON Schema {#schema-value-tool-parameters}

- **What changed:** calling `DoGenerate` / `DoStream` directly with a tool whose
  `Parameters` is a `schema.Schema` (`schema.NewSimpleJSONSchema`,
  `schema.NewSimpleStructSchema`) failed on moonshot (`must be a JSON Schema
  object with type "object"`), sent no `parameters` on openresponses and
  skipped OpenAI's JSON Schema normalization on the Responses API. A bare
  `schema.Validator` (`schema.NewJSONSchema(m)`) was treated as no schema by
  those converters through `ai.GenerateText` too. v0.6 uses the schema's JSON
  Schema everywhere, as TS does with `asSchema(tool.inputSchema).jsonSchema`
  (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `types.Tool{Parameters: schema.NewSimple...}` passed straight to a
  `moonshot`, `openresponses` or `openai` Responses model;
  `Parameters: schema.NewJSONSchema(` with any of them.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An invalid tool call's tool error reads as TS getErrorMessage {#invalid-tool-call-error-text}

- **What changed:** TS builds the tool-error of an invalid tool call (unknown tool, invalid input) with `error: getErrorMessage(toolCall.error)` (generate-text.ts 1498, stream-language-model-call.ts 750), the string `AI_NoSuchToolError: Model tried to call unavailable tool ...`. v0.5 gave the typed error, whose `Error()` had no name prefix, so a UI `OnError` returning `err.Error()` wrote `Model tried to call ...` in `tool-output-error`. v0.6: the error's `Error()` is the TS text, `errors.As` still finds `*ai.NoSuchToolError` / `*ai.InvalidToolInputError`, and `ToolErrorContent.Error` holds the prefixed text. The model sees the same text as before. An invalid call whose input does not parse keeps its input text (TS parse-tool-call.ts 116-117): an unknown tool's call with no input reports `input: ""` in `tool-input-error` (v0.5: `{}`).
- **Am I affected?** UI `OnError` / `ToolResult.Error.Error()` / `ToolErrorContent.Error` comparisons for unknown tools or invalid tool input; `tool-input-error` `input` of a call with empty arguments.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As(err, &noSuchTool)` instead of matching the message.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Web fetch: an invalid redirect Location is a tool result {#web-fetch-invalid-redirect}

- **What changed:** A redirect whose `Location` cannot be parsed returned an `Invalid URL` error that failed the tool call; it is now the result `{type: "error", errorCode: "network_error", message: "The server returned an invalid redirect location."}` (TS `dffe4053c1`).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches the `Invalid URL` error from the web fetch tool (`toolwebfetch`).
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the error; read `ErrorOutput.ErrorCode == "network_error"` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Invalid tool calls take Title and ToolMetadata from the tool {#invalid-tool-call-title-metadata}

- **What changed:** v0.5 kept a `Title` / `ToolMetadata` the provider put on a tool call that failed to parse or named an unknown tool. v0.6 sets both from the tool only (none for an unknown tool), as TS `parseToolCall` (`title: tool?.title`) (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Custom providers that set `types.ToolCall.Title` or `ToolMetadata` on model tool calls and read them back from invalid calls (`ToolCall.Invalid`, the tool-error result).
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on that; put them on the `types.Tool` (`Title`, `Metadata`).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Sandbox text encodings are Node Buffer encodings {#sandbox-text-node-encodings}

- **What changed:** `ReadTextFile` / `WriteTextFile` `Encoding` on `ai.ShellSandbox` and the local and Vercel harness sandboxes took WHATWG labels; TS sandbox sessions use `Buffer.from(bytes).toString(encoding)` / `Buffer.from(content, encoding)`. v0.6 takes the Node set and aliases, case-insensitive: `utf8` / `utf-8` (invalid bytes read as U+FFFD), `utf16le` / `ucs2`, `latin1` / `binary` (bytes 0x80-0x9F are U+0080-U+009F, not windows-1252), `ascii` (high bit cleared), `hex`, `base64`, `base64url`; any other name (`iso-8859-1`, `windows-1252`, ...) fails with `Unknown encoding: <name>` (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** `SandboxReadTextFileOptions.Encoding` / `SandboxWriteTextFileOptions.Encoding` set to a WHATWG label other than UTF-8.
- **What to do:** Use the Node name: `latin1` for ISO-8859-1 text; decode other legacy encodings from `ReadBinaryFile` yourself.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Executed tool results have no title {#executed-tool-result-title}

- **What changed:** v0.5 copied the tool call's `Title` onto the `types.ToolResult` of a locally executed tool (and of a tool-not-found error). v0.6 leaves it empty, as TS `execute-tool-call.ts` builds a client tool's tool-result / tool-error without a title. Tool calls and preliminary results keep it (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** You read `ToolResult.Title` from `OnToolCallFinish` / `OnToolExecutionEnd` (`e.ToolOutput.Title`), `StepResult.ToolResults` or a StreamText `ChunkTypeToolResult` part.
- **What to do:** Read the call's title:

  ```go
  // before: title := e.ToolOutput.Title
  title := e.ToolCall.Title
  _ = title
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A provider tool error without a value keeps its error undefined {#provider-tool-error-no-value}

- **What changed:** A provider tool result with `IsError` and no value became a tool-error with error `""` (GenerateText) or `"tool error"` (StreamText). v0.6 keeps the error undefined like TS: `ToolErrorContent.Error` is nil, the response message replays `error-json` `null`, and the UI `tool-output-error` chunk has no `errorText` (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** You read `ToolErrorContent.Error` of provider-executed tools, replay such messages, or read `errorText` from UI chunks.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare that error to `""` / `"tool error"`; check for nil.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Model tool calls take title and metadata from the tool {#model-tool-call-tool-metadata}

- **What changed:** A `ToolMetadata` (or `Title`) a custom provider set on a model `types.ToolCall` was kept when the tool had no `Metadata`, and on provider-executed dynamic calls. v0.6 uses the tool's `Metadata` / `Title` only, and none for provider-executed dynamic calls (TS `doParseToolCall` / `parseProviderExecutedDynamicToolCall`; review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** A custom `provider.LanguageModel` that sets `ToolCall.ToolMetadata` / `Title`, read in `ToolExecutionOptions.ToolMetadata`, step tool calls or results.
- **What to do:** Put the metadata on the tool:

  ```go
  tool := types.Tool{Name: "lookup", Metadata: map[string]interface{}{"trace": "tool"}}
  _ = tool
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Anthropic provider tools drop undeclared input keys {#provider-tool-input-strip-undeclared}

- **What changed:** The parsed input of the Anthropic provider tools whose TS input schema is a flat zod `z.object` (bash, text editor, computer, code execution 20250522, tool search, web fetch, web search, advisor) now drops keys the schema does not declare, as zod's default strip does. The tool call's `Arguments`, `execute` input and replayed messages carry the stripped input. Other provider tools and user tools keep every key (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads extra keys from the input of those Anthropic tools (`ToolCall.Arguments`, the `execute` input).
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on keys the tool schema does not declare.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### toolwebfetch and toolbash count UTF-16 code units {#utf16-lengths}

- **What changed:** `toolwebfetch` offsets, lengths and the URL limit, and `toolbash` truncation, counted bytes or runes. v0.6 counts UTF-16 code units, as TS (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `toolwebfetch` offsets / lengths and `toolbash` truncation on non-ASCII text.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP, Vercel, Topaz, DeepInfra, OpenCode, Codex and Prodia details follow TS {#tools-r91-details}

- **What changed:** Small TS alignments (review round 91): MCP OAuth register / exchange / refresh take a developer-configured `TrustedOrigin` (new `RegisterOAuthClientWithOptions`); MCP Apps `csp.baseUriDomains`; MCP JSON-RPC key order and no `params` on cursor-less list calls; uncapped `tools/call` retry backoff; a closed client reports "closed client" before the capability error; cross-origin MCP and Vercel redirects drop `Proxy-Authorization` and `Cookie`; Vercel git / tarball sources, runtime + image rejected before any request and the stdin `bytesWritten` schema; Topaz video `InvalidResponseDataError`; DeepInfra image error `Data`; OpenCode GitLab null body is "invalid JSON"; Codex sends an empty `ChatGPT-Account-ID`; Prodia keeps large whole numbers as `int64`; typed provider-option literals read in quiverai / deepinfra / voyage / minimax / parallel.
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches these texts or wire details.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match them.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Anthropic advisor tool input is strict {#advisor-input-strict}

- **What changed:** An `anthropic.advisor_20260301` call with input keys had them stripped to `{}`. v0.6 makes such a call invalid (`InvalidToolInputError`), as TS z.object({}).strict() (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Advisor tool calls whose input is not `{}`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An unset tool-caller entry leaves the tool unaffected {#tool-callers-unset-entry}

- **What changed:** `ResolveToolCallerConfiguration` kept a nil callers entry as an empty list, hiding that tool from the model, and returned nil for an empty non-nil tool set. v0.6 skips nil entries and reports an unknown tool for an empty tool set, as TS tool-caller-configuration.ts; a provider caller's `PrepareProviderOptions` gets a typed callee `ProviderOptions` as its JSON object (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `ExperimentalToolCallers` with nil entries, or with an empty `Tools` slice.
- **What to do:** Use an empty non-nil slice to hide a tool.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP, MiniMax, Topaz, Vercel Sandbox and DeepInfra details follow TS (round 92) {#tools-r92-details}

- **What changed:** Small TS alignments (review round 92): MCP experimental events `Subscribe` sends `params: {}` on the first catalog page; input_required retries keep the TS key order; an already-aborted modern MCP request rejects with the abort reason; a failed reply to a server request is reported to `OnError`; MiniMax video null usage members stay null in `providerMetadata.minimax.usage`; Topaz video reports a missing API key before the input-video checks, sends non-integer or non-numeric resolution parts as JS `Number` does (fraction kept, NaN as null), and Topaz `InvalidResponseDataError` data is the schema-parsed response (undeclared keys stripped); Vercel Sandbox rejects runtime + image before resolving credentials; a DeepInfra image call without an API key returns `LoadAPIKeyError`, not a wrapped `ProviderError`.
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches these texts, errors or wire details.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match them.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

## Agents and approvals {#agents-and-approvals}

### A panicking ToolLoopAgent callback does not skip the other one {#agent-callback-panic-isolation}

- **What changed:** `ToolLoopAgent.Generate` and `Stream` run a settings-level
  callback (`AgentConfig.OnStart`, `OnStepStartEvent`, `OnToolExecutionStart`,
  `OnToolExecutionEnd`, `OnStepEndEvent`, `OnEndEvent`, `ErrorHandler`,
  `AbortHandler`) and the matching per-call callback each under its own
  recover. In v0.5 a panic in the settings-level callback skipped the per-call
  one. A panicking `AgentConfig.ErrorHandler` no longer crashes `Generate`,
  which returns the generation error instead. TS `mergeCallbacks` awaits
  `Promise.allSettled`, and `notify` ignores a throwing `onError`
  (tool-loop-agent.test.ts "should rethrow the generate error when onError
  throws", review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Code that relies on a panic in an `AgentConfig` callback
  to stop the per-call callback, or on `ErrorHandler` panics propagating out
  of `Generate`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you panic in callbacks to signal failure.
  Record the failure instead and check it after the call.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Kept approval responses reach the model without providerExecuted {#approval-response-prompt-shape}

- **What changed:** a tool approval response for a provider-executed call,
  which `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and the agents keep in the model prompt,
  now reaches the model as TS's LanguageModelV4 `tool-approval-response`
  part: `ApprovalID`, `Approved` and `Reason` only. v0.5 passed the response
  with `ProviderExecuted: true` and the caller's `ToolCallID` / `ToolCall`
  (TS convert-to-language-model-prompt.ts maps the kept part to
  `{approvalId, approved, reason}`; review round 73). The SDK's providers
  read only the approval ID and decision, so their requests are unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** A custom `provider.LanguageModel` that reads
  `ProviderExecuted`, `ToolCallID` or `ToolCall` of a
  `types.ToolApprovalResponseContent` in `opts.Prompt.Messages`.
- **What to do:** look the call up by `ApprovalID` from the assistant
  message's `types.ToolApprovalRequestContent`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tool-approval requests carry the pre-schema input {#approval-input-schema-input-raw}

- **What changed:** v0.5 put no `inputSchemaInput` on a tool-approval
  request unless `ExperimentalRefineToolInput` changed the input, and then
  used the post-schema input. v0.6 sets it to the input the schema received
  (the JSON-parsed model input, before defaults, `Tool.ValidateInput` or the
  refiner) whenever that differs from the call's final input, as TS
  `parseToolCall` stores `parseResult.rawValue` as the approval's
  `inputSchemaInput`. This holds for `GenerateText`, `StreamText`,
  `ToolLoopAgent.Generate` / `Stream`, the legacy loop
  (`ToolLoopAgent.GenerateAgent` / `Execute` / `ExecuteWithMessages`) and
  `WorkflowAgent.Generate` (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** Tools with `ToolApproval` / `NeedsApproval` whose
  `Parameters` declare `default` values, or that set `ValidateInput` or
  `ExperimentalRefineToolInput`; code reading
  `ToolApprovalRequestContent.InputSchemaInput`, the UI
  `tool-approval-request` chunk's `inputSchemaInput`, or a `UIMessage` tool
  part's `approval.inputSchemaInput`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you compare `InputSchemaInput` with the
  call input: it is now the pre-schema input (for a default-filling schema,
  the input without the defaults).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Approval revalidation runs for tools without Parameters {#approval-revalidation-without-parameters}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ValidateApprovedToolApprovals` (and approval
  resumption) skipped tools without `Parameters`. v0.6 still revalidates
  them: `ExperimentalRefineToolInput` runs on the approved input, and an
  approval whose refined input differs from it is invalid ("Approved tool
  input does not match the validated schema output."). TS revalidates every
  executable tool (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** `ExperimentalRefineToolInput` entries for a tool with
  `Execute` and nil `Parameters`, resumed through
  `ValidateApprovedToolApprovals`, `ResumeToolApprovals`, or `GenerateText`
  / `StreamText` / `ToolLoopAgent` approval resumption.
- **What to do:** no action unless such a refiner is not idempotent; make it
  return the approved input unchanged.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Approved calls of struct-schema tools are revalidated as they were parsed {#approval-struct-schema-revalidation}

- **What changed:** v0.5 (v0.5.2 included) revalidated an approved tool
  call, and `ValidateUIMessages` validated a tool part's input, with a
  struct-tag schema's validator, which always failed on the decoded input
  map: an approved call of a tool whose `Parameters` is
  `schema.NewSimpleStructSchema(...)` became an invalid approval (a tool
  error, never executed) and `ValidateUIMessages` rejected the message. v0.6
  revalidates with the validator the call was parsed with, as TS
  revalidates with the schema the call was parsed with, so a struct-tag
  schema validates the approved input as it validates the parsed call (see
  [every input schema](#tool-input-every-schema-validated)): a valid
  approved call executes, and one that breaks the struct's `validate:` tags
  is an invalid approval (review rounds 80 and 81).
- **Am I affected?** Tools with `Parameters: schema.NewSimpleStructSchema(...)`
  (or another `schema.Schema` whose validator is a `*schema.StructValidator`)
  together with `ToolApproval`, `ValidateUIMessages` or
  `ValidateApprovedToolApprovals`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on those approvals failing.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Legacy ToolLoopAgent

These entries apply to the `Execute`, `ExecuteWithMessages` and
`GenerateAgent` loop of `pkg/agent`.

#### Legacy ToolLoopAgent approval IDs are generated {#legacy-approval-ids}

- **What changed:** the loop names each approval request, and the response of
  an automatic approval or denial, with the run's ID generator
  (`InternalOptions.GenerateID`, by default `aitxt-` plus 24 characters), as
  TS `generateId()`, and signs that ID. v0.5 used the tool call ID, which
  some providers repeat across steps. A step response without an ID also
  takes its ID from `InternalOptions.GenerateID`. Approvals persisted by v0.5
  still resume. `WorkflowAgent` keeps `approval-` plus the call ID.
- **Am I affected?** Code that answers an approval with the tool call ID.
- **What to do:** answer with the request's `ApprovalID`:

  ```go
  response := types.ToolApprovalResponseContent{ApprovalID: request.ApprovalID, Approved: true}
  _ = response // not request.ToolCallID
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Legacy ToolLoopAgent tool results list provider results {#legacy-tool-results-provider}

- **What changed:** as `GenerateText` and TS `step.toolResults`,
  `StepResult.ToolResults` (with `StaticToolResults` /
  `DynamicToolResults`) and `AgentResult.ToolResults`, which
  `WorkflowAgent.Generate` returns too, hold the provider-executed results the
  model returned, before the local ones. A provider-executed call no longer
  adds a `null` result to the step.
- **Am I affected?** Code that treats every entry as a local execution.
- **What to do:** skip provider results:

  ```go
  for _, r := range result.ToolResults {
  	if r.ProviderExecuted {
  		continue // the provider's own result (web search, code execution, ...)
  	}
  	_ = r.Result
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Legacy ToolLoopAgent tool messages carry the output's JSON value {#legacy-tool-output-json-value}

- **What changed:** the `json` output of a tool result in the legacy loop's
  messages (`StepResult.ResponseMessages`, the next step's prompt) is now the
  tool output's JSON value (a struct becomes a map, a `json.Marshaler` its
  decoded value), as TS `createToolModelOutput` (`toJSONValue`) and as
  `GenerateText` already did. v0.5 put the Go value itself there (review
  round 71).
- **Am I affected?** Code that type-asserts `ToolResultOutput.Value` of tool
  messages from `AgentResult.Steps[i].ResponseMessages` or
  `WorkflowResult.Steps` to a Go type returned by a tool.
- **What to do:** no action unless you read that value as your Go type; read
  `types.ToolResult.Result` instead:

  ```go
  func toolOutputs(step types.StepResult) []interface{} {
  	var out []interface{}
  	for _, r := range step.ToolResults {
  		out = append(out, r.Result) // the Go value the tool returned
  	}
  	return out
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Legacy ToolLoopAgent request messages leave out the instructions {#legacy-request-messages}

- **What changed:** with `Include.RequestMessages`, a step's
  `Request.Messages` is the step's messages as TS `generateText` records
  them: no instruction messages, before prompt conversion.
  `WorkflowAgent.Generate` steps record the model prompt with its system
  message first, as TS WorkflowAgent.
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads the instructions from
  `Request.Messages[0]`.
- **What to do:** read the instructions from your own configuration.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The legacy ToolLoopAgent rejects a negative MaxRetries {#legacy-negative-maxretries}

- **What changed:** a negative `MaxRetries` (on the agent or from
  `PrepareCall`) fails the run with `*errors.InvalidArgumentError`
  ("maxRetries must be >= 0") before the model call, as TS `prepareRetries`.
  v0.5 silently made no retries.
- **Am I affected?** A negative `MaxRetries` on `AgentConfig` or a
  `PrepareCall` result.
- **What to do:** use 0 to disable retries.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### A legacy ToolLoopAgent step timeout is context.DeadlineExceeded {#legacy-step-timeout-deadline}

- **What changed:** the per-step deadline (`Timeout.PerStep`, or a
  `PrepareCall` `Timeout` override, which now applies as in TS) reaches the
  model call as a deadline. A timed-out step fails with an error wrapping
  `context.DeadlineExceeded`. v0.5 surfaced `context.Canceled`.
- **Am I affected?** `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` used to detect a step
  timeout.
- **What to do:**

  ```go
  // v0.5
  if errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) { /* step timed out */ }
  // v0.6
  if errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) { /* step timed out */ }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The legacy ToolLoopAgent step timeout covers tool execution {#legacy-step-timeout-tools}

- **What changed:** in v0.5 the per-step deadline (`Timeout.PerStep`) ended
  when the model call returned, so a tool could keep running past it. As in
  TS, the step timeout now also bounds the step's tools. A tool that used to
  finish after the step deadline is cancelled (its ctx is done), and the run
  ends with `context.DeadlineExceeded` before the next step, even if the tool
  ignored the cancellation.
- **Am I affected?** `Timeout.PerStep` on the legacy agent with tools that
  can run longer than the step.
- **What to do:** size `Timeout.PerStep` for the model call plus its tools, or
  use `Timeout.Total` or a timeout inside the tool.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### An empty ActiveTools leaves no tools active in the legacy loop {#legacy-empty-active-tools}

- **What changed:** an empty, non-nil `ActiveTools` leaves no tools active,
  as TS `filterActiveTools` and `GenerateText`. v0.5 ignored it and sent every
  tool. A tool call against the empty set is a `NoSuchToolError` that
  `RepairToolCall` can repair.
- **Am I affected?** `ActiveTools: []string{}` on the legacy agent, or a
  filter that can return an empty slice.
- **What to do:** use nil to keep all tools.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The legacy ToolLoopAgent follows the PrepareCall tools, tool callers and output {#legacy-preparecall-tools-output}

- **What changed:** `GenerateAgent`, `Execute` and `ExecuteWithMessages` (and
  `WorkflowAgent.Generate`, which runs this loop) resolve tool search and
  `ExperimentalToolCallers` from the tools and tool callers that the first
  `PrepareCall` returns, and parse `AgentResult.Output` with the `Output` it
  returns. Its `ToolCallConcurrency` is validated before the run starts: an
  invalid value fails before `OnStart`, even when the model calls no tool,
  and an invalid configured value that `PrepareCall` replaces is no error.
  The output's `ParseCompleteOutput` gets the last step's usage. In v0.5 the
  loop used the configured tools, tool callers and output (a `ToolSearch`
  added by `PrepareCall` failed with "toolSearch must be bound by an AI SDK
  generation.") and passed the total usage. TS `ToolLoopAgent` passes the
  `prepareCall` result to `generateText`, which parses with
  `lastStep.usage`.
- **Am I affected?** `PrepareCall:` functions that set `Tools`,
  `ExperimentalToolCallers`, `ToolCallConcurrency` or `Output`, and
  `ParseCompleteOutput` implementations that read `opts.Usage`.
- **What to do:** no action unless your `PrepareCall` changes those fields
  and relied on the configured ones winning, or your output reads the usage.
  For the total usage, read `AgentResult.Usage`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Legacy ToolLoopAgent steps carry the call ID {#legacy-step-call-id}

- **What changed:** each `types.StepResult` of the legacy loop now has
  `CallID` set to the run's call ID (the `CallID` of its `OnStartEvent` and
  `OnFinishEvent`), as TS step results' `callId` and as `GenerateText` steps
  already did. v0.5 left it empty. `WorkflowAgent.Generate` steps carry the
  workflow run's call ID (review round 70).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `StepResult.CallID` from
  `AgentResult.Steps`, `WorkflowResult.Steps` or the step lists of
  `OnStepStartEvent` / `OnFinishEvent`, or compares step results with
  `reflect.DeepEqual` against values without a `CallID`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on an empty `CallID`.

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The agent loop leaves provider-executed calls of unknown tools to the provider {#agent-provider-dynamic-unknown-tool}

- **What changed:** the legacy `ToolLoopAgent` loop (`Execute`,
  `GenerateAgent`, hence `WorkflowAgent.Generate`) no longer answers a
  provider-executed (dynamic) tool call whose tool is not in `Tools` with a
  `tool not found: <name>` error result, and no longer calls `OnToolError`
  for it. As in TS (`parseToolCall` keeps the call; only client calls run
  locally), it is the provider's call: the loop records a pending
  provider-executed result, as for a registered provider tool (review
  round 70).
- **Am I affected?** `OnToolError` callbacks or tool results that matched
  `tool not found:` for provider-executed (`ProviderExecuted: true`) calls.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on that error.

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The legacy loop stops waiting for a deferred provider tool on its error result {#agent-deferred-provider-error-result}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ToolLoopAgent.GenerateAgent` / `Execute` /
  `ExecuteWithMessages` (and `WorkflowAgent.Generate`) kept a deferred
  provider tool call pending when its result was an error, and kept calling
  the model until the step limit. v0.6 ends the wait on any provider result,
  as TS `pendingDeferredToolCalls` reads the raw model response, where an
  error result is a tool-result too (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** Provider tools with `SupportsDeferredResults` (for
  example Anthropic code execution) whose results report `IsError`.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The legacy loop's invalid tool call errors carry no tool metadata {#agent-invalid-tool-error-no-tool-metadata}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ToolLoopAgent.GenerateAgent`/`Execute`/
  `ExecuteWithMessages` gave the tool-error for an invalid tool call the
  tool's metadata. v0.6 leaves it off, as TS `ToolLoopAgent.generate`
  (which is `generateText`); the error is dynamic. The invalid tool-call part
  keeps the metadata (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** `types.Tool.Metadata` read from
  `ToolErrorContent.ToolMetadata` (or `ToolResult.ToolMetadata` of an invalid
  call) in legacy agent step content or results.
- **What to do:** read the metadata from the invalid `ToolCallContent` part
  instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### AI Gateway authentication errors are wrapped {#gateway-auth-error}

- **What changed:** `GenerateAgent`, `Execute` and `ExecuteWithMessages`
  return an unauthenticated AI Gateway error with the TS message that says how
  to authenticate, as `GenerateText`, `StreamText`, `GenerateObject`,
  `StreamObject` and the batch cancel/list calls do (TS `wrapGatewayError`).
  A telemetry integration's `OnStepError` now gets the wrapped error too, from
  `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and the legacy loop (TS notifies
  `onStepError` with `wrapGatewayError(error)`); its `OnError` keeps the
  original error.
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches the gateway error text, including in a
  `TelemetryIntegration.OnStepError` implementation.
- **What to do:** match with `errors.As` on
  `*gatewayerrors.GatewayAuthenticationError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### WorkflowAgent

#### WorkflowAgent approval IDs are approval-toolCallId {#workflow-approval-ids}

- **What changed:** as in TS, `Generate` and `Stream` name the approval of
  tool call `X` `approval-X` and sign that ID. v0.5 used the call ID in
  `Generate` and a random ID in `Stream`. Approvals persisted by v0.5 still
  resume, since the response is matched against the stored request's ID.
- **Am I affected?** Code that answers an approval with the tool call ID, or
  builds the ID itself.
- **What to do:** answer with the request's `ApprovalID`, as in the
  [legacy example](#legacy-approval-ids).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Resumed approval results carry their model output {#workflow-resumed-output}

- **What changed:** the tool message holding resumed approvals' results (the
  first of `ResponseMessages`, and the next model call's prompt) has
  `ToolResultContent.Output` set (text for a string, else JSON, or the tool's
  `ToModelOutput`) and no `Input`, as TS WorkflowAgent's `modelResult`. It
  held the raw `Result` and the input.
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `.Result` or `.Input` from those parts.
- **What to do:** read `Output`:

  ```go
  if part, ok := resumed.ResponseMessages[0].Content[0].(types.ToolResultContent); ok && part.Output != nil {
  	_ = part.Output.Value // v0.5: part.Result
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent PrepareStep overrides carry over to later steps {#workflow-preparestep-carryover}

- **What changed:** as TS's model-call iterator, the next step's hook gets the
  previous step's model, instructions, tool choice, active tools, runtime and
  tools contexts and generation settings, and a hook that returns them
  unchanged keeps them. v0.5 reset them every step in `Generate` and kept
  only the model and instructions in `Stream`. Also: in `Stream`, a
  `PrepareStep` `Headers` or `MaxRetries` now reaches the model call, and a
  negative `MaxRetries` fails the step. A `PrepareStep` `ActiveTools` narrows
  the call's active tools in `Generate`. A `PrepareStep` that returns
  different `Headers` replaces the call's, dropping the
  `ai-sdk-agent/workflow` user-agent suffix.
- **Am I affected?** `PrepareStep` hooks that change a value for one step
  only.
- **What to do:** set the value back on later steps:

  ```go
  hook := func(_ context.Context, o workflow.LanguageModelCallOptions) (workflow.LanguageModelCallOptions, error) {
  	if o.StepNumber == 0 {
  		t := 0.2
  		o.Temperature = &t
  	} else {
  		o.Temperature = nil // v0.6: undo the step-0 override explicitly
  	}
  	return o, nil
  }
  _ = hook
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### A failed WorkflowAgent Stream run fires OnEnd {#workflow-stream-failed-onend}

- **What changed:** a failed (not aborted) `Stream` run fires `OnEnd` after
  `OnError`, with the steps completed so far, as TS stream mode. When the run
  failed by a thrown error (a model call that failed to open, a failed
  `PrepareStep`), its `Messages` are the input messages only. A run whose
  context is already cancelled fires `OnStart` before `OnAbort`. The `OnEnd`
  messages follow a `PrepareStep` messages or instructions override.
- **Am I affected?** `OnEnd:` on `WorkflowAgent.Stream` treated as success.
- **What to do:** check for a failure in `OnError` (or the stream's error)
  before you treat `OnEnd` as success.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent Stream usage is input + output summed over the steps {#workflow-stream-usage}

- **What changed:** in `WorkflowAgent.Stream` / `StreamWithOptions`, the
  `OnEnd` `Usage` and `TotalUsage`, the result's `Usage()` / `TotalUsage()`
  and the telemetry end usage are TS stream mode's `aggregateUsage`: input and
  output tokens summed over the steps with a missing count as 0, and
  `TotalTokens` = input + output. The three counts are always set (v0.5: nil
  when no step reported them), and a provider's own total is not used. A
  transformed `finish` part does not change the total. Token details are still
  summed. `Generate` is unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** `OnEnd:` on `WorkflowAgent.Stream` or `.Usage()` on a
  `WorkflowStreamResult` that checks `InputTokens == nil` or reads
  `TotalTokens`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you rely on nil counts; check for 0
  instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The WorkflowAgent Stream root span ends with the run {#workflow-stream-root-span-end}

- **What changed:** the `ai.workflowAgent.stream` root of a `Stream` run ends
  when the run ends, as TS reports its root `onEnd`: for a completed run with
  the last step's text, finish reason and the stream-mode usage; for a failed
  run with the error and then the end event (only when a step completed); for
  a run cancelled mid-stream with an abort. v0.5 ended the root, with no
  usage, as soon as the stream opened, so later failures never reached it.
  Every root event of a `Stream` run (start, end, error, abort, and the
  resumed approval tool events) now carries the run's `CallID`, as TS
  `callId`, so each registered integration ends its own root span; v0.5 sent
  an empty `CallID`, and with two integrations the first one's root span
  never ended.
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry integrations or dashboards that read the
  `Stream` root span's duration, status or usage, or that count
  `OnEnd`/`OnError` events per call; custom integrations that key on an empty
  `TelemetryStartEvent.CallID` for `ai.workflowAgent.stream`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the root ending at stream
  start.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### WorkflowAgent.Stream has no default step limit {#workflow-stream-no-step-limit}

- **What changed:** `NewWorkflowAgent` sets `IsStepCount(20)` only for
  `Generate`. `Stream` is unbounded unless `StopWhen` is set, as TS defaults
  only generate to 20 steps. v0.5 applied the 20-step default to both.
- **Am I affected?** `WorkflowAgent.Stream` without `StopWhen` on the agent
  or the call.
- **What to do:** set `StopWhen`:

  ```go
  opts := workflow.WorkflowStreamOptions{StopWhen: []ai.StopCondition{ai.IsStepCount(20)}}
  _ = opts
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### The WorkflowAgent root span is ai.workflowAgent.stream for Generate too {#workflow-root-span}

- **What changed:** TS uses that operation ID for both methods. v0.5 named the
  generate root `ai.workflowAgent.generate`. The root now opens after the
  approved tools from the message history ran, so those resumed tool calls get
  no tool span, and an error before that point reports no telemetry.
- **Am I affected?** Queries or dashboards that match
  `ai.workflowAgent.generate`.
- **What to do:** match `ai.workflowAgent.stream`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### WorkflowAgent answers a provider tool call that got no result {#workflow-missing-provider-result}

- **What changed:** when a provider-executed tool call gets no result in the
  model's response, `WorkflowAgent` adds an empty text tool result
  (`Output.Type == "text"`, empty value) after the call in the step's
  assistant message and logs a warning, as TS `resolveProviderToolResult`.
  In v0.5 the call kept a pending result with no output. The run still ends
  after that step unless the step also had client tool calls or a
  deferred-result tool is pending (TS `modelCallIterator`), in `Generate`
  and `Stream` (review round 70).
- **Am I affected?** `WorkflowResult.ResponseMessages`,
  `Steps[i].Response.Messages` or `ToolResults` read after a
  provider-executed call (`ProviderExecuted: true`) the provider did not
  answer; the "did not receive a result from the stream" warning.
- **What to do:** no action unless you treated a provider tool result with
  no output as "not answered".

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowRunMultiplexer ends on a finish chunk {#workflow-multiplexer-finish}

- **What changed:** `WorkflowRunMultiplexer` no longer treats an SSE event
  named `done` as the end of a run. It ends on a `{"type":"finish"}` chunk, as
  TS `WorkflowChatTransport`. It returns a failed POST's error before it calls
  `OnChatSendMessage`, which now sees only successful sends.
- **Am I affected?** Servers that end a run with a `done` event and no
  `finish` chunk, or `OnChatSendMessage` callbacks that expect failed sends.
- **What to do:** end each run with a `finish` chunk; handle failed sends from
  the returned error.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### WorkflowAgent resumed-tool telemetry carries the tool context {#workflow-resumed-tool-telemetry-context}

- **What changed:** the telemetry tool start and end events of a tool that
  `WorkflowAgent` runs after an approval carry `ToolContext`: the tool's
  `ToolsContext` entry filtered by `Telemetry.IncludeToolsContext` (an empty
  map when nothing is included). v0.5 sent nil. TS telemetry dispatcher
  `filterToolContext` (review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry integrations that read
  `TelemetryToolCallStartEvent.ToolContext` /
  `TelemetryToolCallFinishEvent.ToolContext` and treat nil as "WorkflowAgent
  resume".
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent `PrepareCall` sees the agent's generation settings {#workflow-preparecall-agent-settings}

- **What changed:** v0.5 handed `PrepareCall` the call's `MaxRetries` when
  the call set one; v0.6 hands it the agent's generation settings
  (`Temperature`, `MaxTokens`, `TopP`, `TopK`, penalties, `StopSequences`,
  `Seed`, `MaxRetries`, `Reasoning`, `Headers`, `ProviderOptions`), as TS
  prepareInvocation does (`{...this.generationSettings}`). Settings given on
  the call still win over the hook's result for the model call (review
  round 73).
- **Am I affected?** `WorkflowAgent.PrepareCall` hooks that read
  `LanguageModelCallOptions.MaxRetries` (or the other settings) while also
  passing them per call in `WorkflowGenerateOptions` / `WorkflowStreamOptions`.
- **What to do:** no action unless the hook relied on seeing the per-call
  value; read it from your own call options.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Resolved provider tools are provider-executed only when marked {#workflow-resolve-provider-executed}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ResolveSerializableTools` made every
  `type: "provider"` def provider-executed; v0.6 does so only when the def
  has `isProviderExecuted` / `providerExecuted`, and keeps
  `SupportsDeferredResults` only for those, as TS `serializable-schema.ts`
  resolve does. A client-executed provider tool (for example Anthropic bash,
  computer, text editor or memory) keeps running its `Execute` after a
  workflow round trip (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** `SerializableToolDef` values with `Type: "provider"` and
  no `IsProviderExecuted`, including v0.5 defs serialized from tools whose
  `ProviderOptions` carried `"type": "provider"`.
- **What to do:** set `IsProviderExecuted: true` on defs of provider-executed
  tools you build by hand (`SerializeToolSet` already does).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent Stream leaves reasoning files out of the conversation {#workflow-stream-reasoning-files}

- **What changed:** in `WorkflowAgent.Stream` / `StreamWithOptions`, a step's
  reasoning-file parts are no longer in the step's `Response.Messages`, the
  next step's prompt or the `OnEnd` event's `Messages`; the step's `Content`
  and `ReasoningFiles()` keep them. TS WorkflowAgent's stream mode builds the
  assistant message without them (model-call-iterator.ts:
  `case 'reasoning-file': if (mode !== 'generate') break;`). v0.5 sent them
  to the model again. `Generate` keeps them, as TS's generate mode does
  (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** `WorkflowAgent` stream runs with models that emit
  reasoning files (`ChunkTypeReasoningFile`), and code that reads them from
  step `Response.Messages` or `OnEnd` `Messages`.
- **What to do:** read reasoning files from `StepResult.ReasoningFiles()`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### SerializeToolSet resolves DescriptionFunc {#workflow-serialize-description-func}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `workflow.SerializeToolSet` wrote a tool's static
  `Description` and ignored `DescriptionFunc`; v0.6 calls `DescriptionFunc`
  (with no tools context and no sandbox) and writes its result, as TS
  `serializeToolSet` resolves a function description. The new
  `SerializeToolSetWithOptions(ctx, tools, SerializeToolSetOptions{ToolsContext, ExperimentalSandbox})`
  passes the tool's context entry and the sandbox (review round 74).
- **Am I affected?** Tools with a `DescriptionFunc` passed to
  `SerializeToolSet`.
- **What to do:** no action unless the function needs its context; then call
  `SerializeToolSetWithOptions`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change
