#### Resolved dynamic tools carry no title, metadata or strict {#workflow-resolve-dynamic-tools}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ResolveSerializableTools` resolved a
  `Type: "dynamic"` def as a plain tool with its `Title` and `Strict`; v0.6
  resolves it as a dynamic tool (`Type: "dynamic"`) with only its
  description, input examples, provider options and schema, as TS
  `resolveSerializableTools` builds `dynamicTool({...})`. Function tools keep
  title, metadata and strict (review round 74).
- **Am I affected?** `SerializableToolDef` values with `Type: "dynamic"` that
  set `Title`, `Metadata` or `Strict`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on those fields; set them on
  the resolved tool yourself.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent stream opens one telemetry root {#workflow-stream-single-telemetry-root}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `WorkflowAgent.Stream` / `StreamWithOptions`
  opened the `ai.workflowAgent.stream` root and then a second root,
  `ai.streamText`, for the delegated stream, so integrations received two
  `OnStart`/`OnFinish` (and two `OnError`/`OnAbort`) per run and the
  OpenTelemetry step spans nested under the inner root. v0.6 opens only the
  workflow root and reports the steps, model calls and tools under its call
  ID, as TS `WorkflowAgent.stream` does. `LegacyOpenTelemetry` names the
  step spans after the root operation, as TS `state.operationId`:
  `ai.generateText.doGenerate` under the workflow root (v0.5:
  `ai.streamText.doStream` under the inner root), without the
  `ai.response.msToFirstChunk` stream timing (review round 75).
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry integrations or span queries that read the
  `ai.streamText` root of a WorkflowAgent stream run, count `OnStart` /
  `OnFinish` events per run, or match `ai.streamText.doStream` step spans of
  a WorkflowAgent stream run.
- **What to do:** read the `ai.workflowAgent.stream` root and its
  `ai.generateText.doGenerate` step spans instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent hook fields left nil keep their value {#workflow-prepare-hook-nil-fields}

- **What changed:** v0.5: a `PrepareCall` / `PrepareStep` hook that returned
  a field as nil cleared it in generate mode (temperature, topP, topK,
  maxTokens, penalties, stop sequences, seed, headers, reasoning, provider
  options), and both modes dropped nil headers; in stream mode a
  `PrepareStep` returning `ActiveTools: []string{}` still sent every tool.
  v0.6: a nil field keeps its current value and an empty non-nil
  `ActiveTools` sends no tools, as TS prepareCall / prepareStep apply only
  defined keys (`mergePrepareStepGenerationSettings`) and filter by
  `activeTools: []` (review round 76). `Tools`, `ExperimentalSandbox`,
  `CallOptions` and `CustomData` follow the same rule: a hook that returns
  them as nil keeps the current tools, sandbox, call options and custom data
  (TS prepareCall / prepareStep have no `tools` key; prepareStep applies
  `experimental_sandbox ?? sandbox`). Return an empty, non-nil `Tools` slice
  to send no tools (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** `WorkflowAgent.PrepareCall`, `WorkflowAgent.PrepareStep`,
  `WorkflowStreamOptions.PrepareStep`, `WorkflowGenerateOptions.PrepareStep`
  hooks that set a `LanguageModelCallOptions` field to nil to clear it
  (including `Tools`, `ExperimentalSandbox`, `CallOptions`, `CustomData`), or
  return `ActiveTools: []string{}`.
- **What to do:** no action unless a hook clears a setting with nil. To drop
  a setting now, give it an explicit value (for example
  `o.Temperature = &zero`); to send no tools, set
  `o.ActiveTools = []string{}`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Rebuilt workflow tools validate their input like TS {#workflow-resolved-tool-input-errors}

- **What changed:** v0.5: core validated a tool from
  `ResolveSerializableTools` against its `Parameters` map with core's
  validator: it filled schema defaults first (so `Execute` got them) and
  worded failures like `$.city: required: property is missing`. v0.6: the
  tool carries `ValidateInput` (new `types.Tool` field) set to the Ajv-worded
  validator TS `resolveSerializableTools` attaches, so the tool error reads
  like TS (`data is missing required property "city", data has unexpected
  property "town"`, wrapped as `InvalidToolInputError` with a
  `TypeValidationError` cause). Like TS Ajv (no `useDefaults`), it fills no
  defaults: a missing required property with a schema default fails, and
  `Execute` gets the input as the model sent it. Provider tools and defs
  with `HasOwnValidator` are not validated there (TS `jsonSchema()` without
  a validator). `Parameters` stays the map, and the exported
  `ValidateSerializableToolInput` keeps its v0.5 behavior (defaults filled,
  `workflow: invalid input for ...` text) (owner decision 2026-11-01,
  review round 76). A definition with `Type: "provider"` and no `ID` (rebuilt
  as a function tool) also passes `ValidateInput` unvalidated, as TS skips
  validation for every provider definition (review round 77). Schema `pattern` and `patternProperties` keys are matched with JavaScript u-mode semantics like Ajv: `\s` / `\S` include the Unicode spaces and `\v`, `.` does not match `\r`, U+2028 or U+2029, and `\uXXXX` / `\u{...}` escapes are code points (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Callers of `ResolveSerializableTools` that match tool
  error text, rely on schema defaults reaching `Execute`, or rely on core
  rejecting input for `HasOwnValidator` or provider defs.
- **What to do:** no action unless one of those applies. Match the Ajv text
  or use `errors.As` with `*ai.InvalidToolInputError`; apply defaults in the
  tool, or call `workflow.ValidateSerializableToolInput` yourself in
  `Execute`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent client tool results carry no provider metadata {#workflow-client-tool-result-metadata}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `WorkflowAgent` (both modes) copied a client tool
  call's provider metadata onto its result: the step `Content` tool-result /
  tool-error `ProviderMetadata`, `ToolResults[i].ProviderMetadata` and the
  next prompt's tool-result `ProviderOptions`. v0.6 leaves them empty for
  client (not provider-executed) tools, as TS WorkflowAgent `executeTool`
  builds results without `providerOptions`. The tool call keeps its
  metadata, and `GenerateText` / `StreamText` still copy it, as TS core
  (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads those fields on results of a
  `WorkflowAgent` run.
- **What to do:** no action unless you read them; read the tool call's
  `ProviderMetadata` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent stream runs tool input callbacks after the model step {#workflow-stream-tool-input-callbacks}

- **What changed:** under `WorkflowAgent.Stream` / `StreamWithOptions`,
  `Tool.OnInputStart`, `OnInputDelta` and `OnInputAvailable` fire after the
  step's model stream ended, in arrival order, before approvals and tool
  execution, and only for calls whose stream had a `tool-input-start`
  (`OnInputAvailable` is skipped for an invalid call), as TS WorkflowAgent
  replays its recorded tool input lifecycle
  (`invokeToolInputLifecycleCallbacks`). v0.5 fired them while the stream was
  read. The UI and full-stream chunks keep their timing, and plain
  `StreamText` is unchanged (review round 76). The callbacks' `Messages` are
  the step's model prompt with the system message first (TS
  `stepInputMessages`); v0.5 gave the step's input messages without the
  system message (review round 77; see
  [WorkflowAgent tool input callbacks](#workflow-input-callbacks-conversation-prompt)).
- **Am I affected?** Tool input callbacks under `WorkflowAgent.Stream` that
  rely on running mid-stream, or on `OnInputAvailable` for calls streamed
  without a `tool-input-start`.
- **What to do:** no action unless a callback relied on that timing.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent tools see the conversation prompt {#workflow-tool-messages-conversation-prompt}

- **What changed:** under `WorkflowAgent` (both modes),
  `ToolExecutionOptions.Messages` and the approval function's messages are
  the step's model prompt (the instructions as a system message first) plus
  the step's assistant message, and `OnToolExecutionStart` /
  `OnToolExecutionEnd` `Messages` are that prompt without the assistant
  message, as TS WorkflowAgent `executeToolWithCallbacks(iterMessages)` /
  `getToolCallbackMessages`. v0.5 passed the step's input messages
  (`[user]`), as `GenerateText` / `StreamText` still do (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** WorkflowAgent tools or approval functions that index
  into `Messages` (for example `Messages[0]` as the user message), and
  tool execution callbacks that count messages.
- **What to do:** no action unless you index into `Messages`; find messages
  by role instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent tool input callbacks get the conversation prompt {#workflow-input-callbacks-conversation-prompt}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `WorkflowAgent` (both modes) passed the step
  messages (no system message) to `Tool.OnInputStart`, `OnInputDelta` and
  `OnInputAvailable`. v0.6 passes the step's conversation prompt, system
  message first, as the tool start event gets, as TS WorkflowAgent
  `invokeToolInputLifecycleCallbacks({messages: stepInputMessages})`.
  `GenerateText`, `StreamText` and `ToolLoopAgent` are unchanged (review
  round 77).
- **Am I affected?** `types.Tool.OnInputStart`, `OnInputDelta`,
  `OnInputAvailable` reading `Messages` under `WorkflowAgent.Generate` or
  `WorkflowAgent.Stream` with instructions.
- **What to do:** no action unless you index into `Messages`; skip a leading
  `RoleSystem` message.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### WorkflowAgent empty provider results carry no provider metadata {#workflow-empty-provider-result-metadata}

- **What changed:** when a provider-executed tool call gets no result from
  the stream, `WorkflowAgent` answers it with an empty text result (as TS
  does). v0.5 copied the call's provider metadata onto that result, so the
  step content, the stream's tool-result part and the tool message sent
  back to the model carried it as `providerOptions`. v0.6 sends it without
  metadata, as TS `resolveProviderToolResult` (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** `WorkflowAgent` runs with provider-executed tools
  (`types.Tool.ProviderExecuted`) whose results can be missing, if you read
  `ProviderMetadata` on those results or depend on it in the next request.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the copied metadata.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Approval continuations use the server tool's metadata {#approval-continuation-server-tool-metadata}

- **What changed:** v0.5 validated an approved tool call from message history with the `ToolMetadata`, `Dynamic` and `Title` the history carried. v0.6 replaces them with the current server tool's `Metadata`, `Type == "dynamic"` and `Title` before signature verification, input refinement and the `ToolApproval` policy (TS `857ca45151`): history can no longer add, remove or forge them. A tool that no longer exists gets none.
- **Am I affected?** `ToolApproval` policies or `RefineToolInput` functions that read `ToolCall.ToolMetadata`, `ToolCall.Dynamic` or `ToolCall.Title` on a resumed approval; `ValidateApprovedToolApprovals` callers that set those fields on `CollectedToolApproval.ToolCall`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on history-supplied metadata. Put the metadata on the tool (`types.Tool{Metadata: ..., Title: ..., Type: types.ToolTypeDynamic}`).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An empty approval reason is kept {#empty-approval-reason}

- **What changed:** v0.5 treated an empty `Reason` on `types.ToolApprovalResponseContent` and on an execution-denied `types.ToolResultOutput` as absent, so providers sent their default denial text (`Tool call execution denied.`). v0.6 keeps an empty reason that was given, as TS `reason ?? default`: JSON `"reason": ""` decodes with the new `ReasonSet`, a policy `Reason` pointing to `""` sets it, a UI approval with `reason: ""` keeps it, and every provider converter (OpenAI chat and Responses, the OpenAI-compatible family, Anthropic, Bedrock, Google and Interactions, Perplexity, DeepSeek, xAI, Open Responses, Gateway) sends `""`. JSON encoding writes `"reason": ""` for such a reason; telemetry omits an absent reason (`{"denied":true}`) and keeps a given empty one (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Approval responses or UI messages with `"reason": ""`; `ToolApproval` functions returning `Reason` pointing to `""`; code comparing the marshaled JSON of these parts or the telemetry `response` of denied tools.
- **What to do:** No action unless you want the default text for an empty reason: send no reason (omit the key, or `Reason: nil` in the policy result).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ExperimentalDecide checks the model's specification version {#decide-model-version}

- **What changed:** A decision model whose `SpecificationVersion()` is not `"v4"` now fails with `*ai.UnsupportedModelVersionError` before any call (TS `resolveDecisionModel`). Built-in decision models report `v4` (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** A custom `provider.DecisionModel` / `EvaluationModel` that returns another version.
- **What to do:** Return `"v4"` from `SpecificationVersion()`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ToolLoopAgent checks the prompt after the call options and PrepareCall {#agent-prompt-after-prepare-call}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ToolLoopAgent.Generate` / `Stream` rejected a call without a prompt before validating `CallOptions` or running `PrepareCall`. v0.6 follows TS: call options first, then `PrepareCall`, then the prompt of the prepared call. A `PrepareCall` that sets `Messages` makes a prompt-less call valid, and invalid call options are reported before a missing prompt. The error types are unchanged (`*InvalidPromptError` with the `*ValidationError` cause) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Calls to `Generate` / `Stream` with no prompt that expect the prompt error although a `PrepareCall` supplies messages, or tests of the error order with invalid `CallOptions`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The agent loop lists invalid tool call errors first {#agent-invalid-tool-errors-first}

- **What changed:** As in GenerateText ([details](#generate-text-invalid-tool-errors-first)), `GenerateAgent`, `Execute`, `ExecuteWithMessages` and WorkflowAgent `Generate` now list invalid-call tool errors before the executed tool results in `StepResult.Content` (TS generateText and TS WorkflowAgent's generate mode) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads tool parts of `AgentResult.Steps[i].Content` by position.
- **What to do:** Match parts by `ToolCallID`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The agent loop keeps the user-approval reason {#agent-user-approval-reason}

- **What changed:** The `ToolLoopAgent` result loop (`GenerateAgent`, `Execute`, `ExecuteWithMessages`, WorkflowAgent `Generate`) dropped the reason of a `ToolApprovalResult{Status: ToolApprovalStatusUserApproval, Reason: ...}`. v0.6 puts it on `types.ToolApprovalRequestContent.Reason` in the step content and the assistant response message, as TS does (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `ToolApprovalRequestContent.Reason` from `AgentResult.Steps[i].Content` or response messages and expects it empty.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The agent loop parses JSON output from the provider's structured output text {#agent-structured-output-text}

- **What changed:** `GenerateAgent` / `Execute` parsed `AgentConfig.Output` from the final step's text. v0.6 parses a JSON output from the last model response's `StructuredOutputText` when the provider sets it (OpenAI Responses final answer), as TS generateText and `ai.GenerateText` do; `AgentResult.Text` is unchanged. WorkflowAgent `Generate` still parses the step text, as TS WorkflowAgent does (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `AgentConfig.Output` / `AgentGenerateOptions.Output` with an object, array, choice or JSON output and a provider that reports `GenerateResult.StructuredOutputText`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### WorkflowAgent approval resume error text {#workflow-approval-error-text}

- **What changed:** A failing approved tool was answered with `err.Error()`. v0.6 sends `"Error: <message>"`, a failed revalidation the named error (`"AI_InvalidToolInputError: ..."`) and a missing or forged signature `"AI_InvalidToolApprovalSignatureError: Tool approval signature verification failed ..."`, in the model prompt and the streamed tool output, as TS `getErrorMessage(error)` (error.toString()). `InvalidToolApprovalSignatureError` gains `ErrorName()` (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching the error-text tool result or `tool-output-available` output of a resumed approval in `workflow.WorkflowAgent`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match that text; match the suffix.
- **Kind:** error text only

### WorkflowAgent returns step errors without the step prefix {#workflow-agent-errors-unwrapped}

- **What changed:** A `WorkflowAgent` run returned its model, approval or abort error wrapped as `step N failed: <error>`. v0.6 returns the error itself, as TS workflow-agent.ts; `Execute` and `GenerateAgent` of the legacy agent keep the prefix (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** String matching on `step ` / `failed:` in `WorkflowAgent` errors.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As` / `errors.Is` on the returned error.
- **Kind:** error text only

### LangSmithDeploymentTransport follows the TS LangGraph client {#langsmith-transport-ts-client}

- **What changed:** `LangSmithDeploymentTransport` sent no `x-api-key` without `APIKey`, failed on an empty URL, trimmed every trailing slash, never retried, truncated HTTP error bodies and ignored `__interrupt__: null`. v0.6 matches langgraph-sdk 1.14.0: `x-api-key` from `LANGGRAPH_API_KEY` / `LANGSMITH_API_KEY` / `LANGCHAIN_API_KEY` (trimmed, one quote pair removed), an empty URL is `http://localhost:8123`, one trailing slash is trimmed, a connection failure is retried up to 4 times and then is a `*LangGraphConnectionError`, non-2xx is a `*LangGraphHTTPError` (`HTTP <status>: <body>`), a non-event-stream response fails, and `__interrupt__: null` is a `*GraphInterruptError` (review round 91). Round 93: the request goes to the URL's WHATWG serialization (`new URL(url + '/runs/stream')`: host lowercased, dot segments resolved, path percent-encoded), and a URL that does not parse (for example one without a scheme) fails with `Invalid URL` before any request instead of a `*LangGraphConnectionError`. Round 94: the run stream body matches RemoteGraph. `config.recursion_limit` is 25, messages carry no `type` key (the type travels as `role`), and keys follow TS order. A configured `Accept` header is sent as given. A 204 or 205 response fails with `Expected response body from stream endpoint`.
- **Am I affected?** `LangSmithDeploymentTransportOptions`, `HTTP <code>:` matching, those environment variables set in the process.
- **What to do:** Set `APIKey` explicitly if the environment variables hold other keys; match the new error types with `errors.As`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Struct-literal WorkflowAgent stream has no 20-step default {#workflow-literal-stream-steps}

- **What changed:** A `WorkflowAgent` built as a struct literal with no `StopWhen` stopped `Stream` after 20 steps. v0.6 streams without a step limit, as TS workflow-agent.ts; generate keeps the 20-step default (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Struct-literal `WorkflowAgent` values without `StopWhen` used with `Stream`.
- **What to do:** Set `StopWhen` if you relied on the limit.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### WorkflowAgent stream skips OnStepEnd for a paused step {#workflow-stream-paused-step-end}

- **What changed:** In `StreamWithOptions`, `OnStepEnd` / `OnStepFinish` ran for the step that stops the run on a client-side tool (no `Execute`) or a tool approval. v0.6 skips it there in stream mode, as TS workflow-agent.ts; generate mode still calls it, and telemetry still records the step end (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** WorkflowAgent stream callers counting `OnStepEnd` calls or reading the paused step from it.
- **What to do:** Read the paused step from the stream result's `Steps()` or from `OnEnd`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### WorkflowAgent OnAbort gets full step results {#workflow-onabort-full-steps}

- **What changed:** When a `WorkflowAgent` run was cancelled after one or more steps finished, `OnAbort` (and the abort telemetry) received those steps without content, sources, provider metadata, request, response, performance data, steering messages or contexts. v0.6 passes the full `types.StepResult` of each finished step, as TS `onAbort({steps})` receives the finished `StepResult`s (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** `OnAbort` callbacks of a `workflow.WorkflowAgent` that read the steps.
- **What to do:** No action; the fields are now filled.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## UI message streams {#ui-message-streams}

### UI message streams fail on an out-of-sequence chunk {#ui-out-of-sequence}

- **What changed:** when `CreateUIMessageStreamWithOptions` or
  `ToUIMessageStream` has an `OnEnd` or `OnStepEnd` callback, a chunk the
  message state cannot apply (a `text-delta` without its `text-start`, a tool
  result for an unknown tool call) ends the stream, as TS
  `processUIMessageStream`. The error channel carries a
  `*ai.UIMessageStreamError`, later chunks are not delivered, and `OnEnd`
  reports a failed outcome with that error. v0.5 passed the error to
  `OnError` and kept streaming. Without those callbacks the chunks are not
  checked, and `OnError` is no longer called for them, nor twice for an error
  chunk.
- **Am I affected?** Custom chunk producers with `OnEnd` / `OnStepEnd`.
- **What to do:** emit each part's start chunk before its deltas, and treat a
  `*ai.UIMessageStreamError` on the error channel as the stream's failure:

  ```go
  var uiErr *ai.UIMessageStreamError
  if errors.As(err, &uiErr) {
  	// a chunk arrived out of sequence
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An out-of-range KeepAliveMs is an error {#keep-alive-range}

- **What changed:** a `UIMessageStreamResponseInit.KeepAliveMs` that points to
  zero, a negative duration or more than 2147483647ms makes the
  `Create*` / `Pipe*` UI message stream response helpers return
  `keepAliveMs must be a positive finite timer duration no greater than
  2147483647` before writing anything, as TS `createSseStreamWithKeepAlive`
  throws. v0.5 turned keep-alive off for zero or negative values. A nil
  `KeepAliveMs` is unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** `KeepAliveMs` set to a pointer to 0 or a negative value
  to turn keep-alive off.
- **What to do:** leave it nil:

  ```go
  init := &ai.UIMessageStreamResponseInit{} // KeepAliveMs nil: no keep-alive
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### UI message state follows TS for four chunk edge cases {#ui-message-state-edge-cases}

- **What changed:** the message that `OnEnd`, `OnStepEnd` and
  `ReadUIMessages` build keeps a tool part's own `title` when a
  `tool-output-available` or `tool-output-error` chunk carries one (TS passes
  the invocation's title), keeps an empty `id` on a reasoning part, keeps the
  earlier finish reason when a `finish` chunk has `"finishReason": null`, and
  leaves `input` out of a tool part that never received one (v0.5 set it to
  JSON `null`): a streaming tool part whose partial input cannot be parsed, a
  `tool-output-available` or `tool-output-error` chunk for such a part, and a
  `tool-input-available` or `tool-input-error` chunk with no `input` key
  (review round 68). TS `processUIMessageStream` does each of these.
- **Am I affected?** Only if you write such chunks yourself (through
  `UIMessageStreamWriter.Write` or a merged stream) and read the built
  message's parts, for example `part["title"]` or `part["input"] == nil`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on a chunk title replacing the
  part's title or on a `null` partial input. Check for a missing key:

  ```go
  func hasInput(part map[string]interface{}) bool {
  	_, ok := part["input"] // v0.5: present with a nil value
  	return ok
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ToUIMessageStream OnEnd reports the finish chunk's reason {#ui-on-end-finish-reason}

- **What changed:** the `finishReason` that `ToUIMessageStream` (and
  `StreamTextResult.ToUIMessageStream`) passes to `OnEnd`/`OnFinish` is the
  reason of the `finish` chunk the stream sent. With `SendFinish: false`, or
  when the stream fails before its `finish` chunk, it is now empty
  (`types.FinishReason("")`); v0.5 passed the source's last step or call
  reason. TS `handleUIMessageStreamFinish` passes `state.finishReason`,
  which only a `finish` chunk sets. The value is still a `types.FinishReason`.
- **Am I affected?** `OnEnd:` or `OnFinish:` callbacks on a UI message
  stream with `SendFinish: false` (for example a stream you merge into
  another) that read `e["finishReason"]`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you do that. Read the reason from the
  result instead:

  ```go
  func finishReason(result *ai.StreamTextResult) types.FinishReason {
  	return result.FinishReason() // v0.5: OnEnd's e["finishReason"] with SendFinish false
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### One error chunk for a StreamText run that ends with an error part {#ui-single-error-chunk}

- **What changed:** when a `StreamText` run ends with an `error` part (the
  model call fails to open, the run fails before its first model stream, a
  later step fails to prepare or open, or the model stream ends without
  output), `StreamTextResult.ToUIMessageStream`, `CreateUIMessageStream` and
  the UI message stream response helpers write that part's `error` chunk
  only (and, for a later step that fails to start, no `finish` chunk after
  it; see [UI finish and abort](#ui-finish-and-abort-chunks)). v0.5 wrote a second `error` chunk for the same error when the stream
  closed with it (review round 69). TS `streamText` closes its stream
  normally after such a part, so `toUIMessageStream` writes one. The error
  channel still carries the error, and `OnEnd` still reports a failed
  outcome.
- **Am I affected?** Clients or tests that count `"type":"error"` chunks in a
  UI message stream, or expect two of them for one failed run.
- **What to do:** no action unless you count error chunks. Expect one:

  ```go
  func errorChunks(chunks <-chan ai.UIMessageChunk) int {
  	n := 0
  	for c := range chunks {
  		if c["type"] == "error" {
  			n++ // v0.5: two for a run that ended with an error part
  		}
  	}
  	return n
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI message streams end like the source stream {#ui-finish-and-abort-chunks}

- **What changed:** `StreamTextResult.ToUIMessageStream`,
  `CreateUIMessageStream`, `ToUIMessageStream` and the UI response helpers
  write a `finish` chunk only when a StreamText source has its call-level
  `finish` part (a later step that fails to start ends with its `error`
  chunk). A run that ends with an `abort` part writes one `abort` chunk.
  v0.5 wrote a second one when the stream then closed with the abort error.
  A source that ends after an `abort` part gets nothing more (v0.5 added an
  error chunk, `finish-step` and `finish`), and its `OnEnd` outcome is
  `aborted`. TS writes `finish` for a `finish` part only and records an
  `abort` part as the aborted outcome (review round 70). A raw provider
  stream passed to `ToUIMessageStream` still gets a synthesized `finish`.
- **Am I affected?** Clients or tests that expect a `"type":"finish"` chunk
  after an error, or count `"type":"abort"` chunks.
- **What to do:** no action unless you do that. Treat `error` or `abort` as
  the end:

  ```go
  func ended(c ai.UIMessageChunk) bool {
  	switch c["type"] {
  	case "finish", "error", "abort":
  		return true // v0.5: a finish chunk followed every StreamText run
  	}
  	return false
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI file chunks carry the file's provider metadata {#ui-file-provider-metadata}

- **What changed:** the UI `file` and `reasoning-file` chunks now include
  `providerMetadata` when it is set only on
  `GeneratedFileContent.ProviderMetadata` /
  `ReasoningFileContent.ProviderMetadata` (TS `part.providerMetadata`). v0.5
  dropped it unless the stream chunk also carried it (review round 70).
- **Am I affected?** Clients that compare UI `file` chunks exactly, with
  providers that put metadata only on the file (for example openresponses).
- **What to do:** no action unless you compare file chunks exactly. Read the
  field when present:

  ```go
  func fileMetadata(c ai.UIMessageChunk) interface{} {
  	return c["providerMetadata"] // v0.5: missing for file-only metadata
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ToUIMessageStream maps start-led part sequences one to one {#ui-start-part-one-to-one}

- **What changed:** when the source given to `ai.ToUIMessageStream` starts
  with a `provider.ChunkTypeStart` part (a `StreamText`, harness or
  `NewStreamTextResultFromParts` stream, or your own part slice), each part
  now maps to its UI chunk as in TS `toUIMessageStream`. The start part
  writes the `start` chunk and the finish part writes the `finish` chunk
  where it occurs. Nothing is added at the end: no closing `text-end` /
  `reasoning-end`, no `finish-step` for the `finish` part, no `finish` when
  the source has no finish part, and no incomplete-stream `error` chunk. v0.5
  framed these sources like raw provider streams. `NewStreamTextResultFromParts`
  streams (`Stream()`, `FullStream()`) now begin with a `ChunkTypeStart` part,
  as TS `HarnessStreamTextResult` does. A raw provider stream (no leading
  start part) keeps its framing, but its `start` chunk is now written after
  the first source chunk arrives (owner ruling 2026-10-30, review round 71).
- **Am I affected?** `ai.ToUIMessageStream(` on part slices or streams that
  begin with `provider.ChunkTypeStart`; code that reads
  `NewStreamTextResultFromParts(` results' `Stream()` / `FullStream()` and
  expects `ChunkTypeStartStep` first.
- **What to do:** no action unless you rely on the added chunks. Send
  `ChunkTypeFinishStep` and `ChunkTypeFinish` parts yourself, and skip a
  leading start part:

  ```go
  func firstStepPart(s provider.TextStream) (*provider.StreamChunk, error) {
  	for {
  		c, err := s.Next()
  		if err != nil || c.Type != provider.ChunkTypeStart {
  			return c, err
  		}
  	}
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI custom, approval and single-part chunks follow TS {#ui-custom-chunk-metadata}

- **What changed:** a UI `"type":"custom"` chunk now carries the part's
  `providerMetadata` also when the provider set it on `CustomContent` only
  (Anthropic fallback parts, for example). v0.5 dropped it. A
  `"tool-approval-request"` chunk leaves out `inputSchemaInput` when it
  equals the tool call input. `ai.ToUIMessageChunk` reads its part as a
  `StreamText` part, as TS `toUIMessageChunk`: a `ChunkTypeStart` part gives
  the `start` chunk and a `ChunkTypeFinish` part the `finish` chunk (with
  `finishReason` and the message metadata; none with `SendStart` /
  `SendFinish` false). v0.5 returned no chunk for a start part and
  `finish-step` for a finish part (review round 71).
- **Am I affected?** Clients that read `"custom"` or
  `"tool-approval-request"` chunks; calls of `ai.ToUIMessageChunk(` with
  start or finish parts.
- **What to do:** no action unless you depend on the missing field or on the
  old single-part results. Map the step end with `ChunkTypeFinishStep`:

  ```go
  func stepEndChunk() (ai.UIMessageChunk, bool) {
  	// v0.5: ChunkTypeFinish gave "finish-step"; v0.6 gives "finish"
  	return ai.ToUIMessageChunk(provider.StreamChunk{Type: provider.ChunkTypeFinishStep}, ai.UIMessageStreamResultOptions{})
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Approved tool calls send one approval pair in UI streams {#ui-auto-approval-no-duplicate}

- **What changed:** for a tool call that `ToolApproval` approves
  automatically, the UI message stream of `StreamText` now carries one
  `tool-approval-request` / `tool-approval-response` pair before the
  `tool-output-available` chunk, as TS does. v0.5 sent the pair twice: once
  for the approval parts and again when the tool result arrived (review round
  72).
- **Am I affected?** Clients that count or replay `"tool-approval-request"` /
  `"tool-approval-response"` chunks from `ToUIMessageStream` or
  `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponse` with `ToolApproval` set to approved (or a
  function returning `ToolApprovalStatusApproved`).
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the second pair.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### A failing ConsumeSSEStream no longer breaks CreateUIMessageChunksResponse {#ui-consume-sse-error-body}

- **What changed:** the body of the `*http.Response` returned by
  `CreateUIMessageChunksResponse` now ends normally after `data: [DONE]`
  when the `UIMessageStreamResponseInit.ConsumeSSEStream` callback returns
  an error, as TS `createUIMessageStreamResponse` does (it does not await
  `consumeSseStream`). v0.5 ended the body with the callback's error.
  `PipeUIMessageChunksToResponse` still returns that error to its caller
  (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads the body of
  `CreateUIMessageChunksResponse` (or `agent` helpers built on it) and checks
  for the `ConsumeSSEStream` error there.
- **What to do:** check the error inside your `ConsumeSSEStream` callback
  instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### UI message streams keep empty text deltas with provider metadata {#ui-empty-text-delta-metadata}

- **What changed:** `ToUIMessageStream` (and the `StreamTextResult` UI helpers)
  now write a `text-delta` chunk with an empty `delta` when the stream part
  carries provider metadata, such as a Gemini thought signature, and the UI
  message's text part gets that `providerMetadata`, as TS `toUIMessageChunk`
  does. v0.5 dropped every empty text delta (review round 74).
- **Am I affected?** Clients or tests that read UI message stream chunks and
  assume every `text-delta` has a non-empty `delta`, or compare the UI text
  part's `providerMetadata`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you assumed non-empty deltas.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI start chunks carry the response message id as TS does {#ui-start-message-id}

- **What changed:** `CreateUIMessageStreamWithOptions` now adds the response
  message id to a `start` chunk written without a `messageId`: the continued
  assistant message's id when the last `OriginalMessages` entry is an
  assistant message, else the `GenerateMessageID` result (the built-in
  generator by default), also when that is empty. v0.5 forwarded the start
  chunk unchanged. `ToUIMessageStream` and the `StreamTextResult` UI helpers
  now send an empty generated id as `"messageId":""` instead of none, call
  `GenerateMessageID` once instead of twice when it returns an empty id, and
  send no `messageId` when the continued assistant message has no `id` (v0.5
  sent a generated one). TS `handleUIMessageStreamFinish` injects the id
  whenever it is not null or undefined (review round 75).
- **Am I affected?** `CreateUIMessageStreamWithOptions` callers whose
  `Execute` writes `{"type": "start"}` without `messageId`; a
  `GenerateMessageID` that returns `""`; `OriginalMessages` whose last
  assistant message has no `id`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you compare the exact start chunk. Write
  your own `messageId` on the start chunk to choose the id.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI message metadata keeps an object over a number or boolean {#ui-metadata-merge-scalar}

- **What changed:** when a `start`, `finish` or `message-metadata` chunk
  carries a number or boolean `messageMetadata` after object metadata, the
  UI message that `OnEnd` / `OnFinish` and `ReadUIMessageStream` build keeps
  the object, as TS `processUIMessageStream` merges metadata with
  `mergeObjects`. v0.5 replaced the object with the number or boolean. A
  string or array value still replaces it (see
  [Message metadata merge with non-object values](./known-differences.mdx))
  (review round 75). Metadata values that encode as a JSON object (a struct,
  a typed map such as `map[string]string`, `json.RawMessage`, a pointer to a
  struct) are now merged like a `map[string]interface{}`, at the top level
  and nested; v0.5 replaced the existing metadata with them. The merged
  value is a `map[string]interface{}` (review round 76). A metadata value
  that encodes as JSON null (a nil pointer or map, `json.RawMessage("null")`)
  is ignored, as TS ignores `messageMetadata: null`; v0.5 set the message
  metadata to it (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** `MessageMetadata` callbacks or `Execute` writers that
  send a number or boolean `messageMetadata` after object metadata;
  `OriginalMessages` whose last assistant message has typed `metadata`, or
  chunks written with typed `messageMetadata`, read back from `OnEnd` /
  `OnFinish` / `ReadUIMessages`; chunks written with a nil-pointer, nil-map
  or `null` `messageMetadata`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the replacement; send an
  object (`{"value": 5}`) to change the metadata.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A caller header replaces the UI stream default header {#ui-stream-response-header-override}

- **What changed:** the UI message stream response and pipe helpers set
  their default headers (`Content-Type`, `Cache-Control`, `Connection`,
  `X-Vercel-AI-UI-Message-Stream`, `X-Accel-Buffering`) only when the caller
  did not set that header, as TS `prepareHeaders` does. v0.5 added the
  values of `UIMessageStreamResponseInit.Header` next to the default, so for
  example `Cache-Control` was sent twice (`no-cache` and the caller's value).
  `UIMessageStreamResponseInit.Headers` already replaced the default (review
  round 77).
- **Am I affected?** `UIMessageStreamResponseInit{Header: ...}` with one of
  the default header names, passed to
  `CreateUIMessageStreamResponseWithInit`,
  `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponseWithInit`, `PipeUIMessageChunksToResponse`,
  `CreateUIMessageChunksResponse` or
  `StreamTextResult.ToUIMessageStreamResponse`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the default value being
  sent as well.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ConsumeSSEStream no longer holds up the response {#ui-consume-sse-not-awaited}

- **What changed:** the `UIMessageStreamResponseInit.ConsumeSSEStream`
  callback gets a buffered copy of the SSE stream, as TS tees the stream and
  does not await `consumeSseStream`. A callback that reads slowly or late no
  longer blocks the response writes, and the body of the `*http.Response`
  from `CreateUIMessageStreamResponseWithInit`,
  `StreamTextResult.ToUIMessageStreamResponse` or
  `CreateUIMessageChunksResponse` ends after `data: [DONE]` without waiting
  for the callback to return. In v0.5 the body ended only after the callback
  returned. The copy is dropped once the callback returns. The `Pipe*`
  helpers no longer wait for it either: see
  [Pipe helpers do not wait for ConsumeSSEStream](#ui-pipe-consume-sse-not-awaited)
  (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads a response body to EOF and then expects
  `ConsumeSSEStream` to have finished (for example, it reads a buffer the
  callback filled).
- **What to do:** signal completion from the callback (close a channel) and
  wait for it.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Pipe helpers do not wait for ConsumeSSEStream {#ui-pipe-consume-sse-not-awaited}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponse`,
  `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponseWithInit` (and
  `StreamTextResult.PipeUIMessageStreamToResponse`) and
  `PipeUIMessageChunksToResponse` returned only after the
  `UIMessageStreamResponseInit.ConsumeSSEStream` callback returned, and
  returned its error. v0.6 returns once the response is written (after
  `data: [DONE]`), as TS `pipeUIMessageStreamToResponse` does not await
  `consumeSseStream`, and returns only write errors. The callback keeps
  reading its buffered copy in its own goroutine and never touches the
  response writer (owner ruling 2026-11-02, review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Code that checks the error a `Pipe*` helper returns for
  the `ConsumeSSEStream` failure, expects the callback to have finished when
  the helper returns, or uses the request's context (`r.Context()`) or the
  `http.ResponseWriter` inside the callback: the callback can outlive the
  handler, and the request context is cancelled when the handler returns.
- **What to do:** handle the error inside the callback, and give it a
  context that outlives the request:

  ```go
  // Before
  err := ai.PipeUIMessageStreamToResponseWithInit(r.Context(), result, w, &ai.UIMessageStreamResponseInit{
  	ConsumeSSEStream: func(sse io.Reader) error { return saveStream(r.Context(), sse) },
  })
  if err != nil {
  	log.Printf("pipe or save failed: %v", err)
  }

  // After
  saveCtx := context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())
  err := ai.PipeUIMessageStreamToResponseWithInit(r.Context(), result, w, &ai.UIMessageStreamResponseInit{
  	ConsumeSSEStream: func(sse io.Reader) error {
  		if err := saveStream(saveCtx, sse); err != nil {
  			log.Printf("save failed: %v", err)
  		}
  		return nil
  	},
  })
  if err != nil {
  	log.Printf("pipe failed: %v", err)
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ValidateUIMessages rejects a data part without data {#ui-validate-data-part-requires-data}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `ValidateUIMessages` (and `SafeValidateUIMessages`,
  `ValidateUIMessagesForAgent`) accepted a `data-*` part with no `data` key.
  v0.6 rejects it with `Invalid input: expected nonoptional, received
  undefined for data` (in the `Type validation failed` error), as TS
  `uiMessagesSchema` (`data: z.unknown()`) under zod 4 (owner ruling
  2026-11-02). Any present value, `null` included, passes. Tool parts keep
  their v0.5 required `input`/`output` checks (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Stored or client-sent UI messages with
  `{"type": "data-...", ...}` parts that have no `data` key.
- **What to do:** no action unless you store such parts; write `"data":
  null` (or the value) when you build them.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ConsumeSSEStream keeps its copy after a client disconnect {#ui-consume-sse-outlives-disconnect}

- **What changed:** when the response write fails (the client disconnected)
  while a `UIMessageStreamResponseInit.ConsumeSSEStream` callback is still
  reading, the callback's copy keeps receiving every SSE event through
  `data: [DONE]`, including the event whose write failed. The UI message
  stream keeps running until the copy ends or the callback returns, as a TS
  `tee()` cancels its source only when both branches are cancelled. In v0.5
  the copy ended at the failed write and the UI stream was cancelled. The
  Pipe helpers still return the write error right away. The model call
  itself runs until the `StreamText` ctx ends, as in v0.5 (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** Code that passes `ConsumeSSEStream` to
  `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponse(WithInit)`, `PipeUIMessageChunksToResponse`,
  `CreateUIMessageStreamResponseWithInit`, `CreateUIMessageChunksResponse`
  or `StreamTextResult.ToUIMessageStreamResponse`, and relies on a
  disconnect truncating the copy.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on that. To stop the run on a
  disconnect, cancel the `ctx` you passed to `StreamText` (for example
  `r.Context()`), or return from the callback.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A failed UI conversion ends the SSE stream without [DONE] {#ui-failed-stream-no-done}

- **What changed:** when the UI conversion of a `StreamTextResult` fails (a
  callback such as `MessageMetadata`, `OnError` or `OnEnd` panics, or a
  chunk cannot be applied to the message state), the SSE stream ends
  without `data: [DONE]`, as TS's errored stream never reaches
  `JsonToSseTransformStream`'s flush. The `*http.Response` body from
  `CreateUIMessageStreamResponseWithInit` / `ToUIMessageStreamResponse`
  ends with that error instead of EOF, and a `ConsumeSSEStream` copy's
  `Read` returns it. `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponse(WithInit)` returns it as
  before. v0.5 wrote `data: [DONE]` and ended the body and the copy
  normally. The chunk-channel helpers (`PipeUIMessageChunksToResponse`,
  `CreateUIMessageChunksResponse`) cannot see a failure and still end with
  `[DONE]` (review round 79). A cancelled ctx passed to these helpers counts as a failed conversion too: the stream ends without `data: [DONE]`, the body or copy ends with `context.Canceled` (or the deadline error), and `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponse(WithInit)` returns it, as a cancelled TS reader never reaches the flush. Once that ctx is cancelled nothing more is written, so when the same ctx also drives `StreamText`, the abort chunk it produces after the cancellation is never written (before, it was written or dropped at random); pass a separate ctx to the helper (TS UI helpers take no abort signal) to get it (review round 80).
- **Am I affected?** Clients or tests that expect `data: [DONE]` after a
  failed stream, or that treat a body read error as a transport failure;
  `ConsumeSSEStream` callbacks that treat any `Read` error as fatal. Also code that passes a ctx it cancels (a timeout) and expects `[DONE]` or the abort chunk.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on that; handle the read
  error as the stream's failure.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### A null inputSchemaInput or tool input is kept {#ui-null-input-kept}

- **What changed:** `ToolUIPartApproval` and `ToolUIPart` now tell a JSON
  `null` from an absent key (unexported presence flags set by
  `UnmarshalJSON`). An approval's `"inputSchemaInput": null` is validated by
  `ValidateUIMessages` (null against the tool input schema) instead of the
  part's `input`, is written back on marshal, and reaches the model messages:
  `ConvertToModelMessages` sets the new
  `types.ToolApprovalRequestContent.InputSchemaInputSet`, the request part
  marshals `"inputSchemaInput": null`, and `ValidateApprovedToolApprovals`
  validates null. An `output-error` part's `"input": null` is validated (a
  failure turns the part into a `dynamic-tool` part, as for any failed
  output-error input), and a null input on `input-streaming` /
  `output-error` parts is written back. TS checks
  `hasOwnProperty(approval, 'inputSchemaInput')` and
  `toolPart.input !== undefined`. v0.5 read both nulls as absent and dropped
  them (review round 80).
- **Am I affected?** Persisted UI messages with `"inputSchemaInput": null`
  or an `output-error` / `input-streaming` tool part with `"input": null`,
  passed to `ValidateUIMessages` or `ConvertToModelMessages` or
  re-marshalled; code that compares decoded parts with `==` or
  `reflect.DeepEqual` against literals; positional (unkeyed) composite
  literals of `ToolUIPart` or `ToolUIPartApproval`, which no longer compile
  (use keyed fields).
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on those nulls being dropped
  or on the part input being validated instead.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### A cancelled UI ctx stops message processing {#ui-cancel-stops-processing}

- **What changed:** once the ctx passed to `ToUIMessageStream`,
  `CreateUIMessageStream(WithOptions)` or a Pipe/Response helper is
  cancelled, no further chunk updates the message state. A part read while
  the cancellation happened (StreamText's abort part when the same ctx, or
  an abort in the same moment, ends the run) is dropped, so `OnEnd` reports
  `isCancelled: true` with outcome `unknown` instead of `isAborted: true`
  with outcome `aborted` for an abort chunk that was never written. TS
  cancels the pipeline, and `handleUIMessageStreamFinish` calls `onEnd`
  without processing another chunk (review round 81). `ReadUIMessages`
  likewise sends no snapshot once its ctx is cancelled, and
  `StreamTextResult.ChunksContext` sends no chunk after its ctx is cancelled
  (the chunk stays on the shared `Stream()` cursor).
- **Am I affected?** `OnEnd` handlers that read `isAborted`, `isCancelled`
  or `outcome` when the request ctx (or a ctx cancelled with the StreamText
  ctx) is passed to the UI helper; `ReadUIMessages` or `ChunksContext`
  consumers that keep reading after cancelling.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on `isAborted` there. To
  always see the abort, pass the UI helper a ctx that is not cancelled with
  the StreamText ctx (for example `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())`).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ReadUIMessageStream parses SSE like the TS chat transport {#read-ui-message-stream-sse}

- **What changed:** v0.5 scanned `data:` lines one by one. v0.6 frames events like TS
  `parseJsonEventStream` (DefaultChatTransport): one leading byte order mark
  is dropped; LF, CRLF and bare CR end lines; multi-line `data:` fields are
  joined with "\n"; an event is dispatched only at a blank line, so an
  unterminated last event is dropped; invalid UTF-8 becomes one U+FFFD per
  invalid subpart, as TextDecoder does (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** Callers of `ai.ReadUIMessageStream` that read SSE whose last event
  has no closing blank line, that use bare-CR line ends, or that rely on a
  BOM-prefixed stream's first line being skipped.
- **What to do:** No action unless you produce SSE without a blank line after the
  last event; end every event with "\n\n" (the SDK's own writers already do).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### UI tool parts without input, empty denial reasons {#ui-tool-part-absent-input}

- **What changed:** A UI tool part decoded without an `input` key (for example one whose output or approval request arrived before any input) is encoded without it again, and `ReadUIMessages` snapshots leave it out, as TS does (v0.5 wrote `"input": null`). A new dynamic tool part created by a `tool-input-delta` has no `rawInput` until the next delta. An approval `reason` of `""` is kept, so an output-denied part converts to an `error-text` of `""` instead of `"Tool call execution denied."` (TS `approval?.reason ?? ...`). `ReadUIMessages` writes no snapshot for a chunk type it does not know. A decoded `"rawInput": null` is kept as well: it is encoded again and `ConvertToModelMessages` logs the `rawInput` deprecation for it, as TS does for an own `rawInput` that is not undefined (review round 86). `ValidateUIMessages` still drops `rawInput` (null included) from states other than input-streaming and output-error, as TS does (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `"input"` from encoded UI messages, compares `ReadUIMessages` snapshots, sends `reason: ""` in tool approval responses, or stores `"rawInput": null`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you require the `input` key; treat a missing key like null.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Workflow chat transport parses every SSE data line {#workflow-transport-sse-data}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `WorkflowChatTransport` trimmed each SSE event's data and skipped empty and whitespace-padded `[DONE]` events. v0.6 skips only an exact `[DONE]` and parses any other data as JSON; a parse failure ends the attempt, as an invalid chunk already did (TS `parseJsonEventStream`; review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** Servers that send `data:` lines with no value, or `[DONE]` with extra spaces, to `workflow.WorkflowChatTransport`.
- **What to do:** No action unless your server sends such events; send `data: [DONE]` exactly.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI message stream SSE events keep the TS key order {#ui-sse-key-order}

- **What changed:** The UI SSE helpers (`ToUIMessageStreamResponse`, `PipeUIMessageStreamToResponse(WithInit)`, `CreateUIMessageStreamResponse(WithInit)`, `PipeUIMessageChunksToResponse`, `CreateUIMessageChunksResponse`) wrote each chunk's JSON with sorted keys (`{"delta":"Hi","id":"t1","type":"text-delta"}`). v0.6 writes the key order TS `toUIMessageChunk` creates (`{"type":"text-delta","id":"t1","delta":"Hi"}`). Keys TS does not create follow, sorted. Nested objects keep Go's order (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Byte-level comparisons of UI SSE bodies (`data: {`), golden files.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare the bytes; JSON values are unchanged.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI tool-input-start writes dynamic: false for a tool outside the tool set {#ui-tool-input-start-dynamic-false}

- **What changed:** A `tool-input-start` chunk for a tool missing from the tool set (an unknown tool, a provider-executed tool not in `Tools`, or any tool when the UI conversion has no tool set) now carries `"dynamic": false` (or `true` for a provider dynamic tool), as TS. v0.5 wrote `dynamic` only when true (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Clients or tests that check for the absence of `dynamic` on `tool-input-start`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare chunks exactly; a missing `dynamic` and `false` mean the same.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI message metadata merges non-object values like TS {#ui-metadata-merge-mergeobjects}

- **What changed:** Message metadata from `start`, `finish` and `message-metadata` chunks merges as TS `mergeObjects`. An object followed by a string or array gets the value's index keys (`{"a":1}` + `"ab"` gives `{"0":"a","1":"b","a":1}`). A number or boolean followed by a number or boolean gives `{}`. An array followed by any value gives an object of its index keys plus the value's keys. v0.5 replaced the metadata with the incoming value. Where TS throws (a string, number or boolean followed by a value with keys), the v0.5 replacement stays (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** `messageMetadata` callbacks or writers that send a string or array after object metadata, or mix scalar metadata values; readers of `metadata` in `OnEnd` / `ReadUIMessages` messages.
- **What to do:** Send object metadata:

  ```go
  chunk := ai.UIMessageChunk{"type": "message-metadata", "messageMetadata": map[string]interface{}{"status": "done"}}
  _ = chunk
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Agent UI stream helpers end a failed run without [DONE] {#agent-ui-helpers-stream-errors}

- **What changed:** `agent.PipeAgentUIStreamFromUIMessagesToResponse` and `agent.CreateAgentUIStreamResponseFromUIMessages` now pass the run's error channel to the chunk helpers (`UIMessageStreamResponseInit.StreamErrors`), so a failed agent stream ends without `data: [DONE]`, and the pipe helper returns the error, as TS `pipeAgentUIStreamToResponse` / `createAgentUIStreamResponse`. v0.5 wrote `[DONE]` (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Clients that wait for `data: [DONE]` after an agent run fails; callers ignoring the pipe helper's error.
- **What to do:** Treat the end of the body without `[DONE]` as a failure, and check the returned error.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Empty text deltas from transforms reach the UI stream {#ui-stream-empty-text-delta}

- **What changed:** v0.5's `ToUIMessageStream` dropped an empty `text-delta` without provider metadata. v0.6 maps it, and calls `MessageMetadata` for it, when the source is a StreamText result, as TS does. StreamText itself still drops empty deltas of a step's first text block (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** An `ExperimentalTransform` (or a hand-built part stream) that emits `ChunkTypeText` with `Text: ""`.
- **What to do:** No action unless your UI rejects empty deltas.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### UI stream callback order and provider tool errors {#ui-stream-metadata-before-onerror}

- **What changed:** `ToUIMessageStream` called `OnError` before `MessageMetadata` for an error or tool-error part, and called `OnError` for provider-executed tool errors too (the result was not used). v0.6 runs `MessageMetadata` first, and a provider-executed tool error keeps its own text without an `OnError` call, as TS toUIMessageStream / toUIMessageChunk (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `UIMessageStreamResultOptions.OnError` with side effects (logging, counting) or that depends on running before `MessageMetadata`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you count `OnError` calls for provider tool errors.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### WorkflowChatTransport reconnect error text for a malformed frame {#workflow-chat-transport-parse-error-text}

- **What changed:** A reconnect that gave up after malformed frames reported `Last error: Error: invalid UI message chunk: ...`. v0.6 reports the TS `AI_JSONParseError: JSON parsing failed: Text: <data>.\nError message: SyntaxError: <parser message>` (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `invalid UI message chunk` in `ReconnectToStream` errors.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match that text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### UI MessageMetadata part types match TS {#ui-message-metadata-tool-error}

- **What changed:** `MessageMetadata` received `type: "tool-result"` for a failed tool result, and harness / `NewStreamTextResultFromParts` streams called it for the Go-only `response-metadata` / `ai.stream.firstChunk` markers. v0.6 passes `tool-error` and no longer calls it for those markers (TS to-ui-message-stream.ts, harness-stream-text-result.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `UIMessageStreamResultOptions.MessageMetadata` reading `part["type"]`.
- **What to do:** Match `"tool-error"`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Invalid tool calls produce one UI error chunk and one OnError call {#ui-invalid-tool-call-once}

- **What changed:** For an invalid tool call the UI stream wrote `tool-input-error` and then a `tool-output-error` for the tool error `StreamText` injects, calling `OnError` twice. v0.6 writes only `tool-input-error` and calls `OnError` once (TS 8585d7e56f) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** UI consumers expecting `tool-output-error` after `tool-input-error`, or counting `OnError` calls.
- **What to do:** Read the error from `tool-input-error`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ValidateUIMessages part type, state and invalid-value errors match TS {#validate-ui-messages-selector}

- **What changed:** A part with a missing or unknown type, or a tool part with an unknown state, failed with Go texts, and a non-finite number was an `*InvalidArgumentError`. v0.6 reports TS selector issues at `[i].parts[j].type` / `.state` and a non-finite number as a `*errors.ValidationError` with the TS "Invalid JSON value ..." text (TS 11323f9894) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `ValidateUIMessages` error text or `*InvalidArgumentError`.
- **What to do:** Match `*errors.ValidationError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

## Telemetry and events {#telemetry-and-events}

The [telemetry recording change](#telemetry-record-pointers) is listed under
compile-time changes.

### An empty Telemetry.Integrations turns telemetry off for the call {#empty-integrations}

- **What changed:** as in TS (`integrations: []`), a non-nil `Integrations`
  replaces the globally registered integrations, so an empty slice means none:
  no spans or events for that call. v0.5 used the global integrations for an
  empty slice. nil still uses them.
- **Am I affected?** `Integrations: []telemetry.TelemetryIntegration{}`, or a
  helper that can build an empty slice.
- **What to do:** leave the field nil to use the global integrations:

  ```go
  // v0.5: an empty slice used the global integrations
  tel := &telemetry.Options{Integrations: []telemetry.TelemetryIntegration{}}
  // v0.6: leave it nil for the global integrations
  tel = &telemetry.Options{}
  _ = tel
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry spans come from the ai and gen_ai tracers {#tracer-names}

- **What changed:** as in TS, `LegacyOpenTelemetry` (and `GetTracer`) without
  a `Tracer` uses `otel.Tracer("ai")`, and the GenAI `OpenTelemetry`
  integration uses `otel.Tracer("gen_ai")`. v0.5 used `"ai-sdk"` for root
  spans and `"go-ai"` for child spans. Every span of a call now comes from the
  root's tracer, including a `Tracer` you pass. `telemetry.TracerName` is
  deprecated and unused.
- **Am I affected?** Instrumentation-scope filters or sampler rules that match
  `ai-sdk` or `go-ai`.
- **What to do:** match the new names, or pass your own `Tracer`:

  ```go
  // v0.5: spans had the instrumentation scope "ai-sdk" or "go-ai"
  // v0.6: "ai" (legacy) or "gen_ai" (GenAI), or your own tracer's name
  legacyScope, genAIScope := telemetry.LegacyTracerName, telemetry.GenAITracerName
  _, _ = legacyScope, genAIScope
  ```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### EnrichSpan and runtime context attributes follow TS {#enrich-span-attributes}

- **What changed:** an `EnrichSpan` value that is an object, a struct, `nil`
  or a mixed-type array is now dropped (TS `sanitizeAttributes`). v0.5 wrote
  it as a JSON string attribute. A `[]interface{}` of one primitive type
  becomes a typed slice attribute, and Go's other integer and float kinds stay
  numbers. A runtime context value that is an object (any string-keyed map, a
  struct or a pointer to one) is flattened into
  `ai.settings.context.<path>` attributes instead of one JSON string.
- **Am I affected?** Dashboards or queries that read those JSON-string
  attributes.
- **What to do:** read the flattened keys, or return primitives from
  `EnrichSpan`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Evaluate telemetry and event names are decide names {#decide-telemetry-names}

- **What changed:** with the evaluate-to-decide rename, span and attribute
  names are `ai.decide*` and `ai.decision.*`, the GenAI operation is
  `gen_ai.operation.name=decide`, and the diagnostic channel events are
  `experimental_onDecideStart`, `experimental_onDecideEnd`,
  `experimental_onDecisionModelCallStart` and
  `experimental_onDecisionModelCallEnd`. The deprecated
  `DiagnosticEventOnEvaluate*` constants now hold the decide values.
- **Am I affected?** Queries, dashboards or diagnostic subscribers that match
  the evaluate names.
- **What to do:** match the decide names.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### A cancelled GenerateText reports OnError to telemetry {#generate-cancel-telemetry}

- **What changed:** a cancelled or timed-out `GenerateText` (and the legacy
  `ToolLoopAgent` loop) reports `OnError`, not `OnAbort`, to telemetry
  integrations, as TS `generateText` has no abort telemetry. The root and step
  spans carry the error status. `GenerateTextOptions.OnAbortEvent` /
  `OnAbort` still fire.
- **Am I affected?** Custom telemetry integrations that count aborts in
  `OnAbort` for `GenerateText`.
- **What to do:** handle the cancellation in the integration's `OnError`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Step-start events report the call's tools and the output {#step-start-call-tools}

- **What changed:** `OnStepStartEvent.Tools` of `GenerateText`, `StreamText`,
  `ToolLoopAgent.Generate` / `Stream` and the legacy `GenerateAgent` /
  `Execute` loop is the call's tool set. In v0.5 it was the step's narrowed
  set (after `ActiveTools`, `FilterActiveTools`, tool search and tool-caller
  routing). `OnStepStartEvent.Output` is now the call's output specification;
  v0.5 left it nil. TS `stepStartEvent.tools` is "the tools available for
  this generation", and `stepStartEvent.output` is the output.
- **Am I affected?** `OnStepStart:` callbacks (and `OnStepStartEvent:` in
  `AgentConfig`) that read `e.Tools` or expect `e.Output` to be nil.
- **What to do:** read `e.ActiveTools` for the step's limit, or read the
  tools sent to the model from `OnLanguageModelCallStart`:

  ```go
  opts := ai.GenerateTextOptions{
  	OnLanguageModelCallStart: func(ctx context.Context, e ai.LanguageModelCallStartEvent) {
  		sent := e.Tools // v0.5: OnStepStartEvent.Tools
  		_ = sent
  	},
  }
  _ = opts
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry OnStepError fires for every failed step {#telemetry-step-error}

- **What changed:** a telemetry integration's `OnStepError` now runs, before
  `OnError`, for every `GenerateText` or `StreamText` step that fails after
  it started. In v0.5 it ran only when the model call failed to open; any
  other failure closed the step span through `OnError` alone. Now it also
  runs for a `GenerateText` tool choice violation, tool approval or
  tool-context error, or parse or download failure after the model call, and
  for a `StreamText` step whose model stream fails mid-step, ends without
  output, or whose tool input callback, download or conversion fails. TS
  `generateText` and `streamText` notify telemetry `onStepError` for the open
  step on every failure (`notifyStepError`). The error is wrapped for an
  unauthenticated AI Gateway request (see
  [Gateway auth error](#gateway-auth-error)); `OnError` keeps the error
  itself.
- **Am I affected?** Custom `TelemetryIntegration` implementations with an
  `OnStepError` method that assume it only runs when a model call fails to
  open, or that compare its `e.Error` with the provider's error by `==` or by
  text.
- **What to do:** no action unless your integration does that. Match errors
  with `errors.As`:

  ```go
  func isGatewayAuth(err error) bool {
  	var auth *gatewayerrors.GatewayAuthenticationError
  	return errors.As(err, &auth) // also true for the wrapped error
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry step-end, end, tool-end, error and abort run after the callbacks {#telemetry-callback-order}

- **What changed:** `GenerateText` and `StreamText` now call the user
  callbacks before the telemetry integrations for the same event: the step's
  `OnStepEnd` / `OnStepEndEvent` before the integration `OnStepEnd`, `OnEnd`
  / `OnEndEvent` before the integration `OnFinish` (onEnd), and
  `OnToolExecutionEnd` before the integration `OnToolExecutionEnd`. v0.5 ran
  the telemetry first for both end events, for the `StreamText` step end and
  for every tool end (the `GenerateText` step end already ran the callbacks
  first). `GenerateObject`, `StreamObject`, `ExperimentalDecide` and the
  legacy `ToolLoopAgent` (also `WorkflowAgent.Generate`) follow the same
  order: `OnStepEnd` before the telemetry step end, `OnEnd` before the
  telemetry end, `OnToolExecutionEnd` before the telemetry tool end,
  `OnStepError` before the telemetry step error, and `ExperimentalDecide`'s
  `OnStart` before the telemetry `OnDecideStart`. `WorkflowAgent` likewise
  calls `OnToolExecutionEnd` of an approval-resumed tool, `OnError` and
  `OnAbort` before the root telemetry `OnToolExecutionEnd`, `OnError` and
  `OnAbort`. `StreamObject` now parses and repairs the final JSON (calling
  `RepairText`) before `OnStepEnd`, and a `ToModelOutput` failure still calls
  `OnToolExecutionEnd` (with the raw output) before the call fails. TS
  `notify([callback, telemetry.onX])` starts the callback first, and TS
  WorkflowAgent awaits its callbacks before the telemetry dispatcher (review
  rounds 70 and 71). `OnStepError` also runs before the telemetry
  `OnStepError` (see [Later step failure](#streamtext-continuation-failure-end)).
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry integrations whose `OnStepEnd`, `OnFinish`,
  `OnToolExecutionEnd`, `OnError` or `OnAbort` shares state with your
  `OnStepEnd`, `OnEnd`, `OnEndEvent`, `OnToolExecutionEnd`, `OnError` or
  `OnAbort` callbacks and depends on running first, for example a span the
  callback expects to be ended already. `RepairText` and `OnStepEnd` of
  `StreamObject` that depend on their order; `OnToolExecutionEnd` callbacks
  that assume a `ToModelOutput` failure never reaches them.
- **What to do:** no action unless you rely on that order. Do the work that
  must come last in the integration:

  ```go
  type lastRecorder struct {
  	telemetry.NoopTelemetryIntegration
  }

  func (lastRecorder) OnFinish(ctx context.Context, e telemetry.TelemetryFinishEvent) {
  	// runs after OnEnd / OnEndEvent (v0.5: before them)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry step end runs every OnStepEnd before the deprecated OnStepFinish {#telemetry-step-finish-fanout}

- **What changed:** `FireOnStepEnd` / `FireOnStepFinish` call `OnStepEnd` on
  every integration, then `OnStepFinish` on the integrations that still
  implement it, each under its own recover. v0.5 called both per integration
  in turn, so a panicking `OnStepEnd` skipped that integration's
  `OnStepFinish`. TS dispatcher `onStepEnd: mergeCallbacks(onStepEnd,
  onStepFinish)` (review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Integrations that implement the deprecated
  `OnStepFinish` and depend on it running right after their own `OnStepEnd`,
  before other integrations' `OnStepEnd`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you depend on that order. Implement
  `OnStepEnd` only.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Rerank telemetry `ai.ranking` uses TS keys {#telemetry-ranking-keys}

- **What changed:** v0.5 wrote each `ai.ranking` entry on the
  `ai.rerank.doRerank` span as `{"Index":2,"RelevanceScore":0.9}`; v0.6
  writes `{"index":2,"relevanceScore":0.9}`, as TS
  `JSON.stringify(ranking entry)` does (LegacyOpenTelemetry, and
  OpenTelemetry with `Reranking`) (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Dashboards or tests that parse the `ai.ranking` span
  attribute.
- **What to do:** read `index` and `relevanceScore`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GenAI embeddings span operation name is `embeddings` {#telemetry-embeddings-operation-name}

- **What changed:** v0.5 set `gen_ai.operation.name` on the OpenTelemetry
  (GenAI) `embeddings <model>` child span of `Embed` / `EmbedMany` to
  `ai.embed.doEmbed` / `ai.embedMany.doEmbed`; v0.6 sets `embeddings`, as TS
  `open-telemetry.ts` `onEmbedStart` does (review round 74). The span name
  and the root span are unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** Dashboards, queries or tests that filter the GenAI
  `embeddings` span by `gen_ai.operation.name`.
- **What to do:** match `gen_ai.operation.name = "embeddings"`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Legacy doEmbed span `ai.usage.tokens` is an integer {#legacy-embed-usage-tokens-int}

- **What changed:** v0.5 recorded `ai.usage.tokens` on the
  LegacyOpenTelemetry `ai.embed.doEmbed` / `ai.embedMany.doEmbed` span as a
  float attribute (`attribute.Float64`); v0.6 records it as an integer
  (`attribute.Int64`), like the root `ai.embed` / `ai.embedMany` span. TS
  records the same integer `usage.tokens` on both spans, which OpenTelemetry
  JS exports as an int value (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Exporters, span processors or tests that read
  `ai.usage.tokens` on the `ai.embed.doEmbed` / `ai.embedMany.doEmbed` span
  as a float (`Value.AsFloat64()`, `.(float64)`, a `doubleValue` query).
- **What to do:** read it as an integer (`Value.AsInt64()`, `.(int64)`).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### generateSpeech records an empty text {#telemetry-speech-empty-text}

- **What changed:** v0.5 left `ai.request.text` (both OpenTelemetry
  integrations) and the GenAI `gen_ai.input.messages` off the
  `GenerateSpeech` root span when the text was empty; v0.6 records them
  with the empty text, as TS `onAudioOperationStart` records any text that
  is present (review round 75). Transcription spans are unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** Span queries that test for the presence of
  `ai.request.text` / `gen_ai.input.messages` on `ai.generateSpeech` spans.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the attribute being absent
  for an empty text.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ai.prompt.toolChoice and ai.schema honor RecordInputs {#otel-input-gated-toolchoice-schema}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `OpenTelemetry` (GenAI) recorded
  `ai.prompt.toolChoice` (with `OpenTelemetryOptions.ToolChoice`) and
  `ai.schema` (with `OpenTelemetryOptions.Schema`) even when `RecordInputs`
  was false. v0.6 treats both as input attributes and omits them when
  `RecordInputs` is false, as TS (`{input: () => ...}` in open-telemetry.ts).
  `ai.schema.name`, `ai.schema.description` and `ai.settings.output` are
  unchanged; `LegacyOpenTelemetry` already gated both (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** `OpenTelemetryOptions{ToolChoice: true}` or
  `OpenTelemetryOptions{Schema: true}` together with
  `RecordInputs: telemetry.Bool(false)`; dashboards reading
  `ai.prompt.toolChoice` or `ai.schema`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on those attributes while not
  recording inputs; set `RecordInputs` to true (or leave it unset) to keep
  them.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An unset finish reason is not recorded on spans {#otel-unset-finish-reason}

- **What changed:** v0.5: an event with an empty `FinishReason` recorded
  `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons` as `[""]` (GenAI chat span and roots,
  legacy step span) and `ai.response.finishReason` as `""` (legacy step and
  root spans). v0.6 records neither attribute for an empty finish reason, as
  TS drops `[undefined]` (`sanitizeAttributeValue`) and undefined values
  (`selectAttributes`) (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** custom `TelemetryIntegration` callers or models that
  report `FinishReason: ""`; queries on `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons` /
  `ai.response.finishReason` expecting an empty value.
- **What to do:** no action unless you query for an empty finish reason;
  treat a missing attribute as unset.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenTelemetry span names keep the space for an empty model ID {#otel-empty-model-id-span-names}

- **What changed:** v0.5: a GenAI `OpenTelemetry` span for a model with an
  empty model ID was named after the operation alone (`invoke_agent`,
  `chat`, `embeddings`, `rerank`, `decide`, `ai.harness`), and the chat span
  had no `gen_ai.response.model`. v0.6 keeps TS's
  `` `${operationName} ${modelId}` `` form, including the trailing space
  (`invoke_agent `, `chat `, `ai.harness `), and the chat span records
  `gen_ai.response.model: ""`. This happens for a harness turn without a
  model (TS `modelId ''`) or a model whose `ModelID()` returns `""` (review
  round 78).
- **Am I affected?** You filter or alert on GenAI span names, or on
  `gen_ai.response.model`, for calls whose model ID is empty:
  `telemetry.NewOpenTelemetry`, `harness.Agent` with no `Model`, a custom
  `provider.LanguageModel` with `ModelID() == ""`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you match these span names exactly. If you
  do, trim the name (`strings.TrimSpace(span.Name())`) or match the `"chat "`
  prefix.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenTelemetry ends open spans in TS order on abort or error {#otel-abort-error-span-end-order}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `OpenTelemetry.OnAbort` / `OnError` ended the open
  decision and chat spans first, then the step span, then the tool spans,
  then the embedding and rerank spans, and ended parallel tool spans and
  `EmbedMany` batch spans in random (map) order. `LegacyOpenTelemetry` did
  the same without the chat span. v0.6 ends them in TS order: GenAI ends the
  tool spans (in start order), then chat, step, the embedding spans (in
  start order), rerank, decision and the root; legacy ends tool spans, step,
  embeddings, rerank, decision and the root. TS holds these spans in
  insertion-ordered `Map`s and ends children before their parents (review
  round 79).
- **Am I affected?** Only if you depend on the order in which spans reach
  your exporter or `SpanProcessor.OnEnd` (for example
  `tracetest.SpanRecorder.Ended()` indexes) after an aborted or failed call,
  with `telemetry.NewOpenTelemetry` or `telemetry.NewLegacyOpenTelemetry`
  registered.
- **What to do:** no action unless you index ended spans by position after
  an abort or error; look spans up by name or span ID instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The legacy embed root span records InputTokens usage {#embed-root-usage-tokens-input-tokens}

- **What changed:** in v0.5 the legacy `ai.embed` / `ai.embedMany` root span
  had no `ai.usage.tokens` when the embedding model reported only
  `types.EmbeddingUsage.InputTokens` (`Tokens` and `TotalTokens` 0), even
  though its `ai.embed.doEmbed` span recorded that count. v0.6 reads the same
  counters on the root as on the doEmbed span (`Tokens`, then
  `InputTokens`, then `TotalTokens`), as in TS, where both record
  `usage.tokens` (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** A custom `provider.EmbeddingModel` that sets only
  `EmbeddingUsage.InputTokens`, with `telemetry.LegacyOpenTelemetry`
  reading `ai.usage.tokens` on the root span. The built-in providers set
  `Tokens`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the root span having no
  `ai.usage.tokens` for such a model.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A provider-reported 0 embedding token count is recorded {#embedding-usage-zero-reported}

- **What changed:** v0.5 never recorded a 0 embedding token count in
  telemetry: a zero `types.EmbeddingUsage` could not be told apart from "the
  provider sent no usage", so `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` (GenAI `embeddings`
  span) and `ai.usage.tokens` (legacy `ai.embed.doEmbed` /
  `ai.embedMany.doEmbed` spans and the `ai.embed` / `ai.embedMany` roots)
  were left out. v0.6 adds `EmbeddingUsage.Reported` (`json:"-"`), set by
  the built-in embedding providers when the response carried a usage
  object, and records 0 when it is set, as TS records `usage.tokens: 0`.
  Usage the provider did not send (TS `usage: undefined`, recorded as NaN
  and dropped) is still left out, and so is a provider that leaves
  `Reported` false (v0.5.2 custom models). Set by OpenAI, Azure, Fireworks,
  Together, Mistral, Ollama, Alibaba, Perplexity and Gateway when the
  response has `usage`; Vertex `:embedContent` when
  `usageMetadata.promptTokenCount` is present; Vertex `:predict`, Cohere,
  Voyage and Bedrock always (a NaN token count is still dropped; Bedrock's
  batched `DoEmbedMany` leaves usage unreported when any batch is NaN);
  Google never. `EmbedMany` sets it only when every batch reported usage,
  and for a call split into no batches (TS sums `usage.tokens` with NaN for
  a batch without usage, and 0 for no batches). `testutil.MockEmbeddingModel`
  sets it (review round 80). When the call or any `EmbedMany` batch sent no
  usage, `Usage.Tokens` keeps the sum of the reported batches (0 when none),
  as v0.5, and `ai.usage.tokens` is left off the `ai.embed` / `ai.embedMany`
  root span, where TS's is NaN (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** You read `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` or
  `ai.usage.tokens` on embedding spans; you implement
  `provider.EmbeddingModel`; or you compare whole `types.EmbeddingUsage`
  values from a built-in provider, `EmbedResult.Usage`,
  `EmbedManyResult.Usage`, `EmbedOnFinishEvent.Usage` or
  `testutil.MockEmbeddingModel`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you compare whole usage structs (compare
  `Tokens` / `InputTokens` / `TotalTokens`, or add `Reported: true` to the
  expected value) or want a custom model's 0 recorded:

  ```go
  result := &types.EmbeddingsResult{
  	Embeddings: vectors,
  	Usage:      types.EmbeddingUsage{Tokens: 0, Reported: true},
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### FireOnStepError publishes an onStepError diagnostic message {#diagnostic-on-step-error}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `telemetry.FireOnStepError` published no
  diagnostic message. v0.6 publishes `telemetry.DiagnosticEventOnStepError`
