  (`"onStepError"`, carrying the `TelemetryErrorEvent`) after the
  telemetry-disabled check, like every other lifecycle event; TS dispatches
  `onStepError` through the same dispatcher as the other events (review
  round 80).
- **Am I affected?** A `telemetry.SubscribeDiagnostic` handler that assumes
  a fixed set of `DiagnosticMessage.Type` values.
- **What to do:** no action unless your handler rejects unknown types;
  handle or ignore `telemetry.DiagnosticEventOnStepError`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Step spans record the auto tool choice {#telemetry-step-tool-choice-auto}

- **What changed:** v0.5 left `ai.prompt.toolChoice` off the
  `ai.generateText.doGenerate` / `ai.streamText.doStream` span (and off the
  GenAI `step N` span with `OpenTelemetryOptions.ToolChoice`) when the call
  set no tool choice, and `TelemetryStepStartEvent.ToolChoice` was the zero
  value. v0.6 sends the prepared tool choice, `{"type":"auto"}` when none is
  set, as TS `prepareToolChoice` does for its `stepToolChoice`
  (GenerateText, StreamText and ToolLoopAgent; review round 82). The
  callback events (`OnStepStart`) keep the choice as given.
- **Am I affected?** Span queries or tests on `ai.prompt.toolChoice`, and
  custom integrations that read `TelemetryStepStartEvent.ToolChoice` and treat
  `Type == ""` as "no tools".
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the attribute being absent;
  treat `{"type":"auto"}` as the default.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### WorkflowAgent step spans omit the step prompt and tools {#workflow-step-span-no-prompt}

- **What changed:** v0.5 recorded `ai.prompt.messages` and `ai.prompt.tools`
  on WorkflowAgent step spans (`ai.generateText.doGenerate` under
  `ai.workflowAgent.stream`), and `TelemetryStepStartEvent.PromptMessages` /
  `StepTools` were set for them. v0.6 leaves them (and `ToolChoice`) unset, in
  generate and stream mode, as TS WorkflowAgent's `model-call-iterator.ts`
  dispatches its step start without `promptMessages`, `stepTools` or
  `stepToolChoice` (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** Span queries on `ai.prompt.messages` / `ai.prompt.tools`
  for WorkflowAgent runs, or integrations reading `PromptMessages` /
  `StepTools` on WorkflowAgent step events.
- **What to do:** read the conversation from the root `ai.prompt` attribute
  or the `OnLanguageModelCallStart` event instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Tool telemetry records the TS tool shapes {#telemetry-prompt-tools-shape}

- **What changed:** v0.5: the legacy step span's `ai.prompt.tools` held each
  `types.Tool` marshaled with Go field names (`{"name":"weather",
  "description":"","parameters":{...}}`, `"parameters":null` for a tool
  without a schema), and the GenAI chat span's `gen_ai.tool.definitions`
  wrote its objects with sorted keys. v0.6: both record the tools as TS
  `prepareTools` hands them to the model, in TS key order:
  `{"type":"function","name","inputSchema",...}` (with `description`,
  `inputExamples`, `providerOptions`, `strict` when set) and
  `{"type":"provider","name","id","args"}`. A struct schema keeps its field
  order. Harness turns record TS turn telemetry's `{"type":"function","name",
  "description","inputSchema"}` and leave out `inputSchema` for a tool
  without one. WorkflowAgent chat spans record the same definitions as
  generateText/streamText, and nothing for a step without tools (TS
  `toLanguageModelToolDefinitions`, 4d6d830c14; review round 86; rounds
  84-85 recorded TS's earlier `serializeToolSet` definitions and `[]`).
  A tool without a schema reports TS `asSchema(undefined)`,
  `{"type":"object","properties":{},"additionalProperties":false}`
  (round 85).
- **Am I affected?** Span queries or exporters that parse `ai.prompt.tools` (the
  `parameters` key) or compare `gen_ai.tool.definitions` text byte for byte.
- **What to do:** No action unless you read `parameters` from `ai.prompt.tools`;
  read `inputSchema` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### WorkflowAgent root span is invoke_agent {#workflow-agent-operation-name}

- **What changed:** The OpenTelemetry (GenAI) root span of a `WorkflowAgent` run was `ai.workflowAgent.stream <model>` with `gen_ai.operation.name=ai.workflowAgent.stream`. It is now `invoke_agent <model>` with `gen_ai.operation.name=invoke_agent`, like generateText/streamText (TS `4d6d830c14`). The legacy integration is unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** Dashboards or tests that match `ai.workflowAgent.stream` on `telemetry.NewOpenTelemetry` spans.
- **What to do:** Match `gen_ai.operation.name == "invoke_agent"`; tell workflow runs apart by `gen_ai.agent.name` / function id.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Chat spans carry server.address and server.port {#otel-server-address}

- **What changed:** OpenTelemetry `chat` spans of text and object calls gain `server.address` and `server.port` when the provider reports its request URL (OpenAI Chat, and Azure / Bedrock Mantle through it, as in TS 934100cad4). Only the origin is used; nothing is recorded without a URL. The call-end event gains `Request` (`ai.LanguageModelCallRequest{URL}`), steps gain `Request.URL` (origin only).
- **Am I affected?** Span attribute consumers that reject unknown keys; `LanguageModelCallEndEvent` equality checks.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Provider usage and runtime context attributes keep one value per name {#otel-usage-key-collision}

- **What changed:** v0.5 emitted one `ai.usage.*` / `gen_ai.usage.*` attribute per provider usage key, so two keys that normalize to the same name (`inputTokens`, `input_tokens`) gave duplicates and the span kept an arbitrary one. v0.6 emits one attribute holding the later key's value, as TS `getProviderUsageAttributes` writes into one object. A plain map is read in sorted key order (array-index keys first); an ordered object (`*jsonorder.Object`) keeps its key order. Runtime context attributes (`ai.settings.context.*`) follow the same rule for a dotted key and a nested path, and a later value the sanitizer drops (NaN) removes the attribute. A struct context flattens in its JSON field order, and `json.Number` values are numbers (review round 87b).
- **Am I affected?** Span consumers that read `ai.usage.*`, `gen_ai.usage.*` or `ai.settings.context.*` from provider usage maps or runtime contexts with colliding keys (`ProviderUsage`, `SpeechResult.Usage`, `RuntimeContext`); runtime contexts holding `json.Number`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on which duplicate survived.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Empty inputExamples are kept in tool definitions {#tool-definitions-empty-input-examples}

- **What changed:** A tool with a non-nil empty `InputExamples` now shows `"inputExamples":[]` in `ai.prompt.tools` and `gen_ai.tool.definitions`, and a workflow `SerializableToolDef` keeps `"inputExamples":[]` in its JSON (TS `inputExamples != null`). A nil slice is still omitted (review round 87b).
- **Am I affected?** `InputExamples: []types.ToolInputExample{}`; tests comparing those attributes or serialized workflow tool JSON.
- **What to do:** No action, or leave `InputExamples` nil to omit the key.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### User OnStart runs before the telemetry root start {#telemetry-start-after-onstart}

- **What changed:** GenerateText, StreamText, the ToolLoopAgent and WorkflowAgent started the telemetry root (integration `OnStart`) before the user `OnStart`, and ran `OnStart` with the root span's context. v0.6 runs the user callback first, outside the root span, as TS `notify([onStart, telemetry.onStart])`. `OnStepStart` and later callbacks still run inside it (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** An `OnStart` callback that reads the span from its `ctx` (`trace.SpanFromContext`) or depends on an integration having started first.
- **What to do:** Read the span in `OnStepStart` or later callbacks.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### WorkflowAgent root telemetry follows the TS durable report {#workflow-root-telemetry-durable}

- **What changed:** The WorkflowAgent root's call ID is now `call-` plus 24 alphanumerics (it was 16 hex characters). Its end event carries what TS reports for a durable run: the final step's reasoning and provider metadata, the tool calls, tool results and files of every step, and the detailed usage. The OTel integrations record `ai.response.reasoning`, `ai.response.toolCalls`, `ai.response.providerMetadata`, `ai.response.files`, the `gen_ai.output.messages` tool parts and the cache / reasoning token counts on the root (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Custom `telemetry.TelemetryIntegration`s that parse `TelemetryStartEvent.CallID` / `TelemetryFinishEvent.CallID` from a `WorkflowAgent` or read its root `TelemetryFinishEvent`; dashboards on the root span's attributes.
- **What to do:** No action unless you parse the call ID's format.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Recording flags filter what integrations and diagnostic subscribers see {#telemetry-recording-flags-filter}

- **What changed:** With `RecordInputs` or `RecordOutputs` false, v0.5 passed every event unfiltered to custom integrations and `SubscribeDiagnostic` handlers. v0.6 gives every integration (built-in and custom) and every diagnostic subscriber a filtered copy, as TS `filterTelemetryEvent` does in its dispatcher (TS 8da48db187, 883fcde5f9): input fields (prompt, system, messages, tools, schema and its name / description, embedding values, rerank query / documents, decide state / questions / provider options, tool args) or output fields (text, content, reasoning, tool calls and results, files, object, embeddings, ranking, answers) are emptied, provider metadata and request / response bodies are dropped, and every error becomes `AI SDK operation failed`. This covers the `ObjectOn*Event`s that `GenerateObject` / `StreamObject` publish on the channel. `ExecuteTool` integrations get nil args when inputs are hidden; the tool still gets its input. Built-in spans record the generic error text, no `ai.response.providerMetadata` and, with inputs hidden, no `ai.schema.name` / `ai.schema.description`; a failed tool with outputs hidden gets an error status without a message. Application callbacks and results are unchanged (review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** Custom `telemetry.TelemetryIntegration`s, `SubscribeDiagnostic` / `SubscribeDiagnosticTyped` handlers or span assertions, combined with `RecordInputs: telemetry.Bool(false)` or `RecordOutputs: telemetry.Bool(false)`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you read those fields with a flag off; turn the flag back on to keep them. `telemetry.FilterEvent` applies the same filter to your own events.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry tool definitions redact provider credentials {#telemetry-tool-definition-redaction}

- **What changed:** The telemetry tool definitions (`ai.prompt.tools`, `gen_ai.tool.definitions`) showed the `openai.mcp` `authorization` and header values and the `domainSecrets[].value` of the `openai.code_interpreter` container and `openai.shell` environment network policies. v0.6 writes `[redacted]` there on a telemetry-only copy, as TS otel `redactToolDefinition` (TS 7bb309d132). The provider request still sends the real values (review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** Span assertions on those attributes for OpenAI MCP, code interpreter or shell tools.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GenAI output messages keep an empty tool call ID and media type {#genai-output-empty-ids}

- **What changed:** In `gen_ai.output.messages` (step / inference spans and the root span), a tool call or tool result with an empty ID wrote `"id": null`, and a generated file with an empty media type wrote `"mime_type": null`. v0.6 writes `""`, as TS `formatOutputMessages` writes the values as given (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Span consumers that read `gen_ai.output.messages` from providers or tools that produce empty tool call IDs or file media types.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry copies redact provider tool credentials {#telemetry-credential-redaction}

- **What changed:** Custom telemetry integrations and diagnostic subscribers received the OpenAI code interpreter / shell network policy domain secrets and MCP `authorization` / header values (in `StepTools`, model-call `Tools`, `Tool.ProviderOptions` and request / response bodies). v0.6 replaces them with `"[redacted]"` in every telemetry copy, as TS 7.0.167 does (404ce3b14d, 9f37b45262). Provider requests and application results are unchanged (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** A `telemetry.TelemetryIntegration` or `telemetry.SubscribeDiagnostic` handler that reads `ProviderArgs`, `ProviderOptions` or `Body` of `openai.mcp`, `openai.code_interpreter` or `openai.shell` tools.
- **What to do:** No action unless you read those credentials from telemetry; read them from your own configuration.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry: integral numbers are int attributes {#telemetry-int-attributes}

- **What changed:** OTel attributes whose value is a whole number (temperature 1, usage counts, integral rates, EnrichSpan / runtime-context numbers) were float64. v0.6 records them as int64, as OpenTelemetry JS exports them (`intValue` when `Number.isInteger`) (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code or backend queries that expect a double type for `gen_ai.request.temperature`, `gen_ai.request.top_p`, `ai.settings.*`, `gen_ai.usage.*` / `ai.usage.*` provider usage, `ai.settings.context.*` or EnrichSpan attributes.
- **What to do:** No action unless you check the attribute type; read the value as a number.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### OnError without an error still marks the span as an error {#telemetry-onerror-nil-status}

- **What changed:** A `TelemetryErrorEvent` without an `Error` left the root and open child spans without a status. v0.6 sets the ERROR status (no message, no exception event), as TS records `event.error ?? event` (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that fires `OnError` with a nil error and expects the span status unset.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Span exception.type is the TS error name {#span-exception-type}

- **What changed:** Span error events recorded the Go type (`*errors.ProviderError`) with a stack trace. v0.6 records the TS error name (`AI_APICallError`, `AI_RetryError`, `AbortError`, ...) without a stack trace on GenAI and legacy spans (TS record-span.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry queries on `exception.type` or `exception.stacktrace`.
- **What to do:** Query the TS names.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### http.response.status_code only for the error itself or RetryError.LastError {#span-status-code-unwrap}

- **What changed:** `http.response.status_code` was set for any error that wrapped a `ProviderError`. v0.6 sets it only for the error itself or `RetryError.LastError`, as TS record-span.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry queries on `http.response.status_code` for wrapped errors.
- **What to do:** No action unless you query that attribute for wrapped errors.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Legacy ai.prompt.messages is the V4 prompt {#legacy-prompt-messages-v4}

- **What changed:** The legacy step `ai.prompt.messages` was the Go message JSON. v0.6 writes the TS LanguageModelV4 prompt (stringifyForTelemetry): system and instruction messages lead as `{role:"system",content:"..."}`, V4 part keys (no `tool_calls` / `fileData` / `mimeType` / `signature` / `result`), file data as a string, tool messages combined, empty tool messages dropped (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry queries or tests on `ai.prompt.messages`.
- **What to do:** Update parsers to the V4 prompt shape.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Legacy root ai.prompt keeps a string prompt as the user message {#legacy-root-prompt-string}

- **What changed:** The legacy root `ai.prompt` of a string prompt held a Go message with content parts and extra keys. v0.6 writes `{"role":"user","content":"hi"}` for `Prompt: "hi"`, and root messages have only role / content / providerOptions, as TS standardizePrompt (review round 91). `ToolLoopAgent.Execute` and `GenerateAgent` with `Prompt` record the root `ai.prompt` user message with string content too (`{"role":"user","content":"Hello"}`), as GenerateText (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** Telemetry queries on the root `ai.prompt`.
- **What to do:** Update parsers.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Legacy ai.response.object keeps the model key order {#legacy-response-object-order}

- **What changed:** The legacy `ai.response.object` re-sorted keys. v0.6 keeps the order the model returned (TS legacy-open-telemetry.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Golden tests on `ai.response.object`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare the attribute byte for byte.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### No ai.toolCall.durationMs on legacy tool spans {#legacy-tool-span-duration}

- **What changed:** Legacy tool spans had a Go-only `ai.toolCall.durationMs` attribute. v0.6 drops it, as TS legacy-open-telemetry.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Queries on `ai.toolCall.durationMs`.
- **What to do:** Use the span duration.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### No ai.settings attributes on legacy speech and transcription spans {#legacy-audio-settings}

- **What changed:** Legacy speech and transcription root spans could carry `ai.settings.*` (`ai.settings.maxRetries`). v0.6 records none, as TS onAudioOperationStart (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Queries on `ai.settings.*` of `ai.generateSpeech` / `ai.transcribe` spans.
- **What to do:** No action unless you query those.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GetBaseAttributes skips credential headers in any case {#telemetry-base-attributes-credential-headers}

- **What changed:** `telemetry.GetBaseAttributes` left out `Authorization`, `x-api-key` and `api-key` only in that exact spelling, so `authorization` became an `ai.request.headers.authorization` attribute. v0.6 matches them case-insensitively (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** Callers of `GetBaseAttributes` that relied on lower-case credential headers in the attributes.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Telemetry tool-call inputs keep the model's key order {#telemetry-tool-input-key-order}

- **What changed:** `ai.response.toolCalls`, `ai.toolCall.args`, `gen_ai.tool.call.arguments` and the `tool_call` arguments in `gen_ai.output.messages` wrote a tool call's input with sorted keys. v0.6 keeps the order the model sent (`GenerateText`, `StreamText`, `ToolLoopAgent`, `WorkflowAgent` and harness tool spans), as TS writes `toolCall.input`; `TelemetryToolCallStartEvent.ArgsText` carries the raw input text (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares these attributes as text.
- **What to do:** Compare parsed JSON.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Speech and transcription end events carry the model's provider metadata {#audio-end-event-provider-metadata}

- **What changed:** The telemetry end event (`TelemetryFinishEvent.ProviderMetadata`) of `GenerateSpeech`, `Transcribe` and `ExperimentalStreamTranscribe` held the result's `{}` default when the model reported no provider metadata, so the OTel integrations recorded `ai.response.providerMetadata: "{}"`. v0.6 passes the model's own value (nil when it reported none), and no attribute is recorded then, as TS passes `result.providerMetadata`; the results still default to `{}` (review round 93).
- **Am I affected?** Custom telemetry integrations that read `TelemetryFinishEvent.ProviderMetadata` of these operations, or span queries on `ai.response.providerMetadata`.
- **What to do:** Treat a nil map as no metadata.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Errors {#errors}

### Errors are returned without Go prefixes {#errors-unprefixed}

- **What changed:** errors are returned as is, without Go-added prefixes such
  as "... failed at step N:", "generation failed:", "output parsing failed:"
  or "failed to start stream:". `InvalidArgumentError` text is now
  `Invalid argument for parameter <field>: <message>`, and
  `TypeValidationError` text is on one line.
- **Am I affected?** `strings.Contains(err.Error(), ...)` checks or tests that
  compare error text.
- **What to do:** match with `errors.As` / `errors.Is`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Error messages quote names as TS does {#error-name-quoting}

- **What changed:** messages that quote a tool, model, question or separator
  name (`CreateIDGenerator`, `ExperimentalToolCallers`, tool search,
  `ExperimentalStreamTranscribe`, decision validation,
  `DecisionUnsupportedQuestionTypeError`) put the name between plain double
  quotes instead of Go `%q` escaping. The text changes only for a name with
  `"`, `\`, non-ASCII or control characters. The download URL check
  (`CreateURLDownload`, `GenerateVideo` downloads) writes a blocked hostname
  without quotes, as TS: `URL with hostname printer.local is not allowed`
  (v0.5: `"printer.local"`).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares these messages.
- **What to do:** match these errors with `errors.As`, not by their text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### An unknown decision model ID is a NoSuchModelError {#decide-unknown-model}

- **What changed:** `ExperimentalEvaluate` and `ExperimentalDecide` with a
  string `Model` whose provider returns no model fail with
  `*providererrors.NoSuchModelError` (`ModelType: "decisionModel"`, message
  `No such decisionModel: <id>`), as TS `resolveDecisionModel`. v0.5 returned
  a plain error, `no such evaluationModel: <id>`. The adapter from
  `provider.AdaptEvaluationModelProvider` reports a missing model the same
  way.
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares an `ExperimentalEvaluate` or
  `ExperimentalDecide` error with `no such evaluationModel`.
- **What to do:** match the type (`errors.Is(err, providererrors.ErrModelNotFound)`
  also matches):

  ```go
  var nsm *providererrors.NoSuchModelError
  if errors.As(err, &nsm) {
  	log.Printf("unknown decision model %s", nsm.ModelID)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** error text only

### Registry resolution errors follow the TS registry {#registry-resolution-errors}

- **What changed:** the `registry` resolvers return the errors TS
  `createProviderRegistry` throws:
  - A provider with no video or decision factory (`ResolveVideoModel`,
    `ResolveDecisionModel`, `ResolveEvaluationModel`) is a
    `*providererrors.NoSuchModelError` with the full registry ID
    (`ModelID: "provider:model"`, message `No such videoModel: provider:model`).
    v0.5 returned `*registry.NoSuchProviderError`.
  - A provider factory that returns a nil model with a nil error is the same
    `NoSuchModelError`, for every model type. v0.5 returned the nil model with
    a nil error.
  - An unknown provider is still a `*registry.NoSuchProviderError`, and
    `errors.As` with a `*providererrors.NoSuchModelError` target and
    `errors.Is` with `providererrors.ErrModelNotFound` now also match it (TS
    `NoSuchProviderError` extends `NoSuchModelError`). Its
    `ModelID` is the provider ID, as TS `getProvider` sets it (v0.5: the model
    part of the ID; empty from `GetProvider`). `Files` and `Skills` report
    `ModelType: "languageModel"` (v0.5: `"files"` / `"skills"`).
  - `Files` / `Skills` on a registered provider without that capability
    return the TS text with the Go method name, `The provider "x" does not
    support file uploads. Make sure it exposes a Files() method.` (and
    `... does not support skills. Make sure it exposes a Skills() method.`).
    v0.5: `No such provider: x (provider "x" does not support file uploads;
    make sure it exposes a Files() method; available providers: ...)`. The
    error is still a `*registry.NoSuchProviderError` (with `Reason` set), and
    it matches neither `NoSuchModelError` nor `ErrModelNotFound` (TS throws a
    plain `Error`).
- **Am I affected?** `errors.As(err, &nsp)` with `*registry.NoSuchProviderError`
  after `ResolveVideoModel` / `ResolveDecisionModel` / `ResolveEvaluationModel`;
  `errors.As` checks for `*providererrors.NoSuchModelError` or
  `errors.Is(err, providererrors.ErrModelNotFound)` placed before a
  `NoSuchProviderError` check; reads of `NoSuchProviderError.ModelID` or
  `.ModelType` (including after `GetProvider`); matches on the text of a
  `Files` / `Skills` error (`does not support file uploads`); code that checks
  a resolved model for nil.
- **What to do:** check for the provider error first, then the model error:

  ```go
  var nsp *registry.NoSuchProviderError
  var nsm *providererrors.NoSuchModelError
  switch {
  case errors.As(err, &nsp):
  	log.Printf("unknown provider %s", nsp.ProviderID)
  case errors.As(err, &nsm):
  	log.Printf("no %s %s", nsm.ModelType, nsm.ModelID)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Non-JSON error bodies use the HTTP status text {#non-json-error-body}

- **What changed:** a non-2xx response with an empty or non-JSON body uses the
  HTTP status text as `ProviderError.Message` (for example `Bad Gateway`).
  The body stays in `ResponseBody`.
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads the body from `Message`.
- **What to do:** read `StatusCode` and `ResponseBody`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Unparsable structured output has a JSONParseError cause {#object-parse-json-parse-error}

- **What changed:** when `GenerateObject`, `StreamObject` or an `Output`
  cannot parse the model's text, the `NoObjectGeneratedError.Cause` (and the
  error `RepairText` receives) is a `*providererrors.JSONParseError` holding
  the text, instead of the raw `*json.SyntaxError`. The parser error is its
  `Unwrap()`. TS: `NoObjectGeneratedError.cause` is a `JSONParseError`
  (review round 72).
- **Am I affected?** Code that type-asserts `Cause.(*json.SyntaxError)` or
  compares the cause or the `RepairText` error directly. `errors.As` with
  `*json.SyntaxError` still works.
- **What to do:** match with `errors.As`:

  ```go
  func isUnparsable(err error) bool {
  	var nog *ai.NoObjectGeneratedError
  	if !errors.As(err, &nog) {
  		return false
  	}
  	// v0.5: _, ok := nog.Cause.(*json.SyntaxError)
  	return providererrors.IsJSONParseError(nog.Cause)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Output validation failures have a TypeValidationError cause {#output-type-validation-cause}

- **What changed:** when the value an `ObjectOutput`, `ArrayOutput` or
  `ChoiceOutput` parsed fails validation (schema, missing `elements` or
  `result`, array length, an element, or a choice not in the options), the
  `NoObjectGeneratedError.Cause` is a `*providererrors.TypeValidationError`
  holding the value, as in TS. Its text is `Type validation failed: Value:
  <json>.\nError message: <reason>`. v0.5 used the validator's error or a
  plain error, and the choice reason now ends with a period, as in TS. The
  validator's error is still the cause's `Unwrap()` (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Code that type-asserts or compares
  `NoObjectGeneratedError.Cause` from `GenerateText` / `StreamText` with an
  `Output`, or matches its text.
- **What to do:** match with `errors.As`:

  ```go
  func isInvalidOutput(err error) bool {
  	var nog *ai.NoObjectGeneratedError
  	var tve *providererrors.TypeValidationError
  	return errors.As(err, &nog) && errors.As(nog.Cause, &tve)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Empty provider error messages are kept {#empty-provider-error-message}

- **What changed:** An error body or stream error whose `message` is `""` produced a generic or fallback text (the raw JSON for DeepSeek stream errors, the default "xAI STT error" / "OpenAI realtime error"). v0.6 uses the empty message, as the TS schemas accept it. Groq and Cerebras HTTP errors also follow their TS schemas: a body missing a required field (Groq `type`; Cerebras `type`, `param` or `code`) is not parsed as a provider error (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** `ProviderError.Message` / `StreamProviderError.Message` from moonshot, fireworks, zai, xai, deepseek, groq, cerebras, OpenAI batch item errors (`BatchError.Message`, `BatchError.Code`), xAI / OpenAI realtime transcription stream errors, `providererrors.NormalizeStreamProviderError`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the fallback texts.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Response header maps keep the last Set-Cookie line {#set-cookie-last-line}

- **What changed:** Response header maps joined repeated `Set-Cookie` lines with `, `. v0.6 keeps the last line, as TS `extractResponseHeaders` (`Object.fromEntries` over fetch's Headers iterator, which yields each Set-Cookie line separately); other repeated headers stay joined (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** `ProviderError.ResponseHeaders["Set-Cookie"]`, `ResponseMetadata.Headers`, `providerutils.ExtractHeaders`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you parse several cookies from that value.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider-package argument errors have no parameter prefix {#invalid-argument-bare-message}

- **What changed:** TS has two `AI_InvalidArgumentError` classes: the `ai` package's (`Invalid argument for parameter <x>: <message>`) and `@ai-sdk/provider`'s, whose message is the bare text. Go errors that port the provider class (provider packages, `parseProviderOptions`' `invalid <provider> provider options`, provider-utils such as `CreateIDGenerator`, `NewDecisionLanguageModel`, base URL validation) now have the bare message, built with `providererrors.NewArgumentError`. The type, its fields and `errors.As` matching are unchanged (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches `Invalid argument for parameter` in provider error text.
- **What to do:** Match with `errors.As` on `*providererrors.InvalidArgumentError` and read `Field`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Error bodies with `__proto__` keys give the status text {#error-body-secure-parse}

- **What changed:** TS parses error bodies with secureJsonParse, so a body with a `__proto__` (or `constructor.prototype`) key does not parse: the message is the response status text and no error data is kept. v0.6 does the same in every provider's error handler (`providererrors`, the shared nested error schema and each provider's own parser). Groq, Z.ai and Fireworks also give the status text for a body that does not match their error schema (v0.5 said `HTTP <code>: <body>`), and Perplexity keeps no data for such a body (review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches provider error text or reads `ProviderError.Data` for unusual error bodies; Groq, Z.ai and Fireworks error text for non-JSON or non-schema bodies.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match that text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### JSON payloads with `__proto__` keys fail like TS {#json-proto-payloads-fail}

- **What changed:** v0.5, and v0.6 up to review round 95 as a known difference, accepted JSON with a `__proto__` key or a `constructor` object with a `prototype` key. v0.6 rejects it wherever TS parses with `secureJsonParse` / `safeParseJSON`, with TS's `*providererrors.JSONParseError` (`JSON parsing failed: Text: <text>.` and `Error message: Object contains forbidden prototype property`): a provider or server response body fails the call (as the cause where TS reports "Invalid JSON response"); a stream chunk or WebSocket event is an error part (finish reason `error` where TS sets it) and its raw chunk value is nil; a tool-call input is an `InvalidToolInputError`; `GenerateObject`, `StreamObject` and `Output` parsing give `NoObjectGeneratedError` "No object generated: could not parse the response."; `jsonparser.ParsePartialJSON` returns the failed-parse state; `DecisionLanguageModel` gives "Decision did not return valid JSON." (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** Only if a provider, proxy, mock server or model returns JSON with a `__proto__` key or a `constructor.prototype` member, at any depth. Search test fixtures for `__proto__`.
- **What to do:** No action unless your fixtures or servers send such keys. Match the failure with `errors.As(err, &jsonErr)` for `var jsonErr *providererrors.JSONParseError`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider errors follow each provider's TS error schema {#error-schema-message-data}

- **What changed:** TS `createJsonErrorResponseHandler({errorSchema, errorToMessage})` gives the schema message and the parsed data (undeclared keys stripped) for a matching body, and the HTTP status text with no data otherwise. v0.5 read many body shapes (`error.message`, `message`, `detail`, ...) and kept any JSON body as `ProviderError.Data`. v0.6 follows each schema: Cohere (`{message}`), Mistral (`{object: "error", message, type, param, code}`), Together reranking and images (`{error: {message}}`); Fireworks images (TS createStatusCodeErrorResponseHandler) and Fireworks embeddings outside `fireworksErrorStructure` give the status text. The generic extraction remains only for providers with no TS twin (Ollama, LMNT, the Replicate language model) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches `ProviderError.Message` or reads `ProviderError.Data` from `cohere`, `mistral`, `together` reranking / image or `fireworks` image / embedding errors.
- **What to do:** Read `pe.ResponseBody` for the raw body; expect `Data` only for schema bodies.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### VideoGenerationError text is the provider message {#video-generation-error-text}

- **What changed:** `providererrors.VideoGenerationError.Error()` returned `video generation failed [provider=<p>, model=<m>]: <message>`. v0.6 returns the message alone (`: <cause>` appended when set), as TS provider video models throw `AISDKError({message})` (for example BFL `BLACK_FOREST_LABS_VIDEO_GENERATION_FAILED` carries the status error). `Provider` and `ModelID` are still fields (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `video generation failed [provider=` in error text (BFL, Topaz, Google, fal video `DoGenerate`).
- **What to do:** Match the message, or read `Provider` / `ModelID` with `errors.As`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Missing settings return LoadSettingError {#load-setting-error-typed}

- **What changed:** A missing AWS region (Bedrock, Bedrock Mantle, Bedrock Anthropic, Anthropic AWS), Google Vertex project or location (Gemini, MaaS, Vertex xAI) or KlingAI access / secret key is a typed `*providererrors.LoadSettingError` with TS `loadSetting` text (`<description> setting is missing. Pass it using the '<name>' parameter or the <ENV> environment variable.`). v0.5 returned plain errors (Mantle and Anthropic AWS without the final period; KlingAI wrapped it as `klingai error (code 401): Authentication failed - ...`; `*klingai.Error` still matches) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching those error strings.
- **What to do:** Use `providererrors.IsLoadSettingError(err)` or `errors.As`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Error and warning texts quote values like TS (round 90) {#r90-template-quotes}

- **What changed:** Messages that quote a value use the TS template form `"value"` instead of Go `%q` escaping: registry `Files()` / `Skills()` unsupported errors, WorkflowAgent "does not require approval", the WorkflowChatTransport orphan warning (with TS's docs link); MiniMax video, QuiverAI, Topaz video, Vercel snapshot timeout and Cursor auth warnings; Google, Gateway and Perplexity errors (Perplexity's unknown role reads `Unsupported role: <role>`); OpenAI, Azure, xAI and the other provb provider warnings; BFL video and Cartesia texts (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching these texts for values that contain quotes, backslashes or non-ASCII characters.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match exact text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### ProviderError.Data is the TS error schema output {#provider-error-data-schema}

- **What changed:** TS `createJsonErrorResponseHandler` sets `APICallError.data` to the error schema's output (undeclared keys removed, loose objects kept) for a matching body and leaves it undefined otherwise; `createStatusCodeErrorResponseHandler` never sets it. v0.5 kept any parsed JSON body as `ProviderError.Data`. v0.6 follows the schemas for Cartesia, Deepgram, fal, Luma, Replicate, AssemblyAI, Gladia, BFL, KlingAI, ByteDance, OpenAI (and the OpenAI-compatible wrappers), Azure, DeepSeek, Together, xAI, Fireworks, Z.AI, GMI Cloud, Hugging Face, Alibaba, Moonshot, Open Responses, Cerebras, Baseten, QuiverAI, Voyage, Hume, Prodia, ElevenLabs, Fish Audio and Topaz, and for the status-code download errors. Deepgram keeps the v0.5.2 `Data["requestId"]` key. Cerebras and Baseten also give the status text for a body outside their schema, and a status-code download error always gives the status text (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading undeclared keys from `ProviderError.Data`, or relying on `Data` (or the message) for a body outside the provider's error schema.
- **What to do:** Read `pe.ResponseBody` for the raw body.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Open Responses error message follows its schema {#openresponses-error-schema}

- **What changed:** v0.5 picked a message from any of `error.message` / `message` / `detail`. v0.6 uses the message only for a body matching `{error: {message, type?, param?, code?}}`, else the status text, as the TS default error handler (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching Open Responses error messages for non-standard error bodies.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match that text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Generated-image download errors are not wrapped {#image-download-errors}

- **What changed:** OpenAI, OpenAI-compatible, xAI, Fireworks and Replicate image downloads wrapped failures as `failed to download image: ...`. v0.6 returns a URL validation, transport or abort error unchanged (`*DownloadError`, context errors) and an HTTP failure as a `ProviderError` with the status text and no data, as TS getFromApi with createStatusCodeErrorResponseHandler. OpenAI edit inputs are downloaded before the request (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `failed to download image`.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As` / `errors.Is`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### KlingAI and ByteDance AISDKError texts {#video-aisdk-error-text}

- **What changed:** Errors TS throws as AISDKError have the bare TS text (`No task ID returned from API`, `KlingAI Motion Control requires providerOptions.klingai with videoUrl, characterOrientation, and mode.`, `No videos in response. Response: ...`), not `klingai error (<NAME>): ...` / `bytedance error (code 500): Video generation failed - ...`. The types and `ErrorCode` are unchanged (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Matching `err.Error()` of `*klingai.Error` / `*bytedance.Error`.
- **What to do:** Match `ErrorCode` or use `errors.As`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Schema provider error headers are lower case {#schema-error-response-headers}

- **What changed:** `NewSchemaProviderError` built `ResponseHeaders` with Go canonical keys (`Retry-After`). v0.6 uses the TS lower-case keys (`retry-after`), like `NewProviderError`; `NewProviderErrorFromResponse` keeps canonical keys. New `(*ProviderError).ResponseHeader(name)` reads a header in either casing (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `ResponseHeaders["Retry-After"]`-style lookups (grep `ResponseHeaders\["[A-Z]`).
- **What to do:** Use `perr.ResponseHeader("retry-after")`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### DownloadError text prefers the cause {#download-error-cause}

- **What changed:** `DownloadError.Error()` used the status text even when a cause was set (`Failed to download u: 200 OK`). v0.6 uses the cause whenever it is set (`Failed to download u: unexpected EOF`), as TS download-error.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching `Failed to download` texts.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Aborted calls return the abort error, not a status-0 ProviderError {#transport-errors-unchanged}

- **What changed:** A cancelled, timed-out or (for some providers) unreachable call was wrapped in a status-0 `*ProviderError`. v0.6 returns an abort or timeout as is (`errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)`, no `*ProviderError`) in every provider error handler, as TS handleFetchError; xAI, Moonshot, DeepSeek, Together, Fireworks, Ollama and Open Responses also return their "Cannot connect to API" error unchanged, and Azure Speech / MAI image and the OpenAI model catalog use it (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Code that type-asserts `*ProviderError` on cancelled or timed-out calls, or matches `provider error (0)` texts.
- **What to do:** Check `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` / `context.DeadlineExceeded` first.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Stream provider error payloads without a string message are kept {#stream-provider-error-normalization}

- **What changed:** `NormalizeStreamProviderError` built a `*StreamProviderError` from a payload whose error had no string message, and skipped an empty `type`. v0.6 returns the error unchanged without a string message and keeps an empty `type`, as TS normalize-stream-provider-error.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Perplexity / Mistral conversation stream errors checked for `*StreamProviderError`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Transport timeouts are the retryable Cannot connect to API error {#transport-timeouts-retryable}

- **What changed:** A connect, TLS or response-header timeout of the HTTP transport (`http.Transport.ResponseHeaderTimeout`, a dialer timeout, an OS `ETIMEDOUT`) was returned as is, like your own context's deadline, and was not retried. v0.6 returns it as the retryable `Cannot connect to API: <cause>` error, which the core retries, and it no longer matches `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)`. This holds for the shared provider client and for the provider calls that use their own `http.Client` (Azure Speech and MAI image, Google batch and the Files processing poll, Vertex Chirp transcription, Bedrock, Anthropic batch results, the OpenAI model catalog, BFL polls and image downloads). Your context's cancellation or deadline and an `http.Client` `Timeout` are still returned unchanged. TS handle-fetch-error.ts retries undici `UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT`, `UND_ERR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` and `ETIMEDOUT` the same way (review round 92). Round 93 applies it to the polls and downloads of BFL video, Replicate, KlingAI, ByteDance, fal, Gladia, Rev.ai and Luma, and to LMNT speech (was `request failed: ...`); and to the Topaz image download, the MiniMax video status poll and image download, the Fireworks, xAI and OpenAI-compatible image downloads and the HeyGen video status poll (TS getFromApi `handleFetchError`): a connection that fails before any response there is the retryable `Cannot connect to API: <cause>` error, not a `*providererrors.DownloadError`. A rejected URL or redirect keeps its `DownloadError`. The Google Files resumable upload and the realtime client secret call `fetch` directly in TS, so their transport failure is returned as is (review round 93).
- **Am I affected?** Code that checks `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)` for transport timeouts, or counts attempts against a slow endpoint; BFL poll or image download errors matched as a raw `*url.Error` value; `errors.As(err, *providererrors.DownloadError)` on a poll or download network failure of those providers.
- **What to do:** Match the transport timeout with `var ne net.Error; errors.As(err, &ne) && ne.Timeout()`. Set `MaxRetries` to 0 to keep a single attempt.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Retry-After headers are read like TS {#retry-after-js-parsing}

- **What changed:** The retry delay read `retry-after-ms` and `retry-after` with `strconv.ParseFloat` and an HTTP date with `http.ParseTime`. v0.6 reads them as TS getRetryDelayInMs does: JS `parseFloat` (a numeric prefix such as `2s` is 2 seconds; an ISO date reads as its year in seconds and is ignored as too long) and `Date.parse` for dates, so a date with a numeric zone is honored (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** Servers that send `Retry-After` / `retry-after-ms` values that are not a plain number or an RFC 1123 date.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The non-retryable RetryError message keeps quotes as is {#retry-non-retryable-message-quotes}

- **What changed:** The `RetryError` message for a non-retryable error after earlier retries wrote embedded single quotes as `\'`. v0.6 writes the message as is, like TS: `Failed after 2 attempts with non-retryable error: 'can't do that'` (retry-with-exponential-backoff.ts; review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches `RetryError.Message` or `Error()` text that contains an apostrophe.
- **What to do:** Match the unescaped text, or use `errors.As` and `RetryError.LastError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### A cancel during a retry backoff returns the context error {#retry-abort-during-backoff}

- **What changed:** When the context ended during the wait between retries, v0.5 returned `context cancelled after N attempts: <last error>`, and v0.6 up to round 94 returned the context's cause. v0.6 returns the context's own error (`context.Canceled` or `context.DeadlineExceeded`), as TS `delay()` rejects with its own `AbortError` whatever the abort reason (review round 95).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches that text, uses `errors.Is` against the last attempt's error, or expects a custom cause after a cancel during retries.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` or `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Provider responses are decoded with exact keys {#provider-response-keys-case-exact}

- **What changed:** Provider response bodies, stream frames and error bodies were decoded with `encoding/json`, which also fills a field from a differently cased key (`"Message"` filled `message`). v0.6 decodes provider responses with exact keys, as the TS zod schemas do: a key that differs only in case is ignored (review round 94). Round 95 extends this to MCP server messages (`pkg/mcp`, including `CallToolResult`, tool lists and OAuth metadata), the Vercel sandbox API responses (`pkg/harness/sandbox/vercel`, including the network policy), the Gateway and Google Interactions lenient decoders, the Open Responses response body, the shared OpenAI-compatible stream decoders, the `askUserQuestions` tool input, a workflow chat transport's finish chunk, the OpenAI Responses prompt tool arguments and outputs that TS validates with zod, and the MCP `Mcp-Name` header source (`params.name` / `params.uri`). Provider options and other values you build keep matching keys without regard to case, as in v0.5 (see the known difference "Option keys match case-insensitively").
- **Am I affected?** Only if a provider, MCP server, sandbox API, proxy or mock server sends response keys whose case differs from the documented API (for example `"Choices"`, `"ID"` or `"IsError"`).
- **What to do:** No action for real provider APIs. Fix mock servers and test fixtures to send the documented key names.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Providers {#providers}

### All providers

#### Provider IDs follow TS config.provider {#provider-ids}

- **What changed:** `Provider()` on models returns the TS `config.provider`
  value: `groq.chat`, `mistral.embedding`, `openai.image` and so on. v0.5
  returned the bare provider name. Telemetry `ai.model.provider` and
  `SerializedModel.Provider` change accordingly. Provider option and metadata
  keys do not change, and payloads serialized by earlier versions still
  restore. Round 90: `perplexity` embedding models report `perplexity.embedding`, Vertex MaaS chat models `vertex.maas.chat` and Vertex xAI chat models `googleVertex.xai.chat`; workflow deserialization accepts the old and new keys.
- **Am I affected?** `model.Provider() == "groq"` and similar comparisons,
  or dashboards grouped by `ai.model.provider`.
- **What to do:** compare the prefix:

  ```go
  isGroq := strings.HasPrefix(model.Provider(), "groq")
  _ = isGroq
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Responses and stream chunks are validated {#response-validation}

- **What changed:** about 25 more providers reject a 2xx body that does not
  match the TS schema with "Invalid JSON response", and malformed stream
  chunks become error parts. Some bodies accepted before now fail, such as
  Cohere's legacy plain-array `embeddings`.
- **Am I affected?** Custom base URLs, proxies or mock servers that return
  non-standard JSON.
- **What to do:** return the provider's documented response shape.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Invalid provider options fail the call {#provider-options-validation}

- **What changed:** invalid provider options fail with
  `*InvalidArgumentError` across chat, embedding, media and transcription
  providers, as TS `parseProviderOptions`. A missing API key fails at request
  time with `LoadAPIKeyError`, and unsupported model types return
  `*NoSuchModelError` with the TS text. The OpenAI and Azure Responses
  models (the default OpenAI language model) validate their provider options
  against the TS Responses options schema; these now fail with
  `*InvalidArgumentError` (review round 76):
  - a wrong value type for any documented option (for example a number for
    `store`, a string for `parallelToolCalls`);
  - an unknown enum value for `serviceTier` (`auto`, `flex`, `priority`,
    `fast`, `ultrafast`, `default`), `truncation` (`auto`, `disabled`),
    `textVerbosity` (`low`, `medium`, `high`), `promptCacheRetention`
    (`in_memory`, `24h`), `systemMessageMode` (`system`, `developer`,
    `remove`), `reasoningEffortUpdate` (`none` to `max`), `reasoningMode`
    (`standard`, `pro`) and `reasoningContext` (`auto`, `current_turn`,
    `all_turns`);
  - an `include` entry other than `reasoning.encrypted_content`,
    `file_search_call.results`, `web_search_call.results` and
    `message.output_text.logprobs`;
  - `logprobs` other than `true`, `false` or a number from 1 to 20 (v0.5
    sent `logprobs: 0` as `top_logprobs: 0`);
  - `allowedTools` without `toolNames`, with an empty `toolNames`, or with a
    `mode` other than `auto` / `required`;
  - a `contextManagement` entry that is not
    `{type: "compaction", compactThreshold: <number>}`;
  - `nil` for `includeWebSearchSources`, `logprobs`, `reasoningEffortUpdate`,
    `reasoningMode`, `reasoningContext`, `passThroughUnsupportedFiles`,
    `systemMessageMode`, `forceReasoning`, `compactionTrigger` or
    `allowedTools` (TS `.optional()` members; the `.nullish()` ones still
    accept `nil`).
  Azure validates `providerOptions["azure"]`, or `providerOptions["openai"]`
  when `azure` is absent or `nil`. Unknown keys are still ignored.
- **Am I affected?** `ProviderOptions:` with wrong value types or values the
  provider does not accept; `providerOptions["openai"]` /
  `providerOptions["azure"]` maps passed to `ResponsesModel` /
  `LanguageModel`, including `"logprobs": 0`.
- **What to do:** fix the options; match these errors with `errors.As`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Anthropic, Bedrock and Google content is in response order {#result-content-order}

- **What changed:** `result.Content` from Anthropic, Bedrock and Google keeps
  the order of the provider's response.
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `Content` by index.
- **What to do:** switch on each part's type instead of its position.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Empty stop sequences are sent {#empty-stop-sequences}

- **What changed:** an explicitly empty `StopSequences` (`[]string{}`) is now
  sent as an empty list by the AI Gateway (`stopSequences: []`), Groq,
  Mistral (chat and conversations) and Vertex xAI (`stop: []`), as TS
  forwards `stopSequences` as given. nil stays absent (review round 74).
  Round 95 adds the OpenAI, DeepSeek, Moonshot, Alibaba, Fireworks,
  Together, Z.AI and GMI Cloud chat APIs (`stop: []`, TS
  `stopSequences != null`); Hugging Face gives its `stopSequences`
  unsupported warning for an empty list too.
  Round 96 adds Ollama (`stop: []`).
- **Am I affected?** Callers that pass a non-nil empty `StopSequences` to
  those providers.
- **What to do:** no action unless an API rejects the empty list; pass nil
  instead.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Chat, completion and OpenAI-compatible streams read past [DONE] {#sse-read-past-done}

- **What changed:** v0.5: OpenAI Chat and completion streams, every
  `OpenAICompatStream` provider (fireworks, together, zai, gmicloud, ollama,
  Vertex xAI), deepseek, alibaba, moonshot, groq, mistral (chat and
  Conversations), the AI Gateway language model, Google and Vertex Gemini,
  Perplexity, Hugging Face, Cohere and openresponses ended (or flushed and
  finished) the stream at a `data: [DONE]` frame. v0.6 skips the frame and
  keeps reading; the finish part comes once, when the response body ends, so
  frames after `[DONE]` are processed. TS `parseJsonEventStream` drops
  `[DONE]` and its `flush()` runs at the end of the body (review round 78;
  the OpenAI, Azure and xAI Responses streams changed in round 77, see
  [Responses [DONE] and created_at](#responses-stream-done-created-at)).
  Google Interactions background streams (`Interactions(...)` with
  `background: true`) no longer count a `data: [DONE]` frame as an event of
  a resume attempt, so a server that sends only `[DONE]` makes the stream
  fail after 3 attempts with `google.interactions: SSE stream closed without
  producing any events.` instead of reconnecting forever, as TS
  `streamGoogleInteractionEvents` (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** Only if a server sends SSE frames after `data: [DONE]`,
  or keeps the connection open after it: `DoStream` on `openai.ChatModel`,
  `openai.CompletionModel`, `deepseek`, `alibaba`, `moonshot`, `groq`,
  `mistral` (`LanguageModel`, `ConversationModel`), `gateway`,
  `google`/`googlevertex`, `perplexity`, `huggingface`, `cohere`,
  `openresponses`, and providers built on `streaming.NewOpenAICompatStream`;
  `google` Interactions background streams against a server that sends
  only `[DONE]`.
- **What to do:** no action unless your server keeps the body open after
  `[DONE]`; close the response body (or cancel the context) when the stream
  is done.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### created: 0 is the epoch, and first-chunk response metadata {#chat-created-zero-epoch}

- **What changed:** v0.5 gave no `ResponseMetadata.Timestamp` when a
  deepseek, alibaba, moonshot, groq or mistral response (generate or stream)
  carried `created: 0`, when an OpenAI Responses batch result had
  `created_at: 0`, or when an openresponses result had `created_at: 0`.
  v0.6 reports the Unix epoch, as TS `createLanguageModelResponseMetadata`
  (`created != null`) and open-responses `new Date(created_at * 1000)`. groq
  and mistral also accept a fractional `created` with millisecond precision
  (v0.5 failed the response). deepseek, alibaba, groq and mistral streams
  now emit their `ChunkTypeResponseMetadata` part on the first valid chunk
  even when it has no id, model or created (TS `isFirstChunk`). Alibaba
  `DoGenerate` now returns `ResponseMetadata` (id, model ID, timestamp,
  headers); v0.5 returned none. OpenAI image provider metadata keeps
  `created: 0` and empty `size`/`quality`/`background`/`outputFormat` (TS
  `response.x ?? undefined`). OpenAI Chat and OpenAI-compatible chat keep
  treating `created: 0` as absent (TS `created || undefined`), and
  `providerutils.BuildResponseMetadata` still treats 0 as absent (review
  round 78). A fractional `created` / `created_at` (TS `z.number()`) no
  longer fails the call or the stream chunk: deepseek, alibaba, moonshot,
  OpenAI chat / completion / image / Responses / batch / files / skills, xAI
  Responses / batch / files, Hugging Face, openresponses, the
  OpenAI-compatible stream and the gmicloud, together, fireworks, zai,
  ollama and Vertex xAI generate paths decode it as a float and set
  `Timestamp` to `new Date(created * 1000)` (milliseconds kept). The
  OpenAI-compatible generate paths keep `created: 0` as no timestamp (TS
  `created || undefined`). openresponses `created_at: null` is the epoch;
  an absent Hugging Face stream `created_at` is no timestamp (was the
  epoch). The exported `OpenResponsesResponse.CreatedAt`,
  `responses.ResponsesAPIResponse.CreatedAt` and `ResponseCreatedEvent`
  fields keep their `int64` type (whole seconds). Provider metadata
  `createdAt`, `expiresAt`, `updatedAt` and `created` stay `int64` for whole
  seconds and are `float64` for a fractional value (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** Code that checks `ResponseMetadata.Timestamp.IsZero()`
  on these providers, counts `ChunkTypeResponseMetadata` stream parts, reads
  `GenerateResult.ResponseMetadata` from alibaba (was nil), or reads
  `ProviderMetadata["openai"]["images"]` entries' `created`, `size`,
  `quality`, `background` or `outputFormat` keys; code that type-asserts
  `ProviderMetadata["openai"]["images"][i]["created"]` or the `createdAt`,
  `expiresAt`, `updatedAt` metadata values to `int64` (a fractional wire
  value is a `float64`).
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the missing timestamp,
  metadata part or keys, or on a fractional `created` failing the call.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Non-stream responses accept fractional numbers {#json-response-fractional-numbers}

- **What changed:** v0.5 failed `DoGenerate` (and other non-stream calls)
  with `failed to decode JSON response: json: cannot unmarshal number 1.5
  into Go struct field ... of type int` when a count or other integer field
  of the response held a fractional or exponent number. TS response schemas
  use `z.number()`, which accepts them. v0.6 decodes such a body again with
  the numbers under the reported fields truncated toward zero (as the
  streams already did), so the call succeeds; integer counts keep their
  integer part, and float fields keep their exact value. A body that decodes
  as before is unchanged (review round 79). When such a body also violates the schema elsewhere, the error names that violation (Go's decoder reports only the first type error), not the accepted fractional number. Since review round 80 this also covers the responses decoded outside `DoJSON`: OpenAI and xAI files (`bytes`), realtime client secrets (`expires_at`), the OpenAI Responses batch body and result lines, OpenAI decisions, xAI image/video `cost_in_usd_ticks` and batch results, Azure MAI image usage and Azure speech transcription, and OpenAI-compatible generate `accepted_prediction_tokens` / `rejected_prediction_tokens` (gmicloud, together, fireworks, zai, ollama, Vertex xAI).
- **Am I affected?** Only if you relied on such a response failing the
  call, for example with an OpenAI-compatible server that sends
  `"prompt_tokens": 1.5`. `FileMetadataResult.ByteSize`, `ClientSecretResult.ExpiresAt` and `XAIImageMetadata.CostInUsdTicks` receive the truncated value.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the decode error.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### An empty finish_reason is a finish reason in deepseek, alibaba and moonshot streams {#stream-empty-finish-reason}

- **What changed:** v0.5 deepseek, alibaba and moonshot streams treated
  `"finish_reason": ""` as no finish reason (in v0.6 a stream without one
  finishes with `FinishReasonInterrupted`). v0.6 records it, as TS
  `finish_reason != null`: the finish part has `FinishReasonOther` and
  `RawFinishReason: ""` (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** Code that checks `FinishReasonInterrupted` on these
  providers' streams when the server sends an empty `finish_reason`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on that.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Base64 string file payloads are forwarded as given {#file-data-string-forwarded}

- **What changed:** for a file part with `FileData{Type: FileDataTypeData,
  DataString: ...}`, v0.5 Anthropic (and Bedrock Anthropic / Vertex Anthropic)
  decoded the string with `base64.StdEncoding` and re-encoded it (a string
  that is not standard base64 failed the call, base64url was rewritten),
  Google and Vertex Gemini re-encoded a string `base64.StdEncoding` accepted
  (a newline was dropped) and could not detect a bare media type (`image`,
  `image/*`) from an unpadded or base64url string, and Mistral, Cohere image
  parts and Perplexity image input sent an empty data URL because they read
  only `FileData.Data`. v0.6 forwards the string unchanged, as TS
  `convertToBase64` does, and decodes it like TS `convertBase64ToUint8Array`
  only where the bytes are needed (Anthropic text/plain documents and media
  type detection) (review round 81). Round 82 extends this to the shared
  OpenAI chat / Groq / OpenAI-compatible converter (image, audio, PDF and
  text parts; a compatible `text/*` part decodes the string with
  `convertBase64ToUint8Array`), alibaba (image parts), moonshot (image and
  video data URLs; `text/*` parts decode the string), deepseek (image and
  tool-result images; v0.6 drafts decoded and re-encoded the string,
  rewriting base64url or unpadded strings and dropping undecodable ones),
  huggingface (image parts), openresponses (user and tool-result file parts,
  which were dropped) and the OpenAI Responses media type sniff: before,
  these sent an empty payload.
- **Am I affected?** `FileData.DataString` file parts sent to `anthropic`,
  `bedrock` (Anthropic models), `googlevertex/anthropic`, `google`,
  `googlevertex`, `mistral`, `cohere`, `perplexity`, `openai` (chat and
  Responses), `groq`, OpenAI-compatible providers, `alibaba`, `moonshot`,
  `deepseek`, `huggingface` or `openresponses`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the old error for a string
  that is not base64.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### A base64 string file payload is sniffed from its prefix only {#file-data-string-prefix-sniff}

- **What changed:** v0.5: a `FileData.DataString` payload with a bare media
  type (`image`, `image/*`, `application`) was decoded whole to detect the
  subtype, so a string whose tail is not base64 failed (or was "could not be
  auto-detected"). v0.6: only the sniffed prefix is decoded, as TS
  `detectMediaType` (`decodePrefix`); a prefix that is not base64 returns
  the base64 decode error (TS `atob` throws). This covers Anthropic, Google
  and Google Interactions, Perplexity, Mistral, Cohere, Bedrock, the OpenAI
  Responses converter, and the shared OpenAI chat / Groq / OpenAI-compatible
  converter (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** `FileData{Type: types.FileDataTypeData, DataString: ...}`
  with a `MediaType` without a subtype.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the old "could not be
  auto-detected" error for an undecodable string.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Speech request.body keeps the TS key order {#speech-request-body-key-order}

- **What changed:** `SpeechResult.Request.Body` for Google Gemini TTS, Google
  Vertex Chirp 3: HD (Cloud TTS) and Mistral speech was marshaled from Go
  maps, which sort keys. v0.6 keeps the key order of the TS request literal
  (`JSON.stringify(requestBody)`) (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** code that compares `SpeechResult.Request.Body` as a
  string for `google`, `googlevertex` or `mistral` speech models.
- **What to do:** compare the parsed JSON instead of the string.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Alibaba, Moonshot and OpenAI completion: an empty choices array is an error {#chat-empty-choices-typeerror}

- **What changed:** v0.5 returned an empty result (finish reason `other`, no
  usage) from `DoGenerate` when the response had `"choices": []`. v0.6
  follows TS, which reads `choices[0]` without a guard: Alibaba and Moonshot
  return the error `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'message')`,
  OpenAI completion `... (reading 'logprobs')` and OpenAI-compatible
  completion `... (reading 'text')` (review round 81). Mistral already did
  this ([Mistral empty choices](#mistral-empty-choices-error)); OpenAI chat,
  DeepSeek and the OpenAI-compatible chat providers return
  `InvalidResponseDataError` "Response did not contain any choices." as TS
  does.
- **Am I affected?** `alibaba` / `moonshot` chat `DoGenerate`, or `openai`
  `CompletionModel` (`OpenAICompatible` or not) `DoGenerate`, against an
  endpoint that can answer `"choices": []`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the empty result; treat the
  error as a malformed response.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### A streamed tool call without a function name ends the stream {#chat-stream-missing-tool-name-no-finish}

- **What changed:** v0.5 deferred a streamed tool call whose first delta had
  no `function.name`: the OpenAI, Groq, Alibaba, DeepSeek, Moonshot and
  Mistral (chat and Conversations) streams kept emitting parts, promoted the
  call when a later delta named it, and otherwise emitted the error chunk
  `Expected 'function.name' to be a string.` when the stream ended (OpenAI
  chat followed it with a finish chunk). v0.6 follows TS, whose
  `StreamingToolCallTracker` throws `InvalidResponseDataError` from
  `processDelta` as soon as such a call starts: the stream ends with that
  error chunk (`Err` is a `*providererrors.InvalidResponseDataError`), with
  no later part, tool call or finish chunk. A new call whose `type` fails
  the OpenAI / Groq check (`Expected 'function' type.`) ends the stream the
  same way. OpenAI-compatible streams (`openai.Config{OpenAICompatible:
  true}` and the providers built on `streaming.OpenAICompatStream`) keep TS
  `pendingToolCalls`: a nameless delta with an `index` waits for its name,
  and a call never named ends the stream with the error and no finish chunk;
  a nameless delta without an `index` ends it at once (review round 81).
  They port TS `pendingToolCalls` exactly (review round 82): a call whose
  `function.name` never arrives fails at the end of the stream *before* any
  tool call is finalized, so no `tool-call` part of another call precedes
  the error; a nameless pending entry without an id that resolves to the
  only unfinished call continues it instead of failing; the first non-null
  `id` and `extra_content` of a pending index are kept (a present empty `id`
  is not replaced by a later one); a thought signature is taken only from
  the delta that creates the call.
- **Am I affected?** Stream consumers of those providers that expect a
  finish chunk, or later parts, after that error, or that relied on a
  native (not OpenAI-compatible) provider naming a call after its first
  delta; callers of `streaming.StreamingToolCallTracker.BufferNamelessIndexedCalls`,
  `ToolCallDelta.IDPresent` and `ToolCallDelta.MetadataPresent`.
- **What to do:** treat the error chunk as the end of the stream.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### An unknown tool choice type fails like TS {#unknown-tool-choice-type}

- **What changed:** v0.5 sent a `types.ToolChoice{Type: X}` whose type is not
  auto, none, required or tool as `"auto"` (moonshot dropped it; anthropic,
  google, mistral, cohere and bedrock sent auto or nothing). v0.6, with at
  least one tool, fails before the request with
  `*providererrors.UnsupportedFunctionalityError` `'tool choice type: X'
  functionality not supported.`, the TS `prepareTools` exhaustive check, in
  openai chat and Responses, azure, xai Responses, alibaba, moonshot,
  together, fireworks, gmicloud, ollama, cerebras, deepinfra,
  `openai.Config.OpenAICompatible`, anthropic, google and Vertex (function
  tools only), groq (unless the JSON response tool replaces the choice),
  mistral (chat and, since review round 84, the Conversations model
  `Provider.ConversationModel`), cohere and amazon-bedrock (standard tools; Anthropic models
  through the Anthropic tool preparation). huggingface returns the TS error
  text `Unsupported tool choice type: [object Object]`; open-responses sends
  `"tool_choice": "X"` as given; deepseek and zai already warn and send none
  ([DeepSeek](#deepseek-unknown-tool-choice)) (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** Code that builds `types.ToolChoice` with a custom
  `Type` string, or converts one from user input without checking it.
- **What to do:** Use `types.ToolChoiceAuto`, `types.ToolChoiceNone`,
  `types.ToolChoiceRequired` or `types.ToolChoiceTool`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Text file parts are decoded like TS TextDecoder {#text-file-part-textdecoder}

- **What changed:** v0.5 turned the bytes of a text file part into a Go
  string (invalid runs collapsed to one U+FFFD, or kept raw; a leading BOM
  kept). v0.6 decodes them as TS `new TextDecoder().decode(bytes)`: a leading
  UTF-8 BOM is dropped and every maximal invalid subpart becomes one U+FFFD.
  This covers `text/*` file parts sent to OpenAI-compatible chat models
  (openai `Config.OpenAICompatible`, together, fireworks, gmicloud, zai,
  ollama, Vertex xai) and Moonshot, Anthropic `text/plain` document sources
  (TS `convertBytesDataToString`), Cohere document text, Prodia text outputs
  and the web fetch tool's UTF-8 bodies (a truncated sequence is one U+FFFD,
  as TS). The MCP event webhook and Prodia job metadata parse their JSON from
  the decoded text, as TS, so a body with a leading BOM is accepted (it was
  a 400 / parse error) (review round 83). Amazon Bedrock event-stream
  payloads (Converse stream events, Bedrock-Anthropic chunk bytes and
  exception messages), Groq `responseFormat: "text"` transcriptions and the
  xAI video status body are decoded the same way (a BOM-prefixed Bedrock
  event used to be a JSON parse error part); for provider JSON, error, SSE,
  JSON Lines and WebSocket bodies see
  [Provider UTF-8 decoding](#sse-invalid-utf8) (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `types.FileContent` / `FileData` byte data with a
  `text/...` media type whose bytes start with `EF BB BF` or are not valid
  UTF-8; `toolwebfetch` results of non-UTF-8 bodies;
  `ExperimentalCreateMCPEventWebhook` senders that prefix a BOM.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the BOM or the raw bytes
  reaching the model.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Provider streams, JSON Lines, WebSocket frames and error bodies decode UTF-8 like TS {#sse-invalid-utf8}

- **What changed:** v0.5 parsed provider bytes as given: encoding/json writes one
  U+FFFD per invalid byte, and a leading BOM made a JSON body or event fail
  to parse. v0.6 decodes them as TS does before parsing: SSE event data (TS
  `TextDecoderStream`, every SSE provider), JSON Lines batch results (TS
  `parseJsonLines`: the stream's leading BOM dropped), WebSocket frames (TS
  `readWebSocketMessageText`: a text frame's leading BOM dropped, a binary
  frame through TextDecoder), JSON response bodies through the shared HTTP
  client and provider error bodies (`ProviderError.Message`, `Data` and
  `ResponseBody`; TS `response.text()`). Each maximal invalid UTF-8 subpart is
  one U+FFFD (review round 84). Round 85 extends this to the JSON bodies
  providers read without the shared client: anthropic files and skills,
  google files, batch and realtime, Vertex transcription and Cloud TTS,
  bedrock, perplexity, mistral speech and transcription, heygen, groq
  transcription, gateway config, credits, spend, generation and realtime;
  openai, xai, deepseek, together and azure files, batch uploads, skills,
  realtime client secrets, transcription and images, and the xai, alibaba
  and azure Speech / MAI error parsers; topaz, deepinfra, prodia, cartesia,
  bfl, luma, fal, klingai and replicate, the grokbuild and opencode
  subscription lookups and the OPA policy client. Round 86 extends this to
  the error bodies of streamed and raw requests: every provider's stream
  error (the shared client's `DoStream`), and anthropic (with the Bedrock
  and Vertex wrappers), Google and Vertex, bedrock, heygen, perplexity,
  groq, elevenlabs and gateway; the Bedrock Anthropic error rewrite writes
  the TS `JSON.stringify` text (`{"type":"error","error":{"type":"error",
  "message":...}}`, no HTML escapes) to `ResponseBody`.
- **Am I affected?** Provider responses, events or frames that carry invalid UTF-8 or
  start with `EF BB BF`; code comparing `ProviderError.ResponseBody` bytes;
  `ProviderError.Message` of streamed calls whose error body starts with a BOM.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Top-level Reasoning rounds to the closest supported level {#reasoning-closest-supported-level}

- **What changed:** TS `61a315e333` maps a top-level `reasoning` level to the closest level the model supports, rounding down first and never to `none`; an unsupported `none` maps to the lowest level, with a `compatibility` warning. v0.6 does the same: Anthropic sends `xhigh` as `high` on models without xhigh effort and warns for `none` on between_tools models; Groq GPT-OSS sends `none` as `low`; Mistral GLM-5.3 sends `medium` as `low` and `xhigh` as `high`; Perplexity sends `none` as `minimal`; Gemini 2.5 Pro sends `none` as `thinkingBudget: 128` (Pro rejects 0) and Gemini 3 warns for `none`; Bedrock sends `xhigh` as `high` unless the model supports xhigh, gpt-oss `low`/`medium`/`high`, other OpenAI models the value as is (`none` included), and nothing for Nova 2 `none`. OpenAI, xAI, DeepSeek, Moonshot, Fireworks and QuiverAI have their own entries.
- **Am I affected?** `Reasoning:` / `types.ReasoningXHigh` / `types.ReasoningNone` with anthropic, groq, mistral, perplexity, google, googlevertex or bedrock.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the old effort; set the provider option (`effort`, `reasoningEffort`, `reasoningConfig`, `thinkingConfig`) to pin it.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### SSE parsing: all fields decoded, eventsource-parser field rules {#sse-field-decoding}

- **What changed:** `streaming.SSEParser` decodes invalid UTF-8 in every field (`event`, `id`, `data`) to U+FFFD, ignores an `id` that contains NUL, drops a leading mis-decoded BOM (`ï»¿`), and sets `Retry` only for a value of ASCII digits, as TS eventsource-parser 3.0.8 does.
- **Am I affected?** `SSEEvent.Event`, `SSEEvent.ID`, `SSEEvent.Retry` readers on streams with invalid UTF-8, NUL ids or non-numeric retry values.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### A tool input that is a JSON string holding JSON is sent as that JSON {#string-tool-input-json}

- **What changed:** TS `4ae6986cd8` sends a string tool input whose text is valid JSON as that JSON instead of double-encoding it. In Go such an input is a JSON string literal in `ToolCallContent.Input` / `ToolCall.RawArguments` (for example a Go `string` input `"{\"a\":1}"`). v0.6: Anthropic and Bedrock send it as the object (objects only), Google any value, Mistral, Groq, Cohere and the OpenAI-compatible family the text; OpenAI Chat keeps it when it holds an object (else `{}`). Text that is not JSON is sent as its JSON string by Mistral, Groq, Cohere and the OpenAI-compatible family (v0.5: the raw text). A Google server tool call whose string input is not JSON fails, as TS `secureJsonParse` throws.
- **Am I affected?** Assistant tool calls replayed with a string `Input` or with `RawArguments` that are a JSON string literal or not JSON, on anthropic, bedrock, google, mistral, groq, cohere and OpenAI-compatible providers.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the double-encoded form; pass the input as a map.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### OpenAI Responses and DeepSeek replay non-JSON tool arguments as a JSON string {#tool-call-arguments-text}

- **What changed:** OpenAI Responses and DeepSeek replay tool-call argument text that is not valid JSON as its JSON string literal, and Responses keeps a non-object JSON value instead of `{}` (TS `4ae6986cd8`). The OpenAI-compatible family, Groq and Cohere follow the same rule (see the previous entry). Round 86: OpenAI Responses and DeepSeek also send a JSON string literal holding JSON (`"{\"a\":1}"`) as the JSON it holds; xAI Responses legacy `ToolCalls`, Moonshot and Alibaba (TS `JSON.stringify(part.input)`) send an invalid call's raw argument text as its JSON string literal; Open Responses sends a JSON string literal as the text it holds and invalid text as is. Round 86b: a replayed tool call whose input text is valid JSON is sent as JSON.stringify of the parsed input, like TS (compact, JS number spelling `1.0` -> `1`, `1e2` -> `100`, `-0` -> `0`, array-index keys first, a duplicate key's last value) on OpenAI chat and Responses, xAI Responses, Moonshot, Alibaba, the OpenAI-compatible family, Groq, Cohere, DeepSeek and Mistral; `providerutils.CompactJSON` and zodlite validation `Value:` texts follow the same rule; Responses sends whitespace-only input text, or JSON behind U+00A0, as a JSON string literal instead of `{}`.
- **Am I affected?** Assistant tool calls replayed with `RawArguments` that are not JSON or not an object, on `openai` Responses or `deepseek`; string-literal or invalid `RawArguments` on `xai` Responses, `moonshot`, `alibaba` or `openresponses`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### AI Gateway

#### Provider tools reach the AI Gateway as provider tools {#gateway-provider-tools}

- **What changed:** the Anthropic provider tools (`pkg/providers/anthropic/tools`,
  all 23 constructors), the OpenAI tools (`openaitool.*`, `CustomTool.ToTool`,
  `responses.NewLocalShellTool`, `NewShellTool`, `NewApplyPatchTool`,
  `NewComputerTool`, `NewProgrammaticToolCallingTool`) and the xai tools
  (`xai.WebSearch`, `XSearch`, `CodeExecution`, `ViewImage`, `ViewXVideo`,
  `ImageGeneration`, `FileSearch`, `MCPServer`) now carry the TS provider tool
  identity: `Type: types.ToolTypeProviderDefined` (`"provider"`),
  `ProviderID` (the TS id, equal to the tool `Name`, for example
  `anthropic.web_search_20250305`) and `ProviderArgs` (the TS camelCase args,
  for example `{"maxUses": 3, "allowedDomains": ["example.com"]}`; `{}` for
  tools without config). A gateway model sends them as provider tools
  (`{type: "provider", id, name, args}`) as TS does; v0.5 sent them as
  function tools named `anthropic.*` / `openai.*` / `xai.*`, which the routed
  model treated as client functions. The xai MCP args include
  `authorization`, as TS sends it. The google tools (`GoogleSearchTool`,
  `CodeExecutionTool`, ...) now have a `Name` (`google_search`,
  `code_execution`, ...), so the gateway gets a non-empty tool name, and are
  `ProviderExecuted` with TS's input schemas. Other effects, all as in TS:
  chat models of other providers (and Bedrock, for non-Anthropic provider
  tools) drop these tools with an `unsupported` `provider-defined tool <id>`
  warning instead of sending them as functions; the Mistral conversation
  model drops every non-Mistral provider tool; realtime tool definitions skip
  them; telemetry tool definitions (`gen_ai.tool.definitions`) record
  `{type: "provider", name, id, args}`; an Anthropic Message Batch that
  contains `ComputerToolset20260801()` fails with
  `UnsupportedFunctionalityError` (its calls come back named `computer`);
  `ComputerToolset20260801` rejects an unknown member in its configs with a
  `TypeValidationError`; an Anthropic, OpenAI or xai provider tool given only
  `Type` / `ProviderID` / `ProviderArgs` (for example after
  `workflow.ResolveSerializableTools`) is now sent with its configuration
