  instead of dropped as unsupported or sent bare. Direct Anthropic, OpenAI
  Responses and xai requests are unchanged (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Gateway models with `anthropic/tools`, `openai/tool`,
  `responses.New*Tool()` or `xai` tool constructors or the google tools; code
  that reads `Tool.Type`, `ProviderID`, `ProviderArgs`, `Name` or
  `ProviderExecuted` of those tools (`Tool.Type == "provider"` checks);
  these tools passed to other providers or to realtime models;
  `ExperimentalBatch` requests with `ComputerToolset20260801`.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### AI Gateway tools have no title {#gateway-tool-title}

- **What changed:** `tools.NewExaSearch`, `NewParallelSearch`,
  `NewPerplexitySearch`, `NewTakoSearch`, `NewBrowserbaseSearch` and
  `NewBrowserbaseFetch` (`pkg/providers/gateway/tools`) no longer set
  `Tool.Title` ("Exa Search", ...). TS gateway tools have no title, so their
  tool calls, results and UI message parts carry none; v0.5 sent `title` on
  them. Their args also keep an explicitly empty list
  (`IncludeDomains: []string{}` sends `includeDomains: []`), as TS sends the
  config object (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `Title` of these tools or
  `ToolCall.Title` / the UI part `title` of their calls; callers that pass
  empty, non-nil domain or filter slices.
- **What to do:** no action unless you display the title. Set it yourself:

  ```go
  package main

  import (
  	"fmt"

  	"github.com/digitallysavvy/go-ai/pkg/provider/types"
  	gatewaytools "github.com/digitallysavvy/go-ai/pkg/providers/gateway/tools"
  )

  func exaSearchWithTitle() types.Tool {
  	search := types.Tool(gatewaytools.NewExaSearch(gatewaytools.ExaSearchConfig{}))
  	search.Title = "Exa Search"
  	return search
  }

  func main() {
  	fmt.Println(exaSearchWithTitle().Title)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Gateway tool outputs without a value {#gateway-tool-output-null}

- **What changed:** a tool result whose output value is nil (a tool that
  returned nothing: `{Type: "json", Value: nil}`) reaches the AI Gateway as
  `{type: "json", value: null}`, as TS; an empty `content` output is sent with
  `value: []`. v0.5 omitted `value`, which the gateway's prompt schema
  requires (review round 74). A tool's empty `InputExamples` list (non-nil)
  is sent as `inputExamples: []`. A stream `error` part whose `error` is a
  string or number is an error part carrying that text; v0.5 failed the
  stream with a parse error (review round 76). A stream `error` part whose
  error object has a field of an unexpected type (for example a string
  `statusCode`) is still an error part, with that field left unset; v0.5
  failed the stream with a parse error (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** Gateway models with tools that return nil, or `content`
  tool outputs without parts.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Gateway returns the caller's cancellation unchanged {#gateway-caller-cancellation}

- **What changed:** TS `9ca8bcf731` (ruling 4): `context.Canceled` and the caller's ctx deadline are returned unchanged. v0.5 turned a cancellation into a retryable 500 `GatewayResponseError` "Invalid error response format" and the caller's deadline into a `GatewayTimeoutError`. A transport timeout while the ctx is live is still a `GatewayTimeoutError`.
- **Am I affected?** `IsGatewayTimeoutError` / `GatewayResponseError` checks around ctx cancellation on gateway language, video and realtime client secret calls.
- **What to do:** Check `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)` / `context.Canceled`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Anthropic

#### Anthropic provider tools are matched by id {#anthropic-provider-tool-id}

- **What changed:** the Anthropic provider now selects provider tools by
  their id (`Tool.ProviderID` for `Type: "provider"` tools, else `Tool.Name`),
  as TS does with `tool.id`, and treats `Tool.Name` as the caller's tool name
  (TS: the tools record key). A provider tool under a custom name (a
  constructor tool with `Name` changed, or a TS-shaped
  `types.Tool{Type: "provider", ProviderID: "anthropic.web_search_20260318", Name: "research", ProviderArgs: ...}`)
  is sent; v0.5 dropped it with an `unsupported` "provider-defined tool"
  warning. Its calls and results come back under the custom name. A tool
  literal named after a known Anthropic id without options
  (`types.Tool{Name: "anthropic.web_search_20260209"}`) is now sent with empty
  args instead of dropped; only unknown ids warn. A TS-shaped advisor tool
  without a `model` string fails with `*TypeValidationError`. The "code
  execution tool is required when using skills" warning checks the tool id.
  `ExperimentalBatch` rejects a provider tool whose custom name differs from
  its Anthropic wire name (`UnsupportedFunctionalityError`, "aliased provider
  tool names in batches"), as TS; tools keeping the constructor's id as their
  name are still accepted. `prompt.AnthropicToolsetNames` finds a renamed
  computer toolset by id too (review round 74). A forced
  `ToolChoice{Type: types.ToolChoiceTool, ToolName: ...}` that names a
  provider tool keeping its constructor id (`"anthropic.bash_20250124"`) now
  sends the tool's Anthropic wire name (`tool_choice.name: "bash"`), the name
  the request declares it under; v0.5 sent the id, which matched no tool.
  Batch results (`ExperimentalGetBatchResults`) of such tools come back under
  the tool's `Name`, as `DoGenerate` returns them, when read through the
  Provider that started the batch; v0.5 reported the wire name (review round
  75). An empty (non-nil) `allowedCallers` provider option or `InputExamples`
  list is sent (`allowed_callers: []`, `input_examples: []`) with the
  `advanced-tool-use-2025-11-20` beta, as TS (`!= null`); v0.5 dropped empty
  lists (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** Code that changes `Name` on a
  `pkg/providers/anthropic/tools` constructor result, builds provider tools
  as `types.Tool{Type: "provider", ProviderID: "anthropic.…"}` or as
  `types.Tool{Name: "anthropic.…"}` literals, uses container skills, or sends
  renamed provider tools in `ExperimentalBatch`; `ToolChoiceTool` with an
  `anthropic.*` tool name; `ExperimentalBatch` with tools from
  `pkg/providers/anthropic/tools` and code that matches batch tool calls by
  `"bash"`, `"web_search"`, ...; `allowedCallers: []` /
  `ToolOptions{AllowedCallers: []string{}}`,
  `InputExamples: []types.ToolInputExample{}`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you match batch result tool calls by the
  wire name; match the tool's `Name` instead. To use TS-style names:

  ```go
  package main

  import (
  	"fmt"

  	"github.com/digitallysavvy/go-ai/pkg/provider/types"
  	anthropictools "github.com/digitallysavvy/go-ai/pkg/providers/anthropic/tools"
  )

  func renamedBash() types.Tool {
  	shell := anthropictools.Bash20250124()
  	shell.Name = "shell"
  	return shell
  }

  func main() {
  	fmt.Println(renamedBash().Name)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Anthropic bash tools run through the sandbox session {#anthropic-bash-sandbox-session}

- **What changed:** the default execute of `tools.Bash20250124()` and
  `tools.Bash20241022()` runs the command through the call's sandbox session
  (`ToolExecutionOptions.ExperimentalSandbox` holding a
  `providerutils.SandboxSession`) with `Run`, and returns
  `tools.BashSandboxOutput` (`{"exitCode", "stdout", "stderr"}`), as TS
  `sandbox.run({command, abortSignal})`. It never runs a command on the
  host. v0.5 ignored a `SandboxSession` and only used a sandbox with an
  `Execute(ctx, command, opts)` method or a `RuntimeContext` with
  `ExecuteCommand`; those still work. Without any sandbox the error text is
  TS's `Sandbox session is not available` (v0.5: `Sandbox is not available`)
  (review round 76). A nil pointer (or other nil value) held in
  `ExperimentalSandbox` or `RuntimeContext` counts as no sandbox, as TS
  `!sandbox`; its methods are never called on the nil receiver (review round
  77).
- **Am I affected?** `Bash20250124()` / `Bash20241022()` without your own
  `Execute`; code matching `"Sandbox is not available"`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you match the error text; match
  `"Sandbox session is not available"`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Anthropic reasoning parts with an empty signature are replayed {#anthropic-empty-reasoning-signature}

- **What changed:** an assistant reasoning part whose
  `providerOptions.anthropic.signature` (or provider metadata) is present but
  empty is sent as a `thinking` block, and one with an empty `redactedData`
  as a `redacted_thinking` block, as TS (`!= null`). v0.5 dropped them with
  the `unsupported reasoning metadata` warning (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** Prompts that replay `ReasoningContent` with
  `"signature": ""` or `"redactedData": ""` under the `anthropic` key.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on those parts being dropped.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Anthropic reasoning provider options are validated {#anthropic-reasoning-options-validated}

- **What changed:** v0.5 dropped a reasoning part whose
  `ProviderOptions["anthropic"]` had a non-string `signature`/`redactedData`
  (`null` included) or was not an object, with the warning "unsupported
  reasoning metadata". v0.6 returns `*errors.InvalidArgumentError`
  ("invalid anthropic provider options"), as TS
  `parseProviderOptions(anthropicReasoningMetadataSchema)` throws. A typed
  map such as `map[string]string` is now read through its JSON form (v0.5
  ignored it). Applies to the Anthropic, Bedrock Anthropic and Vertex
  Anthropic prompt conversion (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** `types.ReasoningContent` with
  `ProviderOptions: {"anthropic": ...}` holding `"signature": nil`, a number,
  or a non-map value; code matching the "unsupported reasoning metadata"
  warning for those parts.
- **What to do:** no action unless you build reasoning parts by hand; pass
  the signature as a string or leave the key out.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### An undetected application file part fails with the TS error {#anthropic-application-subtype-error}

- **What changed:** an inline `application` (no subtype) file whose bytes are
  not a PDF failed with the `UnsupportedFunctionalityError` "media type:
  application"; it now fails with TS's `file of media type "application" must
  specify subtype since it could not be auto-detected` (TS
  `resolveFullMediaType` in the application branch; review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** Matching `Functionality == "media type: application"`.
- **What to do:** Give a full media type.
- **Kind:** error text only

#### An empty SSE data event is a stream error part {#anthropic-stream-empty-data-error}

- **What changed:** an SSE event with an empty `data:` field was skipped; it
  is now a stream `error` part (a `JSONParseError` with empty text) and the
  stream continues, as TS `parseJsonEventStream` (eventsource-parser
  dispatches the event, `safeParseJSON("")` fails) (review round 82). The
  other providers already did this.
- **Am I affected?** Anthropic (and Bedrock / Vertex Anthropic) streams
  through proxies that send empty `data:` events; code that treats any
  `ChunkTypeError` as fatal.
- **What to do:** No action unless such a proxy is in your path.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Anthropic provider tool error results keep any present errorCode {#anthropic-tool-result-error-code}

- **What changed:** when an Anthropic provider tool error result (web_fetch,
  web_search, tool search, code execution) is replayed, any present
  `errorCode` is sent as `error_code` (an empty string or a number included;
  typed Go values are read as their JSON object), as TS `errorCode ??
  'unavailable'` / `?? 'unknown'` does. v0.5 sent the default for a
  non-string or empty code (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** Replayed provider tool results with `errorCode` in their output value.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Anthropic native structured output only for representable schemas {#anthropic-native-structured-output-schema}

- **What changed:** Auto structured output uses `output_config.format` only for a schema Anthropic can represent: no `$ref` cycles, no open objects (maps, `additionalProperties: true`, free-form objects) and at most 10 optional properties (counted per `$ref` use); otherwise the JSON response tool (TS `f4c5f1e959`, `b0d51fecc0`, `2cd0a97424`). Opus/Sonnet 5.5 and Fable 5.1, which reject forced tool use, use native output on Bedrock and Vertex for a representable schema (v0.5 sent the JSON tool with `auto` and could return empty output). Claude 5 models on Bedrock support native output and strict tools. With the JSON tool, `ToolChoice` none drops the user tools and a named choice keeps only that tool. New helper `anthropic.SupportsNativeStructuredOutputSchema`.
- **Am I affected?** `ResponseFormat` with `Type: "json"` on anthropic, bedrock (Anthropic models) and googlevertex/anthropic.
- **What to do:** Set `providerOptions.anthropic.structuredOutputMode: "outputFormat"` to force native output.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Anthropic returns the text when the JSON response tool is not called {#anthropic-json-tool-text-fallback}

- **What changed:** With the JSON response tool, a response that never calls it (stop reason other than `tool_use` / `pause_turn`) returns its final text from DoGenerate and emits it at `message_stop` in DoStream; text before a tool block is dropped and held-back stream text keeps its citations (TS `e10aebbad6`). v0.5 dropped the text.
- **Am I affected?** Anthropic-family calls with `ResponseFormat` JSON that fall back to the JSON response tool.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Anthropic schema sanitizer keeps definitions next to $ref {#anthropic-sanitizer-ref-definitions}

- **What changed:** v0.5 reduced any schema node with `$ref` to `{"$ref": ...}`, dropping its `definitions` / `$defs`, so a top-level `$ref` schema (the default shape of a reflected Go struct schema) sent as `output_config.format` or a tool schema referenced a missing definition and Anthropic returned 400. v0.6 keeps the sanitized `definitions` and `$defs` next to the `$ref` (TS `f4c5f1e959`). Since round 86 the raw-schema path (`json.RawMessage`, `[]byte`, `*schema.OrderedSchema`, struct schemas) also writes each node's keys in the TS sanitizer's order (`$schema`, `$id`, `title`, `description`, `default`, `const`, `enum`, `type`, `anyOf`, `allOf`, `definitions`, `$defs`, `properties`, `additionalProperties`, `required`, `items`, `format`; `$ref` first on a reference node), properties in the input's order (JSON key order only).
- **Am I affected?** Anthropic, Bedrock-Anthropic or Vertex-Anthropic calls with a JSON schema whose root (or a nested node) has `$ref` plus `definitions` / `$defs`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Amazon Bedrock

#### Anthropic provider tools on Bedrock {#bedrock-anthropic-provider-tools}

- **What changed:** Bedrock now runs the Anthropic tool preparation over
  Anthropic provider tools, as TS: invalid tool args (for example advisor
  `MaxTokens` below 1024) fail the call with `*TypeValidationError` before the
  request; an unknown provider tool gets the Anthropic
  `provider-defined tool <id>` warning before Bedrock's `tool <id>` warning,
  and a rejected forced tool choice warning comes before the per-tool
  warnings. Tools are matched by id. A tool keeping the constructor's id as
  its name (`tools.Bash20250124()`) is still declared as `bash`, but the
  model's `bash` calls now come back named `anthropic.bash_20250124` (v0.5
  returned `bash`, which GenerateText could not match to the tool), and
  history calls of the tool are sent as `bash` (v0.5 sent
  `anthropicbash_20250124`). A provider tool with a custom name is declared
  under that name (TS `tool.name`). Warning features for unsupported tools
  use the tool id (review round 74). Forcing such a tool
  (`ToolChoice{Type: types.ToolChoiceTool, ToolName: "anthropic.bash_20250124"}`)
  sends `additionalModelRequestFields.tool_choice.name: "bash"`, the toolSpec
  name (review round 75).
- **Am I affected?** Anthropic provider tools
  (`pkg/providers/anthropic/tools`) with Bedrock models: code that reads
  `ToolCall.ToolName` of their calls, matches warnings, or relied on invalid
  tool args reaching the API.
- **What to do:** no action unless you matched the wire name (`"bash"`) in
  tool call names; match the tool's `Name` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Bedrock budgetTokens warning for non-Anthropic models {#bedrock-budget-tokens-warning}

- **What changed:** for a non-Anthropic Bedrock model, the
  `budgetTokens applies only to Anthropic models on Bedrock and will be
  ignored for this model.` warning is reported whenever
  `providerOptions.amazonBedrock.reasoningConfig.budgetTokens` (or
  `ModelOptions.ReasoningConfig.BudgetTokens`) is set, as TS. v0.5 only
  reported it for `type: "enabled"` (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** Code that counts or matches `Warnings` with
  `Feature == "budgetTokens"` on non-Anthropic Bedrock models with a
  disabled or untyped `reasoningConfig`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you assert on the exact warnings list.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Amazon Bedrock: embedding token counts follow TS {#bedrock-embedding-token-count}

- **What changed:** v0.5 read a Nova response without `inputTokenCount` as 0
  tokens and parsed the `x-amzn-bedrock-input-token-count` header with
  `strconv.Atoi`. v0.6 follows TS `amazon-bedrock-embedding-model.ts`: a Nova
  body without `inputTokenCount` reads the header, the header is parsed like JS
  `Number()` (missing is NaN, empty is 0, fractions and `0x` hex kept), and
  Titan and Nova body counts keep fractions in `Tokens` (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** `bedrock` embedding `EmbeddingUsage.Tokens` /
  `InputTokens` for Nova models, or Cohere models whose responses carry an
  unusual token-count header.
- **What to do:** no action unless you read `Usage.Tokens` without checking
  `math.IsNaN`; a NaN count marshals as `null` and is left out of telemetry.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Bedrock s3 parts without a subtype and text tool-result files fail with the TS errors {#bedrock-url-media-type-errors}

- **What changed:** an s3 URL file part (user content or tool-result
  content) whose media type has no subtype (`image`, `video/*`) now fails
  with `'file of media type "image" must specify subtype since it is not
  passed as inline bytes' functionality not supported.` (was `Unsupported
  image mime type: image, ...`), and a `FileDataTypeText` file block in
  tool-result content fails with the `*UnsupportedFunctionalityError` `'tool
  result file data of type "text"' functionality not supported.` (was a plain
  `unsupported tool result file data for media type: ...` error), as TS
  `resolveFullMediaType` and `convertToolResultOutput` (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** Bedrock `FileContent` / `FileContentBlock` with an
  `s3://` URL and a bare media type; code matching the old error strings.
- **What to do:** Give s3 parts a full media type (`image/png`); send text in
  tool results as a `TextContentBlock`.
- **Kind:** error text only

#### A Bedrock text file part is always a document {#bedrock-text-file-document}

- **What changed:** a user `FileContent` with text data (`FileData.Type ==
  text` or the legacy `Text`) is always a Bedrock `document` block, as in TS
  (`getAmazonBedrockDocumentFormat`). v0.5 made it an image or video block of
  the text bytes when its media type was `image/*` or `video/*`; v0.6 fails
  such a part with `Unsupported file mime type: image/png, ...` (review round
  83).
- **Am I affected?** Bedrock text file parts whose `MediaType` is not a
  document type.
- **What to do:** No action unless you sent text parts with an image or
  video media type; use a document media type (`text/plain`,
  `text/markdown`, ...).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Bedrock Anthropic models receive disabled thinking {#bedrock-anthropic-disabled-thinking}

- **What changed:** Bedrock Anthropic models now receive `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` for `Reasoning: types.ReasoningNone` or `reasoningConfig.type: "disabled"` (nothing for Claude 3.5 and older); models that reject it use between_tools or adaptive thinking (low effort) with a warning, and `xhigh` / `max` are lowered to `high` where rejected (TS `22b44614a2`). v0.5 sent nothing.
- **Am I affected?** `ReasoningNone` or `"reasoningConfig": {"type": "disabled"}` on `bedrock` Anthropic models.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Bedrock named tool choice keeps every tool {#bedrock-named-tool-choice}

- **What changed:** A named `ToolChoice` on Bedrock Anthropic models that accept forced tool use keeps every function tool and sets `disable_parallel_tool_use: true` unless `disableParallelToolUse: false` (TS `62a4dcd641`). v0.5 sent only the named tool.
- **Am I affected?** `types.SpecificToolChoice(...)` on `bedrock` Anthropic models.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Bedrock document names are unique {#bedrock-unique-document-names}

- **What changed:** Two document parts with the same name (or a file named like a generated `document-N`) made Bedrock reject the request; they now get ` (2)`, ` (3)` suffixes within 200 characters (TS `b6de62e95a`).
- **Am I affected?** Bedrock requests with several file parts of the same filename.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Google and Vertex

#### Google provider metadata keeps only the keys TS declares {#google-metadata-declared-keys}

- **What changed:** v0.5 copied `promptFeedback`, `groundingMetadata` (and
  each `groundingMetadataPerRequest` entry), `urlContextMetadata` and
  `safetyRatings` into `ProviderMetadata["google"]` (and `"vertex"` /
  `"googleVertex"`) exactly as the API sent them. v0.6 keeps only the keys
  the TS zod schemas declare, in schema order, in `DoGenerate` results and
  the stream finish part, as TS parsing strips undeclared keys (review
  round 79). The declared keys are:
  - `promptFeedback`: `blockReason`, `safetyRatings`;
  - `safetyRatings[]`: `category`, `probability`, `probabilityScore`,
    `severity`, `severityScore`, `blocked`;
  - `groundingMetadata`: `webSearchQueries`, `imageSearchQueries`,
    `retrievalQueries`, `searchEntryPoint.renderedContent`,
    `groundingChunks[]` (`web.{uri,title}`,
    `image.{sourceUri,imageUri,title,domain}`,
    `retrievedContext.{uri,title,text,fileSearchStore,customMetadata}`,
    `maps.{uri,title,text,placeId}`), `groundingSupports[]`
    (`segment.{startIndex,endIndex,text}`, `segment_text`,
    `groundingChunkIndices`, `supportChunkIndices`, `confidenceScores`,
    `confidenceScore`), and `retrievalMetadata` (`{webDynamicRetrievalScore}`
    when that is a number, otherwise `{}`);
  - `urlContextMetadata`: `urlMetadata[].{retrievedUrl,urlRetrievalStatus}`.

  Keys v0.5 callers could read that are now dropped include
  `groundingMetadata.googleMapsWidgetContextToken`,
  `groundingMetadata.searchEntryPoint.sdkBlob`,
  `groundingChunks[].web.domain`, `groundingChunks[].retrievedContext.ragChunk`,
  `groundingChunks[].maps.placeAnswerSources`,
  `promptFeedback.blockReasonMessage` and any `retrievalMetadata` key other
  than `webDynamicRetrievalScore`. `usageMetadata` is unchanged (loose in
  TS).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads any other key from those values of
  `ProviderMetadata["google"]`, `["vertex"]` or `["googleVertex"]` for
  `google` or `googlevertex` Gemini models.
- **What to do:** read such a key from the raw response instead:
  `GenerateResult.RawResponse` for `DoGenerate`, or `ChunkTypeRaw` parts
  with `IncludeRawChunks` for streams.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google decodes unpadded and URL-safe base64 payloads {#google-base64-forgiving-decode}

- **What changed:** v0.5 decoded Gemini `inlineData`, Interactions image
  and video `data`, Gemini TTS and speech translation audio and Vertex Cloud
  TTS `audioContent` with `base64.StdEncoding`, so an unpadded or URL-safe
  (`-`, `_`) payload became an empty generated file or failed the speech
  call. v0.6 decodes them like TS `convertBase64ToUint8Array` (URL-safe
  characters mapped to the standard alphabet, padding optional, whitespace
  ignored) (review round 80). Round 81 applies the same TS
  `convertBase64ToUint8Array` decode to Mistral speech `audio_data`, Amazon
  Bedrock image bytes (`Image`/`Images`; `Base64Images` keeps the wire
  strings), Perplexity decision image sniffing and Bedrock Converse media
  type sniffing. Round 92 applies the same decode to `Transcribe`
  `AudioBase64`, `GenerateVideo` prompt images, references and base64
  videos, and Cartesia, Gladia, Rev.ai and Fish Audio `AudioBase64` (ASCII
  whitespace in the string is now ignored), and to every base64 file data
  string decoded through `types.DecodeFileDataString` (prompt and tool
  result file data, uploads, the provider files APIs).
- **Am I affected?** Only if you relied on such payloads producing an empty
  file or an error: Google / Vertex, `mistral` speech `DoGenerate`, `bedrock`
  image `DoGenerate` `Image`/`Images`, `perplexity` decisions with a bare
  image media type; `ai.Transcribe` with `AudioBase64`, `ai.GenerateVideo`
  base64 inputs or outputs, `cartesia` / `gladia` / `revai` / `fishaudio`
  `TranscriptionOptions.AudioBase64`, Cartesia realtime
  `input-audio-append`, and base64 strings with line breaks in
  `types.FileData`.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google Vertex: one embedding response per call {#vertex-embedding-one-response}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `DoEmbedMany` on the `:predict` endpoint returned the
  HTTP response once per input, so `EmbedMany` `Responses` had one entry per
  value. v0.6 returns one response per request, as TS doEmbed and embedMany do
  (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** `googlevertex` text embedding models (not
  gemini-embedding-2) and code that indexes `EmbeddingsResult.Responses` or
  `EmbedManyResult.Responses` by input position.
- **What to do:** index `Responses` by request (one per `MaxEmbeddingsPerCall`
  batch), not by value.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google Live transcription and translation streams {#google-live-close-and-silence}

- **What changed:** the close-before-finish error now ends with
  " (code unknown)." as TS does when no close code is known
  ("Google Live transcription WebSocket closed unexpectedly before finishing
  (code unknown).", same for "Google Live translation" and "Vertex Live
  transcription"). In speech translation, an audio part that is not base64
  now counts as turn activity, and a -32768 PCM sample is no longer treated
  as silence (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** code that matches these error strings exactly, or
  depends on when a Live translation stream finishes after audio ends.
- **What to do:** match with `strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), "... closed
  unexpectedly before finishing")`.
- **Kind:** error text only

#### Google Interactions keeps an empty signature {#google-interactions-empty-signature}

- **What changed:** an empty `signature` in an Interactions response step is
  kept in `providerMetadata.google.signature` (stream and generate) and sent
  back on the next turn, as TS (`signature != null`); v0.5 dropped it on both
  sides (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading the presence of
  `providerMetadata.google.signature`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Google Live transcription takes an empty languageCode {#google-live-empty-language-code}

- **What changed:** in Google Live transcription
  (`TranscriptionModel.DoStream`), an `inputTranscription.languageCode` of
  `""` now replaces an earlier language in the finish part, as TS
  (`languageCode != null`) and the Vertex live stream (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** `TranscriptionStreamPart.Language` on the finish part of
  a google live transcription stream.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google Live translation falls back to the top-level transcript text {#google-live-translation-source-fallback}

- **What changed:** Google Live speech translation reads the source
  transcript as TS `serverContent?.inputTranscription?.text ??
  message.inputTranscription?.text`: a `serverContent.inputTranscription`
  without `text` now falls back to the top-level `inputTranscription`; v0.5
  used the top-level one only when the `serverContent` object was absent
  (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** `SpeechTranslationStreamPart` source-transcript parts
  and the finish `SourceText` of a google translation stream.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google sends a present empty thoughtSignature {#google-empty-thought-signature}

- **What changed:** v0.5: an empty `providerOptions.google.thoughtSignature` (or
  `vertex` / `googleVertex`) on an assistant part was dropped, and a Gemini 3
  function call with one got the `skip_thought_signature_validator` sentinel
  and a warning. v0.6: like TS (`thoughtSignature != null`), a present empty
  signature is sent as `""` and counts as a signature (no sentinel, no
  warning). An empty `serverToolCallId` together with a `serverToolType` still
  makes a server `toolCall` part (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `"thoughtSignature": ""` in provider options or metadata you replay.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on an empty signature being dropped; remove the key instead.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Google URL and reference file parts need a full media type {#google-reference-file-media-type}

- **What changed:** a user file part sent by URL or by provider reference
  (`FileData{Type: FileDataTypeReference}`) with only a top-level media type
  (`image`, `image/*`) now fails with `UnsupportedFunctionalityError` `file of
  media type "image" must specify subtype since it is not passed as inline
  bytes`, as TS `resolveFullMediaType` does. v0.5 sent `mimeType: "image"` for
  a reference and completed a URL part's type from its extension (`.png`,
  `.gif`, `.webp`, else `image/jpeg`). This applies to URLs the model takes
  directly (Gemini files and YouTube URLs; every http(s) and `gs:` URL on
  Vertex), including `types.ImageContent{URL: ...}` without a `MimeType`;
  other URLs are downloaded and their type detected as before. The Google and
  Vertex image models are unaffected (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `FileDataTypeReference` parts, `FileContent{URL:` with `MediaType:
  "image"` or `"image/*"`, and `ImageContent{URL:` without `MimeType`, sent to
  Google or Vertex.
- **What to do:** Give the full type: before `MediaType: "image"`, after
  `MediaType: "image/png"` (or `ImageContent{URL: u, MimeType: "image/png"}`).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google sends an empty continuationToken {#google-empty-continuation-token}

- **What changed:** `providerOptions.google.continuationToken: ""` is now sent as
  `"continuationToken": ""` (TS `!= null`); v0.5 dropped it (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `continuationToken` provider options set to `""`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you pass an empty token; omit the key instead.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Google Live errors keep an empty server message {#google-live-empty-error-message}

- **What changed:** a Google / Vertex Live transcription or speech translation server
  error with an empty `message` now fails with that empty message, as TS
  `message ?? 'Google Live API error'` does (`'Vertex Live API error'` on
  Vertex); v0.5 used the default text (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `"Google Live API error"` or `"Vertex Live API error"`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the default text.
- **Kind:** error text only

#### Google Interactions history keeps empty fields like TS {#google-interactions-step-fields}

- **What changed:** a replayed `function_call` step always carries `id`, `name` and
  `arguments` (`{}` for a call without arguments; v0.5 left out `arguments`,
  and an empty `id` or `name`). A `google.processing_call` /
  `google.processing_result` custom part with an empty `processingId` /
  `processingCallId` is sent as a step (v0.5 dropped it with a warning).
  Provider-option `responseFormat` entries keep an empty `mimeType` or
  `schema: {}`, and an empty `imageConfig: {}` adds the default image entry
  and its deprecation warning (agent calls list it as dropped) (review round
  84).
- **Am I affected?** `InteractionsModel` calls with `processingId`, `imageConfig` or `responseFormat` provider options, or zero-argument tool calls in history.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Google Interactions agent streams keep the part metadata {#google-interactions-agent-stream-metadata}

- **What changed:** the synthesized stream of a non-streaming agent interaction gives
  each tool call the part's own `providerMetadata.google` (an empty
  `signature` included), so the next turn sends the signature back as TS
  does (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** Streamed Interactions agent tool calls whose `ProviderMetadata["google"]["signature"]` you read.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google in-stream errors with only an RPC status are classified {#google-stream-error-status-code}

- **What changed:** An in-stream error that carries only an RPC status (`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, `UNAVAILABLE`, ...) now gets the mapped HTTP `StatusCode` and retryability (TS `8ad78735f7`); v0.5 reported no status code and never retried.
- **Am I affected?** `StreamProviderError.StatusCode`, `IsRetryable` on google / googlevertex streams.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Interactions toolChoice none sends no tools {#google-interactions-tool-choice-none}

- **What changed:** `ToolChoice` none on Google Interactions models sends no tools (provider tools included), no `tool_choice` and no warnings (TS `a32df11498`). v0.5 sent the tools with `tool_choice: none`, so provider-executed tools still ran.
- **Am I affected?** `types.NoneToolChoice()` on `google` Interactions models.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Gemini streamed function args close on their final chunk {#gemini-stream-function-args-close}

- **What changed:** A streamed string argument's final chunk (or a value-less terminator) closes the string at once (TS `9f108f8a57`), so the `tool-input-delta` split differs from v0.5. The assembled input is unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares Gemini `ChunkTypeToolInputDelta` parts one by one.
- **What to do:** No action; concatenate the deltas.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Google response schema key order {#google-response-schema-key-order}

- **What changed:** `generationConfig.responseJsonSchema` is written in the key order of TS `sanitizeResponseJsonSchema`: the schema's other keys first, then `enum` (from `const`), `properties`, `items`, `additionalProperties`, `anyOf`, `oneOf`; v0.5 kept the input order (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares the Google / Vertex request body text with a `ResponseFormat` schema (`ResponseFormat{Type: "json", Schema: ...}`).
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare request bytes; compare parsed JSON.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### OpenAI

#### OpenAI Responses promptCacheOptions is validated {#openai-prompt-cache-options}

- **What changed:** only `mode` (`implicit` or `explicit`), `ttl` (`30m` or
  `12h`) and `prewarm` are sent, as TS. Invalid values such as `ttl: "24h"`
  fail before the request with `InvalidArgumentError`, and unknown keys are
  dropped. Chat Completions is unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** `promptCacheOptions` in OpenAI Responses provider
  options.
- **What to do:** use the documented values.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI chat streams fail the call on an error before output {#openai-chat-stream-error}

- **What changed:** an error frame after the empty first assistant chunk or a
  finish-only chunk now fails the call (retryable), as TS, instead of
  streaming an error part. OpenAI-compatible chat models are unchanged.
- **Am I affected?** Code that handles these failures as an `error` part.
- **What to do:** handle the error from the call (or `Err()`).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI provider tools are dropped by the Chat Completions model {#openai-chat-drops-provider-tools}

- **What changed:** `openaitool.*` tools, `CustomTool.ToTool` and the
  `responses.New*Tool()` tools are provider tools (`Type: "provider"`), so
  the OpenAI Chat Completions model drops them with an `unsupported` warning
  `tool type: provider`, as TS `prepareChatTools` does. v0.5 sent them as
  function tools named `openai.web_search`, `openai.code_interpreter`, a
  custom tool's name, etc. (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** `ChatModel(` / `openai.NewLanguageModel(` (Chat
  Completions) models called with `openaitool.` or `responses.New` tools.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on those function tools; use
  the Responses model (`LanguageModel(` / `ResponsesModel(`) for OpenAI
  provider tools.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI tool_search calls are named after the tool {#openai-tool-search-tool-name}

- **What changed:** tool_search calls and outputs from the Responses model
  carry the declared tool's name (`openai.tool_search` for
  `openaitool.ToolSearch`) in generate and stream, as TS
  `toolNameMapping.toCustomToolName` does. v0.5 named them `tool_search`,
  which matched no tool: a client-mode call could not run and a replayed call
  was not recognized. `ToolChoice` `AllowedTools` naming
  `openai.tool_search` is dropped with an `unsupported` warning (TS); v0.5
  sent a function entry (review round 73). `AllowedTools` naming
  `tool_search` (the provider tool name) reaches a tool_search tool declared
  under another name and is dropped the same way; a name of a provider tool
  the Responses model does not send (another provider's tool) is sent as a
  function entry with the TS "not part of the tools for this request"
  warning (v0.5 dropped it; review round 74).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `ToolName == "tool_search"` on tool calls,
  results or stream chunks.
- **What to do:** match `"openai.tool_search"` (or your tool's `Name`).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI shell calls in a hosted container are provider-executed {#openai-shell-container-provider-executed}

- **What changed:** a `shell_call` for a `responses.NewShellTool(...)` with a
  `container_auto` or `container_reference` environment is
  `ProviderExecuted`, as TS `isShellProviderExecuted`; the SDK does not run
  it locally. v0.5 never matched the Go constructor and reported these calls
  client-executed (review round 73).
- **Am I affected?** `responses.NewShellTool(responses.WithShellEnvironment(`
  with `Type: "container_auto"` or `"container_reference"`, especially with
  an `Execute` set.
- **What to do:** no action; OpenAI runs these commands.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI provider tool config is validated before the request {#openai-tool-args-validation}

- **What changed:** the Responses model validates each OpenAI provider tool's
  args (web_search, web_search_preview, code_interpreter, file_search,
  image_generation, mcp, custom, tool_search, shell) against the TS args
  schema, as TS `validateTypes` does, and fails with a
  `*providererrors.TypeValidationError` (an alias of `ValidationError`)
  whose text is TS's "Type validation failed: Value: <args JSON>.\nError
  message: <issues>" before sending. v0.5 sent invalid config to OpenAI
  (review rounds 73 and 74). The issue list after "Error message:" is zod's
  issue JSON, as TS (review round 75).
- **Am I affected?** `openaitool.` configs with values outside the TS enums
  or ranges (`SearchContextSize`, `Quality`, `OutputCompression` > 100,
  `PartialImages` > 3, `Size` not `WxH`), `FileSearchConfig` without
  `VectorStoreIDs`, grammar `CustomToolFormat` without `Syntax` /
  `Definition`, `ToolSearchArgs.Execution` other than server / client, shell
  `MemoryLimit` outside 1g / 4g / 16g / 64g; `openaitool.MCP` without
  `ServerLabel`; `openaitool.MCP` without `ServerURL` and `ConnectorID`.
- **What to do:** fix the config; handle the `*providererrors.ValidationError`
  with `errors.As` if you surface it.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### openaitool.ToolSearch keeps the search schema in its options {#openai-tool-search-parameters}

- **What changed:** `openaitool.ToolSearch(...)` sets the tool's
  `Parameters` to TS's call input schema (`{arguments, call_id}`), so client
  calls are validated against the input they carry; the search-arguments
  schema (`ToolSearchArgs.Parameters`) is in the new
  `ToolSearchOptions.Parameters` and `ProviderArgs["parameters"]`, and is
  still sent as `parameters`. v0.5 put it in `Parameters`, so a schema with
  `required` rejected every client call (TS `toolSearchInputSchema`,
  `args.parameters`; review round 73). The client `Execute` input is the
  call input `{arguments, call_id}`, as before.
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `Parameters` of the tool
  `openaitool.ToolSearch(` returns.
- **What to do:** read the search schema from the options:

  ```go
  func searchSchema(tool types.Tool) map[string]interface{} {
  	// v0.5: tool.Parameters.(map[string]interface{})
  	opts, _ := tool.ProviderOptions.(openaitool.ToolSearchOptions)
  	return opts.Parameters
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Expanded OpenAI parallel tool calls keep the model's key order {#openai-parallel-call-input-order}

- **What changed:** when the Responses model expands the internal `parallel`
  wrapper call, each nested tool call's `RawArguments` is TS's
  `JSON.stringify(parameters)`: the model's key order (JS integer-like keys
  first). v0.5 sorted the keys (review round 74).
- **Am I affected?** Code comparing `ToolCall.RawArguments` text of calls
  expanded from a `parallel` wrapper (`providerMetadata.openai.parallelToolCall`
  set).
- **What to do:** no action unless you compare the raw text; compare
  `Arguments` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI Responses function and shell tool wire details follow TS {#openai-responses-tool-wire-r75}

- **What changed:** (1) a function tool's `providerOptions.openai.outputSchema`
  is normalized like its input schema before sending (TS
  `prepareFunctionTool` → `normalizeOpenAIJsonSchema`): `propertyNames` and
  regex-lookaround `pattern`s are removed, each with the TS `compatibility`
  warning. (2) Per tool, the async warning comes before the schema warnings,
  as TS. (3) An explicitly empty `allowedCallers` list is sent as
  `allowed_callers: []` (TS forwards it whenever it is not null). (4) A shell
  environment's explicitly empty `FileIDs`, `Skills`,
  `NetworkPolicy.AllowedDomains` or `NetworkPolicy.DomainSecrets` is sent as
  `[]` (TS `mapShellEnvironment`). v0.5 sent the output schema raw and
  dropped empty lists (review round 75).
- **Am I affected?** Function tools with `providerOptions.openai.outputSchema`
  that use `propertyNames` or lookaround patterns; code that asserts the
  order of tool `Warnings`; `allowedCallers: []`;
  `responses.ShellEnvironment` / `ShellNetworkPolicy` with empty non-nil
  slices; code that marshals `responses.FunctionToolDef`,
  `ShellToolDefEnvironment` or `ShellNetworkPolicy` (new `MarshalJSON`
  methods, additive).
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the raw output schema, the
  warning order or the omitted empty lists. Use nil instead of an empty
  slice to keep a field out of the request.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### OpenAI Responses stream reads to the end of the stream {#openai-responses-stream-r76}

- **What changed:** the Responses stream no longer stops at the first
  `response.completed` / `response.incomplete` / `response.failed`: like TS
  it keeps reading later events and emits the one finish part when the
  stream ends, with the last reported usage, service tier and reasoning
  context. A frame that is not JSON finishes the stream with finish reason
  `error` (as a schema failure already did). Text and reasoning ids follow
  the message or reasoning item added at each output index. A streamed
  `apply_patch_call` counts toward the `tool-calls` finish reason only when
  its tool call is emitted. The programmatic tool calling `program`
  tool-call (generate) and `program_output` tool-result parts (generate and
  stream) carry `providerMetadata.openai.itemId`. With `include: nil` and
  logprobs on a GPT-6 reasoning model, the request has no `include` key;
  `promptCacheOptions: nil` fails with `*InvalidArgumentError`. v0.5 stopped
  reading at the terminal event and had none of the rest (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** Responses streams whose server sends events after
  `response.completed`; code that waited for the finish chunk before the
  body ended; finish reason checks after a malformed frame;
  `program` / `program_output` content metadata; `"include": nil` with
  `"logprobs"`; `"promptCacheOptions": nil`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the stream ending at
  `response.completed` or passed `promptCacheOptions: nil` (omit the key
  instead).
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI Responses replays encrypted reasoning from provider options {#openai-reasoning-encrypted-content-provider-options}

- **What changed:** v0.5 sent a replayed reasoning part's `encrypted_content`
  only from the Go `ReasoningContent.EncryptedContent` field. v0.6 reads
  `ProviderOptions["openai"]["reasoningEncryptedContent"]` (the key the
  Responses result metadata reports, which response messages and UI messages
  carry), then the part's `ProviderMetadata`, then the field, as TS
  `convertToOpenAIResponsesInput`. With `store: false`, multi-step calls now
  keep the previous step's reasoning instead of dropping it with the
  "Reasoning parts without encrypted content are not supported when store is
  false" warning. Azure reads its `azure` key (review round 77). A
  `reasoningEncryptedContent` of `""` is replayed as `encrypted_content: ""`
  (TS `!= null`); before round 78 Go treated it as absent.
- **Am I affected?** `ResponsesModel` / Azure calls with `"store": false` and
  `"include": []string{"reasoning.encrypted_content"}` over several steps or
  replayed history; code expecting that warning.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Empty serviceTier and echo fields are reported {#empty-service-tier-reported}

- **What changed:** v0.5 left an empty `service_tier` (and an empty
  `reasoning.context`, `prompt_cache_key`, `safety_identifier`) out of
  provider metadata. v0.6 reports a present empty string, as TS
  (`typeof === 'string'` for OpenAI, `!= null` for xAI). Null or absent
  values are still left out (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading `ProviderMetadata["openai"]["serviceTier"]`
  / `["reasoningContext"]` (OpenAI and Azure Responses generate, stream
  finish, batch results) or
  `ProviderMetadata["xai"]["serviceTier" | "promptCacheKey" | "safetyIdentifier"]`
  (xAI Responses and batch) that treats key presence as "set".
- **What to do:** no action unless you check key presence; compare the value
  instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Responses streams skip [DONE]; created_at 0 is a timestamp {#responses-stream-done-created-at}

- **What changed:** the OpenAI, Azure and xAI Responses streams no longer end
  at a `data: [DONE]` frame: like TS `parseJsonEventStream`, the marker is
  skipped and the finish part comes when the body ends, after any later
  events. A `created_at` of `0` now gives `ResponseMetadata.Timestamp` = the
  Unix epoch (TS `new Date(0)`), in generate and stream; v0.5 left it zero.
  A fractional `created_at` keeps its milliseconds (review round 77).
- **Am I affected?** Servers or proxies that send events after `[DONE]` or
  keep the body open after it; code testing
  `ResponseMetadata.Timestamp.IsZero()` for these models.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the stream ending at
  `[DONE]`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI chat and completion streams report the response headers {#openai-stream-response-headers}

- **What changed:** v0.5 OpenAI chat and completion `DoStream` never put the
  HTTP response headers on a stream part, so `StreamTextResult.ResponseHeaders()`
  and the step response headers were empty for these models. v0.6 puts them
  on the first `ChunkTypeResponseMetadata` part (TS `doStream` returns
  `response.headers`); a stream whose chunks carry no id, model or created
  gets a headers-only response-metadata part just before the finish part
  (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** `DoStream` callers of `openai` chat and completion
  models (and wrappers built on them) that count or position
  `ChunkTypeResponseMetadata` parts, or that expect `ResponseMetadata.Headers`
  to be nil.
- **What to do:** no action unless you assume exactly one response-metadata
  part with an id, or none.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI computer tool input follows TS mapComputerAction {#openai-computer-input-order}

- **What changed:** the OpenAI Responses `computer` tool call input
  (`ToolCall.RawArguments`, `Arguments`) follows TS `mapComputerAction`: each
  action's declared keys in TS order (`click`: type, button, x, y, keys),
  `keys` only when not null (keypress keeps it), undeclared action keys
  dropped, `scroll_x`/`scroll_y` as `scrollX`/`scrollY`, pending safety
  checks `{id, code?, message?}`. v0.5 passed every wire key through with
  sorted keys (`"keys": null` kept). `Arguments["actions"]` is still
  `[]map[string]interface{}` (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares the computer tool input text, or
  reads `keys` (null) or undeclared keys from `Arguments["actions"]`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you read those keys; a missing `keys`
  means none.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### An empty computer_call actions list stays empty {#responses-computer-empty-actions}

- **What changed:** an OpenAI Responses `computer_call` with `"actions": []`
  and a singular `action` now has an empty `actions` input (TS
  `actions ?? (action != null ? [action] : [])`); v0.5 fell back to
  `[action]`. Only an absent or null `actions` uses `action` (review round
  82).
- **Am I affected?** `computer` tool-call `Arguments["actions"]` /
  `RawArguments` from `openai.ResponsesModel`.
- **What to do:** No action unless your executor relied on the fallback for
  an empty list.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### File uploads follow the TS form and metadata {#openai-files-upload-parity}

- **What changed:** OpenAI `Files().UploadFile` with inline data (bytes or
  text) now sends the `file` part first, then `purpose` and
  `expires_after[...]` (TS FormData order; stream uploads keep the fields
  first); `expires_after[seconds]` is `String(expiresAfter)` (`1e+21`, not
  `1000000000000000000000`); a `null` `purpose` / `expiresAfter` option is an
  invalid-options error (TS `.optional()` rejects null). OpenAI and xAI file
  results keep a present empty `filename` / `purpose` / `status` in
  `ProviderMetadata`, and `Filename` is `response.filename ?? filename` (a
  present empty response filename is no longer replaced by the request's).
  xAI `teamId` / `expiresAfter` / `filePath` `null`, or a non-string
  `filePath`, are invalid-options errors (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** `openai.Provider.Files().UploadFile`,
  `xai.Provider.Files().UploadFile` / `GetFileMetadata`, servers that depend
  on multipart part order.
- **What to do:** No action unless you pass `null` options or read empty
  metadata values.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### An empty skill default_version is reported {#skill-empty-default-version}

- **What changed:** OpenAI `Skills().UploadSkill` reports
  `ProviderMetadata["openai"]["defaultVersion"]` for a present empty
  `default_version` (TS `!= null`); v0.5 dropped it (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** Code that checks the presence of `defaultVersion`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Realtime client-secret URL encodes the model id like encodeURIComponent {#realtime-url-model-encoding}

- **What changed:** `DoCreateClientSecret` of the OpenAI and xAI realtime models builds
  `?model=` with JavaScript `encodeURIComponent` (space `%20`, `! ' ( ) *`
  literal) instead of `url.QueryEscape` (`+`, `%21`...) (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `ClientSecretResult.URL` for model ids with spaces or `!'()*`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Files reference ids and media types use JavaScript trim {#files-id-js-trim}

- **What changed:** the OpenAI and xAI files APIs reject a reference id that is empty
  after JavaScript `trim()` (a BOM-only id is now rejected, an id of U+0085
  accepted), and the downloaded media type (OpenAI, xAI, DeepSeek) is trimmed
  the same way, as TS (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `types.ProviderReference` ids made only of Unicode whitespace or a BOM.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI reasoning efforts: sent unchanged or rounded {#openai-reasoning-rounding}

- **What changed:** v0.5 dropped `providerOptions.openai.reasoningEffort` (and a top-level `Reasoning`) outside a GPT-6 model's effort list with an `unsupported` warning, and sent any top-level level to other models as is. v0.6 follows TS `61a315e333` / `d07b475bc8`: the provider option is sent unchanged; a top-level `Reasoning` is rounded to a supported effort (down first, never to `none`) with a `compatibility` warning `reasoning "<x>" is not directly supported by this model. mapped to effort "<y>".`; standard GPT-5.x models have effort lists (5.0 minimal-high, 5.1 none-high, 5.2-5.5 plus xhigh, 5.6+ plus max); Chat Completions never sends `max` to such a model; GPT-5.6+ Chat with function tools and no provider effort sends `reasoning_effort: "none"` with a compatibility warning when a level was requested; gpt-6.1-terra accepts `none`. `prompt_cache_retention` and Responses logprobs are no longer stripped on GPT-5.
- **Am I affected?** `openai` Chat/Responses models with `Reasoning` or `reasoningEffort`; code matching the `only supports the following reasoning efforts` warning; `LanguageModelCapabilities.SupportedReasoningEfforts` (now non-nil for standard GPT-5.x).
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the effort being dropped; set `providerOptions.openai.reasoningEffort` to send an exact value.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### OpenAI transcription defaults and streaming models {#openai-transcription-defaults}

- **What changed:** v0.5 sent `response_format=verbose_json` and `timestamp_granularities[]=segment` for every non-gpt-4o model whenever `providerOptions.openai` was set (gpt-transcribe returned 400), and always sent verbose_json for whisper-1. v0.6 (TS `9026c7de79`, `599a97f9bf`, `226b7daf82`, `8375141ef4`): response_format defaults only for whisper-1 (verbose_json) and gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize (diarized_json); an explicit `responseFormat` wins; segment timestamps only for verbose_json; new `languages` / `keywords` options; `DurationInSeconds` falls back to `usage.seconds`; DoTranscribe rejects gpt-live-transcribe; DoStream (openai and azure) accepts gpt-live-transcribe and gpt-transcribe, sends `languages: [language]` for them and omits `delay` (with a warning) for gpt-transcribe; `IsRealtimeTranscriptionModelID` also reports gpt-live-transcribe.
- **Am I affected?** `openai` / `azure` transcription models; `IsRealtimeTranscriptionModelID`.
- **What to do:** Set `providerOptions.openai.responseFormat` / `timestampGranularities` explicitly to keep the old fields.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Unfinished OpenAI web searches are tool errors {#openai-unfinished-web-search}

- **What changed:** v0.5 returned a `searching` / `in_progress` `web_search_call` as a successful tool result; v0.6 (TS `436374eb36`) reports every status but `completed` as a tool error with `{status}` (generate, stream, batch, azure).
- **Am I affected?** `ToolResultContent` / tool-result chunks of `web_search` on openai and azure Responses.
- **What to do:** No action unless you read those results.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI Decisions send JSON state as input_json {#openai-decisions-input-json}

- **What changed:** v0.5 sent every JSON state part as `input_text`; v0.6 (TS `47b1d8b2f7`) sends an object or a non-empty array of objects (at most 32 levels, 10,000 values, 256 KiB) as `{type: "input_json", data}` and supports `DecisionModelOptions.PromptCacheKey` (`prompt_cache_key`, no longer an unsupported warning).
- **Am I affected?** OpenAI decision models with JSON state parts or a `promptCacheKey` option.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### OpenAI strips a propertyNames schema without type {#openai-property-names-typeless}

- **What changed:** A JSON Schema `propertyNames` without `type` (a record with enum keys) failed the call with `UnsupportedFunctionalityError`; v0.6 (TS `7eac057241`) strips it with the `JSON Schema propertyNames` warning.
- **Am I affected?** OpenAI strict schemas with `propertyNames` that omit `type`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### xAI

#### xAI provider tool config is validated before the request {#xai-tool-args-validation}

- **What changed:** the xAI Responses model validates the args of
  `xai.WebSearch`, `xai.XSearch`, `xai.ImageGeneration`, `xai.FileSearch` and
  `xai.MCPServer` against the TS args schemas, as TS `prepareResponsesTools`
  does with `validateTypes`, and fails with a
  `*providererrors.TypeValidationError` ("Type validation failed: Value:
  <args JSON>.\nError message: <zod issues>", zod's exact issue list) before
  sending. v0.5 sent invalid config to xAI (review round 74). A hand-built
  `xai.mcp` tool whose `ProviderOptions` is an `xai.MCPServerConfig` now
  sends its config (v0.5 sent `server_url: ""` and dropped the rest). The
  args of TS-shaped xAI tools (`ProviderArgs` only) are sent as given, so
  `maxNumResults: 0` or an empty `serverLabel` reaches the wire (review round
  75).
- **Am I affected?** `xai.WebSearchConfig` with more than 5 `AllowedDomains` /
  `ExcludedDomains`; `xai.XSearchConfig` with more than 20 `AllowedXHandles` /
  `ExcludedXHandles`; `xai.ImageGenerationConfig.Action` other than auto /
  generate / edit; `xai.FileSearchConfig` without `VectorStoreIDs`;
  `xai.MCPServerConfig` without `ServerURL`; hand-built `Type: "provider"`
  xai tools whose `ProviderArgs` have the wrong types;
  `types.Tool{Name: "xai.mcp", ProviderOptions: xai.MCPServerConfig{...}}`.
- **What to do:** fix the config; handle `errors.As(err, &tve)` with
  `var tve *providererrors.TypeValidationError` if you surface it.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### xAI Responses function tools without a description omit the key {#xai-function-tool-description}

- **What changed:** a function tool with an empty `Description` is sent to
  the xAI Responses API without a `description` key (TS
  `description: tool.description` is undefined); v0.5 sent
  `"description": ""` (review round 76).
- **Am I affected?** xAI Responses requests with function tools that have
  no `Description`.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### The first xAI provider tool with an id names its output {#xai-provider-tool-name-first}

- **What changed:** when two provider tools share an id (for example two
  `xai.web_search` tools), v0.5 labeled the provider-executed output items
  with the last one's `Name`. v0.6 uses the first one's, as TS resolves
  `webSearchToolName` and the others with `tools.find` (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** xAI Responses calls with more than one provider tool of
  the same id under different names.
- **What to do:** no action; pass one tool per id.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### xAI batch results always carry response metadata {#xai-batch-response-metadata-always}

- **What changed:** v0.5 left an xAI batch text result's `ResponseMetadata`
  nil when the response had no id, model or created. v0.6 always sets it, as
  TS `getResponseMetadata` always returns an object (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** `result.ResponseMetadata == nil` checks on xAI batch
  results.
- **What to do:** check the fields (`ID`, `ModelID`, `Timestamp`) instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### xAI video: empty strings are values, edit/extend always send video {#xai-video-empty-values}

- **What changed:** matching TS `xai-video-model.ts`: a status `error.message` of
  `""` is the detail (`Video generation failed: `), `status: ""` with a
  `video.url` stays pending, `video.url: ""` does not fall back to
  `file_output.public_url`, a 202 or 200 status body with a leading BOM is
  parsed, `mode: "edit-video"` / `"extend-video"` without `videoUrl` sends
  `"video": {}`, and a missing `request_id` fails with `No request_id returned
  from xAI API.` (the response is no longer appended) (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `xai.VideoModel` `DoStart` / `DoStatus` / `DoGenerate`; matching the old error text `Response: {RequestID:`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the old error text.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### xAI image: an empty b64_json is image data {#xai-image-empty-values}

- **What changed:** as TS: an image with `b64_json: ""` counts as base64 data (no
  download of its `url`), in the image model and in batch results;
  `XAIImageItemMetadata.RevisedPrompt` is nil for an empty `revised_prompt` in
  the image model (batch keeps it, as TS) (review round 84).
- **Am I affected?** `xai.ImageModel` results whose API response has empty strings in these fields.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### xAI Responses citations, incomplete finish and none reasoning {#xai-responses-citations-finish}

- **What changed:** xAI Responses streams emitted each `url_citation` source twice; v0.6 once (TS `0eb8de60dc`). DoGenerate reports `length` / raw `max_output_tokens` for an incomplete response (TS `3d6ac0ae11`; was `other` / `incomplete`). `Reasoning: none` on Grok 4.5+ is sent as `low` with a compatibility warning (TS `61a315e333`).
- **Am I affected?** xAI Responses source counts, finish reasons, and `ReasoningNone` on Grok 4.5+.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI Responses replays RawArguments without Arguments {#openai-responses-raw-arguments}

- **What changed:** In v0.5 OpenAI (and Azure) Responses sent `arguments: "{}"` for a legacy `Message.ToolCalls` entry that had `RawArguments` but nil `Arguments`. v0.6 sends the raw text (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** `types.ToolCall{RawArguments: ...}` without `Arguments` in a prompt for `openai.ResponsesModel` / Azure Responses.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Azure, DeepSeek and Alibaba

#### Azure DeepSeek models send strict tools {#azure-deepseek-strict-tools}

- **What changed:** v0.5: an Azure DeepSeek model
  (`azure.Provider.DeepSeekModel`) rejected `strict: true` tools with
  "DeepSeek strict tool calls require a beta base URL ending in `/beta`."
  (and rejected mixing strict and non-strict tools). v0.6 sends them, as TS
  `createDeepSeekModel`, which leaves `supportsStrictToolCalls` unset so
  `prepareTools` runs neither check. The new field
  `deepseek.Config.UncheckedStrictToolCalls` does this; Azure sets it, and
  the deepseek provider keeps its `/beta` checks (review round 78).
- **Am I affected?** Azure DeepSeek calls with `types.Tool{Strict: &strict}`.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Alibaba resolves a top-level or wildcard image media type {#alibaba-image-media-type-sniffed}

- **What changed:** v0.5 sent alibaba inline image data with a top-level or
  wildcard media type as `data:image;base64,...` or
  `data:image/*;base64,...`. v0.6 resolves the subtype from the bytes
  (`data:image/png;base64,...`), as TS `formatImageUrl`
  (`resolveFullMediaType`), and returns an error when it cannot (review
  round 78).
- **Am I affected?** alibaba calls with `types.FileContent` / `ImageContent`
  media type `"image"` or `"image/*"` and inline bytes.
- **What to do:** no action, or give the full media type.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### DeepSeek warns on an unknown tool choice type {#deepseek-unknown-tool-choice}

- **What changed:** v0.5 deepseek sent `"tool_choice": "auto"` for a
  `types.ToolChoice` whose `Type` is not auto, none, required or tool. v0.6
  sends no `tool_choice` and adds the warning `{Type: "unsupported",
  Feature: "tool choice type: <type>"}`, as the TS `prepareTools` default
  branch (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** deepseek or Azure DeepSeek callers that build
  `types.ToolChoice{Type: ...}` with a custom string.
- **What to do:** no action unless you pass such a type; use a standard
  `ToolChoice`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Alibaba sends a text prompt as a user message {#alibaba-text-prompt-messages}

- **What changed:** v0.5 alibaba sent `types.Prompt{Text: ...}` as a user
  message with string content, skipped JSON-mode instruction injection for
  it, and dropped the text entirely for `Prompt{System, Text}`. v0.6 sends
  the text as one user message with `content: [{"type": "text", "text":
  ...}]`, converted like any other message (JSON-mode instruction and cache
  markers apply), next to the system message. TS has only the standardized
  message prompt (review round 79).
- **Am I affected?** Direct `alibaba` `DoGenerate` / `DoStream` callers that
  set `types.Prompt.Text`. `ai.GenerateText` and `ai.StreamText` send
  messages and are not affected.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Azure MAI image errors read the message with the TS schema {#azure-mai-image-error-message}

- **What changed:** v0.5 reported the HTTP status text for an MAI image error
  body `{"error":{"message":""}}`, and used the message of a body whose
  `error.code` was not a string or number. v0.6 follows TS `maiErrorSchema`:
  an empty message is kept, and an invalid `code` (or a missing message)
  gives the status text (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** `ProviderError.Message` from `azure` MAI image models
  (`mai-image-*`).
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the message text.
- **Kind:** error text only

#### Azure MAI image size and aspect ratio follow JavaScript Number parsing {#azure-mai-image-size-number}

- **What changed:** `ImageGenerateOptions.Size` and `AspectRatio` for MAI-Image models
  are parsed like TS `split('x'|':').map(Number)`: whitespace is allowed,
  extra parts are ignored, `"1024"` sends only `width`, a non-number part is
  sent as `null` (JSON) / `NaN` (edit form) instead of being dropped, and
  `"16:9:1"` is 16:9 instead of an invalid-aspect-ratio warning (review round
  84).
- **Am I affected?** `azure` image models `mai-image-*` with a malformed `Size` or `AspectRatio`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on malformed sizes being dropped.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### DeepSeek and Moonshot reasoning rounding {#deepseek-moonshot-reasoning-rounding}

- **What changed:** DeepSeek: `medium` is sent as `low` (was high) and `xhigh` as `high` (was max). Moonshot: `medium` as `low`, `xhigh` as `high`; Kimi K3 `none` as `low` with a compatibility warning (was dropped with an unsupported warning); Kimi K2.7 `none` gives a compatibility warning; unknown models send `thinking: {type: disabled}` for `none` (was dropped with a warning). TS `61a315e333`.
- **Am I affected?** `Reasoning` on `deepseek` and `moonshot` models.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the old effort; set `reasoningEffort` / `thinking` provider options to pin it.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Azure Speech 502 stays a retryable 502 {#azure-speech-502-retryable}

- **What changed:** An Azure Speech 502 whose body says `reset reason: protocol error` was a non-retryable 400 with a voice message; v0.6 keeps status 502, `Azure Speech request failed with status 502.` and the default retryability (TS `c3b6fef1ff`).
- **Am I affected?** Azure Speech (MAI-Voice / TTS) error handling that matched the 400.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### DeepSeek files: provider options are parsed first {#deepseek-files-options}

- **What changed:** In v0.5 DeepSeek `UploadFile` validated the file before `providerOptions.deepseek`, reported `expiresAfter` problems with its own error texts (`Field` `providerOptions.deepseek.expiresAfter`), and ignored `expiresAfter: nil`. v0.6 parses the options first, as TS does: an `InvalidArgumentError` with `Field` `providerOptions` and message `invalid deepseek provider options` (the zod issue is the cause), and null is rejected. A returned `filename: ""` stays in `ProviderMetadata["deepseek"]["filename"]`, and the result then has no filename (it used to have the request filename) (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching the old DeepSeek files error text or field, or passing `expiresAfter: nil`.
- **What to do:** Match `InvalidArgumentError` with `Field == "providerOptions"`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### OpenAI-compatible providers

#### gmicloud, ollama and alibaba report unknown usage for a response without usage {#compat-missing-usage-null}

- **What changed:** v0.5 gmicloud and ollama `DoGenerate` returned, for a
  response without a `usage` object (or with `usage: null`),
  `Usage.InputTokens`, `OutputTokens` and `TotalTokens` pointing to 0 and a
  `Usage.Raw` map with `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens` and
  `total_tokens` set to 0. v0.6 returns the null usage (all three pointers
  nil, `Raw` nil), as TS `convertOpenAICompatibleChatUsage(undefined)`
  returns `createNullLanguageModelUsage()`; together, fireworks and zai
  already did this. A `usage` object is mapped as before (review round 80).
  Since review round 81 Alibaba `DoGenerate` does the same (TS
  `convertAlibabaUsage(undefined | null)`).
- **Am I affected?** gmicloud / ollama / alibaba callers that dereference
  `result.Usage.InputTokens` (or `OutputTokens`, `TotalTokens`) or read
  `Usage.Raw` without a nil check.
- **What to do:**

  ```go
  // Before: n := *res.Usage.InputTokens
  var n int64
  if res.Usage.InputTokens != nil {
  	n = *res.Usage.InputTokens
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI-compatible generate results keep the TS reasoning and content order {#compat-generate-content-order}

- **What changed:** OpenAI-compatible generate results (gmicloud, together,
  fireworks, zai, ollama, Vertex xAI) follow TS doGenerate order
  (869601f532): `reasoning_content` (or `reasoning`) comes before the
  thinking parts of an array `content`, and when a thinking part follows
  text in `content`, `GenerateResult.Content` holds each text part as a
  `TextContent` in wire order. v0.5 put the thinking parts first and kept
  the text only in `Text`, joined, which pkg/ai places after all reasoning.
  `Text` is unchanged (review round 80).
- **Am I affected?** Direct `DoGenerate` callers of these providers that
  read `GenerateResult.Content` and expect only `ReasoningContent` parts, or
  rely on the order of reasoning parts when both `reasoning_content` and
  thinking parts are sent.
- **What to do:** no action unless you assume `Content` has no
  `TextContent`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI-compatible completion results drop an empty text part {#compat-completion-empty-text}

- **What changed:** an OpenAI-compatible completion `DoGenerate` result
  (`openai.Config{OpenAICompatible: true}` `CompletionModel`) whose `text` is
  empty has no `TextContent` in `Content` (TS `choice.text.length > 0`); v0.5
  always added one. Native OpenAI completion keeps the part (TS always
  pushes it). `Text` is unchanged (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Direct `DoGenerate` callers of an OpenAI-compatible
  completion model that index `result.Content[0]`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you index `Content`; read `Text`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI-compatible streams keep tool call thought signatures {#compat-stream-tool-thought-signature}

- **What changed:** an OpenAI-compatible chat stream (`openai.Config{OpenAICompatible:
  true}`) puts a tool call delta's `extra_content.google.thought_signature`
  on the `tool-call` chunk as
  `ToolCall.ProviderMetadata[<provider options key>].thoughtSignature`, as
  TS `extractMetadata` does and as `DoGenerate` already did; v0.5 dropped it
  in streams (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Streams from Gemini behind an OpenAI-compatible
  endpoint where you inspect tool call provider metadata.
- **What to do:** no action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Cerebras reports the request body it sends {#cerebras-request-body-extras}

- **What changed:** v0.5 merged the `providerOptions.cerebras` fields
  `parallel_tool_calls`, `top_logprobs`, `logit_bias`, `service_tier`,
  `reasoning_format` and `prompt_cache_key` into the request only at the
  HTTP transport, so `GenerateResult.RawRequest` and the stream's
  `RequestBody()` lacked them. v0.6 reports the body that was sent, as TS
  `request.body` is the transformed body (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads or compares `cerebras`
  `GenerateResult.RawRequest` or `provider.StreamRequestBody.RequestBody()`.
- **What to do:** no action unless you compare the reported body.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Ollama reports the deprecated openai-compatible key warning {#ollama-compat-key-warning}

- **What changed:** ollama `DoGenerate` / `DoStream` now report the
  `deprecated` warning "Use 'openaiCompatible' instead." for
  `providerOptions["openai-compatible"]` (and the deprecated raw common
  option names), and put the prepare-tools warnings after the option
  warnings, as TS openai-compatible `getArgs` does; v0.5 dropped the first
  and listed tool warnings first (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** Reading `GenerateResult.Warnings` / stream-start
  warnings of `ollama` models with `providerOptions["openai-compatible"]`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare warning lists; use the
  `openaiCompatible` key.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI-compatible completion streams follow TS {#compat-completion-stream-parity}

- **What changed:** an OpenAI-compatible completion stream
  (`openai.Config{OpenAICompatible: true}` `CompletionModel`, for example the
  baseten and deepinfra wrappers) ends with finish reason `other` when no
  `finish_reason` arrives (TS starts from `{unified: 'other'}`; v0.6 drafts
  reported `interrupted`), emits a text delta for an empty `text`, and turns
  an `{"error": ...}` chunk into an error part followed by the finish part
  instead of failing the stream before output (TS has no
  `throwIfOpenAIStreamErrorBeforeOutput` there). Native OpenAI and
  compatible completion streams report no raw finish reason after a parse
  or error chunk (`{unified: 'error', raw: undefined}`) (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** `CompletionModel.DoStream` of OpenAI-compatible
  providers: `FinishReasonInterrupted`, `Err()` after an error chunk,
  `RawFinishReason`.
- **What to do:** Handle the error part; no action otherwise.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Z.ai id lengths count UTF-16 units {#zai-id-length-utf16}

- **What changed:** zai `requestId` (6..64) and `userId` (6..128) lengths are
  counted in UTF-16 code units like the TS zod schema; v0.5 counted bytes and
  rejected valid non-ASCII ids (review round 82).
- **Am I affected?** zai `requestId` / `userId` with non-ASCII characters.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### Moonshot MFJS schema normalization keeps the TS key order {#moonshot-mfjs-key-order}

- **What changed:** v0.5 Moonshot MFJS normalization of a raw or ordered tool
  or response schema wrote an added `type: "object"` on a root union, and a
  `type` moved into `anyOf` branches, after the other keys, and sent an empty
  tuple `items: []` as `prefixItems: null`. v0.6 uses the TS key order
  (`{ type, ...rest }` puts `type` first) and sends `prefixItems: []`
  (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** Moonshot tool `Parameters` or `ResponseFormat.Schema`
  given as JSON text, an `*schema.OrderedSchema` or a struct schema, with a
  root `anyOf` / `oneOf` of objects, a `type` beside `anyOf`, or an empty
  tuple `items`.
- **What to do:** No action (key order and the empty tuple only).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Fireworks reasoning effort sent as given {#fireworks-reasoning-effort}

- **What changed:** Fireworks no longer rewrites `minimal` to `low` and `xhigh` / `max` to `high`: the provider option is sent unchanged and top-level levels as is, except `none` on gpt-oss / glm-5p3 / muse-spark (sent as `minimal`) and `minimal` on nemotron / inkling (sent as `low`), with compatibility warnings (TS `61a315e333`).
- **Am I affected?** `fireworks` models with `Reasoning` or `reasoningEffort`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the rewrite.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### OpenAI-compatible reasoningEffort null {#compat-reasoning-effort-null}

- **What changed:** OpenAI-compatible providers (gmicloud, together, fireworks, zai, ollama, alibaba, deepinfra and cerebras through the openai compat mode): `reasoningEffort: nil` (null) is accepted and sent as `"reasoning_effort": null`, beating the top-level `Reasoning`; a raw `reasoning_effort` provider option is passed through (no deprecation warning) and overwritten only when an effort resolves (TS `5006d83c4e`).
- **Am I affected?** Provider options `reasoningEffort: nil` or a raw `reasoning_effort` key on OpenAI-compatible providers.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

#### Cerebras: deprecated-key warning once {#cerebras-r85}

- **What changed:** `DoGenerate` reported the deprecated `openai-compatible` provider-options key warning twice; now once, as TS. An explicit `reasoningEffort: nil` is sent as `reasoning_effort: null` (TS `5006d83c4e`).
- **Am I affected?** Code counting Cerebras warnings or passing `reasoningEffort: nil`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

#### OpenAI-compatible thought signatures follow `??` {#compat-thought-signature-nullish}

- **What changed:** In v0.5 an empty `providerOptions[<provider>].thoughtSignature` on a replayed tool call fell back to `providerOptions.google.thoughtSignature`. v0.6 follows TS `??`: the provider's empty value wins, and no `extra_content` is sent (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** OpenAI-compatible family tool calls (ollama, Vertex xAI) with an empty provider-key `thoughtSignature` and a Google one.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Cohere

#### Cohere generate content lists text parts in wire order {#cohere-generate-text-parts-in-order}

- **What changed:** v0.5 put Cohere's response text only in
