- **What changed:** Groq usage filled `InputDetails.TextTokens` / `ImageTokens` from `prompt_tokens_details`; the TS schema reads only `cached_tokens`, so v0.6 leaves them unset (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading Groq `Usage.InputDetails.TextTokens` / `ImageTokens`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### HeyGen, Gateway decision and Vertex embedding option errors use the TS text {#provider-option-error-text-r89}

- **What changed:** Provider option errors use the TS parseProviderOptions form: argument `providerOptions`, message `invalid heygen provider options`, `invalid gateway provider options` or `invalid <key> provider options` (Vertex embeddings), with the schema problem as the cause. Gateway used the argument `providerOptions.gateway.models` with the problem in the message (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching those error strings.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the old text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### xAI API errors keep the response and use the TS message {#xai-api-errors}

- **What changed:** In v0.5, xAI image, transcription, speech, files, batch and video-status errors had no `ResponseHeaders`, `ResponseBody` or `Data`; a non-JSON error body became the message; the speech error ended with `(trace: <id>)`. In v0.6 these errors carry the response headers, body and parsed data (retries honor `retry-after` / `retry-after-ms`); a body that matches no xAI error shape gives the HTTP status text; `{code, error}` bodies give `<code>: <error>` (Responses model and video start too); the trace id is only in the response headers (`pe.ResponseHeader("x-trace-id")`; TS xaiFailedResponseHandler) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches `ProviderError.Message` from `xai` models, parses `(trace:` out of speech errors, or relies on the raw body in `Message`.
- **What to do:** Read `pe.ResponseHeader("x-trace-id")` (case-insensitive) and `pe.ResponseBody` instead.
- **Kind:** error text only

### More provider API errors keep response headers, body and data {#provider-error-response-metadata}

- **What changed:** In v0.5, Fireworks, Z.ai, Moonshot, Cerebras, DeepInfra image and OpenAI decision-model API errors had no (or incomplete) `ResponseHeaders`, `ResponseBody` and `Data`, so retries ignored `retry-after`. In v0.6 they are filled and retries wait as the provider asks. The OpenAI decision model gives the status text for a non-JSON body and keeps an empty `error.message`; a DeepInfra image body outside the error schema gives the status text (was the raw body or `Unknown error`); Fireworks flux-kontext image results carry the download's response headers (TS createJsonErrorResponseHandler) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that counts retry timing for those providers, or reads their `ProviderError.Message` for non-schema bodies.
- **What to do:** No action unless you depend on the old retry timing or message.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Responses embedded-error and no-output errors carry the response {#responses-embedded-error-metadata}

- **What changed:** The error returned for a 200 Responses reply with an `error` object or without `output` (OpenAI, Azure, Open Responses) now carries `ResponseHeaders` and `ResponseBody`; the OpenAI embedded error is explicitly non-retryable (TS APICallError fields) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that inspects these errors' `ResponseHeaders` / `ResponseBody` / `IsRetryable`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenAI-family error messages follow the error schema {#openai-error-message-schema}

- **What changed:** OpenAI, Azure, DeepSeek, Together and the OpenAI-compatible wrappers whose TS model uses the default error structure (DeepInfra, Vertex MaaS, Together completions; Fireworks completions use `fireworksErrorStructure`) give `error.message` only for bodies of the OpenAI error shape (an empty message is kept), and the HTTP status text otherwise. v0.5 also read top-level `message` / `detail` / string `error`. Wrappers set this with the new `openai.Config.CompatErrorStructure`; Baseten and Cerebras keep their own shapes (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches `ProviderError.Message` for such bodies.
- **What to do:** Read `pe.ResponseBody` for the raw body.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Together rerank, xAI transcription words and video headers follow TS {#openai-family-r89-details}

- **What changed:** Together reranking sends an explicit empty `RankFields` as `rank_fields: []`; xAI transcription word metadata keeps a present `speaker: null`; xAI and Alibaba video result headers join repeated values with `, ` (last `Set-Cookie` wins), keys stay Go canonical (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Together rerank calls with an empty `RankFields`; readers of xAI word metadata or repeated video response headers.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### KlingAI and ByteDance errors unwrap to a ProviderError {#klingai-bytedance-error-response}

- **What changed:** `*klingai.Error` / `*bytedance.Error` from an HTTP response now unwrap to a `*providererrors.ProviderError` carrying status, response headers, body and data; retries honor `retry-after-ms` / `retry-after` (TS APICallError). `errors.As` on the provider types keeps working (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `errors.As(err, &pe)` with `pe *providererrors.ProviderError` on KlingAI / ByteDance errors now succeeds; retry timing after 429 / 503.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Gladia poll errors carry data {#gladia-poll-error-data}

- **What changed:** `ProviderError.Data` on Gladia result-poll errors is the parsed `{error:{message,code}}` body (TS zod output) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading Gladia poll `ProviderError.Data`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MiniMax video API errors unwrap to a ProviderError {#minimax-video-api-error-unwrap}

- **What changed:** A MiniMax video API error (`*minimax.Error` with a status code, start or status poll) now unwraps to a `*providererrors.ProviderError` with status, response headers and body, so retries honor `retry-after(-ms)` (TS APICallError). Duration warnings and the failed-task code use JS number formatting (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `errors.As(err, &pe)` with `pe *providererrors.ProviderError` on MiniMax video errors; text of MiniMax duration warnings.
- **What to do:** No action unless you depended on no match.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Prodia, DeepInfra, Cerebras, QuiverAI and web fetch details follow TS {#tools-providers-r89-wire}

- **What changed:** Prodia txt2vid and image JSON bodies use the TS key order and the fixed `Accept` header wins over a call header; img2vid keeps an empty file media type (an octet-stream `input` part) and sends an empty image; Prodia download errors are the `DownloadError` unchanged. DeepInfra image edits send form fields in TS order (an option named like a fixed field replaces it, values use JS `String()`), and a size without `x` sends the width only. A Cerebras structured-output response with a legacy `function_call` finish keeps its tool calls. The web fetch tool reports any uppercase network error code (TS formatErrorCode); QuiverAI option errors use JS `String()` text (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Those providers' request bodies, multipart order or error text.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Google Vertex Anthropic: call headers win over provider headers {#vertex-anthropic-header-precedence}

- **What changed:** v0.5: `Options.Headers` / `HeadersResolver` values were set last and replaced call headers, the generated token for a call `Authorization`, and the computed `anthropic-beta` value. v0.6: call headers win over provider headers, a provider `anthropic-beta` adds to the computed betas (a static `Options.Headers` value in the TS position, after the computed and before the call and option betas), and a call `x-vertex-ai-labels` header beats a provider one, which beats the generated one (TS getHeaders / getBetasFromHeaders) (review round 89). Round 90: a `HeadersResolver` value is resolved once per call before the request is built, and its `anthropic-beta` takes the same TS position as a static one.
- **Am I affected?** `googlevertex/anthropic.Options.Headers`, `HeadersResolver`, `anthropic-beta`, `GenerateOptions.Headers` with the same header names.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on a provider header overriding a call header.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Anthropic option checks and forced-tool fallback follow TS {#anthropic-r89-request-options}

- **What changed:** `block_binding` and its beta are sent only for `adaptive` (and the binding-only) thinking, and an invalid `display` / `blockBinding` on other thinking types is dropped instead of rejected (zod strips undeclared union keys); empty `mcpServers` name / url, skill IDs and fallback models are accepted (`z.string()`); a fractional Files API `size_bytes` no longer fails (metadata keeps the number); a forced-tool fallback that keeps no declared tool sends `tools: []` (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Anthropic calls with those option combinations.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Bedrock option fallback, error data and guard content follow TS {#bedrock-r89-options-and-errors}

- **What changed:** An explicit `amazonBedrock: null` falls back to the legacy `bedrock` options for embeddings and images; Bedrock API errors carry `Data` (`{message, type?}`); an empty `guardContentQualifiers` list is sent as `[]` (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Bedrock calls with those inputs, or code reading Bedrock `ProviderError.Data`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ElevenLabs conflict text, keyterm length and realtime errors follow TS {#elevenlabs-r89}

- **What changed:** With a composition plan plus both `forceInstrumental` and `musicLengthMs`, the error names `forceInstrumental` (TS loop order); `keyterms` length counts UTF-16 units; a realtime error event with a non-string `error` ends the stream with an error instead of being ignored until the socket closes, and `error: ""` is used as the message (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** ElevenLabs callers matching those texts or using non-BMP keyterms.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Anthropic and Bedrock error texts quote values like TS {#anthropic-template-quotes}

- **What changed:** Error texts that quote a value (Anthropic batch and request errors, Bedrock Converse, the Anthropic prompt converter) use the TS template form `"value"` instead of Go `%q` escaping (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching those error strings with escaped quotes or non-printable characters.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Anthropic results have one text part per text block {#anthropic-text-block-parts}

- **What changed:** `GenerateResult.Content` now has one `TextContent` per Anthropic text block when a response has several (for example with citations) or a single empty one; v0.5 left `Content` nil and merged them into `Text`. `Text` is unchanged, as TS doGenerate pushes a text part per block (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that assumes `Content == nil` for plain-text Anthropic responses, or counts text parts.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on that.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A null Anthropic tool input stays null {#anthropic-null-tool-input}

- **What changed:** A null tool input in Anthropic and Bedrock Converse responses is `RawArguments == "null"` (was `"{}"`); Converse array or string inputs keep their JSON text; a null provider-executed input is replayed as `null`, as TS JSON.stringify (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading `RawArguments` of such tool calls.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Anthropic and Anthropic AWS error texts and types {#anthropic-r90-error-texts}

- **What changed:** An invalid `providerOptions.anthropic` message is exactly `invalid anthropic provider options` with the details in the cause (TS parseProviderOptions); `anthropicaws.New` missing region / workspace ID errors are `*providererrors.LoadSettingError` with the TS loadSetting text; a bad ElevenLabs `/with-timestamps` body is an APICallError "Invalid JSON response"; temperature clamp warnings print JS numbers (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `invalid anthropic provider options: `, `anthropicaws: AWS region is required`, `workspaceId is required`, or those warning texts.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As` with `*providererrors.InvalidArgumentError` / `*providererrors.LoadSettingError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Anthropic prompt details follow TS {#prova-r90-details}

- **What changed:** Assistant parts read their cache control before the part type (reasoning, custom and file parts use a breakpoint, a later breakpoint past four is dropped); `toolsetName: ""` still marks a toolset member; a circular-reference check walks non-record map keywords; ElevenLabs reads `error.message` when `detail` does not match (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Anthropic prompts with cache control on non-text assistant parts, empty toolset names; ElevenLabs error bodies with both `detail` and `error`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Gemini streamed tool arguments: dotted array indexes {#gemini-dotted-array-indexes}

- **What changed:** Streamed Gemini tool calls whose partial-argument path addressed an existing array with a dotted numeric segment (`items.0.name`) were not converted to array indexes (v0.5.2 could panic). v0.6 converts canonical numeric segments to indexes before the contiguous-index check, as TS 304dec5081 (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Streaming with `google` / `googlevertex` tool calls (`ChunkTypeToolInputDelta`, `ToolCall.Arguments`).
- **What to do:** No action unless you worked around malformed array arguments.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Gateway takoSearch: variable cost options and output schema {#gateway-tako-search-variable-cost}

- **What changed:** `tools.TakoSearchConfig` gains `VariableCost *bool`, `MaxVariableCostUSD *float64`, `NoMaxVariableCost` (sends null) and `ExtraBody`. The tool's output schema follows TS 0e869b40fa, so result fields such as `relevance_score` are present (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `tools.NewTakoSearch`, `Tools.TakoSearch`, code that reads takoSearch results by schema.
- **What to do:** No action unless you validate takoSearch output against the old schema.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Mistral: generated reasoning ids and normalized conversation errors {#mistral-r90-stream-ids-errors}

- **What changed:** Streamed reasoning blocks had the fixed id `reasoning-0`; they now get a generated id per block (TS generateId; new `mistral.Config.GenerateID`). A Conversations `conversation.response.error` event carries `Err` as a normalized `*providererrors.StreamProviderError`, as TS normalizeStreamProviderError, and a missing execution start has an `Err` too (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches reasoning chunk ids equal to `"reasoning-0"`, or reads `StreamChunk.Err` / `Text` of Mistral conversation error chunks.
- **What to do:** Use the block id from the start chunk; set `GenerateID` for deterministic ids; use `errors.As` with `*providererrors.StreamProviderError`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider request text follows TS JSON.stringify and defaults {#provg-r90-request-text}

- **What changed:** Cohere rerank object documents are JSON.stringify'd in key order; Gateway rerank documents are always the V4 union; Groq transcription defaults the media type to `audio/wav`; `Request.Body` of Google Gemini TTS, Vertex Chirp TTS, Mistral, OpenAI and xAI speech is JSON.stringify of the request body (key order of the TS literal, `<`, `>`, `&` literal) and OpenAI / xAI send that text; Mistral sends JS number text for temperature and stream content indexes; Gateway video sends present empty `frameImages` / `inputReferences`; Gateway error messages re-serialize the body in its own key order (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares request bodies or `SpeechResult.Request.Body` text, Groq transcription without `MimeType`, Gateway error text for non-gateway error bodies.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare exact request or error text.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Numeric options and usage counts follow z.number() {#provg-r90-numbers}

- **What changed:** Numeric provider options accept every Go number kind and fractions and reject NaN and Infinity (Gemini option fields, Google embedding `outputDimensionality`, Groq `temperature`, Mistral options, Google Files `pollIntervalMs` / `pollTimeoutMs`). Fractional usage counts no longer fail, zero or truncate before summing: Cohere, Mistral and Vertex embedding `Usage.Tokens`; DeepSeek, Mistral, Bedrock Converse and Open Responses language-model usage (truncated into the integer `Usage` fields instead of left at zero); Mistral transcription `ProviderUsage` (the number kept). Gateway `GetGenerationInfo`, `GetSpendReport` and client secrets decode fractional values into the existing integer fields (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code that relied on these errors, on zero usage for fractional counts, or on truncated token sums.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Gateway option and response parsing {#gateway-r90-parsing}

- **What changed:** `GetGenerationInfo` with an empty ID and `GetSpendReport` with empty dates call the API instead of failing locally; metadata drops models with an empty `modelType`; a client secret with an empty token is accepted; decision `models` given as Go-typed lists are validated in their JSON form; a decision call reports an unsupported file input before invalid options; the realtime WebSocket team scope comes from `Config.TeamIDOrSlug`; a video SSE error event's `ResponseBody` is JSON.stringify of the event (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `GetGenerationInfo(... ID: "")`, `GetSpendReport` without dates, `GetAvailableModels`, `MintRealtimeClientSecret`, decision `ProviderOptions["gateway"]["models"]`, `RealtimeModel.GetWebSocketConfig` with a team header, gateway video error `ResponseBody`.
- **What to do:** Set `TeamIDOrSlug` to scope realtime. No other action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Google and Vertex transcription and live streams {#google-r90-transcription}

- **What changed:** Unary Google transcription skips a text content with a null text, keeps an empty word, reports an empty `usage` object and returns `Warnings: []`. Live transcription and speech-translation streams (Google and Vertex) emit a raw part for every message that parses and none for a message with a `__proto__` key. The Vertex video "No videos in response" error prints the operation like JSON.stringify. Google Interactions send a given empty `responseModalities` list as `[]` (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `TranscriptionResult.Segments` / `ProviderUsage`, `IncludeRawChunks` consumers, Vertex video error text, Interactions `ResponseModalities: []string{}`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenAI parallel tool call metadata keeps the input once {#openai-parallel-wrapper-input}

- **What changed:** `ParallelToolCallMetadata.Input` is set on the first expanded child only (the others are empty and left out of JSON); groups are rebuilt from it in any order, and persisted repeated input is still accepted, as TS 22b8158276 (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading `ParallelToolCallMetadata.Input` on every expanded child.
- **What to do:** Read the input from the first child of the group.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### xAI video options error text {#xai-video-option-error}

- **What changed:** An invalid `providerOptions.xai` for video is exactly `invalid xai provider options`, the details in the cause (TS parseProviderOptions) (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `invalid xai provider options: `.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.Unwrap`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### OpenAI and Azure media details follow TS {#openai-azure-r90-details}

- **What changed:** OpenAI and DeepInfra image edits type a downloaded input or mask by its response content type (was `application/octet-stream`); Azure MAI image uses the downloaded content type; Azure MAI streaming keeps an empty transcript and error code; OpenAI diarized segments keep an empty speaker; OpenAI realtime transcription sends the final part for an empty item id; OpenAI-compatible image-edit provider options named like a form field (`size`, `prompt`, `n`, ...) replace that field in place (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** OpenAI / OpenAI-compatible / DeepInfra image edits from URLs or with such provider options; Azure MAI; OpenAI diarized and realtime transcription.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Multipart uploads use the TS FormData part format {#multipart-formdata-parts}

- **What changed:** Multipart file parts follow TS FormData (undici): the part `Content-Type` is the Blob type, ASCII-lowercased (`application/octet-stream` when empty or not printable); `"`, CR and LF in the field name and filename are written as `%22`, `%0D`, `%0A` (Go wrote `\"` or dropped CR/LF); a skills file with an empty path has no `filename` parameter (was `blob`); string field values and names normalize line breaks to CRLF. This covers every FormData upload (transcriptions, image edits, Files and skills uploads, batches, Prodia, TypeSafe AI) (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Servers or tests that read multipart part headers or field values byte for byte.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### QuiverAI reasoning history without QuiverAI metadata has no item id {#quiverai-reasoning-replay-item-id}

- **What changed:** A replayed reasoning part with no `quiverai` metadata was sent with `"id": ""`, so consecutive such parts merged into one item. v0.6 sends no id and separate items, as TS `itemId: metadata?.itemId` (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `quiverai` language models with `types.ReasoningContent` in the history that does not come from QuiverAI.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### QuiverAI, MiniMax, Voyage, DeepInfra and Topaz request details follow TS {#tools-providers-r90-wire}

- **What changed:** Request body JSON keys follow the TS body literal order (QuiverAI image, MiniMax video, Voyage embedding / rerank, DeepInfra image generate, Topaz video); QuiverAI empty `referenceImages` counts as given; a Topaz image `size` is split like JS (`1024.5x768`, `2048`); Topaz image failures are `*topaz.Error` with the TS AISDKError name and a missing process id or download URL is an `InvalidResponseDataError` (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code comparing those request bodies, passing `referenceImages: []` or odd sizes, or matching Topaz error types.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Cartesia, Replicate, fal and Baseten details follow TS {#provc-r90-details}

- **What changed:** `providerOptions.cartesia.language: ""` is sent as an empty `language` form field (batch) and rejected with the English-only error (streaming), as TS `!= null`; the fal video operation's `submitUrl` keeps the `?fal_webhook=` query; Baseten model URL errors use the TS texts and a `/sync/v1` URL that also contains `/predict` is used for chat (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `providerOptions["cartesia"]["language"] == ""`; fal `submitUrl` with a webhook; Baseten URL error text.
- **What to do:** Omit the Cartesia key to mean unset.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An unfollowed 3xx response is an HTTP error {#http-3xx-status-error}

- **What changed:** The internal JSON and stream clients decoded the body of an unfollowed 3xx response. v0.6 returns an HTTP status error for any non-2xx status, as TS post-to-api.ts (`!response.ok`) (review round 91). Round 94: the provider calls that check the status themselves do the same (TS fetch `response.ok`): Google Files and batch, Groq transcription, Mistral realtime sessions, speech and transcription, Perplexity, HeyGen, Vertex Cloud TTS, the Gateway metadata, credits, generation and spend calls; Anthropic Files, Skills and batch results; OpenAI Files, batch and Skills, xAI Files, batch, image, speech, transcription and video, DeepSeek Files; Deepgram, Cartesia, Hume and Fish Audio speech, the BFL image poll, the fal transcription poll, Luma, ByteDance image and video, the Replicate image download; Topaz, DeepInfra, MiniMax video creation and the Vercel Sandbox stdin upload. An unfollowed 3xx there fails with the provider API error and its status.
- **Am I affected?** Endpoints that answer with a 3xx the client does not follow.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on decoding such a body.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MiniMax video errors follow TS {#minimax-video-errors}

- **What changed:** MiniMax video transport failures were not retryable, a cancelled `DoGenerate` was wrapped as `video generation aborted: ...`, API errors had no `Data`, non-matching error bodies kept their text and NaN / Infinity durations were sent. v0.6 follows TS minimax-video-model.ts: transport failures stay retryable, a cancel returns the context error, errors carry `Data`, non-matching bodies give the status text and non-finite durations warn (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** MiniMax video error handling.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)` for cancels.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Cerebras and DeepInfra unsupported model types return NoSuchModelError {#cerebras-deepinfra-model-types}

- **What changed:** Cerebras `EmbeddingModel` / `ImageModel` / `SpeechModel` / `TranscriptionModel` and DeepInfra `SpeechModel` / `TranscriptionModel` returned inherited OpenAI models that could not work. v0.6 returns a `*NoSuchModelError`, as the TS factories (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Those factory calls.
- **What to do:** Remove the calls; they never worked against these APIs.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Bedrock connection failures are the retryable Cannot connect to API ProviderError {#bedrock-transport-errors}

- **What changed:** A Bedrock Converse generate or stream request that could not reach the API returned `amazon-bedrock request failed: Post ...: <dial error>` (a plain error), and embedding, image and rerank a `ProviderError` with the bare transport text. v0.6 returns a retryable `*providererrors.ProviderError` `Cannot connect to API: <cause>` with the cause wrapped (TS handleFetchError); cancel and deadline errors are returned unchanged. The Anthropic batch results download reads the same way (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching `amazon-bedrock request failed`, or type-asserting a Bedrock connection error.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As(err, &pe)` with `*providererrors.ProviderError` and `pe.IsRetryable()`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Anthropic DoStartBatch drops a configured anthropic-beta header {#anthropic-batch-config-beta}

- **What changed:** `DoStartBatch` sent a provider-level `Config.Headers` `anthropic-beta` header. v0.6 sends none when the batch computes no betas, and otherwise only `providerOptions.anthropic.anthropicBeta` plus the betas the requests need (TS getStartBatchHeaders). Other batch calls still send the configured header (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `anthropic.Config{Headers: {"anthropic-beta": ...}}` with `ExperimentalBatch().DoStartBatch`.
- **What to do:** Pass the betas as `ProviderOptions: {"anthropic": {"anthropicBeta": []string{...}}}` on the start call.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ElevenLabs streaming-only warning names experimental_streamSpeech {#elevenlabs-streaming-warning-text}

- **What changed:** The warning for options ElevenLabs supports only when streaming read `(ExperimentalStreamSpeech)`. v0.6 reads `(experimental_streamSpeech)`, as TS (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching that warning text.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match it.
- **Kind:** error text only

### AWS credential errors use the TS texts {#aws-sigv4-credentials-text}

- **What changed:** anthropicaws, Bedrock Mantle and Bedrock Anthropic missing-credential errors named Go fields, and an incomplete `CredentialProvider` result had Go texts. v0.6 lists the TS options (`2. Provide accessKeyId and secretAccessKey in options`, ...) and fails an incomplete result with `accessKeyId is a required option` / `secretAccessKey is a required option` (aws4fetch), Bedrock included (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching `Provide AccessKeyID and SecretAccessKey`, `Provide Credentials in Config`, `incomplete credentials` or `missing accessKeyId or secretAccessKey`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Go-typed provider option literals are honored {#typed-provider-option-literals}

- **What changed:** Provider options given as Go-typed literals (`map[string]string`, `map[string]bool`, `[]map[string]interface{}`, typed option structs such as `&anthropic.ModelOptions{...}`, `fireworks.WithThinking(...)` or `*zai.ThinkingConfig`, `int32` / `int64` / `float32` numbers) were validated and then dropped, or rejected with `invalid ... provider options`, by many providers. v0.6 reads every options namespace and message / part / tool `ProviderOptions` through its JSON form, so typed literals work like the `map[string]interface{}` form; numbers given as ints reach the wire through the same JSON (TS options are plain JSON values; coordinator ruling round 91). This covers every provider with option readers (repo-wide probe test) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Typed values in `ProviderOptions`, part / message `ProviderOptions` or `types.Tool.ProviderOptions`.
- **What to do:** No action; check that the now-applied options are the ones you meant.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenAI-compatible embedding batches are not split by bytes {#compat-embedding-byte-budget}

- **What changed:** `openai.EmbeddingModel.MaxInputBytesPerCall()` applied the OpenAI byte budget to `Config.OpenAICompatible` wrappers (Baseten, DeepInfra, Vertex MaaS), so `EmbedMany` split their batches. v0.6 returns 0 for those wrappers (TS openai-compatible has no budget) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `EmbedMany` with Baseten, DeepInfra or Vertex MaaS.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Moonshot and DeepSeek content order is reasoning, tool calls, text {#moonshot-deepseek-content-order}

- **What changed:** Moonshot and DeepSeek `DoGenerate` content was reasoning, text, tool calls. v0.6 orders reasoning, tool calls, text, as TS (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads Moonshot / DeepSeek content by index.
- **What to do:** Look parts up by type.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Prompt.Text is one user message for Responses-style models {#responses-prompt-text}

- **What changed:** Open Responses, Hugging Face, xAI and OpenAI / Azure Responses sent a `Prompt.Text` prompt as no input. v0.6 sends it as one user message (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Direct `DoGenerate` / `DoStream` calls with `Prompt{Text: ...}` on those models.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Fireworks async image errors are ProviderErrors with the status text {#fireworks-async-image-errors}

- **What changed:** Fireworks async image submit / poll HTTP failures were wrapped fmt errors (`Fireworks async ... failed`). v0.6 returns a `*ProviderError` with the status text (TS fireworks-image-model.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching `Fireworks async` error texts.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As` with `*ProviderError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Open Responses provider key wins over the openai alias {#openresponses-provider-key}

- **What changed:** When both `providerOptions["openai"]` and `providerOptions[<Config.Name>]` were given, Open Responses let the `openai` alias win. v0.6 lets the provider key win, as TS (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Open Responses calls that pass both namespaces.
- **What to do:** Pass one namespace.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenAI, Azure, xAI, Moonshot, DeepSeek and Open Responses details follow TS {#provb-r91-details}

- **What changed:** Small TS alignments (review round 91): OpenAI chat sends only `mode` / `ttl` of `promptCacheOptions` and warns once per removed system message; OpenAI batch uploads the file part first; OpenAI Decisions schema failures are `Invalid JSON response`; OpenAI Live rejects unknown keys in delegation / voice / input items and present empty `outputModalities` / `tools`; OpenAI Responses configuration-update errors use the TS "Configuration updates ..." text; Azure MAI transcription close errors add ` (code N: reason)`; xAI image moderation / video request_id / realtime client secret errors carry the bare TS text, video limit errors are `invalid xai provider options` and response headers join repeated values; Moonshot / DeepSeek prompt checks return `*InvalidPromptError`; the Open Responses User-Agent suffix is `ai-sdk-open-responses/<v>`; OpenAI-family converters send `content: []` for an empty user message; `BatchResultDownloads.MaxLineBytes` above 2^53-1 is rejected.
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches these texts or wire details.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match them.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ByteDance HTTP error text is the TS message {#bytedance-error-text}

- **What changed:** ByteDance errors read `bytedance error (code 400): bad input`. v0.6 reads the TS message (`bad input`); `Code` and the unwrapped ProviderError are unchanged (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching `bytedance error (code`.
- **What to do:** Match on `Code`, or `errors.As(*ProviderError).Message`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Baseten media models return NoSuchModelError {#baseten-media-no-such-model}

- **What changed:** Baseten `ImageModel`, `SpeechModel` and `TranscriptionModel` returned the inherited OpenAI models pointed at a Baseten URL. v0.6 returns a `*NoSuchModelError`, as TS baseten-provider.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Those Baseten factory calls.
- **What to do:** None for working code; those models could not work against Baseten.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### BFL, AssemblyAI, fal and Gladia errors follow TS {#provc-r91-media-errors}

- **What changed:** v0.6 (review round 91): a BFL video timeout is a `*providererrors.VideoGenerationError` with the same text; an AssemblyAI abort between polls reads `Transcription request was aborted` (still `errors.Is(err, context.Canceled)`); a non-JSON or null fal status error body is an error status `Failed to process error response` (transcription returns that ProviderError message); Gladia `ErrorCode` is `TranscriptionJobFailed` or `TranscriptionResultEmpty` and `Data` holds only status, error_code and result.
- **Am I affected?** Matching these error texts, types or `Data`.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As` / `errors.Is`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### ByteDance usage metadata and status poll key {#bytedance-metadata-poll}

- **What changed:** ByteDance metadata had a `usage` key with a nil value when the response had none, and a status poll without any API key sent an unauthenticated request. v0.6 omits an absent `usage`, makes it a map (`completion_tokens` an int when whole, else float64), and loads the key per request, returning a `LoadAPIKeyError` without one (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Reading ByteDance `ProviderMetadata["bytedance"]["usage"]`, or polling without a key.
- **What to do:** Check for a missing `usage` key.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Media providers join repeated response headers {#media-response-headers-joined}

- **What changed:** BFL, ByteDance, Luma, KlingAI, Replicate, fal, Gladia and Rev.ai kept only the first value of a repeated response header. v0.6 joins them with ", " (TS extractResponseHeaders) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Reading repeated headers from those providers' `Response.Headers` or error `ResponseHeaders`.
- **What to do:** Split on ", " if you need the values.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### BFL video polls send the X-Key credential {#bfl-video-poll-auth}

- **What changed:** BFL FLUX 3 video status polls were sent without `X-Key` or the provider user agent. v0.6 sends both to a trusted polling URL; a missing key is a `LoadAPIKeyError` (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** BFL video polling through proxies that strip or require headers.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Google decision models use the native Gemini Decisions API {#google-decision-native}

- **What changed:** `google.Provider.DecisionModel(id)` wrapped a Gemini language model with `ai.NewDecisionLanguageModel`. v0.6 calls `POST {baseURL}/decisions` and returns native choice / score / boolean answers (TS f70336158e). Language model IDs no longer work; use `google.ModelGeminiDecision1`. `MaxOutputTokens`, `Reasoning` and `providerOptions.google` become `unsupported` warnings, box questions fail with `*errors.DecisionUnsupportedQuestionTypeError`, every question needs `Instructions`, choice and score need 2 to 255 options or levels, a safety-blocked input gives refusals, and `providerMetadata.google` holds `confidence`, `legend`, `usage` and `promptFeedback` (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `.DecisionModel(` or `.EvaluationModel(` on a `google.Provider`.
- **What to do:** Use the decision model ID:

```go
// before
model, _ := google.New(google.Config{}).DecisionModel(google.ModelGemini35FlashLite)
// after
model, _ = google.New(google.Config{}).DecisionModel(google.ModelGeminiDecision1)
```

- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Google Interactions usage has no per-modality input details {#google-interactions-usage-details}

- **What changed:** `Usage.InputDetails.TextTokens` / `ImageTokens` came from `input_tokens_by_modality`; TS convertGoogleInteractionsUsage reports only total, noCache and cacheRead. The breakdown stays in `Usage.Raw` (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `InputDetails.TextTokens` or `InputDetails.ImageTokens` from a Google Interactions model.
- **What to do:** Read `input_tokens_by_modality` from `Usage.Raw`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Google Interactions background streams report GET failures in the stream {#google-interactions-background-get}

- **What changed:** A background `DoStream` (`providerOptions.google.background: true`) opened the GET before returning, so a GET failure failed `DoStream` and core retried with a new POST (a duplicate run). v0.6 returns at once and reports a GET failure as a stream error after stream-start (TS stream-google-interactions.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Error handling of `DoStream` / `StreamText` with background Interactions.
- **What to do:** No action unless you treated a `DoStream` error as the GET failure; read it from the stream.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Missing API key returns LoadAPIKeyError unwrapped {#load-api-key-error-unwrapped}

- **What changed:** Mistral embedding / speech / transcription, Groq transcription, Cohere embedding and Perplexity embedding wrapped a missing key in a status-0 `*ProviderError` (`mistral provider error (0):  - Mistral API key is missing...`). v0.6 returns the `LoadAPIKeyError` itself, as TS (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Type assertions of `*ProviderError` or text matching on missing-key errors from those models.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As(err, &loadErr)` with `*errors.LoadAPIKeyError` (which also worked before).
- **Kind:** error text only

### Google and Vertex transport failures read Cannot connect to API {#google-vertex-connect-errors}

- **What changed:** Google batch upload / download, the Files processing poll and Vertex transcription returned a raw `*url.Error`; Vertex Cloud TTS and embedding added `failed to synthesize speech: ` / `vertex embedding request failed: `. v0.6 returns the retryable TS `Cannot connect to API: ...` error. The Files resumable upload start and upload, and the realtime client secret, still return the transport error itself (TS calls `fetch` directly there); the realtime error no longer includes the `?key=` URL (review rounds 91, 93).
- **Am I affected?** Matching those texts or type-asserting `*url.Error`.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As` for `*url.Error` (still wrapped) or check `IsRetryable()`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Gateway batch status omits inconsistent request counts {#gateway-batch-request-counts}

- **What changed:** `BatchV4Status.RequestCounts` was set from any counts. v0.6 sets it only when all four counts are non-negative integers and pending + completed + failed == total (TS normalizeBatchRequestCounts); succeeded results decode as V4 generate results with their response metadata (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `RequestCounts` from gateway `DoGetBatchStatus` / `DoStartBatch` / `DoCancelBatch`.
- **What to do:** Handle a nil `RequestCounts`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Gateway invalid success bodies give the TS error text {#gateway-success-schema-errors}

- **What changed:** A Gateway metadata, spend-report or client-secret success body that failed the schema gave `Invalid error response format: Gateway request failed: <Go decode text>` with status 500. v0.6 gives `Invalid error response format: Gateway request failed` with the real HTTP status and an "Invalid JSON response" cause; spend rows with a null optional field fail, as TS (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching those error texts.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Gemini sends args: null for a JSON null tool input {#gemini-null-tool-args}

- **What changed:** An assistant tool call whose input is `null` (or the string `"null"`) was sent with `functionCall.args: {}`. v0.6 sends `null`, as TS parseToolCallInput; a call with no input still sends `{}` (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Replaying assistant tool calls with null input to Google or Vertex Gemini.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Vertex xAI provider headers win and compatible options are validated {#vertex-xai-headers-validation}

- **What changed:** A Vertex xAI call header beat a `Config.Headers` value of the same name, and invalid `openaiCompatible` / `openai-compatible` / `googleVertex` options were not checked. v0.6 lets the provider header win (TS node customFetch) and fails invalid options (for example `user: 123`) with `invalid ... provider options` before any request (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** The same header set on provider and call, or invalid compatible options, with `googlevertex/xai`.
- **What to do:** Set per-call values only in the call, and fix the option types.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Gemini streams File Search and RAG document sources {#gemini-stream-document-sources}

- **What changed:** `DoStream` / `StreamText` emitted only URL sources. v0.6 also streams document sources (File Search `fileSearchStore`, `gs://` RAG), each once per stream (TS 540509c068), and `providerMetadata.google.serviceTier` falls back to the `x-gemini-service-tier` header (TS 45337f0e8d) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Counting or filtering stream source parts from Google / Vertex.
- **What to do:** No action unless you assume every streamed source is a URL.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Media download errors keep the server reason phrase {#download-status-reason-phrase}

- **What changed:** A failed provider media download (`StatusCodeDownloadError`) used `http.StatusText` as the `ProviderError` message. v0.6 uses the server's reason phrase, as TS response.statusText (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching media download error messages.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Provider option number keys, integers and URLs follow JS and zod {#option-schema-js-coercion}

- **What changed:** Option checks accepted number keys JS `Number()` rejects (`1_000`, `-0x10`), unsafe integers (`1e20`) and rejected URLs `z.url()` accepts (`file:///tmp/x`, a leading space). v0.6 follows JS and zod 4: OpenAI `logitBias` keys, integer options and BFL / Deepgram URL options (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Those exotic option values.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Rev.ai job errors, headers and config order follow TS {#revai-r91-details}

- **What changed:** Rev.ai job submission and job failures had no error code, repeated response headers kept one value without byte decoding, and the `config` form field had sorted keys. v0.6 sets `ErrorCode` `TranscriptionJobSubmissionFailed` / `TranscriptionJobFailed`, joins and decodes headers (TS extractResponseHeaders) and sends `config` in TS key order (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Rev.ai error handling or wire comparisons.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Bedrock reads AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK when the provider is created {#aws-api-key-env-at-creation}

- **What changed:** Bedrock read `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` on every request. v0.6 reads it once, when the provider is created, and then picks bearer or SigV4 authentication, as TS createAmazonBedrock (`loadOptionalSetting`); Bedrock Anthropic and Bedrock Mantle do the same, and `anthropicaws` reads `ANTHROPIC_AWS_API_KEY` at creation as v0.5.2 did. SigV4 credentials are still loaded per request (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** Setting or rotating `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` after creating a `bedrock`, `bedrock/anthropic` or `bedrock/mantle` provider.
- **What to do:** Create the provider after setting the variable, or pass the key in `Config.APIKey` (or the provider's key or token function) to resolve it yourself.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Provider timestamps are parsed like JS Date {#js-date-timestamps}

- **What changed:** Provider response timestamps were parsed with `time.Parse` (RFC 3339 or RFC 1123), which kept sub-millisecond digits and dropped forms TS `new Date(str)` accepts (a date-only string, a zone-less time, RFC 850). v0.6 parses them like `Date.parse` with millisecond precision: Google Interactions `created` (generate and stream), the Google realtime token `expireTime`, the Gateway response-metadata, batch result and transcription stream timestamps, the speech stream envelope timestamp, Anthropic Files `created_at` / `expires_at`, the xAI batch expiry check and the Bedrock `date` header (review round 92). `harnessutil.ParseJSDate` is unchanged. Perplexity response timestamps keep whole milliseconds of a fractional `created_at` (TS `new Date(created_at * 1000)`; review round 93).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares `ResponseMetadata.Timestamp` (or these fields) with sub-millisecond precision.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on sub-millisecond timestamps.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### BFL and Perplexity URL values follow zod z.url() {#zod-url-values}

- **What changed:** BFL sent `webhookUrl` as given, polled `polling_url` as given, and the z.url() checks (BFL submit and poll, Perplexity citations) rejected a URL with surrounding whitespace. v0.6 follows zod 4 `z.url()`: the value is checked JS-trimmed with the WHATWG URL parser, and BFL sends `webhook_url` and polls `polling_url` as the zod output (trimmed, tab / LF / CR removed); `providerutils.ZodURLValue` is that output (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** BFL `webhookUrl` values with surrounding whitespace or line breaks; servers that answer with such URLs.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Gateway forwards provider options as given {#gateway-provider-options-as-given}

- **What changed:** With `providerOptions.gateway.serviceTier` set, v0.5 removed `serviceTier` / `service_tier` from every other provider's options before sending, and it left out an empty `providerOptions` map (language and decision models). v0.6 forwards the options as given, as TS getArgs does: the Gateway applies `gateway.serviceTier` over a provider's own tier server-side, and an empty map is sent as `{}` (review round 93).
- **Am I affected?** `providerOptions["gateway"]["serviceTier"]` combined with `providerOptions["openai"]["serviceTier"]` (or another provider's tier), or `ProviderOptions: map[string]interface{}{}` on gateway models.
- **What to do:** No action unless you inspect the request body. The served tier is unchanged.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Gateway decision answers and warnings keep only their own fields {#gateway-response-schema-keys}

- **What changed:** Gateway decision answers and the warnings of Gateway models kept every key of the wire object. v0.6 keeps only the fields of the answer's or warning's own type, as the TS discriminated-union schemas do: unsupported / compatibility warnings keep `feature` and `details`, deprecated warnings `setting` and `message`, other warnings `message`. A decision response `model: ""` is kept (TS `??`), and a non-object `providerOptions.gateway` on a decision call fails with "invalid gateway provider options" (review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads Gateway decision answers or `Warnings` of Gateway models for keys of another answer or warning type, or that passes a non-object `providerOptions["gateway"]` to a Gateway decision model.
- **What to do:** No action unless you read those keys.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Google and Vertex error texts for malformed responses follow TS {#provg-r94-error-texts}

- **What changed:** A Google Files upload response without a `file` object returned an empty result; v0.6 fails with the V8 TypeError text TS throws reading `uploadResult.file.state`. The Google and Vertex video "No videos in response" error text is now TS `JSON.stringify` of the zod-parsed operation (unknown keys dropped, schema key order, present nulls and empty strings kept). A Google Interactions file URI that does not parse fails with "Invalid URL", and a parsed one is reported in its WHATWG form (`new URL(uri)`) (review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches these error texts or reads Interactions file URLs byte for byte.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match them.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Google Interactions sends empty string options {#google-interactions-empty-options}

- **What changed:** A present empty `systemInstruction` or `previousInteractionId` Interactions option was left out of the request. v0.6 sends it (TS `?? undefined` keeps `""`), and it drives the conflict warning and compaction. An empty `mediaResolution`, `serviceTier`, `thinkingLevel` or `thinkingSummaries` option now fails like `z.enum` (review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** `providerOptions["google"]` with `systemInstruction: ""`, `previousInteractionId: ""` or an empty enum option on an Interactions model.
- **What to do:** Leave out options you do not want sent.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Realtime and live WebSocket URLs follow the WHATWG URL parser {#websocket-urls-whatwg}

- **What changed:** The WebSocket URLs of the OpenAI, Azure and xAI realtime transcription streams, OpenAI speech translation, ElevenLabs speech and transcription streams, Cartesia, Deepgram and Mistral streaming transcription, the Google realtime, live transcription and speech translation sockets, the Gateway realtime, speech and transcription sockets and Vertex Gemini live transcription were built with `net/url` and a scheme swap. v0.6 builds them like TS `toWebSocketUrl` / `new URL`: the host is lower-cased, a default port is left out, dot segments are resolved, the query uses the WHATWG special-query encoding (`'` is `%27`), and a base URL that does not parse fails with "Invalid URL" before any connection (review round 94).
- **Am I affected?** Only with a custom `BaseURL` that has upper-case host letters, an explicit default port, dot segments or characters the WHATWG parser encodes, or that does not parse.
- **What to do:** No action unless your proxy matches the URL byte for byte.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Open Responses maps the finish reason like TS {#openresponses-finish-reason-ts}

- **What changed:** The Open Responses language model (`pkg/providers/openresponses`) mapped the finish reason with the v0.5 helper. v0.6 maps it like TS `mapOpenResponsesFinishReason`, in generate and stream: `max_output_tokens` is `length` and `content_filter` is `content-filter` even when the response has tool calls (v0.5 said `tool-calls`), any other reported reason (including `""`, `max_tokens`, `stop` and `error`) is `other`, or `tool-calls` when there are tool calls, and only a missing reason is `stop` (review round 95).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `FinishReason` from an Open Responses model, mainly for a response with tool calls cut off by the token limit or the content filter.
- **What to do:** Treat `length` and `content-filter` as possible finish reasons when there are tool calls. The exported `openresponses.MapOpenResponsesFinishReason` keeps the v0.5 mapping.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenAI, xAI and Open Responses keep present empty strings {#provb-empty-string-values}

- **What changed:** Several present `""` values were treated as absent. v0.6 keeps them, as TS `??` / `!= null` does: the OpenAI Responses `conversation`, `previousResponseId`, `user`, `instructions`, `promptCacheKey` and `safetyIdentifier` options are sent as `""`; an Open Responses system message of `""` sends `instructions: ""`; an empty incomplete reason, reasoning `encrypted_content`, `call_id`, `approval_request_id`, item `id` and tool `namespace` are kept in results and provider metadata; an xAI `mcp_call` name `""` and an empty `x-trace-id` header are kept (review round 95).
- **Am I affected?** Code that sets those options to `""`, or reads the listed fields expecting a fallback for `""`.
- **What to do:** Leave out options you do not want sent.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### OpenAI, xAI and DeepSeek validate more responses and options {#provb-r95-validation}

- **What changed:** v0.6 applies more of the TS schemas (review round 95). A DeepSeek Responses generate body is validated against the TS response schema (a usage count that is not a number, or an output element that is not `{type: string}`, is "Invalid JSON response" instead of zero usage or a skipped item). An OpenAI batch result body is validated against the Responses schema (a bad body gives the item an `invalid_response` error). xAI video options reject a present `null` for `mode`, `videoUrl`, `referenceVoiceIds`, `keyframes`, `storageOptions` and `user`, and `storageOptions` takes `publicUrl` as a boolean or an object with an integer `expiresAfter`. An xAI video `202` status body gives `pending`. The Files APIs keep a fractional `bytes` in `providerMetadata.bytes`. An OpenAI stream error `code` that is neither a string nor a number is dropped.
- **Am I affected?** Code that sends `null` xAI video options, or relies on the lenient handling of malformed DeepSeek or OpenAI batch bodies.
- **What to do:** Leave out options you do not set.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Google Interactions built-in tool calls carry no provider metadata {#interactions-builtin-tool-call-metadata}

- **What changed:** Built-in tool calls (Google Search, code execution, URL context and the other server-side tools) of a Google Interactions model carried `providerMetadata.google.interactionId`. v0.6 gives them no provider metadata, in generate and in the synthesized stream, as TS does (review round 95).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `ProviderMetadata["google"]["interactionId"]` from an Interactions built-in tool call.
- **What to do:** Read the interaction ID from the response's provider metadata instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Google Interactions options follow the TS option schema {#google-interactions-option-schema}

- **What changed:** v0.6 checks the Interactions options like TS googleInteractionsLanguageModelOptions (review round 95): the `imageConfig` and deep-research `agentConfig` enums reject `""` and non-strings, `agentConfig` is the dynamic (loose, extra fields allowed) or deep-research union (an empty `type` fails), `responseModalities` items must be strings and environment allowlist `transform` entries must be records of strings. An interaction status or error code of `""` in the stream is a value, not absent, and a background stream is cancelled on the server only when the caller aborts it.
- **Am I affected?** `providerOptions["google"]` on Interactions models with empty or mistyped values in those options.
- **What to do:** Leave out options you do not set, or use the documented values.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Streams that fail to open their WebSocket report the TS socket error {#websocket-open-failure-ts-text}

- **What changed:** When the WebSocket of a streaming model failed to open (a refused handshake such as a 401, an unreachable host), the stream failed with the raw `websocket.Dial` error. v0.6 fails with the provider's TS socket error text, as TS connectToWebSocket's `onerror` does: "ElevenLabs speech stream error.", "ElevenLabs realtime transcription error.", "OpenAI realtime transcription error", "OpenAI realtime translation error", "Azure MAI transcription WebSocket error", "xAI streaming transcription error.", "Cartesia streaming transcription error", "Google Live transcription error", "Google Live translation error", "Vertex Live transcription error.", and the Gateway "Connection error on AI Gateway ... stream" through its error mapping. The dial error stays reachable through `errors.Unwrap` where the provider wraps it, and a stream cancelled before or during the handshake fails with the cancel cause (review round 95).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches the dial error text of these streams.
- **What to do:** Match the TS text, or use `errors.As` on the dial error where it is wrapped.
- **Kind:** error text only

### The Gateway speech stream fails on audio it cannot decode {#gateway-speech-undecodable-audio}

- **What changed:** An audio part whose base64 the Gateway speech stream could not decode was skipped, and the stream then ended with "closed before a finish part". v0.6 fails the stream at that part with `*speechstream.InvalidCharacterError` (`Invalid character`, or `The string to be decoded is not correctly encoded.`, as Node's `atob`), after the earlier parts; the error is not wrapped as a Gateway error, as TS streamSpeech does (review round 95).
- **Am I affected?** Code that streams Gateway speech and matches the "closed before a finish part" error.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As(err, &invalid)` with `var invalid *speechstream.InvalidCharacterError`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Anthropic Skills uploads reject stream file data {#anthropic-skills-stream-upload}

- **What changed:** `SkillsAPI.UploadSkill` with a file of stream data (`FileDataTypeStream`) returned a plain error and left the stream open. v0.6 cancels the stream and returns `*providererrors.UnsupportedFunctionalityError` for "streaming file upload", as TS uploadSkill (convertInlineFileDataToUint8Array) does (review round 95).
- **Am I affected?** Code that uploads skills with stream file data and matches the old error.
- **What to do:** Pass the file bytes, and use `errors.As` with `*providererrors.UnsupportedFunctionalityError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Vertex errors follow the Vertex error schema {#vertex-error-schema}

- **What changed:** Vertex transcription (Chirp and Gemini), Cloud TTS, embedding and video read the error body with the Google schema: `details` had to be an array and was kept in `ProviderError.Data`. v0.6 uses TS googleVertexErrorDataSchema: `Data` holds code, message and status only, and `error.message` is used whatever `details` holds (review round 93).
- **Am I affected?** `ProviderError.Data` of those models.
- **What to do:** Read details from `ResponseBody`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Deepgram transcription without a channel or alternative fails {#deepgram-empty-channels-error}

- **What changed:** A Deepgram transcription response whose `results` holds no channel (`channels: []`), or whose first channel holds no alternative, returned empty text and segments. v0.6 fails the call with the TypeError text TS throws reading `channels[0].alternatives[0].words` (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'alternatives')` or `(reading 'words')`). A null or absent `results` still gives empty text (review round 93).
- **Am I affected?** `deepgram` transcription callers that expect a result for audio Deepgram returns no channel for.
- **What to do:** Handle the error.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### stream-start comes before a failed setup message {#stream-start-before-setup-failure}

- **What changed:** When the first message a streaming model sends after its WebSocket opens failed (the ElevenLabs speech init message, the OpenAI realtime transcription and speech-translation session update, the Google and Vertex live transcription setup and the Google speech-translation setup), the stream ended with the error and no `stream-start` part. v0.6 emits `stream-start` (with the warnings) first and then the error, as TS `onOpen` enqueues `stream-start` before it sends (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** Code that treats the first part of these streams as either `stream-start` or the failure.
- **What to do:** Expect `stream-start` before the error.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenAI and xAI round 96 details {#provb-r96-details}

- **What changed:** OpenAI Responses `reasoningEffort` and `reasoningSummary` set to `""` are sent (TS `z.string()` and `!= null`: the summary defaults to `detailed`, and non-reasoning models warn). Fractional `maxToolCalls` and `logprobs` (Responses), `maxCompletionTokens` and `logprobs` (chat) and Alibaba `thinkingBudget` are sent as given instead of truncated (TS `z.number()`). OpenAI Files `expiresAfter` of ±Infinity is rejected. A done Responses `function_call` item (OpenAI and xAI) uses its own `call_id`, `name`, `arguments` and `id`, also when they are `""`; an OpenAI Responses stream keeps a present `""` response id and added item id. xAI Responses `incomplete_details` must be an object with a string `reason` (TS schema). The Open Responses stream and the raw chunk of every OpenAI-compatible stream treat a frame with a `__proto__` key as a JSONParseError part with a nil raw value (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** Code that sets those options to `""` or to fractions, or reads tool-call ids from OpenAI or xAI Responses streams whose done item differs from the added item.
- **What to do:** Leave out options you do not set, and pass whole numbers where you want them.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Media provider round 96 details {#provc-r96-details}

- **What changed:** Deepgram and AssemblyAI transcription upload `AudioBase64` as given when `Audio` is empty (TS `postToApi({content: audio})` sends the base64 string); v0.5 uploaded an empty body. The Deepgram live transcription handshake trims HTTP whitespace around the `Authorization` header, like `new Headers().get()`. Deepgram transcription ignores the undeclared `confidence`, `transaction_key` and `request_id` response fields, so a value of another type there no longer fails the call. Cartesia realtime server events are read from the parsed object: a field of another JSON type keeps the event's type (string fields take the JS string of a scalar) instead of turning it into a `custom` event. Cartesia `DoCreateClientSecret` reports a non-JSON success body with the JSON parse error and an error body as UTF-8 text. The TypeSafe score `legend` is checked like the TS schema. A non-finite error `code` / `status` (Gladia, Rev.ai, Hume, KlingAI, Fish Audio, Baseten, AssemblyAI) does not match the error schema, so the message is the status text (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** Direct provider calls that set only `AudioBase64`, and code that matches the listed error texts or Cartesia realtime event types.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## MCP {#mcp}

### MCP OAuth rejects unbound pre-registered confidential credentials {#mcp-oauth-issuer}

- **What changed:** client information with a client secret (or a custom
  authenticator) but no `Issuer` and no stored authorization server
  information fails `mcp.Auth` with an authorization-server mismatch error
  (TS #23579). v0.5 skipped the check. Dynamically registered clients keep
  working.
- **Am I affected?** Pre-registered confidential OAuth clients without
  `Issuer`.
- **What to do:** set `Issuer` to the authorization server the secret was
  registered with, or return it from
  `OAuthAuthorizationServerInformationStore`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP clients have no default request timeout {#mcp-no-default-timeout}

- **What changed:** `MCPClientConfig.RequestTimeoutMS` and the transports'
  `TimeoutMS` default to no timeout, as TS fetch; the caller's ctx governs.
  v0.5 used 30 seconds. JSON-RPC request IDs start at 0,
  `MCPClientError.Error()` is the bare message, and the HTTP and stdio
  transports probe `server/discover` first.
- **Am I affected?** MCP clients that relied on the 30 s default.
- **What to do:** set the timeout, or bound calls with a context deadline:

  ```go
  cfg := mcp.MCPClientConfig{RequestTimeoutMS: 30000} // v0.5 default
  _ = cfg
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### An explicit MCP input schema is the tool's Parameters {#mcp-explicit-input-schema}

- **What changed:** v0.5 copied an explicit `MCPToolSchema.InputSchema` given
  as a `schema.Schema` into a JSON Schema map: sorted keys, JSON Schema
  validation only, and a panic for a typed nil or zero schema value. v0.6
  keeps the schema (or bare `schema.Validator`) you passed as the tool's
  `Parameters`, as TS uses `schemas[name].inputSchema` directly
  (`mcp-client.ts` 1352): its key order reaches the provider and its own
  validator checks tool input (for a struct schema, its `validate:` tags). A
  typed nil or zero schema value is no input schema. Maps and other values
  still become a JSON Schema map (review round 83).
- **Am I affected?** `ConvertToGoAIToolsWithSchemas` or
  `ToolsFromDefinitions` with `MCPToolSchema{InputSchema: <schema.Schema>}`,
  and code reading `tool.Parameters.(map[string]interface{})` on those tools.
- **What to do:** No action unless you type-assert `Parameters` to a map;
  use `schema.ParametersMap(tool.Parameters)` instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP: multi-round-trip requests {#mcp-input-required}

- **What changed:** A modern-protocol `CallTool`, `GetPrompt` or `ReadResource` that returns `resultType: input_required` used to fail with "Server requested additional input, but multi round-trip requests are not supported yet"; it now asks `OnElicitationRequest` (form elicitation) and retries, up to 10 rounds (TS `c3af430640`). Outside that loop the error text is `Input-required results are not supported for <method> on protocol <version>`. A -32022 discovery error naming a supported legacy version now has a guidance message (TS `ade2a33f55`).
- **Am I affected?** MCP clients of 2026-07-28 servers; code matching the old error texts.
- **What to do:** Register `OnElicitationRequest` to answer input requests.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP OAuth token bodies and event callback queries follow URLSearchParams {#mcp-oauth-form-order}

- **What changed:** MCP OAuth token requests send the form body in TS URLSearchParams order with `~` as `%7E` and `*` literal (v0.5 sorted the keys with `url.Values`), and the event callback URL query is re-serialized like `searchParams.set` (the key replaced in place) (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Servers or tests comparing raw token bodies or `MCPEventSubscription.Delivery.URL` strings.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### MCP HTTP transport reports send failures to OnError as often as TS {#mcp-http-onerror-count}

- **What changed:** `HTTPTransport` reports a failed send to `OnError` as often as TS `send()`: twice for a non-ok response, an unexpected or bodiless content type, or a failed authorization after a 401, once for other failures, and once more when the failure happens in the retry after a 401. A caller-cancelled context still suppresses the catch's report. v0.5 reported each failure once (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** `HTTPTransportConfig.OnError` callbacks that count calls or alert per call.
- **What to do:** No action unless you count `OnError` calls; deduplicate by error value if needed.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP HTTP transport starts once {#mcp-http-connect-once}

- **What changed:** `HTTPTransport.Connect` after a successful `Connect` and `Close` fails with `MCP HTTP Transport Error: Transport already started. Note: client.connect() calls start() automatically.`, as TS `start()`; v0.5 started a new lifecycle (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reuses an `*mcp.HTTPTransport` after `Close`.
- **What to do:** Create a new transport (`mcp.NewHTTPTransport`) for each connection.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP SSE transport stays connected after a stream read error {#mcp-sse-read-error-connected}

- **What changed:** After the endpoint event, a read error on the SSE stream is reported to `OnError` and the transport stays connected (`Send` still POSTs, `Receive` waits), as TS. v0.5 marked it disconnected. A clean end of stream still disconnects (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** `SSETransport` callers relying on `Receive` returning after a network failure, or on `IsConnected()` turning false.
- **What to do:** Watch `OnError` and bound requests with a timeout or context.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP stdio transport disconnects when the server exits {#mcp-stdio-server-exit}

- **What changed:** After the stdio server exits on its own, `Send` fails with `*MCPClientError` `StdioClientTransport not connected` (TS) instead of a `*TransportError` for the closed pipe, `IsConnected()` turns false, and `Connect` starts the server again (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching `*TransportError` / "broken pipe" after a server crash.
- **What to do:** Match `*mcp.MCPClientError` (or check `!transport.IsConnected()`).
- **Kind:** error text only

### MCP OAuth always sends the resource parameter {#mcp-oauth-resource-always}

- **What changed:** Without protected-resource metadata, or when `ValidateResourceURL` returned "", v0.5 sent no `resource`, so the authorization server could apply a default audience. v0.6 sends the canonical server URL (fragment removed) on authorize, code exchange and refresh, and `SelectOAuthResourceURL` never returns nil (TS ae13fcea03, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `mcp.Auth` flows against authorization servers that reject `resource`; custom `ValidateResourceURL` implementations that return "" to omit it.
- **What to do:** Return the resource you want from `ValidateResourceURL`; "" no longer omits it.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### MCP OAuth clears refresh tokens without an authorization server binding {#mcp-oauth-unbound-refresh-token}

- **What changed:** A stored refresh token with no authorization server binding could inherit the binding of a client registered in the same call. v0.6 resolves the binding before registration: an unbound token is invalidated, and if it is still stored `Auth` fails with `MCPClientOAuthError` "Stored OAuth tokens without an authorization server binding must be cleared before starting authorization" (TS dc0f7f243d, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** OAuth client providers whose `Tokens` carry a refresh token without `Issuer`, `AuthorizationServer` or `TokenEndpoint`, and that implement neither `OAuthCredentialInvalidator` nor `OAuthTokenInvalidator`.
- **What to do:** Implement invalidation, or store tokens with their authorization server binding.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP OAuth discovery rejects HTTPS to HTTP redirects {#mcp-oauth-https-downgrade}

- **What changed:** OAuth metadata discovery followed a redirect from https to http. v0.6 fails with a `DownloadError` "Redirect from HTTPS to HTTP was blocked" before the http target is requested (TS f608c056a2, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** OAuth metadata URLs that redirect to `http://`.
- **What to do:** No action unless your metadata redirects to http.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP responses match requests by JS Number(id) {#mcp-response-id-number}

- **What changed:** A response whose id is a numeric string (or boolean) now answers the matching request, as TS `onResponse` uses `Number(id)`; v0.5 reported an unknown id and the call waited until its timeout (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** MCP servers that echo ids with a different JSON type.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### MCP elicitation params follow the TS schema {#mcp-elicitation-schema}

- **What changed:** `elicitation/create` accepted params without `requestedSchema` and a null `_meta`, and dropped unknown keys. v0.6 requires `requestedSchema` (any value) and an object `_meta`, keeps unknown keys in the new `ElicitationRequest.Extra` / `ElicitResult.Extra`, and reports the zod messages (TS types.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** MCP elicitation handlers and servers sending loose params.
- **What to do:** No action unless your server omits `requestedSchema`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP send errors are unwrapped {#mcp-send-errors}

- **What changed:** MCP send failures were wrapped in a `*TransportError` with a double "transport error" prefix, and a mid-flight abort was not `Request was aborted`. v0.6 returns the abort reason (before send) or `MCPClientError` "Request was aborted", and transport `MCPClientError` / `UnauthorizedError` unwrapped (TS mcp-client.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching MCP error texts or `*TransportError`.
- **What to do:** Use `errors.As` with `*MCPClientError`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### MCP does not fall back to initialize after a discover network error {#mcp-discover-network-error}

- **What changed:** MCP over HTTP fell back to `initialize` after any `server/discover` failure. v0.6 falls back only for an `MCPClientError`; a network error fails the connection (TS 6141e6fefb) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** MCP HTTP servers that are unreachable during discovery.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP Apps: a non-array tool visibility follows TS {#mcp-app-visibility-raw}

- **What changed:** `SplitMCPAppTools` treated any non-array `_meta.ui.visibility` as unset (model-visible only). v0.6, as TS, matches a string value with substring search (`"app"` puts the tool in the app-visible list only, `"model,app"` in both), and any other non-null value (a number, boolean or object) makes `SplitMCPAppTools` return the error `visibility.includes is not a function`. TS keeps the raw value through its `{...uiMeta}` spread and calls `.includes` on it (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** Only if you call `mcp.SplitMCPAppTools` on servers whose tool `_meta.ui.visibility` is not an array (the MCP Apps spec defines an array).
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on the old fallback; an array `visibility` behaves as before.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### MCP and sandbox round 96 details {#tools-r96-details}

- **What changed:** A JSON-RPC error `code` that is a quoted number (`"-32000"`) fails the message, and a terminated event's `error.code` accepts any integer-valued number (`1.0`, `-3.2e4`) and rejects strings and integers beyond 2^53 (zod `z.number().int()`). Loose capability and server info extras keep the server's member text, so a member Go cannot re-encode is no longer dropped (`Extra()` decodes member by member). An unknown tool-result content part reaches the model as `JSON.stringify` of the parsed part (integer-like keys first, JS number text). An SSE `endpoint` event with a scheme-relative URL (`//host/path`) resolves against the server URL like `new URL` instead of failing. A tool result without `content` whose `structuredContent` holds a non-finite number fails like TS `z.json()`. Vercel Sandbox `ListDirectory` takes a record without a space minus its last UTF-16 unit as the type, as `record.slice(0, -1)` (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** MCP servers that send these shapes, and code that reads loose extras or `ListDirectory` types of unusual records.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Code mode {#code-mode}

### Code mode has a default 10 s CPU limit {#codemode-cpu-limit}

- **What changed:** `ExecutionPolicy.CPUTimeoutMs` (default 10_000, TS
  #23812) stops guest JavaScript that runs longer than that in one invocation
  with `*codemode.CPUTimeoutError`. Time in host tools does not count. v0.5
  allowed pure JS to run up to the 30 s `TimeoutMs`.
- **Am I affected?** Long CPU-bound scripts.
- **What to do:** raise `CPUTimeoutMs` (and `TimeoutMs`) for long-running
  scripts.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode caps concurrent scripts {#codemode-max-workers}

- **What changed:** like TS, code mode runs at most floor(available memory /
  (`ExecutionPolicy.MemoryLimitBytes` + 48 MiB)) sandbox invocations at once,
  at least 1 and at most 32. v0.5 had no limit. A script started over the
  limit fails right away with `*codemode.ConcurrencyError` ("Code mode
  maxWorkers limit reached (N).").
- **Am I affected?** Servers that run many code mode scripts at once.
- **What to do:** raise the limit, or pass 0 to keep v0.5's unlimited
  behavior. `codemode.ResetMaxWorkers()` restores the default:

  ```go
  if err := codemode.SetMaxWorkers(64); err != nil {
  	log.Fatal(err)
  }
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode validates a bare schema.Validator Parameters {#codemode-bare-validator-validated}

- **What changed:** v0.5 code mode validated a tool's input only when
  `Parameters` was a JSON-schema map or a `schema.Schema`; a bare
  `schema.Validator` (for example `schema.NewJSONSchema(...)`) was skipped,
  and a nil `*schema.SimpleStructSchema` panicked. v0.6 validates with every
  schema, as TS code mode validates with `asSchema(tool.inputSchema)`, and a
  nil schema pointer is no schema. Input the validator rejects fails the
  tool call with `Invalid input for tool "<name>": ...` (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Code-mode tools with `Parameters:
  schema.NewJSONSchema(...)` or another bare `schema.Validator`.
- **What to do:** no action unless your scripts pass input that the
  validator rejects.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### wazero is v1.12.0 {#wazero-version}

- **What changed:** wazero moved from v1.9.0 to v1.12.0. Code mode's sandbox
  behaves the same: each invocation still gets its own QuickJS instance,
  memory and host bindings. QuickJS is now compiled once per process into one
