  shared wazero runtime, so sandbox startup is faster.
- **Am I affected?** Modules that pin `github.com/tetratelabs/wazero`
  themselves.
- **What to do:** move your pin to v1.12.0.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode rejects dynamic import() across JavaScript whitespace {#codemode-dynamic-import-js-whitespace}

- **What changed:** The guard against `import(` inside a regex-like token matched only ASCII whitespace and single-line block comments; it now also matches the JavaScript whitespace the engine accepts between `import` and `(` (U+00A0, U+FEFF, the Unicode spaces, line terminators) and multi-line block comments (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Scripts with such text in a regular-expression literal.
- **What to do:** No action; rewrite the regex if a script is rejected.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Detached bridge request errors name the tool {#codemode-detached-tool-name}

- **What changed:** `*codemode.DetachedBridgeRequestError` read "JavaScript runtime returned while N bridge request(s) were still in flight."; v0.6 uses `run`'s unawaited-request text with the tool's property access, as TS 3fab50aa9b: "JavaScript runtime created an unawaited hostFunction bridge request: tools.createSlide." (`tools["update-slide"]` for a name that is not an identifier). The details are run's record; see [Code mode host calls follow `run`](#codemode-host-call-bridge).
- **Am I affected?** `DetachedBridgeRequestError.Error()` text matches.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the message; match the type with `errors.As`.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Code mode host calls follow `run`: lazy, concurrent, detached calls fail {#codemode-host-call-bridge}

- **What changed:** v0.5 ran each `tools.x(input)` call synchronously, in call order, as soon as the expression ran. A call the script never awaited still ran, and only an unawaited approval call in interrupt mode failed. v0.6 follows `run@2.1.8`, as TS code mode 3fab50aa9b does:
  - A call starts only once the script observes it (`await`, `.then`, `Promise.all`).
  - Calls run concurrently on their own goroutines, and their outcomes reach the script in settlement order.
  - A script that returns while a call was never observed, or is still pending, fails with `*codemode.DetachedBridgeRequestError`: "JavaScript runtime created an unawaited hostFunction bridge request: tools.x." or "JavaScript runtime returned while a hostFunction bridge request was still pending: tools.x.". Its `Details` are run's record `{id, kind, name, status}`, where `name` is `__codeMode.tool<N>`, or `__codeMode.missing` for an unknown tool (which also stays in the message). A result returned while host calls are still in flight fails with run's host-side text "JavaScript runtime returned while N bridge request(s) were still in flight." (details `inFlightBridgeRequests`, `totalBridgeRequests`).
  - `MaxInFlightBridgeRequests` (default 32) counts the calls running at once, so a `Promise.all` over more than 32 tool calls fails with `*BridgeLimitError`.
  - Request numbers (the `<outer>:tool-N` tool call IDs, the interrupt IDs and the approval batch order) follow the order calls start: `Promise.all([tools.a(), Promise.all([tools.b(), tools.c()])])` numbers b, c, a. An input that fails to serialize counts as a request.
  - The tool's `ctx` is cancelled when the invocation ends (TS aborts run's invocation signal).
  - A resumed run replays a failed call's rejection instead of running the tool again (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** `codemode.RunCodeMode` and the code mode tool with fire-and-forget `tools.x(...)` calls; `DetachedBridgeRequestError` `Details` keys `inFlightBridgeRequests` / `totalBridgeRequests` / `toolName` / `toolCallId`; `MaxInFlightBridgeRequests`; tools or `OnApprovalRequired` callbacks that are not safe to call concurrently; code that parses `:tool-N`; tools that use `ctx` after `Execute` returns.
- **What to do:** Await every tool call, make tools safe for concurrent calls, and raise the in-flight limit for large fan-outs:
  ```go
  _, err := codemode.RunCodeMode(ctx, codemode.RunInput{
  	JS:    "await tools.log({ msg: 'hi' }); return 1;", // was: "tools.log({ msg: 'hi' }); return 1;"
  	Tools: tools,
  	Options: &codemode.Options{
  		ExecutionPolicy: &codemode.ExecutionPolicy{MaxInFlightBridgeRequests: 64},
  	},
  })
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode checks resumed runs against the ledger {#codemode-replay-checks}

- **What changed:** v0.5 gave a resumed run the recorded result for whatever call came at a recorded index, and ran the approved call with whatever tool and input the replay computed. v0.6 fails with `*codemode.ProtocolError`, as TS run does: "Continuation replay diverged from the recorded host function ledger." (details `bridgeIndex`, `expectedHostFunctionName`, `receivedHostFunctionName`) when a resumed call has another tool or input than recorded, the approved call included; "Duplicate bridge settlement order."; and "Continuation completed before replaying the full host function ledger." (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** `ContinueCodeModeApproval`, `ContinueCodeModeInterrupt`, `RunInput.Continuation`, and scripts whose calls before an interruption depend on something outside the sandbox.
- **What to do:** No action unless you resume continuations of scripts that are not deterministic. Keep the script's inputs to the sandbox the same between the run and its resume.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode Date and Math.random are deterministic {#codemode-deterministic-clock}

- **What changed:** v0.5 used the real clock and QuickJS's random generator. v0.6 installs run's deterministic APIs: `Date.now()`, `Date()` and `new Date()` without arguments read a clock that starts at the run's start time, advances 1 ms per read and moves to the host time when a tool call settles; `Math.random()` is xorshift128+ seeded per run. The continuation token (opaque) now records the seed, the start time and each call's settlement time, so a resumed run computes the same values; tokens from v0.5 still resume (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** Scripts that time work with `Date.now()` inside the sandbox, or that need `Math.random()` to differ across a resume; code that reads `Continuation.Token`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you measure elapsed time inside a code mode script; time the `RunCodeMode` call in Go instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode freezes the JavaScript intrinsics {#codemode-frozen-intrinsics}

- **What changed:** v0.5 let a script change the built-in objects (`Array.prototype`, `Object.prototype`, `Promise.prototype`, `Math`, `JSON`, `Date`, the typed arrays) and reassign the intrinsic globals (`Array`, `Object`, `JSON`, `Promise`, ...). v0.6 freezes them and locks those globals before the script runs, as `run@2.1.8`'s HARDENING_SOURCE does in TS. An assignment to them is ignored in sloppy code and throws a `TypeError` in strict code; `Object.defineProperty` on them throws. Without the freeze, a script could hide its detached tool calls from the bridge's checks (review round 87c, integrator).
- **Am I affected?** Code mode scripts that polyfill or patch built-ins.
- **What to do:** No action unless a script patches built-ins; use local helper functions instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode tools names inherited from Object.prototype call those functions {#codemode-prototype-tool-names}

- **What changed:** `tools.toString()`, `tools.hasOwnProperty(...)` and the other `Object.prototype` functions that are not registered tools call that function (with `this` undefined) instead of a tool call, as TS `run-code-mode.ts` reads `tools` from an object literal: `await tools.toString()` is `"[object Undefined]"`. A registered tool of the same name is still the tool (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Scripts that call a missing tool named like an `Object.prototype` function.
- **What to do:** No action unless a script relies on that name reaching the missing-tool path.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A code mode script awaiting a promise nothing settles times out {#codemode-never-settling-timeout}

- **What changed:** A script that awaits a promise no host call settles (`await new Promise(() => {})`) now fails with the execution timeout error after the timeout, like TS run's manager (`RunTimeoutError`); v0.5 returned a `ProtocolError` at once (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches `*codemode.ProtocolError` for such scripts or relies on the immediate failure.
- **What to do:** Match `*codemode.TimeoutError`, or set a shorter `ExecutionPolicy.TimeoutMs`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode tool arguments devalue cannot serialize fail at the call {#codemode-unserializable-arguments}

- **What changed:** A function, symbol, thenable, class instance or symbol-keyed object passed to a tool now throws a `TypeError` `Host function arguments are not serializable: <devalue message>` (code `RUN_SERIALIZATION_ERROR`) at the call, before any request, as TS run's host function proxy. v0.5 dropped a function or symbol and called the tool (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Scripts that pass such values to tools.
- **What to do:** Pass plain JSON values.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode honors approval funcs and statuses and fails closed {#codemode-approval-fail-closed}

- **What changed:** Code mode ran a host tool without approval when `ToolApproval` was an unnamed func literal, a `ToolApprovalStatus` / string (even "denied") or another type. v0.6 reads them as `pkg/ai` does: user-approval asks, a denied status fails with `*codemode.ToolApprovalDeniedError`, approved / not-applicable / "" run, anything else is a `*codemode.ToolError` (TS tool-invocation.ts requiresApproval) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `codemode` tools with `ToolApproval: func(...)` literals, `types.ToolApprovalStatus*` values or strings.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on those tools running unapproved.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode honors Tool.ValidateInput {#codemode-validate-input}

- **What changed:** Code mode ignored `Tool.ValidateInput`. v0.6 runs it in place of the schema check; an error is `Invalid input for tool "<name>": <msg>` and a returned object is the input approval and `Execute` see (TS validateToolInput) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Code mode tools with `ValidateInput`.
- **What to do:** No action unless your validator rejects input you expected to reach `Execute`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### GetCodeModeInterrupt reads Go generate results {#codemode-interrupt-go-results}

- **What changed:** `GetCodeModeInterrupt` / `UnwrapCodeModeResult` read only a JSON-shaped `toolResults[i].output` map, so they never found the interrupt in a Go result. v0.6 also reads result structs (`ToolResults`, `Content`, value or pointer), typed slices in maps, the `result` key and `ToolResultContent.Output` (TS interrupt-continuation.ts) (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Callers of `GetCodeModeInterrupt` / `UnwrapCodeModeResult` on `GenerateText` results.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Code mode script errors keep their own text {#codemode-error-text}

- **What changed:** A script error whose text contained "context canceled" was reported as a timeout or abort, and a function or symbol result failed with `invalid JSON payload`. v0.6 keeps the script's own error and fails such a result with the guest `SyntaxError`, as TS run-code-mode.ts (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** Matching code mode error texts.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match those texts.
- **Kind:** error text only

## Harness, ACP and fx {#harness-acp-and-fx}

### ACP lifecycle state persisted by v0.5.x does not resume {#acp-state}

- **What changed:** as in TS (#23761), the persisted identity is
  `agentIdentity`, and the identity, configuration fingerprint and
  authentication digest are computed the TS way (including the client app
  version, so TS also rejects state after a package upgrade). Old state fails
  with "agentIdentity must be a string" or "ACP lifecycle state is
  incompatible with the configured ...".
- **Am I affected?** Saved ACP lifecycle state for the Cursor, GitHub Copilot,
  Grok Build and fx adapters.
- **What to do:** discard saved ACP lifecycle state and start fresh sessions
  after upgrading.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A failed harness stop-condition suspension fails the turn {#harness-stop-suspend}

- **What changed:** when a `StopWhen` condition is met inside an `Agent` or
  `AgentSession` and the adapter's `DoSuspendTurn` returns an error
  (including `CapabilityUnsupportedError` from an adapter that cannot
  suspend), the turn fails with that error, as in TS. v0.5 finished the turn
  instead, so the error went unseen.
- **Am I affected?** `StopWhen` with an adapter that cannot suspend.
- **What to do:** handle the error from the turn, or leave out the stop
  condition.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness adapters identify as ai-sdk-harness-id {#harness-client-app}

- **What changed:** harness adapters send `ai-sdk-harness-<id>/<version>` in
  `User-Agent` and `x-client-app`. The exported constants changed with them,
  for example `cursor.ClientAppName` is `ai-sdk-harness-cursor` (was
  `ai-sdk/harness-cursor`) and `cursor.ClientAppVersion` is a release
  version instead of `0.0.0-go`; the same holds for `fx`, `githubcopilot` and
  `grokbuild`.
- **Am I affected?** Server-side checks or tests that match the old values.
- **What to do:** match the new values, or read the constants.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### With AgentSettings.Telemetry unset, the harness agent fires no telemetry {#harness-telemetry-unset}

- **What changed:** a harness agent without `AgentSettings.Telemetry` fires
  no telemetry, even to globally registered integrations.
- **Am I affected?** Harness agents that relied on global integrations.
- **What to do:** set `AgentSettings.Telemetry`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness usage is the turn total, and a failed OAuth refresh fails start {#harness-usage-oauth}

- **What changed:** `harness.Agent.Generate(...).Usage` is the turn total. For
  Claude Code and Codex, a failed OAuth refresh fails start instead of being
  treated as "no credential".
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `Usage` as the last step's, or that
  expects start to fall back when a refresh fails.
- **What to do:** read per-step usage from the steps; handle the start error.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness continuations accept pointer content parts {#harness-continuation-pointer-parts}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `harness.CollectToolApprovalContinuations` and
  `harness.CollectToolResultContinuations` ignored pointer content parts
  (`*types.ToolApprovalResponseContent`, `*types.ToolResultContent`,
  `*types.ToolCallContent`, `*types.ToolApprovalRequestContent`), so a
  trailing tool message built with pointers continued nothing. v0.6 accepts
  values and non-nil pointers, as `ai.CollectToolApprovals` and the core
  approval resume do (review round 81).
- **Am I affected?** Code that builds harness continuation messages with
  pointer parts, or relies on such a message not resuming a paused turn.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on pointer parts being
  ignored; remove those parts from the trailing tool message instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness tool specs keep the schema key order {#harness-tool-spec-schema-order}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `harness.ToolSpec.InputSchema` (in
  `TurnSettings.Tools`, sent to the adapter) was always the tool's JSON Schema
  as a `map[string]any` (sorted keys), and a bare `schema.Validator` gave nil.
  v0.6 sends a struct schema, an `*schema.OrderedSchema` or an MCP tool schema
  with `ParameterKeyOrder` as an `*schema.OrderedSchema` that marshals in its
  source key order, as TS `_toToolSpecs` sends `asSchema(inputSchema)
  .jsonSchema`; a bare validator contributes its `JSONSchema()` (review round
  83).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading `ToolSpec.InputSchema` /
  `TurnSettings.Tools[i].InputSchema` with a `.(map[string]any)` assertion,
  or custom `harness.Harness` adapters that do so.
- **What to do:** No action unless you type-assert `InputSchema` to a map;
  marshal it, or use `schema.ParametersMap(spec.InputSchema)` (an
  `*schema.OrderedSchema` is a `schema.Schema`).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### fx: the built-in terminal tool is removed {#fx-terminal-removed}

- **What changed:** as in TS, fx has no built-in `terminal` tool.
  `inactiveTools: ["terminal"]` now returns an error.
- **Am I affected?** `"terminal"` in fx `BuiltinToolFiltering` lists or tool
  name checks.
- **What to do:** remove it from the lists.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### fx has an opt-in libfx runtime {#fx-libfx-opt-in}

- **What changed:** `fx.CreateFx()` without a `Runtime` behaves as in v0.5.x:
  the sandbox `fx acp` adapter, the v0.5.x `fx.BuiltinTools`, and v0.5.x
  lifecycle state. `fx.Settings{Runtime: fx.RuntimeLibfx}` selects the TS
  `@ai-sdk/harness-fx` runtime, which has other built-in tools
  (`fx.LibfxBuiltinTools`: `read`, `write`, `edit`, `bash`, `grep`, `glob`,
  `ls`, `askUserQuestions`). Its model requests leave from the host process.
  It authenticates with AI Gateway or, as in TS, a codex or grok subscription
  found on the host (`~/.fx`, `$CODEX_HOME` or the OS keyring, `$GROK_HOME`),
  and connects HTTP and SSE `MCPServers` from the host; command (stdio) MCP
  servers are not supported on it. The first session downloads the pinned
  `fx-core.wasm` (about 2.6 MB) into `os.UserCacheDir()/go-ai/fx/`. On a host
  without network access, pre-seed that cache or set `fx.WasmSource.Path`,
  then set `GO_AI_FX_OFFLINE=1`.
- **Am I affected?** Only if you set `Runtime: fx.RuntimeLibfx`. Sessions do
  not move between runtimes: a v0.5.x sandbox-ACP session resumed under
  `RuntimeLibfx`, or a libfx session resumed under the default runtime,
  returns `*fx.IncompatibleStateError`. As in TS, libfx state files are
  accepted for libfx 0.0.13 and 0.0.14; any other version, or a file that
  fails the schema, fails with "Cannot resume fx: the saved state is invalid
  or incompatible." An open turn suspended by a TS host cannot be continued by
  a Go host, or the other way round.
- **What to do:** no action unless you opt in. If you do, update
  `BuiltinToolFiltering` lists and tool-name checks, and finish or drop open
  sessions before you switch.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### ACP host tools are resolved by the adapter {#acp-host-tool-resolver}

- **What changed:** The ACP bridge no longer guesses which tool calls are host tools; it asks the host, which calls `acp.Settings.ResolveHostToolCall` (TS `03dac28304`). `acp.CreateACP` (and so `cursor.CreateCursor`, `githubcopilot.CreateGitHubCopilot`, `grokbuild.CreateGrokBuild` and `fx.CreateFx` on the ACP runtime) now fails with `ACP isMcpToolCall must be a function.` or `ACP resolveHostToolCall must be a function.` when either callback is nil (ruling: match TS, which throws). The Cursor, GitHub Copilot, Grok Build and fx adapters set both.
- **Am I affected?** Only if you build `acp.Settings` yourself and call `acp.CreateACP` (grep `acp.Settings{`, `acp.CreateACP`).
- **What to do:** Set both callbacks:

  ```go
  cfg := acp.Settings{
  	// ...
  	IsMcpToolCall:       func(acp.ToolCall) bool { return false },
  	ResolveHostToolCall: acp.LegacyResolveHostToolCall, // the v0.5 bridge matching
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Recipe-less harnesses set up the sandbox on first use {#harness-deferred-sandbox-setup}

- **What changed:** v0.5 `Agent.CreateSession` always ran `SandboxConfig.OnBootstrap`, created the session work directory and ran `SandboxConfig.OnSession` before `DoStart`. In v0.6, for a harness that does not implement `harness.BootstrapProvider`, these run on the first file, exec or spawn operation on the session's sandbox (or its restricted view). They run once, a failure is returned by that operation and retried by the next, and a session that never touches the sandbox never runs them (TS `8a20b57d41`). Harnesses with a bootstrap recipe (all in-tree adapters) are unchanged. New: `CreateSessionOptions.SandboxCreator` creates the sandbox lazily.
- **Am I affected?** A custom `harness.Harness` without `GetBootstrap` used with `SandboxConfig.OnBootstrap` / `OnSession`; code that expects an `OnSession` error from `CreateSession`; code that type-asserts `AgentSession.GetSandboxSession()` or `StartOptions.SandboxSession` to its own concrete sandbox type.
- **What to do:** No action unless you rely on setup errors from `CreateSession` or on the concrete sandbox type. To run the setup eagerly, perform a sandbox operation right after `CreateSession`:

  ```go
  session, err := agent.CreateSession(ctx, harness.CreateSessionOptions{SandboxSession: sb})
  if err != nil {
  	return err
  }
  // Runs OnBootstrap / OnSession now for a harness without a bootstrap recipe.
  if _, err := session.GetSandboxSession().Run(ctx, providerutils.SandboxProcessOptions{Command: "true"}); err != nil {
  	return err
  }
  ```
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Denied host tool results carry the execution-denied model output {#harness-denied-tool-result}

- **What changed:** A denied host tool call (filtered builtin, configured denial, denied approval) was submitted to the harness with only `Output`. `ToolResultSubmission.ToolResult` is now `&harness.ModelToolResult{Type: "tool-result", ToolCallID, ToolName, Output: {Type: execution-denied, Reason}}`, and an echoed denial no longer runs the tool's `ToModelOutput` (TS `033f8d9a63`).
- **Am I affected?** Custom `harness.PromptControl.SubmitToolResult` implementations or bridges that inspect `ToolResult` / the `toolResult` frame key for denials; `ToModelOutput` functions that handled an execution-denied value.
- **What to do:** No action unless you treat a non-nil `ToolResult` as a successful result.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Native tool names in tool-call harness metadata {#harness-native-tool-name-metadata}

- **What changed:** When a harness tool call's native name differs from its tool name (Claude Code `Bash` for `bash`), the parsed tool call, its approval request and the step now carry `ProviderMetadata[<harnessID>]["nativeToolName"]`, merged over that harness's existing metadata (TS `e7a8988893`).
- **Am I affected?** Code that compares a harness tool call's whole `ProviderMetadata`, or approval gates keyed on it.
- **What to do:** No action; read `nativeToolName` to gate on native names.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Built-in approval pauses report unechoed host results {#harness-builtin-approval-host-result-pause}

- **What changed:** A turn whose assistant message holds an approval-gated built-in tool and a host tool call used to hang waiting for a host-result echo. It now pauses. Each submitted, unechoed host result is reported through `OnPendingToolResult` with `CompletedResult` (and again at a later pause until the runtime echoes it), and the host tool's outcome is surfaced in the paused step (TS `6eab0f7b11`).
- **Am I affected?** Lifecycle code that persists `PendingToolResults` and assumes only client tools appear there; tests counting `OnPendingToolResult` calls.
- **What to do:** No action; a completed pending result is resubmitted on continue without running the tool again.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Bridge frames sent while the socket closes are queued {#bridge-closing-send-queued}

- **What changed:** `bridge.Channel` wrote a frame whenever it was connected and lost it if the socket was already closing. It now queues the frame while the socket is not open and flushes it in order after the reconnect; a frame whose write fails is requeued and the socket is reconnected (TS `2c12e256d2`).
- **Am I affected?** Custom `bridge.Conn` implementations (they count as open while connected) and bridges that could see a frame sent twice when a failed write had in fact been delivered.
- **What to do:** None.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Claude Code start frame fields {#claudecode-start-frame-fields}

- **What changed:** The claude-code `start` frame now carries `readableSkillRoots` (one `<home>/.claude/skills/<name>` per skill) when skills are present, `deferredToolUseId` on a rerun continuation with a pending built-in approval, and the new settings `skillOverrides`, `maxBudgetUsd`, `strictMcpConfig`, `questionTimeoutMs`, `excludeDynamicSections`, `toolHints` when set (TS `e3f24b634c`, `ede06e32cf`, `09b3e1d166`, `6e3e99969d`, `4ab93bbc91`, `31d83e2bd9`). The embedded bridge is the ai@7.0.162 build (1.0.177), which trusts skill-file reads only under `readableSkillRoots` and asks for approval for file tools outside the work directory.
- **Am I affected?** Custom claude-code bridges that reject unknown start keys.
- **What to do:** None.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Vercel sandbox: policies with response rules are not mutated {#vercel-response-rules}

- **What changed:** `SetNetworkPolicy`, `SetRequestTransformations` and `AddRequestTransformations` used to rebuild the policy without native response (deny) rules; they now fail with `HarnessCapabilityUnsupportedError` when the current policy or the session metadata has response rules (TS `44a4261e52`). A custom (object) session policy in the API's `{mode, allowedDomains}` shape is now read as that allowlist instead of allow-all. A custom `vercel.PolicySandbox` implementation must also implement `vercel.PolicySessionInspector` before an object policy it did not create can be mutated.
- **Am I affected?** Resumed Vercel sandboxes whose policy was set with the native SDK; implementations of `vercel.PolicySandbox`.
- **What to do:** Manage such policies with the native SDK, or implement `CurrentSessionNetworkPolicyMetadata(ctx) (json.RawMessage, error)` on your PolicySandbox.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel sandbox: template v3, persistent live sandboxes, relative working directories, cancel kills {#vercel-sandbox-r85}

- **What changed:** Template snapshots are named `ai-sdk-harness-v3-...` (TS `177291adce`; the first session per project builds a new template); `Persistent` reaches the live sandbox (TS `ea39523ce9`); a relative `WorkingDirectory` resolves against the session cwd instead of `/` (TS `5c247e9b85`); cancelling the ctx passed to `Spawn` kills the remote command (TS `269f4c0c32`).
- **Am I affected?** Vercel sandbox users (`pkg/harness/sandbox/vercel`), especially callers passing a relative `WorkingDirectory` or a short-lived ctx to `Spawn`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you passed a request-scoped ctx to `Spawn` for a process that must outlive it; pass a long-lived ctx.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Cursor client-app version {#cursor-client-app-r86}

- **What changed:** The Cursor adapter reports client app `ai-sdk-harness-cursor/1.0.88` (TS @ai-sdk/harness-cursor at ai@7.0.162) instead of `1.0.78` (review round 86).
- **Am I affected?** Only if you match `cursor.ClientAppVersion` or the client-app string Cursor sessions send.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### ACP tool calls named with an empty string {#acp-toolcall-hasname}

- **What changed:** An ACP tool call reported with `name: ""` now counts as named (TS `toolCall.name != null`): `githubcopilot.ResolveHostToolCall` and `acp.LegacyResolveHostToolCall` no longer resolve it by title. New `acp.ToolCall.HasName()` (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Custom resolvers testing `ToolCall.Name != ""`.
- **What to do:** Use `opts.ToolCall.HasName()`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenCode enterprise domain is the WHATWG host {#opencode-normalize-domain}

- **What changed:** The GitHub Copilot enterprise URL host in the OpenCode subscription is the WHATWG `new URL().host` (IDNA: `Bücher.example` becomes punycode, IPv4 forms: `0x7f.1` becomes `127.0.0.1`, percent-decoded host); the fallback strips one `http(s)://` prefix (review round 86b).
- **Am I affected?** Enterprise URLs with non-ASCII or numeric hosts.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenCode GitLab brokering is limited to the chat token exchange {#opencode-gitlab-chat-route}

- **What changed:** v0.5 injected the real `GITLAB_TOKEN` into any sandbox request to the GitLab host carrying the placeholder bearer; v0.6 only into `POST <GITLAB_INSTANCE_URL or https://gitlab.com>/api/v4/ai/third_party_agents/direct_access` with the exact placeholder `Authorization` header (TS 138d66eb78, security).
- **Am I affected?** `opencode.Settings{Provider: "gitlab"}` with `GITLAB_TOKEN` in a brokering sandbox, code in the sandbox calling other GitLab APIs.
- **What to do:** No action unless sandboxed code relied on the token for other GitLab APIs; it now gets the placeholder.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Claude Code turn start timeout and safe start retries {#claude-code-turn-start-timeout}

- **What changed:** v0.5 waited forever for a claude-code turn's first bridge event. v0.6 fails the turn after `claudecode.Settings.TurnStartTimeout` (default 120 s) with `claude-code bridge did not emit a turn event within 120000ms.`, sends `abort` and closes the bridge connection. A start lost on a reconnect is resent once (same `startId`) when the bridge advertises `idempotentStart`; an older bridge that is waiting for a start fails the turn with `claude-code bridge is waiting for a start after reconnect and does not support safe start retries.` (TS 3bf70f83bc). Mid-turn user messages sent while disconnected are replayed once after the start instead of also being queued.
- **Am I affected?** `claudecode.New`, `claudecode.Settings`, turns that legitimately emit no event for over 120 s (for example a very slow first model response), hosts that inspect the `start` frame (`startId`).
- **What to do:** No action unless a first event can take longer than 120 s: set `claudecode.Settings{TurnStartTimeout: 10 * time.Minute}`. There is no value that disables it; as in TS, a value below 1 ms (negative included) fails the turn right after the start is sent.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### `bridge.WithBridgeToken` serializes a WHATWG URL {#bridge-token-whatwg-url}

- **What changed:** `bridge.WithBridgeToken` now parses and serializes the endpoint URL like TS `new URL()` (IDNA host, dot segments, `\` as `/`, a leading-zero port normalized, invalid UTF-8 in the existing query replaced). Ordinary URLs are unchanged (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** `bridge.WithBridgeToken` callers and adapters whose sandbox endpoint URL has a non-ASCII host, `..` segments, backslashes or a port like `:0081`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you compare the returned URL string.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Credential brokering and host URLs use WHATWG parsing {#harness-credential-urls-whatwg}

- **What changed:** Credential brokering match hosts and paths, GitHub Copilot host normalization, inline prompt image data URLs and Vercel port endpoints use WHATWG URL parsing (IDNA hosts, dot segments, lowercase hosts) like TS `new URL` (review round 87).
- **Am I affected?** `harnessutil.CreateCredentialRequestTransformation`, base URLs with non-ASCII hosts or `..` segments, `COPILOT_GH_HOST` / `GH_HOST`, `NetworkSession.GetPortEndpoint`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Embedded bridges at TS 3fab50aa9b {#harness-bridges-3fab50aa9b}

- **What changed:** The embedded bridges are TS `3fab50aa9b`: a repeated `startId` is dropped and `bridge-hello` advertises `idempotentStart`; the claude-code bridge maps dotted `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_{OPUS,SONNET,HAIKU}_MODEL` gateway ids (TS 3bf70f83bc, a9a6fe7200). `bridges.ManifestData` adapters gain `ContentHash`.
- **Am I affected?** claude-code with those variables set.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel mode policies go out as `{"mode": ...}` {#vercel-network-policy-mode-wire}

- **What changed:** `AllowAllNetworkPolicy()` / `DenyAllNetworkPolicy()` go out as `{"mode":"allow-all"}` / `{"mode":"deny-all"}` on create, update and the session policy request, not a bare string (`@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0 `toAPINetworkPolicy`).
- **Am I affected?** `vercel.CreateSessionOptions.NetworkPolicy`, `CreateParams.NetworkPolicy`, `Sandbox.UpdateNetworkPolicy`, `UpdateParams.NetworkPolicy` with a mode policy.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Vercel `Sandbox.Update` updates the session first {#vercel-policy-update-order}

- **What changed:** `Sandbox.Update` with a policy updates the running session first and PATCHes the sandbox after; it skips the session for a stopped, stopping, aborted or failed session (`@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0 `Sandbox.update`).
- **Am I affected?** `vercel.Sandbox.Update` / `UpdateNetworkPolicy`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the PATCH happening when the session rejects the policy.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel Sandbox API retries and stream recovery {#vercel-sandbox-retries-and-streams}

- **What changed:** Vercel Sandbox API requests are retried twice after a network error, 429 or 5xx (`with-retry.js`); interrupted logs streams are re-read and a blocking command whose stream drops falls back to the logs and command endpoints; JSON Lines streams fail on a whitespace-only line, an unknown log stream, or a content type other than `application/x-ndjson`; `ReadFile` requires `application/octet-stream`; `Spawn` accepts `Stdin` (`@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0).
- **Am I affected?** `vercel.APIClient`, `Sandbox.RunCommand`, `SpawnCommand`, `Session.Run` / `Spawn`, `ReadFile`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel Sandbox request bodies follow @vercel/sandbox 3.9.0 {#vercel-sandbox-request-bodies}

- **What changed:** Run-command bodies carry `"args":[]`, `"env":{}`, `"sudo":false`; create carries `"ports":[]`; a snapshot without an expiration has no body; sandbox names in paths use `encodeURIComponent` (`@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0).
- **Am I affected?** Code inspecting Vercel API traffic.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### OpenCode keeps present empty credentials {#opencode-empty-credentials}

- **What changed:** An OpenCode credential or URL that is set but empty is now used as given, like TS's `== null` / `??` checks. Examples: `GITLAB_TOKEN=""`, `XAI_API_KEY=""`, `GITHUB_COPILOT_TOKEN=""`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=""`, `OPENAI_BASE_URL=""`, `GITLAB_AI_GATEWAY_URL=""`, and an empty `accountId` / `enterpriseUrl` in the OpenCode auth store. These empty values now build the brokering rule (it can only send the given empty value), are forwarded, or are written to `OPENCODE_AUTH_CONTENT`. An empty base URL fails with `Invalid URL`, as in TS. The truthy TS checks are unchanged: the AI Gateway key, the OpenAI key, the Google key, `pickOpenAI` / `pickAnthropic`, and `ChatGPT-Account-Id` (opencode-auth.ts, opencode-subscription.ts, opencode-harness.ts; review round 87b).
- **Am I affected?** You set one of `GITLAB_TOKEN`, `XAI_API_KEY`, `XAI_BASE_URL`, `GITHUB_COPILOT_TOKEN`, `POE_API_KEY`, `OPENCODE_API_KEY`, `GITLAB_INSTANCE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `GITLAB_AI_GATEWAY_URL` or `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` to an empty string for the `opencode` harness, or your OpenCode `auth.json` has `"accountId": ""` or `"enterpriseUrl": ""`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you set those variables to `""` to mean unset. Unset them instead.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel Sandbox.Update extends a running session's timeout {#vercel-update-extends-timeout}

- **What changed:** `Sandbox.Update` with an `UpdateParams.TimeoutMs` larger than the running session's timeout first sends `POST /v2/sandboxes/sessions/{id}/extend-timeout` with the difference. A 410 or a 422 `sandbox_stopping` response is ignored; any other error fails before the session policy update and the PATCH. The session timeout is then tracked from the API responses (`@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0 `Sandbox.update`, review round 87b).
- **Am I affected?** `vercel.Sandbox.Update` with `UpdateParams.TimeoutMs`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### A Vercel OIDC token sets the team {#vercel-jwt-claims}

- **What changed:** When the Vercel Sandbox token is a JWT whose payload has a truthy `owner_id`, the client sends that `owner_id` as the `teamId` query of every request, also when an explicit `TeamID` is passed (`@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0 `APIClient` constructor). The explicit `ProjectID` is still the one sent in request bodies and queries; the token's `project_id` only feeds the opt-in OIDC refresh (review round 87b).
- **Am I affected?** `vercel.Credentials{Token: <OIDC JWT>, TeamID: ...}` or `vercel.NewAPIClient` with a team that differs from the token's `owner_id`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you pass an OIDC token together with a different team.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The Vercel Sandbox reads VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN_FILE {#vercel-oidc-token-file}

- **What changed:** With no explicit credentials, `pkg/harness/sandbox/vercel` reads the Vercel OIDC token from the file named by `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN_FILE` on each credential lookup, ahead of `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN`, like `@vercel/oidc` 4.0.0, which `@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0 pins. v0.5 read only `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN`. A missing or empty token file is an authentication failure (cause `The Vercel OIDC token file is empty: <path>`, or the file read error). With `OIDCRefresh`, the request context token still comes first, and the 4.0.0 error texts apply (`The 'x-vercel-oidc-token' header is missing from the request.`, `Invalid token.`). The AI Gateway (`gateway.VercelOIDCTokenFunc`) keeps `@vercel/oidc` 3.2.0, which TS `@ai-sdk/gateway` pins, and does not read the file (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN_FILE` set in the environment of a program that calls `vercel.ResolveCredentials`, `vercel.CreateNetworkSandboxSession`, `vercel.ResumeNetworkSandboxSession` or `vercel.NewLegacyProvider` without explicit `Credentials`.
- **What to do:** No action unless `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN_FILE` and `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` name different tokens: the file now wins.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel OIDC token decoding follows getCredentialsFromOIDCToken {#vercel-oidc-token-decoding}

- **What changed:** The Vercel Sandbox decodes the OIDC token like `@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0: `getTokenPayload` first (three dot-separated parts, a JSON payload), then `getCredentialsFromOIDCToken` (the second part decoded as lenient base64, string `owner_id` and `project_id` claims that may be empty, numeric `exp` / `iat` when present). Empty claims are now accepted (v0.5 rejected them). Every failure is the TS `LocalOidcContextError` (`Could not get credentials from OIDC context. ...`, or the `VercelOidcContextError` text when `VERCEL_URL` is set) and an authentication failure (`*harness.SandboxAuthenticationError` from the session functions); the cause carries the TS reason (`\nInvalid token.`, `Invalid Vercel OIDC token: <zod issues>`). v0.5 returned `vercel sandbox: invalid VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN ...` / `... missing owner_id/project_id claims` errors that were not authentication failures. `isExpired` (`@vercel/oidc` 3.2.0 and 4.0.0) also reads `exp` with JS number conversion (`"1"`, `true`, `null` count as expired) and fails for a `null` payload; this reaches the opt-in refresh and `gateway.VercelOIDCTokenFunc` (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** Code that matches the text `invalid VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` or `missing owner_id/project_id claims` from `vercel.ResolveCredentials`, or that relies on a token with empty claims being rejected.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the v0.5 error texts. Match `*harness.SandboxAuthenticationError` with `errors.As` (or the `Could not get credentials from OIDC context.` text) instead.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Vercel Sandbox request queries keep empty values and the TS order {#vercel-query-empty-values}

- **What changed:** Every Vercel Sandbox API request sends `teamId` first and always, also when it is empty (`teamId=`), then the call's query keys in the `@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0 order (`getSandbox`: `projectId`, `resume`; `listSnapshots`: `project`, `name`, `cursor`, `sortOrder`; `getCommand`: `wait`), with empty values kept (`projectId=`). v0.5 dropped empty values and sorted the keys. The createSandbox body always has `"projectId"` (`""` for an empty project). Snapshot listing follows `pagination.next` while it is not null (also after an empty page or with an empty cursor), and the template snapshot poll stops listing at the first created manual snapshot (review round 87c).
- **Am I affected?** Proxies or test servers that match the exact Vercel Sandbox request URL or require a non-empty `teamId` / `projectId`; `vercel.APIClient.ListSnapshots` callers whose server returns an empty page with a next cursor.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the request URL or query order.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Vercel network policy inspection lists sessions {#vercel-policy-inspection-list-sessions}

- **What changed:** Before mutating a custom network policy it did not create, the Vercel network policy manager lists the sandbox's sessions (`GET /v2/sandboxes/sessions?teamId&project&name[&cursor]`, every page until the current session) and checks that session's network policy for response rules, as TS; v0.6 drafts read the last session response (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Tokens without access to the sessions listing; tests stubbing the Vercel API.
- **What to do:** No action unless you stub the API: serve `GET /v2/sandboxes/sessions`.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Vercel snapshot listing follows every page {#vercel-snapshot-pagination}

- **What changed:** `ListSnapshots` and the template snapshot poll follow every page (`pagination.next` until null), like TS; they stopped after 20 pages (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Accounts with more than 20 pages of snapshots.
- **What to do:** Bound the call with a context.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel array owner_id sends one teamId per element {#vercel-owner-id-array}

- **What changed:** A JWT whose `owner_id` claim is an array sends one `teamId` query per element (none for `[]`), and a `null` element fails every request with `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'toString')`, as `@vercel/sandbox` 3.9.0; Go sent one comma-joined `teamId` (review round 87e).
- **Am I affected?** Vercel tokens with an array `owner_id` claim.
- **What to do:** No action (Vercel issues string owners).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### A pending host tool approval pauses the turn without waiting for other host results {#harness-host-approval-pause}

- **What changed:** A turn with a pending custom host tool approval waited at the step end for the runtime to echo the other host tool results, which could deadlock, and the raw call of a settled host approval was not kept, so a later pause lost its completed result. v0.6 pauses as soon as any approval is pending and keeps the settled call (TS 8e8033a73d, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `harness.HarnessAgent` runs with host tools whose `ToolApproval` is `user-approval`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### claude-code turns keep only their own bridge events {#claude-code-turn-start-id}

- **What changed:** Late events of an aborted claude-code turn reached the next turn (a stream error, a turn finishing without its answer, a wrong conversation id). v0.6 tags each start with a `startId`: a turn keeps only the events of its own start, prompt and rerun turns send a fresh id, attached or replayed continuations keep the persisted one (saved as `bridge.startId` in the resume / continue state), and a bridge with `idempotentStart` gets a pending start resent on every reconnect. A bridge frame whose `startId` is present but not a string is an error part, as TS's frame schema rejects it (TS 5a05735ed0, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `claudecode` sessions with several turns, especially after an abort; code that reads the claude-code resume state.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Bridge suspend closes the WebSocket with the reason suspended {#bridge-suspend-close-reason}

- **What changed:** Detaching or suspending a session closed the bridge WebSocket without a reason, so a bridge started with `--host-disconnect-timeout-ms` aborted a suspended turn. v0.6 closes with status 1000 and reason `suspended` (TS a54f0fd8c6, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** Custom bridge implementations that read the close frame.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### claude-code apiKeyHelper runs without sh on PATH {#claude-code-apikeyhelper-shell}

- **What changed:** The `apiKeyHelper` command ran through `sh -c` and failed when `sh` was not on PATH (Windows PowerShell or cmd). v0.6 falls back to the platform shell (`%ComSpec% /d /s /c`, or `/bin/sh -c`) only when `sh` is not found (TS 7a5a9c6c50, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** claude-code `apiKeyHelper` on hosts without `sh`.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness bridges are resynced to ai@7.0.165 {#harness-bridges-0f3a85f92d}

- **What changed:** The vendored bridges are the TS 0f3a85f92d builds: Deep Agents and OpenCode ask before file access outside the work directory (except active skill directories, sent as `readableSkillRoots`), claude-code no longer loads project `.claude/` settings, hooks, skills or `CLAUDE.md`, Codex honors `codexConfig.model_reasoning_summary`, OpenCode reaches Google and Mistral models through the AI Gateway with the openai-compatible driver, and claude-code maps a dotted `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`. Deep Agents gets `--home-dir` and a `delete` built-in tool (kind edit). Adapter version constants follow the TS packages (TS bb8aa09ec8, 3af9d06557, e4a6112776, f779a62cc9, c7ebb5653a, 95c1307de5; review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `deepagents.CreateDeepAgents`, `opencode.CreateOpenCode`, `claudecode`, `codex`, code that lists `deepagents.BuiltinTools()`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on unprompted outside-workdir access or project-level claude-code configuration.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Grok Build file reads need ACP approval {#grok-build-read-approval}

- **What changed:** Grok Build read files without asking. v0.6 adds `ask = ["Read"]` to `$GROK_HOME/config.toml` (else `~/.grok/config.toml`) in the sandbox before the bridge starts, so reads go through ACP approval (Grok 1.0.49 sends no `locations`, so reads inside the work directory ask too). An invalid TOML file, or a `permission` / `permission.ask` of the wrong type, fails the start (TS deeb2bfb62, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `grokbuild.CreateGrokBuild` sessions.
- **What to do:** No action unless your sandbox config has a non-table `permission`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Cursor and Grok Build ignore repository configs, hooks and MCP servers {#repo-config-isolation}

- **What changed:** Cursor runs with `--disable-project-configs`, and Grok Build sets `GROK_CLAUDE_HOOKS_ENABLED`, `GROK_CLAUDE_MCPS_ENABLED`, `GROK_CURSOR_HOOKS_ENABLED` and `GROK_CURSOR_MCPS_ENABLED` to `false`, so a repository cannot inject hooks or MCP servers (TS e4a6112776, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `cursor` and `grokbuild` sessions on repositories that ship their own hooks or MCP servers.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on repository hooks or MCP servers.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### OpenCode on Windows reads the XDG auth store first {#opencode-windows-auth-store}

- **What changed:** On Windows the OpenCode auth store is read from `%HOME%\.local\share\opencode\auth.json` first; `~/.opencode/auth.json` is only a fallback for providers missing there (TS 89e6bc94d7, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** `opencode` subscription credentials on Windows hosts.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel Sandbox and MCP transports drop credentials on cross-origin redirects {#redirect-credentials-cross-origin}

- **What changed:** The Vercel Sandbox API client and the MCP HTTP / SSE transports with `Redirect: MCPRedirectFollow` (and no caller `CheckRedirect`) drop `Authorization` once a redirect changes origin (port or subdomain included) and keep it dropped for the rest of the chain, as fetch does; the Vercel OIDC refresh transport no longer adds the token and team query to redirect hops (S2, review round 88).
- **Am I affected?** Vercel Sandbox or MCP endpoints that redirect to another origin and expect the credential there.
- **What to do:** No action unless your endpoint redirects to another origin; point the configuration at the final origin.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### claude-code skips apiKeyHelper when a credential is set {#claude-code-apikeyhelper-direct-credential}

- **What changed:** With `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` set (auto-detected authentication), v0.5 still ran the `apiKeyHelper` command from `~/.claude/settings.json` and used its key for the other variable. v0.6 does not run the helper then, as TS `resolveClaudeCodeAuthentication` does; it runs only when no direct credential and no native subscription exists. The settings key must be exactly `apiKeyHelper`, as TS `JSON.parse` reads it (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** claude-code with an `apiKeyHelper` and one of those variables set, or a settings file spelling the key differently.
- **What to do:** No action unless you relied on the helper's key next to an environment key; then set both variables yourself.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### HarnessAgent PrepareCall tools keep the harness builtins {#harness-preparecall-tools-builtins}

- **What changed:** v0.5 used `PrepareCallResult.Tools` as the turn's whole tool set, so a PrepareCall that returned only your tools dropped the harness builtins. v0.6 merges the returned tools over the builtins (yours win on a name clash), as TS `_prepareTurnSettings` does (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `harness.AgentSettings.PrepareCall` returning `PrepareCallResult{Tools: ...}` without the builtins.
- **What to do:** No action unless you removed a builtin by leaving it out; use `InactiveTools` / `ActiveTools` for that.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### HarnessAgent warns when the Sandbox setting is used {#harness-sandbox-deprecated}

- **What changed:** `harness.AgentSettings.Sandbox` is deprecated, as TS `sandbox` is: NewAgent logs ``HarnessAgent: `Sandbox` is deprecated. Supply `SandboxSession` to CreateSession() instead.`` (standard `log` package). It still works (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `harness.AgentSettings{Sandbox: ...}`.
- **What to do:** Pass the sandbox per session (`CreateSessionOptions.SandboxSession` or `SandboxCreator`), or ignore the log line.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### HarnessAgent error texts {#harness-agent-error-texts}

- **What changed:** An `Output` whose ResponseFormat fails returns that error unchanged (v0.5 wrapped it as `harness: output.ResponseFormat failed: ...`), and a non-nil empty `Messages` list reports ``HarnessAgent: messages must contain at least one `role: "user"` entry.`` (v0.5: ``either `prompt` or `messages` is required.``), as TS (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code matching those error strings.
- **What to do:** No action unless you match the old text.
- **Kind:** error text only

### Vercel legacy sandbox provider warns and honors a cancelled ctx {#vercel-legacy-provider-deprecation-warnings}

- **What changed:** `vercel.NewLegacyProvider` logs the two TS deprecation warnings through `harnessutil.Warn`; `CreateSession` / `ResumeSession` return `ctx.Err()` for an already-cancelled ctx, also when wrapping a caller-supplied sandbox (TS throwIfAborted) (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** `vercel.NewLegacyProvider`, `LegacyProvider.CreateSession`, `LegacyProvider.ResumeSession`.
- **What to do:** No action unless you need silence; replace `harnessutil.Warn` to capture or drop the warnings.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Vercel sandbox reads treat file-not-found errors as missing files {#vercel-sandbox-file-not-found}

- **What changed:** `ReadBinaryFile` on a Vercel sandbox session treats an error whose message says the file is missing as a missing file (TS isFileNotFoundError) and checks the context first (review round 89).
- **Am I affected?** Code that expected an error from reading a missing file there.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Codex: an empty CODEX_HOME and empty refresh tokens {#codex-home-empty}

- **What changed:** `CODEX_HOME=""` (set but empty) now means the working directory, and a ChatGPT auth file with an empty `refresh_token` is usable, as TS (`??`, any string) (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** `codex` native subscription with `CODEX_HOME` set to the empty string.
- **What to do:** Unset `CODEX_HOME` instead of setting it to `""`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness auth files and lifecycle state follow TS {#harness-r90-details}

- **What changed:** A refreshed Codex or OpenCode auth store keeps the stored key order (TS object spread) instead of sorted keys; ACP lifecycle state with a null `agentIdentity` / `configurationFingerprint` is rejected (zod `z.string()`) (review round 90).
- **Am I affected?** Code reading the rewritten auth files byte for byte, or ACP lifecycle state with null strings.
- **What to do:** No action.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness file parts stay file parts {#harness-tool-output-file-part}

- **What changed:** `NormalizeToolModelOutput` turned an inline image file part into an `ImageContentBlock` and lost its filename, and kept a legacy image-data part as an `ImageContentBlock`. v0.6 returns a `FileContentBlock` with inline data for both (a file's filename and provider options kept), as TS normalize-harness-tool-model-output.ts (review rounds 91, 92).
- **Am I affected?** `NormalizeToolModelOutput` results checked for `ImageContentBlock`.
- **What to do:** Handle `FileContentBlock`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### Harness bridges authenticate with a per-session public key {#bridge-public-key-auth}

- **What changed:** Bridge-backed adapters (claude-code, acp, cursor, github-copilot, grok-build, codex, opencode, deepagents) authenticated the in-sandbox bridge with a shared token. v0.6 uses a per-session Ed25519 key pair: the sandbox gets only `BRIDGE_AUTH_PUBLIC_KEY` (no `BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOKEN`, no `agent_bridge_token` in the URL), resume state stores `bridge.privateKey` instead of `bridge.token`, and `MintBridgeToken` is deprecated, ignored and warned once at creation; the "cannot use mintBridgeToken ... without an id" error is gone (TS c849d37dac). Token state saved by an older version is rejected, except Claude Code state, which is resumed by respawning the bridge (review round 91).
- **Am I affected?** `MintBridgeToken`, stored resume / continue state, custom bridges or proxies that read the channel token.
- **What to do:** Drop `MintBridgeToken`; start new sessions instead of resuming old token state; treat stored resume state as a secret (it holds the private key).
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Harness turns with an output follow the TS output stage {#harness-output-stage}

- **What changed:** A harness turn with `ExternalStreamOptions.Output` (or a `HarnessAgent` with an output) parsed each step's whole text for `PartialOutput()`, reset it per step, and passed every text delta on unchanged. v0.6 takes `PartialOutput()` from the turn's first text block (not reset per step), publishes only changed values, and groups that block's text deltas with each changed partial, flushing them at text-end and before finish-step, as TS HarnessStreamTextResult (review round 92).
- **Am I affected?** Code that reads `PartialOutput()` or text deltas of a harness turn that sets an Output.
- **What to do:** No action unless you depend on per-delta grouping or on partials from a later step's text.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### askUserQuestions input and output follow the TS schema {#harness-questions-tool-schema}

- **What changed:** The reserved `askUserQuestions` tool accepted `null` for its optional input keys (`header`, `options`, `allowMultiple`, `allowFreeForm`, an option's `description` / `preview`, an answer's `freeform`) and decoded its output with case-insensitive keys (`Action`, `OptionIds`). v0.6 rejects `null` there and a non-boolean `allowFreeForm`, as TS `.optional()`, and matches output keys exactly (review round 96).
- **Am I affected?** Harness hosts or models that send `null` for those keys or differently cased output keys.
- **What to do:** Leave optional keys out instead of sending `null`.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Policy {#policy}

### An OPA HTTP policy response without result is not-applicable {#opa-undefined-result}

- **What changed:** OPA answers an undefined decision (no rule matched) with
  `{}`. v0.5 treated that as a deny. As in TS, it is now not-applicable, and
  the tool call falls through to the other approval rules.
- **Am I affected?** OPA policies that rely on "no match means deny".
- **What to do:** return an explicit deny, for example with a Rego `default`
  rule.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Already changed in v0.5.3 {#already-in-v053}

These changes shipped in v0.5.3. They affect you only if you upgrade from
v0.5.0, v0.5.1 or v0.5.2. The [wrapper API key change](#wrapper-api-keys)
started in v0.5.3 too.

### Cohere uses the v2 API (v0.5.3) {#v053-cohere-v2}

- **What changed:** chat, embed and rerank use the v2 API under the base URL.
  `cohere.DefaultBaseURL` is `https://api.cohere.com/v2` (was
  `https://api.cohere.ai/v1`). A custom `BaseURL` without a version segment
  gets `/v2` appended; one ending in `/v1` or `/v2` is used as is. Embed sends
  `embedding_types: ["float"]`.
- **Am I affected?** A custom Cohere `BaseURL`, or a proxy that only forwards
  v1 paths.
- **What to do:** make sure the endpoint serves the v2 API.
- **Kind:** wire/protocol change

### Every provider retries (v0.5.3) {#v053-retries}

- **What changed:** `GenerateText`, `StreamText`, agents and `GenerateObject`
  retried only AI Gateway models. They now retry every provider (default 2
  retries), honor `ProviderError.Retryable` and retry connection failures.
  408, 409, 429 and 5xx are retried; 400 and 401 are not.
- **Am I affected?** Calls that must not be repeated, or your own retry loop
  around SDK calls.
- **What to do:** set `MaxRetries` to a pointer to 0 to disable SDK retries.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### The default HTTP client has no whole-request timeout (v0.5.3) {#v053-http-timeout}

- **What changed:** the default client had a 60 s whole-request timeout, which
  cut off long streams. It now has no whole-request timeout and a 5 minute
  response header timeout.
- **Am I affected?** Code that relied on the 60 s limit to bound calls.
- **What to do:** bound each call with its context:

  ```go
  ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 60*time.Second)
  defer cancel()
  _ = ctx
  ```

- **Kind:** silent behavior change

### A call to an unknown tool does not stop the loop (v0.5.3) {#v053-unknown-tool}

- **What changed:** a call to an unknown tool is answered with a tool error and
  the `GenerateText` / `StreamText` loop continues. v0.5.2 stopped.
- **Am I affected?** Code that relied on the run stopping.
- **What to do:** no action unless you relied on the stop; use `StopWhen` or
  `RepairToolCall` to control it.
- **Kind:** silent behavior change

## Deprecated (still compiles) {#deprecated}

These identifiers are new deprecations in v0.6.0. They keep working; move to
the replacement when convenient. `staticcheck` (SA1019) reports their use.

| Deprecated | Replacement |
| --- | --- |
| `ai.ExperimentalEvaluate`, `ai.EvaluateOptions`, `ai.EvaluateResult`, `ai.EvaluateUsage`, `ai.EvaluateResponse`, `ai.EvaluateOnStartEvent`, `ai.EvaluateOnEndEvent` | `ai.ExperimentalDecide`, `ai.DecideOptions`, `ai.DecideResult`, `ai.DecideUsage`, `ai.DecideResponse`, `ai.DecideOnStartEvent`, `ai.DecideOnEndEvent` |
| `ai.EvaluationLanguageModel`, `ai.EvaluationLanguageModelOptions`, `ai.NewEvaluationLanguageModel`, `(*ai.DecisionLanguageModel).DoEvaluate` | `ai.DecisionLanguageModel`, `ai.DecisionLanguageModelOptions`, `ai.NewDecisionLanguageModel`, `DoDecide` |
| `provider.EvaluationQuestion`, `EvaluationAnswer`, `EvaluationRounding`, `EvaluationUsage`, `EvaluationResponseInfo`, `EvaluationCallOptions`, `EvaluationResult` | `provider.DecisionQuestion`, `DecisionAnswer`, `DecisionRounding`, `DecisionUsage`, `DecisionResponseInfo`, `DecisionCallOptions`, `DecisionResult` |
| `provider.EvaluationModel`, `provider.EvaluationModelProvider`, `AdaptEvaluationModel`, `AsEvaluationModel`, `AdaptEvaluationModelProvider` | `provider.DecisionModel`, `provider.DecisionModelProvider` |
| `ai.DecideOptions.Provider` / `ai.EvaluateOptions.Provider` (`provider.EvaluationModelProvider`, unchanged from v0.5) | `DecisionProvider` (`provider.DecisionModelProvider`); it wins when both are set |
| `provider.RegisterEvaluationModelDeserializer`, `DeserializeEvaluationModel`, `RegisteredEvaluationModelDeserializerKeys`, `SerializeEvaluationModel` | `RegisterDecisionModelDeserializer`, `DeserializeDecisionModel`, `RegisteredDecisionModelDeserializerKeys`, `SerializeDecisionModel` |
| `providererrors.EvaluationUnsupportedQuestionTypeError`, `IsEvaluationUnsupportedQuestionTypeError` | `DecisionUnsupportedQuestionTypeError`, `IsDecisionUnsupportedQuestionTypeError` |
| `EvaluationModel(modelID)` on the `anthropic`, `gateway`, `google`, `openai` and `typesafeai` providers; `DoEvaluate` on their decision models | `DecisionModel(modelID)`, `DoDecide` |
| `gateway.EvaluationModel`, `gateway.NewEvaluationModel`, `typesafeai.EvaluationModel`, `typesafeai.NewEvaluationModel` | `DecisionModel`, `NewDecisionModel` |
| `gateway.EvaluationFallbackCondition`, `EvaluationFallbackAtLeast`, `EvaluationFallbackMaxConditionDepth`, `EvaluationFallbackMaxConditionsPerList`, `EvaluationFallbackMaxQuestionLength` | `DecisionFallbackCondition`, `DecisionFallbackAtLeast`, `DecisionFallbackMaxConditionDepth`, `DecisionFallbackMaxConditionsPerList`, `DecisionFallbackMaxQuestionLength` |
| `gateway.GatewayEvaluationModelID`, `GatewayEvaluationModelIDs`, `GatewayEvaluationModelLiquidD1`, `GatewayEvaluationModelTypesafeAiJev` | `GatewayDecisionModelID`, `GatewayDecisionModelIDs`, `GatewayDecisionModelLiquidD1`, `GatewayDecisionModelTypesafeAiJev` |
| 26 `gateway.GatewayLanguageModel*` constants removed from the TS Gateway ID list (for example `GatewayLanguageModelAlibabaQwen3Max`) | Any current Gateway model ID; the old strings still work |
| `registry.ResolveEvaluationModel`, `(*Registry).ResolveEvaluationModel`, custom provider `EvaluationModels` / `EvaluationModel` | `ResolveDecisionModel`, `DecisionModels` / `DecisionModel` |
| `telemetry.EvaluateStartEvent`, `EvaluateEndEvent`, `EvaluationModelCallStartEvent`, `EvaluationModelCallEndEvent` | `DecideStartEvent`, `DecideEndEvent`, `DecisionModelCallStartEvent`, `DecisionModelCallEndEvent` |
| `telemetry.FireOnEvaluate*` / `FireOnEvaluationModelCall*`, and `OnEvaluate*` / `OnEvaluationModelCall*` on `LegacyOpenTelemetry` and `OpenTelemetry` | `FireOnDecide*` / `FireOnDecisionModelCall*`, `OnDecide*` / `OnDecisionModelCall*` |
| `telemetry.DiagnosticEventOnEvaluate*`, `DiagnosticEventOnEvaluationModelCall*` | `DiagnosticEventOnDecide*`, `DiagnosticEventOnDecisionModelCall*` |
| `telemetry.OpenTelemetry.ExperimentalEvaluation` | `ExperimentalDecision` |
| `telemetry.TracerName` (unused) | `telemetry.LegacyTracerName`, `telemetry.GenAITracerName`, or your own `Tracer` |
| `acp.TextContentBlock` | `acp.ContentBlock` |
| `harness` stream part `StepToolCallCount` (ignored by the host since TS #23260) | No replacement |
| `openai/tool.WebSearchPreview`, `azure.WebSearchPreview` | `WebSearch` |
| `gateway/tools.ParallelSearchModeOneShot`, `ParallelSearchModeAgentic`, the nested `MaxCharsTotal` option | `ParallelSearchModeBasic`, `ParallelSearchModeAdvanced`, `ParallelSearchConfig.MaxCharsTotal` |
| `openai` transcription stream `StreamingDelay` | `StreamingDelayLevel` |

## Known differences from TS {#known-differences}

A few v0.6.0 behaviors differ from TypeScript on purpose. They are not
bugs. [Known differences from the TypeScript AI SDK](./known-differences.mdx)
lists all of them with the reasons. The ones you are most likely to notice
after upgrading:

- **`ToUIMessageStream` writes a masked `error` chunk** when its source stream
  fails (owner decision 2026-10-29); TS errors the stream instead. The SSE
  helpers then end without `data: [DONE]` and return the error, as TS. See
  [UI message stream: source failure writes an error chunk](./known-differences.mdx).
- **The Vertex Gemini image model accepts URL and `gs://` input images**
  (owner ruling 2026-10-29). Current TS rejects them. Set `MediaType` for a
  URL without an image extension. See
  [Vertex Gemini image model: URL input images](./known-differences.mdx).
- **A `StreamText` tool context failure fails the run** (owner decision
  2026-10-28), as in `GenerateText` and the agents. TS streams an error part
  and finishes. See
  [StreamText tool context failure ends the run](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Gladia sends only `solaria-*` model IDs**; other IDs warn and are not
  sent, as v0.5.2 documented. See
  [Gladia model IDs](./known-differences.mdx).
- **An upload-only video result has a nil `Video`** and `UploadedVideos`
  set; TS throws from its `video` getter. See
  [Upload-only video results](./known-differences.mdx).
- **`codemode.SetMaxWorkers(0)` means unlimited**, kept from v0.5.2; TS
  rejects 0. See
  [Code-mode: never-settling promises and in-flight limits](./known-differences.mdx).
- **`StreamText` returns an invalid `Instructions` value synchronously**, as
  v0.5.2 did; TS reports it through the stream. See
  [Option validation timing](./known-differences.mdx).
- **`WorkflowAgent` root telemetry always follows the TS durable-run report**
  (a `call-…` call ID, final step fields, `onAbort` for an unclosed run);
  outside a Workflow DevKit run TS dispatches live with the literal
  `workflow-agent` call ID. See
  [WorkflowAgent root telemetry outside a durable run](./known-differences.mdx).
- **UI message stream SSE events have sorted JSON keys**, because
  `UIMessageChunk` is a map; TS writes insertion order. The values are the
  same. See [UI message stream JSON key order](./known-differences.mdx).
- **`ToUIMessageStream` frames a raw provider stream** (start, finish-step,
  finish, incomplete-stream error); start-led sources (`StreamText`, harness
  streams) map one to one as in TS. See
  [ToUIMessageStream on a raw provider stream](./known-differences.mdx).
- **`RuntimeContext` stays nil when unset**; TS defaults it to `{}`. See
  [RuntimeContext defaults to nil](./known-differences.mdx).
- **An aborted stream keeps the parts produced before the abort**; TS drops
  parts still queued in its stream pipeline. See
  [Abort keeps the parts produced before it](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Only audited provider tools strip undeclared tool input keys** (the flat
  Anthropic tools); other provider tools keep them, and user tools keep them
  as TS `jsonSchema()` tools do. See
  [Tool call input keeps undeclared keys](./known-differences.mdx).
- **`Reasoning` lists hold reasoning text only**; reasoning file parts are
  returned by `StepResult.ReasoningFiles()`. See
  [Reasoning lists hold reasoning text only](./known-differences.mdx).
- **`ConsumeStream` returns the stream's error**; TS `consumeStream()`
  swallows it. See [ConsumeStream returns the error](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Merged UI message streams interleave by goroutine scheduling**; each
  source keeps its order. See
  [Merged UI message stream order](./known-differences.mdx).
- **UI message metadata followed by a string or array is replaced**; TS
  merges the value's index keys into the object (a number or boolean keeps
  the object, as TS). See
  [Message metadata merge with non-object values](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Anthropic batch results name id-named provider tools after the tool only
  when read through the Provider that started the batch**; elsewhere they
  carry the wire name (`bash`). See
  [Anthropic batch results of id-named provider tools across processes](./known-differences.mdx).
- **The UI message stream end event reports `finishReason` in the v0.5
  shape per helper**: `nil` (or the finish chunk's string) from
  `CreateUIMessageStreamWithOptions`, a `types.FinishReason` (`""` when
  unset) from `ToUIMessageStream`; TS reports the chunk's string or
  `undefined` in both. See
  [UI message stream end event finish reason](./known-differences.mdx).

- **`StreamText` reads the model's stream without a reader**, so the model
  call runs until its ctx ends; TS stalls an unread stream. See
  [StreamText reads the provider stream without a reader](./known-differences.mdx).
- **An invalid tool call's unparsable input is kept only as raw text**
  (`RawArguments`, `ToolCallContent.Input`); the tool-error has no `input`.
  See [Invalid tool call input that is not a JSON object](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Struct-tag schemas make a few Go-only choices** (floats, `time.Time`,
  `[]byte`, described recursive references, `dive` rules). See
  [Struct-tag schemas: Go-only choices](./known-differences.mdx).
- **URL parsing: three IDNA edge cases**: Go's WHATWG URL serializer differs from Node on three classes of `xn--` labels (`xn---xii`, a doubly prefixed label, Arabic Extended-B Bidi) (round 84). See
  [URL parsing: three IDNA edge cases](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Typed URL fields**: a Go URL field that does not parse fails with `Invalid URL`; TS treats such a plain string as base64 data (round 84). See
  [Typed URL fields](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Prompt file download errors**: the default prompt-file downloader keeps the v0.5 `SSRFError` texts and its up-front DNS check; TS reports `DownloadError` texts (round 84). See
  [Prompt file download errors](./known-differences.mdx).
- **TS test replays skipped in round 84**: eight TS cases are skipped for harness limits or Go idiom (azure-keyed OpenAI Responses case, `openai.chat` hint text, chat.ts replay start, a call-site spy, channel errors, provider signal after step end) (round 84). See
  [TS test replays skipped in round 84](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Input checks TS removed in ai@7.0.155**: Go keeps the input checks TS removed in ai@7.0.155, with their v0.5 texts (ruling 5, round 85). See
  [Input checks TS removed in ai@7.0.155](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Durable workflow telemetry**: Durable workflow telemetry: only the operation-name mapping is ported (ruling 6, round 85). See
  [Durable workflow telemetry](./known-differences.mdx).
- **SSE chunk-boundary artifacts**: SSE parsing ignores chunk boundaries where eventsource-parser does not (round 85). See
  [SSE chunk-boundary artifacts](./known-differences.mdx).
- **UI streams close open blocks at a step's finish**: ToUIMessageStream synthesizes text-end / reasoning-end for blocks still open at finish-step (round 85). See
  [UI streams close open blocks at a step's finish](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Google videoMetadata validation errors**: An invalid Google `videoMetadata` is a ValidationError, not a raw ZodError (round 85). See
  [Google videoMetadata validation errors](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Deferred sandbox optional operations**: A deferred harness sandbox session always has Stat / ListDirectory / DeleteFile and reports ErrUnsupported when the sandbox lacks them (round 85). See
  [Deferred sandbox optional operations](./known-differences.mdx).
- **TS test replays skipped in round 85**: TS test replays skipped in round 85: harness limits (class functions, async auth tokens, random ids, a cancelled empty stream, deep-argument truncation) (round 85). See
  [TS test replays skipped in round 85](./known-differences.mdx).
- **ReadUIMessages after a terminating error**: `ReadUIMessages` with `TerminateOnError` calls `OnError` once and stops reading (round 86). See
  [ReadUIMessages after a terminating error](./known-differences.mdx).
- **WebSocket handshake headers with an empty value**: WebSocket handshakes do not send a header whose value is `""` (round 86). See
  [WebSocket handshake headers with an empty value](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Speech translation `TargetLanguage: ""`**: An empty speech translation `TargetLanguage` counts as missing (round 86). See
  [Speech translation `TargetLanguage: ""`](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Serializable tool patterns RE2 cannot compile**: A serializable tool `pattern` with lookaround or a backreference is skipped (RE2) (round 86b). See
  [Serializable tool patterns RE2 cannot compile](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Files results: empty filename on the result struct**: File results: an empty returned `filename` reads as absent on the `Filename` field; the metadata map keeps it (round 86b). See
  [Files results: empty filename on the result struct](./known-differences.mdx).
- **MCP OAuth client authentication parameters**: MCP OAuth: extra `AddClientAuthentication` form parameters are sent in key order (round 86b). See
  [MCP OAuth client authentication parameters](./known-differences.mdx).
- **ElevenLabs stream audio format rate**: ElevenLabs: a fractional native output-format rate has no `Rate` (`*int`) (round 86b). See
  [ElevenLabs stream audio format rate](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Sandbox text: lone surrogates and an empty encoding**: Sandbox text: a UTF-16 lone surrogate reads as U+FFFD and `Encoding: ""` is utf-8 (round 86b). See
  [Sandbox text: lone surrogates and an empty encoding](./known-differences.mdx).
- **AI Gateway OIDC token refresh**: AI Gateway: OIDC refresh is opt-in (`Config.OIDCTokenFunc`), in memory, and never writes or clears the Vercel CLI files (round 87). See
  [AI Gateway OIDC token refresh](./known-differences.mdx).
- **WorkflowAgent ExperimentalTransform**: WorkflowAgent `ExperimentalTransform` still works (deprecated); TS removed it (round 87). See
  [WorkflowAgent ExperimentalTransform](./known-differences.mdx).
- **claude-code TurnStartTimeout zero**: claude-code `TurnStartTimeout: 0` is the 120 s default; a negative value disables it (round 87). See
  [claude-code TurnStartTimeout zero](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Mistral voice agent browser transport**: Mistral voice agent: server session helpers only; the LiveKit browser transport is TS-only (round 87). See
  [Mistral voice agent browser transport](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Realtime transcription WebSocket hint**: Mistral, ElevenLabs, xAI, Vertex Gemini and AI Gateway realtime socket errors have no WebSocket-implementation hint (round 87, widened in round 91). See
  [Realtime transcription WebSocket hint](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Empty string message content**: A user message whose only part is an empty text part loses it; TS keeps a string `content: ''` (round 87). See
  [Empty string message content](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Vercel Sandbox OIDC token refresh**: Vercel Sandbox: OIDC refresh is opt-in (`OIDCRefresh`), in memory, and follows `@vercel/oidc` 3.2.0 (round 87b). See
  [Vercel Sandbox OIDC token refresh](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Vercel Sandbox user agent**: Vercel Sandbox sends the Go SDK user agent, not `vercel/sandbox/<version> (Node.js/...)` (round 87b). See
  [Vercel Sandbox user agent](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Vercel Sandbox JSON Lines errors under backpressure**: Vercel Sandbox: a complete invalid JSON Lines record is always a syntax error after every earlier record (the TS fast-consumer result) (round 87b). See
  [Vercel Sandbox JSON Lines errors under backpressure](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Provider usage and runtime context attributes: colliding keys in a Go map**: Colliding provider usage / runtime context keys in a Go map: the later key in sorted order wins (round 87b). See
  [Provider usage and runtime context attributes: colliding keys in a Go map](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Empty tool and response format descriptions**: A `Description: ""` tool or response format has no `description` (v0.5.2 string fields) (round 87b). See
  [Empty tool and response format descriptions](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Realtime error events with an empty code**: Realtime error events: `code: ""` reads as no code (`RealtimeServerEvent.Code` is a v0.5.2 string) (round 87b). See
  [Realtime error events with an empty code](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Open Responses and AI Gateway: field values of an unexpected JSON type**: Open Responses / AI Gateway: typed fields read a mistyped value as `String(value)` or zero; TS passes it on (round 87b). See
  [Open Responses and AI Gateway: field values of an unexpected JSON type](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Tool output JSON in replayed prompts keeps Go map key order.** See
  [Tool output JSON key order in replayed prompts](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Embedding usage stays a number when a provider sends none** (the sum of
  the reported batches, 0 when none, as v0.5.2); TS returns NaN, and Go drops
  the telemetry attribute where TS's is NaN. See
  [Embedding usage across EmbedMany batches](./known-differences.mdx).
- **The legacy OpenTelemetry integration ends open tool spans on error**; TS leaves them unended. See
  [Legacy telemetry tool spans on error](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Alibaba and Z.ai keep the v0.5 provider option spellings** (alibaba reads
  the `openai-compatible` keys and raw snake_case names; zai forwards `user`
  and `verbosity`). See
  [Provider option spellings kept from v0.5](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Hugging Face byte image data is base64**; TS interpolates the bytes as a
  comma-separated list. See
  [Hugging Face byte image data](./known-differences.mdx).
- **Perplexity unsupported-option warnings follow sorted option names**; TS
  follows the object's insertion order. See
  [Provider option warning order](./known-differences.mdx).

- **DNS protection on by default**: Downloads (prompt files and generated file URLs) stay validated and DNS-pinned by default; TS made the protection opt-in. `DownloadOptions.Transport` is the `createDownload({ fetch })` counterpart (round 91). See
  [DNS protection on by default](./known-differences.mdx).
- **MCP OAuth: DNS protection without a custom fetch**: MCP OAuth hops always use the DNS-pinned transport; there is no runtime-dependent custom-fetch error (round 91). See
  [MCP OAuth: DNS protection without a custom fetch](./known-differences.mdx).
- **toolbash command execution**: `toolbash` spawns commands with bounded output and kills the process group (exit code 124 on timeout) (round 91). See
  [toolbash command execution](./known-differences.mdx).
- **LangSmith deployment stream reconnect**: `LangSmithDeploymentTransport` does not reconnect a dropped run stream; its connection error always says `fetch failed` (round 91). See
  [LangSmith deployment stream reconnect](./known-differences.mdx).
